F is the last NQ/ES trade at or before the board's **content time** c = floor(`asOf`) + `board_content_offset_ms`[era][map] (the measured median moment the board's spot reflects: before `skylit_stamp_change_date` 2026-03-17 QQQ +740, SPY +840, SPX −80 ms; from it QQQ −60, SPY −60, SPX −80 ms; the live stream the same (parity 2026-10-07: live boards = post-03-17 history), SPY's live offset included (owner 2026-10-19: −60 for QQQ and SPY; the four recordings' fitted offsets scatter ±0.4 s around it with no steady shift, findings R15); NDXP's R is QQQ × k, so it reads no futures of its own; findings R4) (Databento trades, history and live alike). The board is applied at max(arrival, τ + `map_latency_sec`), τ = floor(`asOf`) + 1 s (+ 60 s for 1-minute boards the spot-lag test hasn't cleared, §3.1): no measured content time passes τ, so the board is never applied before the market data it holds (owner 2026-10-08). The alignment check measures c on every recording against the futures and alerts if a content time falls after floor(`asOf`) + 1 s. Unit-test the timestamp convention (bar start-vs-end labels are a classic off-by-one bug).
- **Sanity check, not smoothing:** R normally drifts by well under 1 bp per minute, so a sudden jump means a bad input (a glitched or stale `spot`, or the futures price taken at the wrong second), not a real change. A new R is rejected if it differs from the median of the last 5 accepted R values by more than `ratio_outlier_bps` (start 3 bps ≈ 9 NQ pts at 30,000); the update's new strikes are still used, converted with the previous R, and the rejection is logged. **No lock-in:** if `ratio_reaccept_n` (start 3) updates in a row are rejected but agree with each other within the threshold, the shift is real: accept it, reset the median history and flag the day for review. Step 8b reports how often rejections happen; if often, find the cause rather than raising the threshold.
- **Same contract:** F always comes from the session's single front-month contract (the previous-session volume rule, §3.2); a ratio is never carried across a roll.
- **Opening minutes:** SPX/NDXP values right after 09:30 are computed while many stocks haven't opened, so SPX/NDXP levels don't exist until the first update after `ratio_open_skip_sec` (start 120). QQQ/SPY trade at the open and are unaffected. Nothing trades before 09:50 anyway.
- **Rounding:** keep exact floats for features. Round only prices that go into orders (tick 0.25): stops **away** from entry (sizing uses the rounded stop), targets **toward** entry, limit entries to the passive side.
- **Precision gate (step 8b):** at every Skylit update (where the futures price at that second is known), compare the level from the previous update's R with the level from the current R (= the error of a ratio up to one map interval old). Report median / 95th / 99th percentile / worst case in futures points per chain, by time of day and volatility, next to `mapping_p95_max` (1.0 NQ pt for QQQ, 0.5 NQ pt for NDXP, 0.25 ES pt for SPY/SPX): **a diagnostic, not a gate condition** (owner 2026-10-08, §13 Q8b-4; the step change mixes real R drift with the ETF's last-trade noise). **Pass (owner 2026-10-08)**, scored on strike crossings at event moments (relevant nodes) with R known before the crossing (findings R1): (a) **bias**: each chain's median signed error (futures − strike × R) has a 95% day-block bootstrap interval that contains 0 or lies within one futures tick; (b) **estimator**: the chosen estimator's (F1.7) crossing p95 is within 10% of the best possible R's (a centred median, evaluation only); (c) the **alignment check**, in place of spot lag: on every recording, the fitted content time never falls after floor(`asOf`) + 1 s; **and** the owner's Atlas replay check below (the automated Atlas match is a regression check only, §13 Q8b-5). If not met: find the cause (stale `spot`, wrong second, a slow map cadence) before anything else, then re-measure. **Stop floor per level source (owner 2026-10-08):** a stop placed beyond a mapped level uses at least that level's source's mapping error at event moments, the strike-crossing p95 with the chosen estimator (findings R7: most of it remains with the futures still, so it is real level error, not 1-second timing): `stop_buffer_floor` QQQ 4.25 NQ, NDXP 6.50 NQ (QQQ × k N = 20; frozen, owner 2026-10-19, §13 Q8b-frozen; NDXP 6.50 since 2026-10-23, was 6.25; was QQQ 4.0, NDXP pending), chart levels 0 (no mapping); SPY/SPX create no NQ stops. Node bands stay as set (NDXP ±20 until the step-13 reaction-distance report, §5.4b).
- **Dislocation guard (owner 2026-10-14, §13 Q-dislguard; feature_spec F1.8, `features/disloc_guard.py`):** a data-quality guard like the feed-failure rule (§9, §10.3), not a trading rule: it reads only the mapping inputs (futures and spot), and its thresholds are fixed from data quality, never tuned on trade results. Two triggers per chain (QQQ, SPY, SPX, `disl_guard_maps`), either one starts a block, no opening-minutes exception: (1) **per board:** within the chain's trailing `disl_guard_window_sec` (120 s), a level of the board ratios (each board's own F ÷ spot at its apply time) held by two boards within `disl_guard_confirm_sec` (10 s) and an opposite level held by two boards within 10 s are more than `disl_guard_pct` (0.2%) of R apart (a lone spike or dip, or a jump measured from one, never fires); (2) **the bot's R (backstop):** the R in force (F1.7) moves more than 0.2% within the trailing 2 minutes (its range ÷ median). A block runs from the first flagged board until `disl_guard_hold_sec` (60 s) after the last flagged board of either trigger (trailing only: a flag during the hold extends the block forward; a block ends 60 s after its last flag and is never extended or merged in hindsight). Both windows reset only at each session start (owner 2026-10-17, §13 Q-dislguard-2 (b)(i): a board gap does not reset them, so a move made inside a gap is compared with the levels before it). **Features during a block** (owner 2026-10-17, Q-dislguard-2 (a); feature_spec F1.8, `features/block_state.py`): the blocked chain's R-dependent features are null for decisions inside the block, its node-state machines (crossed / flipped / reclaimed, ceiling / floor pause and resume) pause and target selection skips its path nodes; at the block end the paused states are judged at once against the band then in force (a node price is now beyond is crossed at the block end), so a real cross during the block is not missed; the same for QQQ's R-dependent features on events not on QQQ zones. While a chain is blocked: no new entries on zones with a member mapped from that chain (QQQ → its NQ levels; NDXP follows QQQ's block if its R comes from QQQ × k, §13 Q8b-1; SPY/SPX → their ES-mapped levels, which create no NQ event by themselves, §5.2, so their blocks are logged and counted); pending entries at those zones are removed with `exit` as on a feed failure (§5.5b); open trades keep their brackets; levels from the other chains and chart-only zones trade normally. The same `ChainGuard` runs in labels, both simulators and live (rule 4): a pair whose `decision_ts` falls inside a block of a chain in its zone is marked not tradeable (§1.2), never dropped. Every fire is logged with its trigger, chain and size (live: also a Discord note, §9c). `step8b dislocations` counts the fires, the blocks where only the per-board trigger fired (a column, not a verdict) and the false fires on the development and walk-forward months, and on the top-up and demo months once they exist: a **false fire** = a block during which the levels mapped with the bot's R stayed within the chain's tolerance of the levels mapped with a hindsight R (the centred median of the per-board R over ±60 s; report only, never a feature) at every relevant node of every board in the block, i.e. trading would have been safe without it (tolerance: QQQ its `stop_buffer_floor` (4.25 NQ since 2026-10-19, was 4.0); SPY/SPX have none, so the closest existing setting, `atlas_match_tol` ES (0.80 since 2026-10-19, was 0.78), the frozen SPY crossing p95; owner 2026-10-14); revisit the guard if false fires exceed about one a week (findings R13: months 1–6, QQQ 5 blocks on 3 days, SPY 1, SPX 2; false alarms QQQ 2025-11-18 and the 2026-03-30 open).
- **Validation:** (1) unit tests with hand-computed levels, band conversion and rounding directions; (2) bar/snapshot alignment test; (3) **Atlas match (owner's charts):** the owner reads Atlas on **QQQ and SPY** charts, always the front (0DTE) expiration, matching `maxExpirations=1`, and knows NQ and ES reach the same levels at the same time (owner, 2026-10). So for minutes the owner picks, Claude Code lists the QQQ/SPY strikes the bot treats as relevant nodes (strike and % of King; node types are added at step 12, once step 11 builds the classifier) and the moments QQQ/SPY traded at each strike next to the NQ/ES price at the bot's mapped level then (owner 2026-10-08, §13 Q8b-5: the futures-vs-mapped difference is information only, a regression check with `atlas_match_tol` frozen at the crossing p95, QQQ 4.05 NQ / SPY 0.80 ES (owner 2026-10-19, §13 Q8b-frozen; was 4.0 / 0.78), alerting if fewer than `atlas_regression_min_share` (90%) of the crossings pooled over all sample days fall within it; per-day shares are information only; baseline 2026-10-11 with `map_latency_sec` 0.59 and §5.2's relevance rule with the 20% hold (owner 2026-10-11): QQQ 94.6% of 519, SPY 94.6% of 574 (2026-10-09 with a plain ≥ 25% per board: 94.2% of 500 / 95.2% of 495); the tables mark nodes listed only because of the 20% hold ("held"), and a crossing counts only while its node is still relevant on the board in force then, as in the gate); the owner checks in Atlas replay that the same strikes show as nodes of similar size, and that replay check is the Atlas gate condition. A strike Atlas shows that the bot misses (or the reverse) means the node-strength cutoff or `skylit_max_strikes` window differs — find the cause.

### 5.2 Skylit structure
- **The four maps:** SPY, SPX, QQQ and **NDXP** (the daily 0DTE Nasdaq-100 chain).
- **Node types (computed by the bot):** history has no `nodeType` (§3.1), so the bot classifies every strike itself from the per-strike values, the same way in backtest and live: the King is the **confirmed King** (owner 2026-10-25, below: the strike that has led the map by largest |value| over the trailing `king_switch_min_sec`, so a two-strike tug-of-war doesn't flip it); Gatekeeper and significant nodes are relevant nodes (≥ 25% of King, owner 2026-10-05): every relevant node strictly between the board's spot and the confirmed King is a Gatekeeper, every other relevant node is significant, and anything smaller is normal; pika (positive) and barney (negative) are the node's colour, a separate feature (`docs/feature_spec.md` F2.5). % of King (relevance, air pockets, pika clouds, magnets) and King dominance stay against the board's own largest |value|, so % of King never exceeds 1 (owner 2026-10-25). Skylit's own live `nodeType` is not used. Step 7 pulls 40 `/v1/historical` instants (which do carry `nodeType`); once step 11 builds the classifier it compares only King and Gatekeeper with Skylit's labels (owner 2026-10-25): Skylit king ↔ bot King; Skylit gatekeeper (any non-King strike ≥ 25% of King, Skylit's size-only rule) ↔ bot Gatekeeper ∪ significant; Skylit's pika/barney labels are only checked to be positive/negative strikes; a confusion table, the bot classified over the same strike window those instants were pulled with, the `maxStrikes` used stated. Step 12 shows the report to the owner; disagreements go to the owner, who decides whether the definition needs changing.
- **Node strength:** absolute value as a **% of that map's King** (the measure Atlas shows), plus the bot's node type. Sizes are only ever compared within a map — raw gamma values of SPX vs SPY, or NDXP vs QQQ, are on different scales.
- **Relevant nodes (owner's rule):** a node is structure only if it is **≥ 25% of its map's King** (`min_node_pct_of_king: 0.25`). Smaller nodes create no zones or events. The backtest reports how nodes just under the cutoff behaved, so the owner can check the 25%.
- **King (owner):** not required for any setup, but the strongest node on its map and the most likely to act as a floor or ceiling. Node type, % of King and a King-present flag are model features; the model learns how much more a King is worth (no hand-set weight).
- **Combining maps:** all Nasdaq levels sit on one NQ level map, all S&P levels on one ES level map, each node keeping its source map as a feature. QQQ and NDXP nodes within `confluence_tol` merge into one NQ zone and count as confluence (§5.4). S&P nodes are read on ES at the same moment (alignment, Trinity, obstacles) and never create an NQ event by themselves. QQQ vs NDXP and SPY vs SPX count separately, as the owner reads them.
- **Node identity:** a node is tracked by (map, strike), so its history survives R updates and King changes. It stops being structure only below `node_drop_pct` of King (start 0.20; it becomes structure at 0.25), so a node hovering near the cutoff doesn't flicker; % of King is against that same board's King, and the state starts fresh at each session's first applied board (owner 2026-10-11; feature_spec F2.4). Every check and feature uses this one rule (step 8b's gate, Atlas tables and regression alert, and step 11; owner 2026-10-11). Freshness: §5.4.
- **Velocity:** computed by the bot from consecutive snapshots, the same way in backtest and live: `(value_t − value_{t−k}) / |value_{t−k}|`, where value_{t−k} is the latest applied board with τ ≤ τ(current board) − `velocity_lookback_sec` (start 60), so history and stream give the same number; signed (it matches Skylit's `velocityPct`). The lean uses the change in magnitude, (|value_t| − |value_{t−k}|) / |value_{t−k}|, so a negative node growing from −1M to −2M counts as building (owner 2026-10-25). Skylit's live `velocityPct` (absent in history) is display-only, never a model input.
- **Velocity lean (sign fixed by the owner 2026-10-25):** per map, Σ(magnitude velocity × % of King) over relevant nodes **above** the board's spot − the same sum **below** it, averaged over the last `lean_window` minutes; positive = accumulation above spot = bias up, one sign everywhere (feature_spec F2.9, F2.18).
- **Positive / negative nodes (Skylit docs):** positive value = yellow ("pika" colour, dealers long gamma, slows price); negative = purple ("barney", dealers short gamma, speeds price). Magnitude matters more than colour; sign is a separate feature (`docs/feature_spec.md` F2.2).
- **Rolling ceilings/floors (Skylit docs + owner, 2026-10-05; `docs/feature_spec.md` F2.11):** the ceiling is the largest-magnitude node above price (either sign). A **ceiling roll-down** = a closer node, still above price, becomes the largest, whether the old one faded, the new one grew or both (e.g. QQQ 760 and 759, with 759 becoming the largest above price). **Contests over value (owner 2026-10-25):** each board's leader (largest |value| among the candidates above the board's spot) leads until the next board; the confirmed ceiling switches at a board only if it led less than `node_switch_incumbent_max_share` (0.25, owner rule) of the trailing `roll_switch_min_sec` (W, start 60 s) and doesn't lead that board, to the strike with the most lead time (ties → the current leader); no switch in the session's first W, the next switch needs a new full W, a 10-s board gap restarts the window, and on 1-minute days W < 60 behaves as 60; so a 50/50 tug-of-war never switches and a fading ceiling never stays stuck. A switch is a roll only if the new strike was a candidate for the whole window. **Price moves change the ceiling at once:** the confirmed ceiling stays the ceiling while the futures haven't traded beyond its band's upper edge (even if the board spot is above its strike); the first futures trade beyond pauses the roll and re-chooses the ceiling at once (the crossed node excluded until a trade back below its lower edge), and so does the resume: price switches, never rolls (feature_spec F2.11). A **floor roll-up** is the mirror image. Price simply moving through the old ceiling is not a roll. A roll stays **active until it is rolled back** (owner 2026-10-05: a ceiling roll-down is bearish in general and a floor roll-up bullish; it ends when the ceiling or floor rolls back to its original position, and is paused while price is through the current ceiling or floor (beyond its zone) until price comes back fully below the ceiling or above the floor; a new roll-down of the ceiling that formed above price while paused replaces it with a new, active roll; never a timer). The compression of node mass above/below spot (since the open and since the roll began) is a feature too (owner: shrinking upside or downside targets matter). Features: roll active, roll paused, net strikes rolled since the anchor (signed, + = toward price, negative allowed), rolls, roll-backs (full and partial) and pauses today, the shift in pts, the old node's shrink and the new node's growth, minutes since the roll began.
- **Gamma regime (Skylit docs, F2.12):** per map, net gamma within ±`regime_band`% of spot normalised by that map's King, plus the positive share of |gamma| there (the docs' yellow-vs-purple predominance). Maps are never summed; each is a separate feature (+ gameplan HVL, a gameplan-specific input gated by §5.6). Long gamma = slower, levels hold, ranging; short gamma = overshoots, trending.
- **Rug / reverse rug, pika cloud (Skylit docs + owner, 2026-10-05; F2.13–F2.14):** rug = a relevant positive node (≥ 25% of King, so it can cause the bounce) with a negative node of any size at most `rug_gap_strikes` (1) strike step below it (closer counts too; more negative strikes directly below count as well), spot below the positive node; reverse rug = the mirror (a negative node at most 1 strike step above a relevant positive node, spot above the positive node). The earlier placeholder's "no significant floor within `floor_search`" clause is dropped. Pika cloud = adjacent positive strikes, each a **relevant node** (owner 2026-10-24, was ≥ 10% of the King: enters at `pika_cloud_min_pct` 0.25 and stays until it drops below `node_drop_pct` 0.20, against the same board's King, by the Node identity rule below, so clouds don't flicker), at least `pika_cloud_min_nodes` (2) long (context, not a level).
- **Map-shape concepts from the Skylit docs (owner-approved 2026-10-04; model inputs only, never trade rules):** exact definitions in `docs/feature_spec.md`.
  - **Beach ball** (overshoot): a node crossed and then reclaimed (Crossed vs flipped, below). Features: overshoot depth, seconds beyond, count today (F2.7).
  - **Nodes as magnets:** pull = % of King ÷ distance (in expected moves, floored at `magnet_min_dist_em`), summed above and below price per map (F2.10).
  - **Air pockets (owner, 2026-10-05):** runs of adjacent low-magnitude strikes (each < `air_pocket_max_pct`, owner 2026-10-24: 10% of that map's King; was 2%) that price moves through quickly, faster through negative ones; whether price sits in one, the pocket ahead of and behind the trade, and its negative share (F2.15).
  - **King dominance and migration:** King ÷ second-largest node, King distance, confirmed King changes today (the lead-time rule above with `king_switch_min_sec`, owner 2026-10-25), direction and stair-step count (F2.16).
  - **Rainbow road:** counts of relevant positive and negative nodes, sign mix, King dominance and a rainbow flag (`rainbow_min_nodes_per_sign`, `rainbow_max_dominance`) (F2.17).
  - **Whipsaw map:** per-map bias (up / down / pinned from velocity lean with `bias_deadband`), Trinity bias agreement and conflict, and a whipsaw flag (conflict while a floor and a ceiling exist), computed for the QQQ and the NDXP trinity separately (F2.18).
- The exact measurable definitions of every concept in this section live in `docs/feature_spec.md` (step 5; owner approves at step 6).
- **Crossed vs flipped (owner):** a node is **crossed** — the setup is invalidated — the moment price trades beyond its band. That is not yet a GEX flip. It **flips** (ceiling → floor, or floor → ceiling) only when a **5-minute bar settles beyond the band** (open and close both past it by ≥ `flip_margin`, per level type, start 0, tuned at step 13 on months 1–4 in three groups and written per type, owner 2026-10-05, `docs/feature_spec.md` F16.4 — owner: learn the buffer around each type of level; clock-aligned 5-minute bars, `flip_bar_minutes: 5`). **Post-cross retests (owner 2026-10-23, §13 Q-flipretest):** from the cross the level takes the opposite role **provisionally**. Once price is `rearm_dist` beyond the band on the crossed side it arms in that role, and the next touch from the crossed side is an ordinary event in the new role, before any 5-minute settle. Each such event carries one fixed set of cross features, the same for every level type (`docs/feature_spec.md` F2.6b): the depth beyond (points, ATR, expected move), the volume and delta beyond against the time-of-day normal, the seconds beyond and the settled 5-minute bars beyond, the re-entries into the band, the minutes since the cross, and for Skylit nodes the node's % of King change and sign change since the cross. The settled 5-minute bar is now one of these features and confirms the flip (`flip_count`); it no longer gates events. The structure model's held/broke label learns which crossed levels hold in their new role; no new model. A retest breaks when the level is crossed back toward its original side by its own rule. From then until a **reclaim** (price comes back through the band to the original side and leaves it by `rearm_dist`, only once that cross back is confirmed by the level's own rule: for GEX nodes and gameplan zones a trade, for chart levels a settled 1-minute bar on the original side, for highs/lows the reverse sweep resolved as a break, past the window or beyond `sweep_max_depth`; the `rearm_dist` move may come in the confirming bar or sweep window, known at its close; owner 2026-10-31, §13 Q-step11b (8); events then resume in the original role, `reclaimed` is a feature) the level creates no event, and a move beyond the band on the crossed side confirmed by the same rule (a trade for nodes and gameplan zones, a settled 1-minute bar for chart levels, an unswept break for highs/lows) resumes the same cross episode. While a break is unresolved (a high/low inside its sweep window, a chart level with no settled 1-minute bar yet) there is still no event. The golden zone keeps its own orientation flip (§5.8). Checkpoints: the step-13 count report and the pre-declared step-15 test (§12); if the test fails, the retests and the cross features are removed and the 5-minute settle is again the only way into the new role. Flips can repeat both ways (owner): e.g. a ceiling breaks above its band and is pushed back down — it can still hold later as a ceiling; if a 5-minute bar settles above the band it becomes a floor; if price later pushes back through and a 5-minute bar settles below, it flips back to a ceiling. Each node carries `flip_count`, its current role, minutes since the last flip and cross, and its freshness per role, and the model learns how these combine.
- **Trinity alignment** (owner's core read): SPY, SPX and the Nasdaq side (QQQ or NDXP) each have a relevant (≥ 25% of King) ceiling — or floor — whose band contains its own instrument's price ± `zone_proximity` (NQ) or `es_zone_proximity` (ES; default = the NQ value × ES ÷ NQ price at the session open), in the same role (no outcome is used). **Overlap rule (owner 2026-10-24):** the owner reads Trinity as overlap: NQ still inside its Nasdaq zone when ES reaches its S&P zone, even seconds apart. For an event touched at t0 and evaluated at t, map m (SPY, SPX) is aligned if at some moment τ in [t0, t] the NQ price was inside a relevant QQQ/NDXP node in the event's role (band ± `zone_proximity`) **and** the ES price was inside a relevant node of m in the same role (band ± `es_zone_proximity`) at the same moment (last trades ≤ τ, bands and node states as of τ), **and** that node still counts at t: it holds the event's role at t, confirmed or provisional (post-cross retests, below), and hasn't been crossed against that role since its overlap (owner 2026-10-25; this replaces "crossed nodes don't count", so post-cross retests aren't biased; a node with no role yet counts only if ES entered its band from that role's side) (if several of m's nodes overlapped, one that still counts is enough; so not "once true, stays true"); the Nasdaq side likewise (any relevant QQQ/NDXP node with that role, inside at some τ in [t0, t], still counting at t). Data ≤ t only, the same code live and in backtest, empty during a dislocation block of the chain (§5.1). The structure model evaluates at t = t0 (unchanged: the same as the instant check); the pair scorer at its `decision_ts`, so ES arriving after the first NQ touch counts. The aligned count 0–3 uses this rule and Trinity aligned = count 3 (each map overlapped NQ at some moment, not necessarily the same one); the node statistics (sizes, King/Gatekeeper, rug or pika) come from the aligned nodes at their first overlap moment. No new columns. **A King is not required.** S&P bands are checked against ES ticks, Nasdaq bands against NQ ticks. The Nasdaq side counts as aligned if QQQ **or** NDXP is. Features: Trinity aligned yes/no, aligned count 0–3 (SPY, SPX, Nasdaq side), and separately how many of the four maps have a relevant node there (0–4), smallest and average node size (% of King), whether a King/Gatekeeper is among them, rug or pika structure present, per-map lean.
- **Nasdaq first, S&P second (owner):** QQQ and NDXP nodes are tied directly to NQ and matter most (the model learns how much more). SPY/SPX nodes **aligned** with the NQ trade (same role at the same moment on ES) strongly improve it. SPY/SPX nodes **in the trade's path** on ES — especially a Gatekeeper or King between the ES price and the ES equivalent of the target — can stall the bounce off a QQQ/NDXP node. Features: S&P nodes in the path (count, largest % of King, Gatekeeper/King present, distance in ATR); they also count as obstacles for target selection (§6.3).
- **Daily vol context (Tempest, as of the previous close):** for QQQ and SPY (and NDXP/SPX if Tempest covers them), the `/v1/vol/history` fields of §3.1 — SVX 1d/9d/30d/3m/6m, ATM vol, term slope, 30-day risk reversal, butterfly and skew slope, call/put tilt — plus realized vs implied, which the bot computes from NQ's own realized vol over `rv_lookback_sessions` (start 20) and SVX 30d, and Skylit's VIX row (owner 2026-10-20, §13 Q-vix; `/v1/vol/history` serves VIX from 2025-08-28): the same vol fields as the other symbols (feature_spec F2.21) plus its `close`, named **Skylit's VIX reference price**, not the official VIX close (it is the front VIX expiry's forward: it meets the VIX index at expiry and jumps at each monthly roll, so its level is not used: it enters only as its one-session change `vix_ref_chg` = ln(close(T−1) ÷ close(T−2)), null when session T−1 is a roll session by its row's `front_min`, Q-vix (2)). The VIX row comes from the same pre-open Tempest copy with the same `tempest_lag_sessions` lag as the other rows; no FRED or other outside VIX source, in backtest or live. Inputs to the structure, day-type and pair-scorer models (not the trigger model, which works in seconds). Nothing intraday from Tempest is used, because no history exists for it. Backtest and live use the row of session T − `tempest_lag_sessions` (start 1, the previous close); step 7 and step 22 record when the previous session's row (and the VIX value) first becomes available and whether historical rows change on later pulls; if it isn't reliably there by 09:00 ET, `tempest_lag_sessions` becomes 2 for backtest and live alike (decided at step 7 before any events are built; if in doubt, 2). **Owner 2026-10-09:** it also needs the pre-open copy to be final: the step-7 finality check (`step7 tempest-finality`, Mon 2026-10-12 before 09:30) re-pulls the 10-08 and 10-09 rows and compares them with their first pre-open copies; unchanged = 1, anything changed = 2. Only pre-open copies are ever used (§3.1 Tempest). **Decided 2026-10-12: `tempest_lag_sessions` = 1** (both rows unchanged, 0 cells; §13). Step 22 re-checks it live: it records when each previous-session row first appears and re-pulls the pre-open copies on later days; a row missing at 09:00 raises it to 2 for backtest and live alike (the step-21 execution-change re-run follows). **Owner 2026-10-22:** a pre-open copy that is still a draft does not raise it: it stays 1 with the draft rule of §3.1 Tempest (final = QQQ/SPY OHLC present and a whole-number `front_min`; re-pulls until `tempest_draft_cutoff_et`, then the draft is used and flagged; lags never mixed across symbols); the session log counts drafts left at the cutoff so lag 2 can be revisited with numbers (§13 Tempest finality).
- **Multi-expiration context (owner 2026-10-24, §13 Q-multiexp; feature_spec F2.23):** daily context only, from stored `/v1/historical` snapshots with every expiration (`layout=matrix`, §3.1 Multi-expiration snapshots); the 0DTE boards every level and event is built from are unchanged (`maxExpirations=1`). Expirations in three buckets, 0DTE / 1–7 days / 8–45 days, beyond 45 days left out. Two snapshots per session, 09:25 and 10:00 ET, used as one feature set: each decision reads the latest snapshot in force (09:25 until 10:00 + `mx_snapshot_delay_sec`, then 10:00) with a flag for which one. Per side (Nasdaq: QQQ, NDXP, NDX; S&P: SPY, SPXW, SPX), coarse: the net gamma sign of the 1–45-day buckets, whether the 0DTE bucket agrees, and the distance to the largest multi-expiry wall above and below price. Walls are context only, never registry levels. Inputs of the structure model and the pair scorer; ablation group 12.
- **Flags and context:** (hedge nodes: dropped at step 5, since `docs/skylit/` doesn't define them), OPEX week; calendar (days to monthly expiry, quarter-end, CPI/FOMC/NFP/PPI/retail-sales days from `config/calendar.yaml`); `minutes_to_close`; `minutes_to_horizon_end` (minutes from `decision_ts` to the earlier of `session.flat_by` and the next news-blackout start, §9) and `in_session_release_today` (a `blackout: true` event whose window overlaps 09:30–`entry_end`), both from the calendar as known at `preopen_start` and both pair-scorer inputs. Features only; the model decides their weight.

### 5.3 Chart structure (computed from trades)
- **VWAP + σ bands**: anchors = Globex open 18:00 ET (primary) and 09:30 ET (secondary). Bands at 1σ, 2σ.
- **Volume profile**: volume per tick price.
  - Sessions: **daily from 18:00 ET**, **weekly from Sunday 18:00 ET**, both developing. Plus the completed **previous session's** volume profile (owner 2026-10-24, §13 Q-amt): its POC (with a `naked` flag: not traded since it completed), VAH and VAL are levels (VAH/VAL as the new type `va_edge`); no other earlier profiles (no earlier days of the week, no earlier weeks). **Step-13 variants (owner 2026-10-26, §13 Q-vaedge):** `prev_profile_anchor` (start `globex_1800_1700`, the CME day [18:00, 17:00); `rth_0930_1600` = the previous session's RTH only, known from its close; the POC, naked flag, VAH/VAL and the value-context features follow it) and `va_edge_sweep` (start off; on = the previous VAH/VAL are sweep-eligible like highs/lows, below). **One contract only** everywhere: the previous-session profile is its own CME day's front contract, and in a roll week the developing weekly profile keeps only the new contract's days (the days before the roll are dropped; this replaced the shift-and-merge); carried levels are still shifted by the roll spread (F0.4).
  - POC = max volume price (a level; ties: nearest the range midpoint, then nearest the volume median, owner 2026-10-05). Value area = expand from POC one tick at a time (owner 2026-10-05) until 70% of volume → VAH, VAL (context, not levels: see below). Exact rules: `docs/feature_spec.md` F3.2–F3.3.
  - **LVN** (low-volume node, a level): a local minimum of the smoothed developing profile (Gaussian smoothing, σ = `lvn_smooth_ticks` ÷ 2, start 8 ticks; owner 2026-10-05, F3.4) whose volume is at most `lvn_max_pct` (start 0.3) of the smaller of the two peaks on either side (peak = the highest smoothed volume between the LVN and the next LVN or the profile edge). Daily and weekly profiles, recomputed every `profile_update_sec` (start 60) from data ≤ t; identity = profile + price rounded to `lvn_merge_pts` (start 5 NQ pts); band = half-width from the chart-level table (§5.4b); so an LVN keeps its history while the profile develops; it drops out when it no longer qualifies. HVNs are not used (the POC covers them).
  - **Single prints** (a level, owner): TPO over the daily session (from 18:00 ET) in 30-minute periods at `tpo_tick_pts` (start 1 NQ pt) resolution; a single print = a run of ≥ `sp_min_ticks` (start 8) consecutive prices traded in exactly one **completed** period so far (no lookahead). The unfilled part of the run, ± a learned half-width (start 0; set by the step-13 reaction report like other chart levels, owner), is the level's band (a run wider than `max_zone_width` is split). Because the run shrinks as price trades into it, an event uses the band **frozen at the touch** for its audit, cross and label; the live band only changes later touches. It stays a level — across later sessions too — until price trades through the whole run (`filled`); unfilled single prints from earlier sessions carry over, with their age in sessions as a feature, and a zone feature counts how many different sessions' unfilled single prints overlap the zone (stacked single prints, owner 2026-10-24). TPO is used for single prints only.
  - Built from Databento trades (volume at each traded price). The step-12 check compares VAH/VAL/POC against Deepcharts.
- **VAH / VAL = premium and discount (owner):** the **VAH zone** runs from the VAH line up to the profile's high, the **VAL zone** from the VAL line down to the profile's low, for the developing daily and weekly profiles, recomputed every `profile_update_sec` from data ≤ t. They are **not levels**: a touch creates no event, they don't break, and they are not stop or target candidates. In the VAH zone price is at a premium (shorts have more power), in the VAL zone at a discount (longs have more power), mainly on ranging days. Every event records, per profile: `in_vah_zone` / `in_val_zone`, depth into the zone (0 = at the line, 1 = at the profile extreme), distance to the VAH/VAL line (pts, ATR) and `va_direction_fit` (short in VAH, long in VAL); with day type as an input the model learns the ranging-day effect. They are also **structure areas for order flow** (§1.1 principle 1): an order-flow trigger inside them can start an **area event** (§5.4) even with no level there. The backtest tests and validates all of this like any other structure. The **previous session's** VAH and VAL are levels (`va_edge`, above), not zones: once crossed, retests from the other side are events in the opposite role with the cross features (§5.2 Post-cross retests), which is how the model learns when price builds value outside the previous value area.
- **Previous-session value context (owner 2026-10-24, inputs only, coarse; structure model and pair scorer):** the open against the previous session's value area (above VAH / inside / below VAL); the distance to the previous session's POC, VAH and VAL in expected-move units; the overlap of today's developing value area with the previous session's; today's developing-POC drift; and `poc_migration` = today's developing POC as of t minus the previous session's POC in expected-move units, signed to the trade direction (+ = value moving with the trade; owner 2026-10-24, the last addition before the freeze) (feature_spec F3.12). No profile shape, excess, poor extremes, initiative/responsive or weekly-set features. Ablation group 11.
- **IB (NY opening range)**: see §5.8 — adaptive 15–30 minute range from 09:30 ET, not a fixed 60-minute IB, redrawn once at 10:30 if it widened (§5.8). Used two ways: the **fib golden zone** drawn on it (§5.8), and its **high/low as levels** once the range locks (`ib_lock_minute` = 30 → 10:00). **10:30 move (owner 2026-10-16):** if price made a new high or low beyond the 10:00 range between 10:00 and 10:30, the IB high/low move once, at 09:30 + `ib_redraw_minute` (60 → 10:30), to the 09:30–10:30 high/low (the same redraw as the fib): the exceeded 10:00 level is removed and the 10:30 extreme starts as a fresh, unbroken IB level (ceiling/floor by the approach rule); a side that wasn't exceeded keeps its level and state. Otherwise they stay as locked at 10:00. The IB high/low follow the `golden_zone_redraw_1030` switch (owner 2026-10-26, §13 Q-fibvariants): with the 10:30 redraw off they stay as locked at 10:00 all day.
- **IB high/low (owner):** mainly a break-and-retest level. After price breaks below the IB low, the low can act as a **ceiling**; after a break above the IB high, the high can act as a **floor**. Both roles are tested — the range edge before a break and the flipped role after it. Same states as other highs/lows: price past the level and closing back inside within the sweep window = sweep (the level held); past it without a reclaim = crossed (setup invalid); a **5-minute bar settling beyond it = flipped** (owner): from then on a broken low acts as a ceiling and a broken high as a floor.
- **Overnight (ON) high/low** (18:00–09:30): its own structure level.
- **Prior-period extremes**: previous day high/low (PDH/PDL, 18:00–17:00 ET session), previous RTH high/low, previous week high/low. Tested-to-the-tick levels from earlier sessions (e.g. a prior overnight low that held) are tracked as `retested_extreme` levels (tested = within `retest_tol_ticks`, start 4, in the last `retest_lookback_sessions`, start 10; held = the structure label held).
- **Bounce, sweep or break (owner) — highs and lows only** (the sweep-eligible types in the §5.4 registry; **not** VWAP, POC, LVNs, single prints, dark pools or GEX nodes; the previous session's VAH/VAL only if the step-13 variant `va_edge_sweep` wins, owner 2026-10-26, §13 Q-vaedge): price can bounce at the level, or **sweep** it — trade a little past, take the liquidity, and come back. A sweep = price goes beyond the zone by at most `sweep_max_depth` (start 15 NQ pts) and is reclaimed — a 1-minute close not beyond the band (inside it or on the range side) — within `sweep_reclaim_bars` 1-minute bars (start 3); both are tuned by the data (§7.1), and the sweep's depth, time beyond and order flow are model features, so the model learns how sweeps behave (owner). For these levels, trading past the zone is therefore not yet a break: it becomes a break only if the sweep window passes without a reclaim, or at once if price goes beyond the zone by more than `sweep_max_depth` (then the crossed/flipped logic of §5.2 applies). A swept-and-reclaimed level is an entry setup back into the range (entry style (c), §5.5), with the sweep extreme as a stop candidate (§6.2). Features: `swept` yes/no, sweep depth (pts, ATR), seconds beyond, and later the order flow during the sweep. The report compares bounce vs sweep entries per level type.
- **Day type inputs**: IB width vs its `ib_avg_sessions`-session (start 20) average, VWAP crosses so far (a cross = a clock-aligned `daytype_cross_bar_minutes` (5) bar settled on the other side (F0.5: open beyond the level as of the bar's open, close beyond it as of its close; margin 0), not a 1-minute close (owner 2026-10-27, §13 Q-dtcross); the developing VWAP is judged as of each bar's open and close, like a moving band), rotation count (a rotation = a move of ≥ `rotation_min_pts`, start 0.25 × ATR, away from a swing extreme before a new extreme; counted since 09:30), regime, vol.

### 5.4 Structure confluence
- **Level registry (canonical: every other section refers to this list):**
  | Type | Levels | Sweep-eligible (§5.3) |
  |---|---|---|
  | GEX nodes | relevant QQQ and NDXP nodes on NQ. SPY/SPX nodes are **features only** (alignment, Trinity, obstacles, §5.2): they never create an NQ event on their own | no |
  | Opening range | golden zone (§5.8); IB high/low from range lock (§5.3) | IB high/low only |
  | Session highs/lows | ON high/low, PDH/PDL, prior RTH high/low, prior week high/low, retested extremes (§5.3) | yes |
  | Volume profile | daily and weekly POC and LVNs (developing); the previous session's POC, VAH and VAL (completed, owner 2026-10-24); single prints (today's and unfilled earlier ones). The developing VAH/VAL are context and order-flow areas, not levels (§5.3) | no |
  | VWAP | 18:00 and 09:30 anchors, 1σ/2σ bands | no |
  | Expected-move levels | ±1σ and ±2σ around the previous NQ RTH close, from Tempest's own calendar-day one-day move (`em_1d_pct` of QQQ's pre-open row T − `tempest_lag_sessions`; Friday rows wider), fixed for the session, so they match Tempest's chart lines (F3.11; owner 2026-10-14). ES lines (from SPY) are cross-market features only | no |
  | Dark pools | QQQ top prints (§3.1) on NQ: full levels that create events on their own. SPY top prints on ES are cross-market features only. **Not in the first model** (owner 2026-10-06: Skylit's history starts 2026-04-01, `dp_in_model: false`, §3.1 Dark pools) | no |
  | Gameplan | zone edges, when a gameplan exists (§5.6) | no |
- **Chart levels** = every registry level except GEX nodes, the golden zone and gameplan zones; where a rule names highs/lows separately (sweep logic, §5.3), "chart levels" there means the others.
- All registry levels except the containers (golden zone, gameplan zones, below) merge into **one zone** per the Merge rule below → one event, not several.
- **Zone object (one definition for backtest and live):**
  - **Merge:** two levels merge when their bands overlap or their centres are within `confluence_tol`. No chaining past `max_zone_width` (start 60 NQ pts; a single QQQ node band is already ~40 wide): a level that would stretch a zone further starts its own zone. Deterministic order: existing zones keep their still-valid members; the remaining levels are processed in ascending price, each joining the nearest qualifying zone if the merged width stays ≤ `max_zone_width` (ties: larger % of King, then the older zone), else starting a new zone; bands that touch count as overlapping. On every recompute a member stays only while it still meets the merge rule with another kept member and the zone stays ≤ `max_zone_width` (the member farthest from the founding anchors is released first); released members and zones that now overlap are reprocessed in the same pass (the older `zone_id` survives); all updates sharing one timestamp are applied first and zones recomputed once, so arrival order can't change the result.
  - **Edges:** the union of the members' bands. **Invalidation is per member (owner):** when price trades past a GEX node's ±band, that node is invalid (the overshoot went too far) and drops out of the zone's active members; a 5-minute bar settling past its band turns it into a GEX flip, and the node can still hold on a later test. The zone carries on with its remaining members (e.g. a PDH with its sweep logic). The zone is crossed only when every current member is crossed by its own rule; a zone that loses all its members without any crossing before held or broke ends as a timeout for the structure label (unit-tested).
  - **Identity:** `zone_id` is assigned when a zone is created, from its founding members' anchors (node = map + strike, chart level = type + session), stored as a short stable id (e.g. `z17`) with a lookup table to the anchors, and kept while any member remains (members joining or leaving don't change it), so the bot knows a zone is the *same* level after it moves (needed for freshness and re-arm counts). **Every map update is used immediately:** levels and zones are recomputed on each Skylit update (each tick for VWAP, and every `profile_update_sec` for POC, LVNs and VAH/VAL), and all new decisions and the structure label use the updated band. A pending entry is removed (`exit`) if its zone vanishes or moves more than `entry_reprice_tol` (start 2 NQ pts) away from the entry price, so an order never rests at a price that is no longer at the level. The zone itself stays (same `zone_id`) and keeps trading at its new price: the next touch is a new event. Moves that large come mostly from VWAP, a jumping developing POC or a member dropping out of a merged zone; a node's own move from an R update is far smaller. An open trade keeps the stop and target already resting at the broker (v1 only moves the stop for breakeven), so trade labels use the bracket fixed at entry.
  - **Crossed / flipped for every level:** GEX nodes use §5.2; highs/lows (including retested extremes) and IB high/low use §5.3 (sweeps first); single prints cross like other chart levels (against the band frozen at the touch, §5.3) and end when filled. **Every other level** (VWAP and bands, POC, LVNs, and dark-pool levels while they are in the top list; they end when they leave it, §3.1) uses the same §5.2 state machine with one difference (owner): it is **crossed only when a 1-minute candle settles beyond its band** (open and close both past it); trading past the band without settling is not a break, and an audit or touch in progress stays valid. Flipped when a 5-minute bar settles beyond it, reclaimed and retested after a cross (post-cross retests) as in §5.2.
  - **Containers:** the golden zone and gameplan zones are never merged with other levels; levels inside them are counted as confluence features. Golden zone (owner): two containers from the same fib drawing (owner 2026-10-27, §13 Q-gzsplit, §5.8): the **shallow zone** 0.5–0.618 and the **deep zone** 0.618–0.79 (split at `golden_zone_split_fib`), each with its own `zone_id`, touches, re-arm, freshness, touch number today, earlier-touch outcomes (resolved only), structure label and events; levels inside each (nodes, Trinity, chart levels) are that zone's confluence. Touches: the shallow zone by the normal touch rule with `zone_proximity` from the 0 side; the deep zone is touched only by a trade past 0.618 into it from the shallow side, never by coming within `zone_proximity` of 0.618 (owner 2026-10-27, §13 Q-gzsplit answer); its touch is a new touch with its own touch group, never shared with a shallow-zone touch, so after a shallow-zone trade has ended (e.g. stopped past 0.618) the deep zone can still trade, subject as always to one trade per account at a time and the direction lock; `zone_proximity` still applies to touches from outside the golden zone (the shallow zone approached from the 0 side); the deep zone re-arms back on the shallow side of 0.618 and `rearm_dist` away, and the next trade past 0.618 is a new deep-zone touch. Each is crossed when a 1-minute candle settles beyond its own far edge (open and close both past it): the shallow zone past 0.618 (this does not invalidate the setup or flip anything; the deep zone is simply the next zone), the deep zone past 0.79 (the setup's invalidation). There is no limit on tests: price can come back later and hold, and that return is a new event — each zone re-arms once price is back on the original side of its own far edge and `rearm_dist` away from it. (With the orientation flip on, the default, the 0.79 settle also redraws the fib the other way and both new zones arm by the normal rule (§5.8); with `golden_zone_flip` off the drawing keeps its orientation and this re-arm applies; owner 2026-10-26, §13 Q-fibvariants.)
  - **Touch and re-arm:** a touch = price enters the zone ± `zone_proximity` (the single touch rule; freshness, events and labels all use it). The next touch of the same zone counts only after price has left the zone ± (`zone_proximity` + `rearm_dist`) (`rearm_dist` start 10 NQ pts). While a break is unresolved — a high/low inside its sweep window and within `sweep_max_depth`, a chart level beyond its band with no settled 1-minute candle yet, the golden zone beyond 0.79 with no settled candle — price on the far side neither re-arms the zone nor changes its approach side, and coming back inside is the same event (a sweep reclaim or audit), not a new touch. A re-arm on the far side happens only after the level is crossed and flips; after a reclaim it re-arms on the original side (§5.2).
  - **IDs:** `event_id = <UTC timestamp>-<zone_id>`. Each order sent gets a unique `order_id = <event_id>-<entry style><n>-<account>-<leg>` (n = 1, 2… per style per event, since order-flow styles can fire again, §5.7; leg = `NQ` / `MNQ`; e.g. `…-b2-mffur1-MNQ`), so a re-fired style, a second entry style or a second TradersPost account trading the same event never collides (§10.3).
  - **Direction:** approach from below → short (the zone is a ceiling); from above → long (floor). The approach side is the side price was on at the last re-arm. Flipped levels use their new role. Golden zone: the direction from the fib orientation (§5.8); from 10:00 the drawing is static except for the one 10:30 redraw and the orientation flips (owner 2026-10-16), each of which makes a new golden zone. Gameplan zones: the approach rule; agreement with the gameplan's `side` is a feature. A zone moving onto price is not a touch; it arms once price is `rearm_dist` away.
  - **Area events (VAH/VAL, §5.3):** when an order-flow trigger (entry style a or b) fires inside a VAH zone (short only) or a VAL zone (long only) while no level zone is within `zone_proximity` of price, it creates an area event (`event_type: area`; one area event per trigger even when price is inside both the daily and the weekly area — both profiles' features are recorded; `zone_id` = the areas containing price + session; it is its own touch group). Only entry styles (a) and (b) apply, and only stop candidates 1, 3 and 5 (§6.2). "Level zone" here means an active (not crossed) zone. There is no touch and no structure label: the structure model's prediction is null for these events, and the trigger model and pair scorer judge them. Firings in the same area before re-arm are further firings (n = 2, 3…) of the existing area event, subject to `trig_min_gap_sec`; the event ends when price leaves the area or re-arm is met. The next area event in the same area needs `area_rearm_sec` (start 300) or a move of `rearm_dist` away from the last trigger price. A trigger near a level belongs to that level's event instead (and carries the VAH/VAL context features). Area events exist from step 19 (they need order flow). A pending area-event entry is removed (`exit`) on `entry_ttl_sec` or when price leaves the area. **S&P entry features (owner 2026-10-26, §13 Q-areasp; built with area events at step 19):** a VAH-area event has role ceiling, a VAL-area event floor; pair-scorer inputs at each pair's `decision_ts`, over the window from the visit start (checked at the visit start and at every ES trade, NQ trade and board apply in the window, as for level events; the moment NQ last entered the area on this visit; inside both the daily and the weekly area: the earliest moment since which NQ has been continuously inside at least one of the areas containing it at the first firing) to `decision_ts`: `es_at_level` (the same column as for level events, cross-market below) and per S&P map `area_sp_aligned_spy` / `area_sp_aligned_spx` with the aligned node's pct_of_king and King/Gatekeeper flags at its first overlap, and `area_sp_count` (0–2); `trinity_count` / `trinity_aligned` are null (no Nasdaq side). Which other features area events get is in §7.1 Area-event inputs.
  - **Overlapping events:** levels inside a container keep their own zones and events. Events created by the same touch share a `touch_group_id`; training weights a touch group once (pair-row weight = 1 ÷ (events in its touch group × filled pair rows of its event); trigger rows 1 ÷ firings in the event). The simulator and live take at most one of them: the **first** event whose pair passes the thresholds trades, later events in the group are skipped while that entry is pending or filled (if it is removed cleanly and unfilled, the others become eligible again, as for entry styles, §7.1), and highest EV decides only between events in the same second (no hindsight).
  - **No hand-made setup labels (owner):** a zone is described by its full makeup — which level types, how many, how tightly they stack, how big and fresh the nodes are, and how far it sits from the next zone — and the models learn how each of these affects price (§5.4 Confluence). The only flag with a hard rule attached is `in_golden_zone`, because of the 09:50–10:00 window (§1.2). Entry style is learned the same way (§7.1).
- Cross-market features: ES at its own stacked level (SPY dark pool, ES ON/PDH/PDL, SPX/SPY nodes; by the Trinity overlap rule, owner 2026-10-24: at some moment in [touch, t] NQ inside the event's zone ± `zone_proximity` and ES inside the ES zone ± `es_zone_proximity` in the same role, with that ES level still holding that role at t, confirmed or provisional, and not crossed against it since its overlap (owner 2026-10-26, the Trinity rule); the structure model at the touch, the pair scorer at `decision_ts`; §5.2 Trinity; area events (owner 2026-10-26, §13 Q-areasp): the same column, at `decision_ts` only, with ES inside an ES level's band ± `es_zone_proximity` in the event's role (VAH ceiling, VAL floor) at some moment from the visit start to `decision_ts`, by the same Q18 rule, the same ES level set (dark pools out while `dp_in_model` is false)), **NQ/ES divergence** at the touch (one makes a new extreme, the other doesn't — SMT), plus relative delta (NQ vs ES delta, each ÷ its own volume, over `rel_delta_sec`). ("Whether ES is reacting there" was dropped, owner 2026-10-05: Trinity covers it; ES is never traded.) Every NQ-point parameter applied on ES (re-arm, sweep depth, flip margin, merge tolerance, zone width, half-widths, proximity) is the NQ value × ES ÷ NQ price at the session open (owner 2026-10-05).
- **Freshness (owner, all level types):** the more times a level has been tested and held, the less likely it is to hold the next time; fresh levels are strongest. Every level carries `tests_so_far` and `holds_so_far` (today, and since creation for multi-day levels such as PDH/PDL, prior week high/low, weekly POC and LVNs, unfilled single prints and dark pools), plus minutes since the last test. Only tests whose outcome was already resolved at time t count as held; open ones are pending. The model learns the decay; the report shows hold rate by test number per level type.
- **Day memory**: how many levels held vs broke so far today (counting only touches whose outcome was already resolved at time t), and yesterday's day type.
- **Distances are vol-scaled** (ATR = `atr_period`-session (start 14) RTH daily ATR from completed sessions): every distance feature is stored in points **and** in ATR units **and in expected moves**: the bot builds the day's expected move itself: NQ points = the NQ RTH close of session T − 1 (today's front contract) × QQQ's `em_1d_pct` ÷ 100 from the pre-open row of session T − `tempest_lag_sessions`, as stored, never recomputed (Tempest's one-day move in calendar time, weekends and early closes included; ES: ES close × SPY's `em_1d_pct`), identical in backtest and live, so a 30-point miss means the same thing on a quiet day and a wild one. It is the same number as the registry's expected-move lines (F3.11), so 1 EM = the distance to a ±1σ line (owner 2026-10-22, §13 Q-svxwknd).
- Features: count of structures stacked, which types (one-hot), daily vs weekly profile, each member's node strength and freshness, and **proximity**: zone width, spread between the members, each member type's distance from the zone centre, distance from price to the zone edge, and distance (pts and ATR) to the next zone above and below.
- Expected (tested, not assumed): stacked levels hold more often, especially fresh nodes in long gamma; crowded levels may break harder in short gamma. Confluence zones are expected to be stronger targets too (§6.3).
- **Confluence (learned, no hand-set weights — owner):** each zone carries the type, number and proximity features above, plus the largest node's % of King and King/Gatekeeper present. The structure model and the pair scorer learn what each is worth and how they combine (e.g. a King plus the weekly POC stacked tightly vs loosely), from how price actually reacted. 
- Report every run: hit rate by stack count and by pairing type, split by gamma regime — this is the direct test of "more confluence = stronger level".

### 5.4b Zone width, activation and invalidation

Every level is drawn as a **zone** (a band), not a line. Price "at structure" (core principle 1) means inside the zone ± `zone_proximity`.

**Starting values and caps (`config/features.yaml`):**
| Item | Value / question | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Skylit node band (decided) | **QQQ: ±0.375** of the strike (underlying $; frozen from months 1–4, owner 2026-11-12, §13 Q-step13a-2 (7); ±0.5 was the starting value); **SPY: ±0.5**; **SPX: ±5**; **NDXP: ±15** (frozen from months 1–4, owner 2026-11-10, §13 Q-step13a-2; ±20 was the starting value) | Converted to futures with R on every update: QQQ ±0.375 ≈ ±15.5 NQ pts (the ±0.5 start ≈ ±20.6), NDXP ±15 ≈ ±15 NQ pts (the ±20 start ≈ ±20 NQ pts), SPY ±0.5 = SPX ±5 ≈ ±5 ES pts. At the starting values every band was the same ~0.07% of price, so a QQQ node and an NDXP node at one price got the same width (SPY/SPX already match); both Nasdaq maps' measured bands (QQQ ±0.375, NDXP ±15, set by the same rule) keep them about the same share of price again (owner 2026-11-12). Already allows for overshoots and near-misses. `node_band: {QQQ: 0.375, SPY: 0.5, SPX: 5, NDXP: 15}`. SPY/SPX are fixed; the backtest reports NDXP reaction distances (testing ±10/15/20) and the owner confirms |
| Beyond the band (decided) | **Crossed** (setup invalidated): GEX nodes the moment price trades past the band; chart levels when a 1-minute candle settles beyond it; highs/lows by the sweep rule (§5.4 Crossed / flipped). **Flip** = a settled 5-minute bar beyond the band, for all (§5.2); from the cross the level holds the opposite role provisionally and its retests from the crossed side are events (§5.2 Post-cross retests) | Cross = label "broke" for the structure model. How crossed and flipped levels then behave is learned, not assumed (cross features, feature_spec F2.6b) |
| Half-width for each chart level type | Every registry type (§5.4) except GEX nodes, dark pools (`dp_band` is fixed like a QQQ node band; the step-13 reaction report shows dark-pool reaction distances for the owner to confirm), the golden zone and gameplan zones; single prints get a learned half-width around their unfilled run | Owner lets the data decide: Claude Code proposes a start value and a cap for each at step 5 (owner approves at step 6), and the step-13 reaction-distance report on months 1–4 sets the width inside the cap (NQ; ES cross-market levels default to the NQ value × ES ÷ NQ price). The owner may still write his own in the owner notes |
| Gameplan zones | Already have explicit edges | Use as given |
| `zone_proximity` | How far outside a zone price can be and still count as "approaching" | Claude Code proposes the start value at step 5; owner approves at step 6 |

**Learned from data (inside the caps approved at step 6):**
- Skylit node bands are set by the owner (above): SPY/SPX fixed, NDXP ±15 and QQQ ±0.375 set by the reaction-distance report (owner 2026-11-10 and 2026-11-12, §13 Q-step13a-2 (5), (7); ±20 and ±0.5 were the starts). For **chart levels** (the same types), every approach records the **closest distance reached** and whether a reaction followed (reversal ≥ `react_pts` within `react_minutes`); the distribution of reaction distances per level type gives the empirical "active band", and widths are tuned to that, inside the cap approved at step 6. Rule: an approach = price comes within the type's cap + `zone_proximity` of the level line after re-arm (price beyond cap + `zone_proximity` + `rearm_dist` from the line); reaction distance = the deepest point past the line before a reversal of ≥ `react_pts` within `react_minutes`, taken only over approaches that reached the line and were followed by such a reaction (reading B, owner 2026-11-10, §13 Q-step13a-2: near misses, turns before the line, are left out of the widths and reported for the `zone_proximity` grid; price past the line must come back across it after its deepest point within `hold_window_min`, else it is a break; the cap stays even where the measured quantile is above it) (highs/lows: penetration beyond the sweep window excluded; single prints: measured from the near edge of the unfilled run); half-width = the `react_width_q` quantile (start value proposed at step 5) per level type, clipped to the cap. Regime splits are report-only unless pre-declared at step 6. **Frozen from months 1–4 (owner 2026-11-10, §13 Q-step13a-2):** vwap, vwap_band, poc, lvn, em_level and va_edge 12 (the cap); on_extreme 9, pdh_pdl 8.5, prior_rth 8, ib 8.25, retested_extreme 8.25; prior_week 8.25 (the pooled highs/lows fallback, 69 reactions); single_print 0 (its start value); the all-hours widths.
- `distance_to_level_center` (in points and as a fraction of zone width) is also a model feature, so the structure model learns how much a near-miss vs a dead-center touch matters.
- Flip behavior is measured the same way: every post-cross retest (§5.2) is an event in the level's new role with its own structure label; the step-13 count report and the step-15 test (§12) compare the learned version with the 5-minute rule.

**Taps between map updates (resolution):** a new Skylit map only changes **where** the levels are. Between updates each level's band is fixed, and price is checked against it on **every trade**, so a tap that happens and reverses within seconds is still caught:
- Live and backtest alike: every Databento trade (tick) is checked against every band, in exchange sequence.
- Paths are stored as 1-second bars (§6.5), and every re-simulation (breakeven, stops, targets, policy and prop simulators) runs from them. Where two of entry, stop, target or breakeven trigger fall inside the same 1-second bar, the simulator re-reads that second's trades from the cached Databento files, in exchange sequence, to order them. Only when a single trade print meets two conditions at once is it scored the **pessimistic** way and flagged `ambiguous_tick`: entry and stop together → filled and stopped out; entry and target → filled, target not yet reached; stop and target → stopped. ("Not filled" is used only when the other reading would be a win.)

### 5.5 Entries (owner's definitions)
- **Failed audit** (short): price pushes up into a zone from below and is pushed back down out of it. It needs at least one trade inside the zone's band since the last re-arm, and completes at the first 1-minute close outside the band on the approach side after that trade (that bar is the trigger bar). A **sweep reclaim** (the style (c) trigger) = the first 1-minute close on the range side of the level's band within `sweep_reclaim_bars` (a close inside the band keeps the level reclaimed, §5.3, but doesn't trigger). At a high/low level, a sweep through the level and back (§5.3) counts too.
- **Audited hold** (long, the inverse): price tests a zone from above and is pushed back up out of it.
- Both (owner): price may go **as deep into the zone and stay as long as it wants** — no depth or time limit. The only thing that kills the audit is the zone's own invalidation (§5.4 Crossed / flipped: for chart levels a 1-minute candle settling on the other side; for GEX nodes trading past the band; for highs/lows a break after the sweep window); the audit must complete before price re-arms (`rearm_dist`), but a re-arm during the bar whose close completes the audit does not cancel it (re-arm takes effect only after that bar closes). The depth reached and time spent inside are recorded as features.
- **Settled bar** (owner's definition) = a closed candle whose open is beyond the band as of the bar's open and whose close is beyond the band as of its close (bands can move during the bar). Used for the 1-minute chart-level cross (§5.4), the golden-zone 0.79 invalidation (§5.8), the owner's breakeven rule (§6.4) and every 5-minute flip (§5.2).
- Four entry styles compared on the same setups, listed in the owner's order of precision: (a) at the level on **absorption** and (b) mid-bar order-flow trigger (**exhaustion**, delta divergence, imbalance) — most precise; (c) audit-based at the zone edge (failed audit / audited hold / sweep reclaim) — decent precision and confirmation; (d) plain entry at the structure on the touch, no confirmation — least precise, kept as the baseline. The backtest tries all of them and the results decide (owner); the ranking only breaks near-ties (EVs within `style_tie_ev_r`, §7.1). (a) and (b) need the order-flow features built in step 17, so Phase D tests (c) and (d); step 19 compares all four on the same setups.

### 5.5b Entry order types (how each entry style is executed)
Fill rate vs price quality is a real tradeoff: limits get the best price but miss fast moves (and tend to fill on the trades that keep going against you), market orders always fill but slip, stop orders fill only when price moves your way.

| Entry style | Default order | Why |
|---|---|---|
| (a) At the level on absorption | **Marketable limit** (fills at once, slippage capped), or a **stop-limit** 1 tick beyond the current price in the trade direction, limit `max_entry_slip_ticks` beyond the trigger | Absorption reverses fast, so the entry must actually fill (owner); a passive limit risks missing the move and is tested only as a comparison |
| (b) Mid-bar order-flow trigger (exhaustion, etc.) | **Marketable limit** (limit `max_entry_slip_ticks` through the current price) | Momentum has turned; fills like a market order but slippage is capped |
| (d) Plain entry at the structure | **Limit** at the zone edge on the touch, short time-to-live; also compared: a passive **limit at the zone's centre** (`limit_center`; for a single Skylit node the mapped strike), same time-to-live (owner 2026-10-28, §13 Q-strikeentry) | Baseline: shows what confirmation adds; the centre limit tests the owner's read that at GEX nodes the strongest reaction is usually right at the strike |
| (c) Failed audit / audited hold / sweep reclaim | **Stop-limit** 1 tick beyond the trigger bar's extreme in the trade direction, limit `max_entry_slip_ticks` beyond that | Owner's idea: only fills if price confirms the move, still precise; the limit cap stops runaway fills in fast markets |

- Every pending entry has a **time-to-live** (`entry_ttl_sec`, tuned) and is removed (with `exit`, §3.5) on expiry, when its zone is crossed (§5.4 Crossed / flipped; golden zone: a 1-minute candle settling past that zone's far edge, 0.618 for the shallow zone and 0.79 for the deep one (owner 2026-10-27); gameplan zone: traded past its far edge, its `invalidation` being a feature only, §5.6), or when its zone vanishes or moves away after a map update (§5.4). Pending entries are also removed at the start of a news blackout (§9), on a data outage (any §10.3 feed or Skylit staleness or lag, `live_gap`, a clock-offset block or SSD loss, §9), at the start of a mapping-dislocation block for the zones with a member from that chain (§5.1), on `!pause`, and for area events when price leaves the area (§5.4). At `entry_end` any pending entry is removed, and after the trade's last leg ends and after every time-to-live expiry with no leg filled, `exit` also goes to both tickers (§10.3). These are the only removal reasons, and the policy simulator (§7 rule 3b) applies the same ones. At flat-by, `!flat` and `!kill`, the `exit` sent to every ticker also removes every pending entry leg.
- These are defaults; the **backtest decides**. For every event it simulates all applicable order types (limit, marketable limit, stop-limit — the ones §3.5 can send; for style (d) also the passive limit at the zone's centre, owner 2026-10-28, §13 Q-strikeentry) and reports per entry style: fill rate, average slippage, how the **missed** trades would have done (adverse selection), and expected R **including missed trades**. The best order type per entry style (a per-regime split only if pre-declared at step 6) is chosen from that, not assumed — except that style (a) may only use marketable limit or stop-limit; its passive limit is report-only.
- **Targets and stops in every label and simulator:** a target (limit exit) counts as hit only when price trades at least 1 tick through it; a stop fills on touch at the stop ± `exit_slippage_ticks`, or at the last print of the sweep that triggered it if that is worse (owner 2026-11-10/11, §13 Q-step13b (d); a market exit likewise never fills better than the first sweep after its time). This applies to the structure label's reaction level, pair labels, the policy simulator and the prop simulator; labels and the scorer always use these trade-through rules; the per-account sweeps, lives and profile cutoffs and the prop-simulator reports re-simulate each account with its own fill rule from the stored paths (`sim_fills: true` = the step-21 touch fill), and the gate-23 replay compares against the simulator run with the replay account's `sim_fills`.
- **Order latency (backtest = live):** every order, every `exit` and every `breakeven` takes effect `order_latency_sec` after the decision (settings.yaml; start 1.0 s, a conservative placeholder used for every label and simulator run until step 21 measures the real 95th-percentile end-to-end latency, §3.5; if the measured value is larger, labels are rebuilt; step 25 re-checks it against the imported fills). A pending entry can still fill until its `exit` takes effect.
- Backtest fill rules: a passive limit fills only if price trades **through** the limit after it arrives (no queue model). A stop-limit is working from decision + `order_latency_sec`; it triggers on the first trade at or through `stopPrice` after that (or on arrival if price is already through) and then fills like a marketable limit at the triggering trade's time, never worse than `limitPrice`; any unfilled rest becomes a resting limit at `limitPrice` that fills only on a trade through it before removal (step 21 test 4 confirms Tradovate's behaviour when the stop is already through). A marketable limit buy (sell) fills at the best ask (bid) from `mbp-1` at its arrival time (decision + `order_latency_sec`); if the order's size (the §6.1 mixed position at `default_risk_usd` and default costs, in NQ-equivalent contracts: minis + micros ÷ 10, rounded up) exceeds the displayed size there, the rest fills at the limit cap (the pessimistic choice: there is no depth data); anything beyond the cap is unfilled and handled like a live partial fill. Same-timestamp ties use the pessimistic reading in §5.4b (`ambiguous_tick`). Timeout-exit slippage is measured from Databento trades and top of book, and stop exits in the policy and prop simulators fill at the stop ± `exit_slippage_ticks`, the same value sizing uses (a sensitivity run also reports the measured slippage on stops).

### 5.6 Gameplan JSON schema
```json
{"date":"2026-09-29","hvl":[30590,30590],"hvl0":[30600,30600],"regime_line":[30575,30615],
 "zones":[{"id":"E1","side":"sell","zone":[30640,30722],
   "trigger":{"type":"failed_audit","nq_price":null,"note":null,
              "es_gates":[{"direction":"below","price":[7766,7766],"as_written":"ES < 7,766"}]},
   "stop":30734,
   "invalidation":[{"instrument":"NQ","direction":"above","price":30722,"timeframe":"1m_close",
                    "acceptance_minutes":null,"as_written":"1m close above 30,722"}],
   "targets":[{"low":30615,"high":30615,"as_written":"30,615"},
              {"low":30575,"high":30590,"as_written":"the regime line 30,575-590"}],
   "other_conditions":["only with the VIX under 16.50"]},
  {"id":"E1 accepted","side":"buy","zone":null,
   "trigger":{"type":"acceptance","nq_price":[30722,30722],"es_gates":[],
              "note":"15+ min above 30,722"},
   "stop":30700,"invalidation":[],
   "targets":[{"low":30800,"high":30800,"as_written":"30,800"}],"other_conditions":[]}],
 "scenario_sets":[{"frame":"ny_am","label_as_written":"NY AM 09:30-12:00","scenarios":[
   {"id":"S-A","prob":0.5,"bias":"down","desc":"lid refuses, rotation to regime line"},
   {"id":"S-B","prob":0.5,"bias":"up","desc":"lid breaks"}]}],
 "other_conditions":["take the second test, not the first","no trade 30,615-30,640"]}
```
Parser version 3 (owner 2026-10-07; rules in §3.4). **Conditional entries:** an "acceptance through / breakout / breakdown" trade is its own zone item with `zone` null, `trigger.type` acceptance/breakout/breakdown, its NQ level in `trigger.nq_price` ([low, high]), the acceptance condition word for word in `trigger.note`, and its stop and targets; the checks test its stop and targets against `trigger.nq_price`. **`other_conditions`** (a list of strings on each entry and on the gameplan) keeps word for word what this schema has no field for (VIX and 10Y gates, "take the second test, not the first", no-trade gaps, size). **No feature uses `other_conditions` yet** (F8 doesn't read it); it is kept so a later feature can, without a re-parse. F8 uses a conditional entry (zone null) as a single-price zone at `trigger.nq_price` (owner 2026-10-07, §13 step 9 v3). Parser version 4 adds the glossary to the prompt (§3.4) without changing these fields; the effective gameplan (owner overrides applied, §3.4) also carries `owner_overrides` (the overrides applied) and `owner_confirmed`. From version 2: every level is [low, high] (one price → [p, p]); `hvl0` is the HVL0 / HVL 0DTE level; `targets`, `invalidation` and `es_gates` keep their words in `as_written`; a target with no price in the gameplan has `low`/`high` null; `timeframe` ∈ `1m_close`, `settled_bar`, `acceptance` (+ `acceptance_minutes`), `not_stated`, `other`; `direction` ∈ above, below, unclear; `bias` ∈ up, down, neutral, mixed; `frame` ∈ `ny_am`, `session`, `unspecified`. The stored file also carries `status` (`ok` = no review flag, `flagged`) and `flags` (each with `field`, `reason`, `origin` validate/model and `review` true/false, §3.4); only info flags never need the owner. How the features read it (owner 2026-10-07, §13 step 9; exact definitions in feature_spec F8): "settled bar" = the §5.5 settled bar (1-minute, or the timeframe the gameplan names); the scenario set used is the one matching the event's time (`ny_am` from 09:30 to `gameplan_ny_am_end`, `session` otherwise; a lone set at any time); ES gate wording "holding" / "reclaiming" X = above X, "losing" / "rejecting" / "failing" X = below X, anything else unclear (empty feature, never a guess); HVL and HVL0 are two separate features, empty when not given; a target range is a band like any other level (its near edge counts as reached, order by the middle); a gameplan zone's invalidation is a feature only ("invalidation as written has been met", per timeframe), never a trading rule (CLAUDE.md rule 6): the zone itself is crossed when price trades past its far edge.
Features: in_zone, zone_side, scenario_agrees (a scenario's `bias` in the set used matches the trade direction), es_gate_ok, hvl, hvl0, gameplan_invalidated, and the gameplan member-type flag. Missing gameplan → features null (LightGBM handles missing).

**How gameplans are used (28 archived gameplans, 2026-08-12 to 2026-10-02: 26 in Aug–Sep 2026 and 2 in October 2026; with step 10 starting in October or early November they are all in the sealed or top-up months):** If the ledger written at step 4b puts any archived gameplan date in months 1–9 (a later SSD, or a second month-1 merge), that file is held back (no events, candidates or features) until step 20, so months 1–9 stay gameplan-free; Claude Code tells the owner when the ledger is written.
- **From the start:** gameplan zones create events, their `stop` and T1/T2 become stop/target candidates, (a gameplan's own blackout notes are not used: the owner's news rule, §9, replaces them). The models judge these by their makeup like any other zone (what's stacked there, node sizes, freshness, distances), so a gameplan zone that sits on strong structure scores well even before the gameplan itself has been learned; the gameplan boosts setups that already exist on the chart and maps.
- **The gameplan-specific inputs** above (all of them, including the member-type flag and side agreement) enter a model only when its training months hold ≥ `gameplan_min_days` (start 20) gameplan days **and** the gameplan check has passed once: an automated comparison, on gameplan days in already-scored months, of gameplan-zone hold rate and realized R against zones with the same stack count and member types that are not gameplan zones; pass = gameplan zones are not worse (point estimate), reported with intervals. With the archive in the sealed months, the first check runs at step 20 right after the sealed score, before the production fit. Every monthly retrain then re-runs the gameplan ablation (same method as §7 rule 5, on scored months with gameplan days) on scored months and drops the inputs if they hurt.
- The owner keeps uploading a gameplan every day so the data grows.

With all archived gameplans in sealed months, gameplan stop and T1/T2 candidates never appear in training rows before the production fit; in any fold whose scorer has no gameplan rows in training, they are encoded as type `other_price` (distance features only) and a gameplan container's makeup is computed like the golden-zone container. The sealed score is reported with and without gameplan-zone events, and the rule-2b pass rule is applied to the without-gameplan set, run as its own simulation (removing gameplan trades from a list would lose trades they had blocked; months 7–9 have none), and there is no gameplan report before step 20 (months 1–9 have no gameplan days). There is no separate gameplan model: "the gameplan was right" means its zones held rather than getting run through (owner), which is exactly the structure label for gameplan zones. The report shows gameplan-zone hold rates, and the §5.6 gameplan check and the monthly gameplan ablation show what the gameplan adds.

### 5.7 Order flow (Databento)

**Discovery, not a checklist (owner):** every indicator below is computed at every structure touch and handed to the trigger model and the entry-style comparison. None is ranked or preferred because the owner uses it: the backtest finds which indicators, thresholds and combinations actually work as entry triggers, including patterns the owner has never looked for. The trigger report shows each indicator's lift (alone and in combination), on development months 1–6; individual indicators are not pruned by hand (LightGBM's regularisation handles weak ones, §7.2 item 2), and the step-20 gate decides only whether order flow as a whole stays.

**Scope rule:** every detector in this section runs only while price is inside a structure zone (± `zone_proximity`) or a VAH/VAL area (§5.3), at every structure type. Outside zones these features are not computed into events. Each trigger event records which zone it belongs to and what is stacked there.
