**When a trigger fires (one definition for backtest and live):** while price is in scope, every detector is evaluated once per second on data ≤ t. **Detectors that fire:** absorption (style a); exhaustion, delta divergence, footprint imbalance (style b). Big trades, delta, CVD and the top-of-book readings are inputs only. A detector **fires** when its own thresholds (named parameters below, start values proposed at step 5) are crossed. Each firing writes one trigger row (the trigger model's unit), at most once per `trig_min_gap_sec` (start 5) per detector per event. Style (a)'s `decision_ts` is each absorption firing; style (b)'s is each firing of any other detector. Both can fire again within an event (a later firing is a new candidate), and the trigger model and pair scorer judge every one; which firing "worked" is never used to pick it. The detector set and thresholds are frozen on months 1–4 at step 19 (§7.1).
- **Big trades** (like Deepcharts' big-trades view; the owner doesn't rely on them because CVD and the footprint show the same thing, so they are an input only, never a trigger): the fills of one aggressive order (same `ts_event` and side, plus same-side prints within `big_trade_agg_ms`, start 50 ms, at any price: a sweep through several prices is one order, owner 2026-10-30, §13 Q-bigtradepx) are combined into one trade (side B or A only: a side-N print is never an aggressive order, never a big trade and never in the slot percentile, §13 Q-sideN); a big trade = combined size ≥ `big_trade_min_contracts` (the 99th percentile of combined NQ order size per 30-minute RTH slot (`big_trade_slot_min`) over the prior `big_trade_lookback_sessions` (20) full sessions, clipped to 0.5–2× the whole-session value: time of day only, not today's volume, since a busy day adds orders more than it enlarges them; owner 2026-10-17, §13 Q-featB B1). Features: big-trade count, size and side near the level, where they hit relative to the zone, and price progress after them. Big trades with no price progress are one absorption pattern among several (see Absorption below).
- **Delta** per bar = buy-aggressor volume − sell-aggressor volume (side N ignored); **CVD** = running delta; CVD slope.
- **Side N (no aggressor; owner 2026-10-29, §13 Q-sideN; feature_spec F0.1):** a side-N trade counts in volume wherever volume is used (bars, volume profiles, VWAP, the volume baselines, `vol_tod_w`, `rvol_today`) and is left out of everything signed or aggressor-based (delta, CVD, CVD slope, the footprint and imbalances, absorption, exhaustion, aggressive volume vs no-progress, NQ and ES deltas such as `xb_delta_rel` and `rel_delta`, both sides of the delta ratio, big trades). The step-17 order-flow report lists each session's side-N share of front-contract RTH volume (NQ and ES) and flags a session above `side_n_max_share` (0.01) for the owner, never silently used; beside it, each session's unexplained-outside share: the B/A volume printed strictly outside the `mbp-1` best bid/ask in force before its `ts_event` (the last book record with an earlier `ts_event`) by an order (`ts_event`, side) that printed nothing at or inside the touch, ÷ the session's B/A RTH volume (where spread-leg or implied fills labelled B/A would show; months 1–6 median 0.10%, roll periods 0.185%, highest 2.09% on 2025-11-28); report only, no threshold (owner 2026-10-30, §13 Q-sideN).
- **Footprint**: per-price bid/ask volume over a rolling `imb_window_sec`; diagonal imbalance ratio ≥ `imb_ratio`; it fires on a stack of ≥ `imb_stack_min` imbalances in the trade direction (owner 2026-10-05: buyer or seller dominance; window, ratio and stack are tuned, the grid includes a single imbalance).
- **Absorption** (owner: lots of aggressive buyers at the top, or sellers at the bottom, that fail to move price, and then price starts to push away from them; seen on the footprint or as CVD divergence): aggressive volume ≥ `abs_volume` × the volume scale (below) within the last `abs_seconds`, traded within `abs_ticks` of the visit's price extreme so far, wherever that is in the zone (owner 2026-10-05: order flow counts anywhere in the zone; area events: the area's price extreme so far), with no new extreme in that time, plus best bid/ask refill count ≥ `abs_refills` (from `mbp-1`, event-level). It **fires** (style a) only once price has moved `abs_push_ticks` away from the absorption range in the trade direction (owner: he wants to see price start to push away; the grid includes 0 so the data can show whether waiting helps; there is no time limit on the push, owner 2026-10-05: the range stays valid until the event ends or its zone is crossed, and the time to the push is an input). Feature: whether exhaustion or CVD divergence fired in the same zone visit (owner: absorbed **and** exhausted buyers is a great signal; the model learns how much).
- **Exhaustion**: aggressive volume decaying into the extreme (volume in the last `exh_lookback_sec` ≤ `exh_decay_ratio` × the window before it, with a new extreme made), thin volume at the high/low (unfinished auction) is an input only, not a firing condition (owner 2026-10-05).
- **Delta divergence** (the owner's "CVD divergence"): price makes a new extreme by ≥ `div_min_pts` within `div_lookback_sec` while CVD does not. It fires style (b); when it coincides with an absorption range it also feeds the absorbed + exhausted combo feature (it never fires style (a) by itself). This is how the owner's "absorption seen as CVD divergence" is covered: divergence alone is a style (b) trigger; with an absorption range in the same visit it feeds the combo feature and makes stop candidate 5 available.
- **Volume baselines and relative thresholds** (owner 2026-10-17, §13 Q-featB B1; feature_spec F9.10): N(m) = the median NQ volume per ET RTH minute over the prior 20 full sessions (early closes, holidays and damaged sessions left out; fewer than 20 → null); `vol_tod_w` = a window's volume ÷ its pro-rated normal; `rvol_today` = today's volume since 09:30 ÷ the normals of the same minutes, each minute clipped at `rvol_clip_mult` (5) × its normal. The volume thresholds `abs_volume` and `imb_min_volume` (a footprint price's minimum volume) are multiplied by the volume scale = tod_norm_w × clip(`rvol_today`, `of_scale_floor` 0.5, `of_scale_cap` 2.0), where tod_norm_w = the window's normal ÷ a typical minute's normal over the same length: the floor and cap apply to today's part only, never to the time-of-day part (the open and close run well above 2× a typical minute). They are still tuned once at step 19 (months 1–4), and the raw sizes stay inputs. Ratios and counts are not scaled. Multi-window inputs over 5 s and 60 s (`of_context_windows_sec`): delta ratio (÷ buy + sell aggressor volume, §13 Q-sideN), volume vs its time-of-day normal, CVD slope, net refills, big-trade net, aggressive volume vs no-progress (a column whose window equals a tuned or fixed window of the same quantity is not built).
- **Top of book (mbp-1, L1)**: best bid/ask size and its changes near the level, L1 imbalance (bid size vs ask size), spread, refill and pull counts at the best bid/ask.
- **v1 model inputs:** `trades` and `mbp-1` (L1) only — available over the whole window, in history and live.

### 5.8 Opening-range (IB) Fibonacci golden zone setup (owner's setup)
The owner catches many trades by drawing a Fibonacci retracement over the NY opening range and entering in the **golden zone (0.5–0.79)** on an order-flow trigger. (Upper fib 0.79, not 0.786: owner 2026-10-14; the fib levels are the named parameters `golden_fib_levels` and `golden_zone_upper_fib` in features.yaml, owner rules, never tuned.) **Two zones (owner 2026-10-27, §13 Q-gzsplit):** the golden zone is split at 0.618 (`golden_zone_split_fib`, owner rule, never tuned) into a **shallow zone** (0.5–0.618) and a **deep zone** (0.618–0.79), both drawn from the same fib (same orientation, flips and 10:30 redraw); each is its own golden-zone container (§5.4 Containers) with its own events. Whenever the drawing changes (a new extreme before 10:00 that reverses the orientation, a flip, the 10:30 redraw) both get new `zone_id`s. A settle past 0.618 breaks the shallow zone only; a settle past 0.79 breaks the deep zone and flips the drawing (with `golden_zone_flip` on). The deep zone is only ever reached through the shallow zone: the deep zone is touched only by a trade past 0.618 into it from the shallow side, never by coming within `zone_proximity` of 0.618 (owner 2026-10-27, §13 Q-gzsplit answer); its touch is a new touch with its own touch group, never shared with a shallow-zone touch, so after a shallow-zone trade has ended (e.g. stopped past 0.618) the deep zone can still trade, subject as always to one trade per account at a time and the direction lock; `zone_proximity` still applies to touches from outside the golden zone (the shallow zone approached from the 0 side); the deep zone re-arms back on the shallow side of 0.618 and `rearm_dist` away, and the next trade past 0.618 is a new deep-zone touch. Below, "the golden zone" means both zones unless one is named; the 09:45–10:00 window and `in_golden_zone` apply to both, and the step-13 flip / redraw variants are tested on the split drawing.

**Opening range, adaptive (not a hard 30-minute rule):**
- From 09:30 ET the bot tracks the running high/low. From **minute 15** (`ib_fib_start_minute`; owner 2026-10-16, was 20) onward the range counts as "established" and the fib is drawn on it, so a retrace into the golden zone between 09:45 and 10:00 is caught (owner's example: price hit the golden zone and bounced ~200 points at 09:55). Any of the fib levels inside the zone can play out (owner); which one was reached is a feature.
- If price makes a new extreme after minute 15 and before 10:00, the fib is redrawn on the larger range (orientation by the swing rule below). The range is the traded high/low (wicks) on the session's front contract (feature_spec F0.4, F10). If the orientation stays the same the golden zone just moves (same `zone_id`); if the swing rule reverses it, it is a **new golden zone** (new `zone_id`), like a flip: the old zone's open event ends, its pending entries are removed with `exit` (§5.5b), and its touch count and freshness don't carry over (owner 2026-10-26). The range **locks at 10:00** (`ib_lock_minute: 30`, minutes after 09:30; owner's rule, not tuned). From 10:00 the fib is **static**, even if price leaves the range.
- **10:30 redraw (owner 2026-10-16):** at 09:30 + `ib_redraw_minute` (60 → 10:30; owner rule, never tuned), if price made a new high or low beyond the 10:00 range between 10:00 and 10:30, the fib is redrawn **once** on the 09:30–10:30 range, its orientation set by the swing rule below (the redraw resets the anchors: earlier flips no longer apply); otherwise it stays. The IB high/low move with it (§5.3). The redrawn golden zone is a **new zone** (new `zone_id`): an open golden-zone event on the old drawing ends at 10:30, pending entries on it are removed with `exit` (§5.5b), open trades keep their brackets. There is no other redraw after 10:30. **A step-13 variant (owner 2026-10-26, §13 Q-fibvariants):** `golden_zone_redraw_1030` (start on); off = the 10:00 drawing and the IB high/low locked at 10:00 stay all day.
- **Trade window (owner's rule, §1.2): from 09:45 to `entry_end` (15:45)** (`ib_fib_start_minute: 15`; owner 2026-10-16, was 09:50). It is the only setup allowed before 10:00 (09:30 + `ib_lock_minute`). After the range locks (`ib_lock_minute`) the fib **stays drawn on the IB all day** (static, apart from the 10:30 redraw and the orientation flips below): price often comes back to the golden zone later, even after it already hit and played out earlier. Every return is a new event.
- **Repeat-touch features:** touch number of the golden zone today (1st, 2nd, …; each zone, shallow and deep, counts its own), whether the earlier touch(es) worked and how far they ran (**only outcomes already resolved at time t**; unresolved ones are marked pending), minutes since the last touch, minutes since the range locked, and whether price has left the IB since. The model learns whether a later re-test is as good as the first touch, instead of assuming it.
- **Skylit nodes inside the golden zone (owner: the setup works noticeably better with them, especially when QQQ, SPY and SPX all have nodes there):** features = which of the four maps have a relevant node (≥ 25% of King) whose band overlaps the golden zone (Nasdaq maps on NQ, S&P maps via ES), how many (0–4), node sizes and types, Trinity alignment (§5.2) inside the zone. The backtest reports golden-zone results split by how many maps have a node there, as a direct test of this.
- **Earlier start, report (owner 2026-10-16):** the backtest reports golden-zone results split by `decision_ts` in 09:45–09:50 vs later, so the owner can see whether the 09:45 start helps (report only; the window is an owner rule, never tuned).
- **Zone and fib-level report (owner 2026-10-27, §13 Q-gzfib, Q-gzsplit):** the step-15/16 backtest report adds golden-zone results by zone (`golden_zone_part`: shallow / deep) and by `fib_level_reached` (0.5 / 0.618 / 0.705 / 0.79): events, trades, win rate, mean R and expectancy per bucket with bootstrap intervals; any bucket under 100 events is flagged. Development months 1–6 only (§7 rule 2b); report only, no rule follows from it (rule 6).
- **Sample size:** a few golden-zone events per day (first touch plus later re-tests), so several hundred over the 12 months — the same 12 months as every other setup (owner: older data reflects different market conditions). If any golden-zone bucket ends up under ~100 events, the report says so and the model leans on simpler, pooled features for it.

**Fib orientation (owner's rule):**
- The fib is drawn on the leg in the direction of the most recent swing: if the low came first and price has run up to the high, anchor low → high (golden zone below current price = long pullback zone); if the high came first and price has sold off to the low, anchor high → low (golden zone above = short pullback zone).
- **Orientation flip (owner 2026-10-16):** the golden zone depends on the side price approaches from. When a 1-minute candle settles past the deep zone's 0.79 edge (the existing invalidation, §5.4; a settle past 0.618 only breaks the shallow zone and flips nothing, owner 2026-10-27), the fib is redrawn the other way on the same high and low, so the golden zone moves to the other half of the range with the opposite direction. Example: low 20,000 first, then high 20,100 → long zone 20,021–20,050; a candle settles below 20,021 → redrawn high → low → short zone 20,050–20,079; a candle later settling above 20,079 redraws it low → high again (long zone 20,021–20,050). Flips apply from 09:45 and continue all day: before 10:00 a new extreme still redraws the range and sets the orientation by the swing rule; the 10:30 redraw resets the anchors. Each flip makes new golden zones (new `zone_id`s for the shallow and the deep zone; the invalidated setup ends as now, §5.5b) that arms by the normal re-arm rule (§5.4). Flip count, minutes since the last flip (both counted within the current drawing: they restart when the anchors change, at a new extreme before 10:00 or at the 10:30 redraw) and the current orientation are features. **A step-13 variant (owner 2026-10-26, §13 Q-fibvariants):** `golden_zone_flip` (start on); off = the 0.79 invalidation ends the setup as now, the drawing never changes orientation, and later returns re-arm on the original side (§5.4 Containers).
- The swing rule, the 09:45 start (`ib_fib_start_minute` 15), the 10:00 lock (`ib_lock_minute` 30) and the fib levels are owner rules, never tuned; no orientation is learned from outcomes. Only the flip and the 10:30 redraw are tested, as pre-declared step-13 variants (§7.1 Tuning detector parameters; owner 2026-10-26). The step-12 visual check plots it on the sample days for the owner to confirm, including the 09:45 start, a 10:30 redraw and a flip.

**How it enters the system:**
- The golden zone (0.5–0.79 of the established range) is a **structure zone** like any other and goes into the confluence engine: a golden zone that also holds Skylit nodes (best when QQQ, SPY and SPX all have one there), the VWAP, the ON low, a dark-pool level or a gameplan zone scores higher.
- Features: `in_golden_zone`, `golden_zone_part` (shallow / deep; owner 2026-10-27), which fib level was reached (0.5 / 0.618 / 0.705 / 0.79), minutes since 09:30 when the range was established, which drawing the event used (running before 10:00 / locked at 10:00 / redrawn at 10:30), whether the 10:30 redraw happened (known from 10:30), range size at 10:00 and at 10:30, the current orientation, flip count and minutes since the last flip (owner 2026-10-16), range size in points and vs its `ib_avg_sessions`-session average (§5.3), whether the range broke the ON high/low, confluence inside the zone (each zone's own), regime, order-flow trigger readings.
- **Entry:** all four entry styles (§5.5), as at every other zone: (c) audit/sweep reclaim and (d) plain from Phase D, (a)/(b) order-flow triggers (§5.7) from step 19.
- **Stops:** candidates beyond the zone — past the zone's far edge + `stop_buffer` (§6.2 candidate 2: 0.618 for the shallow zone, 0.79 for the deep one; owner 2026-10-27) and past the range extreme (the 1.0 level) + `stop_buffer` (§6.2 candidate 6, golden zone only), plus the usual §6.2 candidates. Learned like §6.2.
- **Targets:** candidates include the range extreme (0 level), fib extensions (−0.272, −0.618), and all usual structures (VWAP, POC, LVNs, single prints, nodes, ON/PD levels). Chosen by the pair scorer (§6.3).
- Data: the fast 09:45–10:00 moves are why everything runs on 1-second bars and trades; order-flow detectors are built at step 17 and join the events at step 19.

---

## 6. Trade management: sizing, stops, targets, breakeven

Every trade has different proportions, so stop and target come from **structure**, and size comes from **fixed dollar risk**.

### 6.1 Position sizing (hard rule, not learned)
- All per-account inputs live in `config/accounts.yaml` (full field list in §6.7).
- NQ = $20/pt ($5/tick); MNQ = $2/pt ($0.50/tick). 10 MNQ = 1 NQ in risk.
- `risk_per_micro = (stop_pts + entry_slip_ticks × 0.25) × 2 + commission + exit_slippage_ticks × 0.50` — sized for the **worst accepted** entry fill, so a slipped fill still can't lose more than the risk amount. `stop_pts` is measured from the order's reference price: the limit price (passive limit), the trigger (stop-limit) or the current price at send (marketable limit). Every marketable or stop-limit order's limit sits exactly `max_entry_slip_ticks` beyond that reference. `entry_slip_ticks` = 0 for passive limit entries and `max_entry_slip_ticks` (start 4) for marketable-limit and stop-limit entries; `exit_slippage_ticks` start 1, set at step 13 (before labels are built) to the measured 95th-percentile stop slippage on development months 1–4 (method in step 13), and re-checked at step 25 against the step-24 imported fills (TradersPost log, or the owner's Orders/Fills upload). A few dollars of extra stop slippage on a third loss is ignored on purpose: firms usually allow slightly more than the stated drawdown (owner), so risk stays at drawdown ÷ N = N lives (N = `max_risk_fraction_divisor`, below). So `risk_usd_per_trade` is **all-in**: a full loss, costs included, is at most that amount. `risk_per_mini` is the same formula with $20/pt, $5/tick exit slippage and `commission_per_mini_rt`.
- `micros = floor(risk / risk_per_micro)`, where `risk` is the capped amount from the order of operations below (profile multiplier, drawdown ÷ N, remaining daily allowance). If `micros < 1` → **skip the trade** (stop too wide for the account). **Mixing NQ and MNQ (owner: allowed, `allow_mixed: true`):** pick minis `n` and micros `m` so the all-in risk lands as close as possible to the allowed risk without exceeding it, with the total size inside the account's limit (`n + m / micro_mini_ratio ≤` max, or `n + m ≤ max_contracts_total` on an account whose firm counts every contract the same (§6.7), and each instrument's own max where the firm sets one). Use minis for the bulk and micros only for the remainder (`m < micro_mini_ratio`), since 10 micros cost ~4× the commission of 1 mini ($19.00 vs $4.68 at MFFU rates). With `allow_mixed: false`, use the single instrument that lands closest. Commissions come from the account's `commission_per_*_rt`.
- **Max position cap (hard rule):** size is capped at the account's current limit: cap = min(the account's `max_minis` / `max_micros` / `max_contracts_total`, the active `scaling_tiers` row's limits for its current profit). Under a `max_contracts_total` limit a micro uses a whole slot, so when that limit binds the search keeps minis (e.g. 5 slots, a stop that fits 4.6 minis' risk: 4 minis + 1 micro, not 4 minis + 6 micros). The bot **sizes down to the cap** rather than sending an order the broker would reject; a capped trade simply risks less than the full amount. An order that would exceed the cap is never sent (checked again right before every webhook).
- **Per-trade risk cap = a static 1/N of the account's total drawdown**, N = `max_risk_fraction_divisor`, set per account on the Accounts page: **2, 3 (default) or 4**, whole numbers only (risk 1/2, 1/3 or 1/4 of the drawdown; 1/2 is the upper limit; nothing below 1/4, since the Conservative and Protect profiles already lower risk; owner 2026-10-14, §13 Q-riskfrac), enforced in the UI, in the config loader (`nqbot/risk.py`) and again right before every webhook. `risk_usd_per_trade` follows the setting: when N changes, the UI sets it to floor(`drawdown_usd` ÷ N) (the owner may type a lower amount). Actual risk = `min(risk_usd_per_trade × profile multiplier (§6.8; Custom: custom_risk_usd), drawdown_usd ÷ N)`. Example: $1,000 drawdown at the default 1/3 → ~$333 per trade (1/4 → $250, 4 lives; 1/2 → $500, 2 lives: after one full loss the account is on its last life), **every trade, regardless of earlier losses**. **Raising risk** (N goes down, e.g. 3 → 2 = 1/3 → 1/2) needs the owner's typed confirmation (§9b) and waits for the account's sweep, taking effect at the next session after it (§6.8); **lowering risk** (N goes up, e.g. 3 → 4 = 1/3 → 1/4) needs no confirmation and applies from the next trade. The cap does not shrink after a loss: N full losses use up the drawdown (a dollar or so is left), and any later loss fails the account. This is deliberate (owner's choice): the most the owner can lose is the cost of the prop account, so the bot should press the edge rather than shrink after losses. The backtest's prop simulator uses this exact rule. The last trade is **not** sized down to fit what's left (owner): after two full losses (~$334 left) the next trade still risks the full $333.
- **Daily loss cap (when the account has one: `bot_daily_loss_cap_usd` if set, else the firm's DLL if `dll_enabled`):** risk is also capped at what's left of today's allowance, so a full loss lands at the cap, not past it (§6.7 item 1).
- **Daily profit cap:** size is also limited so the trade's target, if hit at the best accepted fill (no entry slip), doesn't push the day past the account's daily cap (see §6.7; in consistency mode the cap is `consistency_pct` × (`profit_target_usd` + max(0, base − the lowest end-of-day balance so far)), owner 2026-10-26, §13 Q-consistdip). Example: cap $450 (Rapid EOD eval), risk $333, target 3R → the trade is sized so a win nets ≤ $450 after commissions. If even 1 micro would overshoot the remaining cap, skip the trade.
- Order of operations: for each eligible pair, risk = min(fixed risk × profile multiplier, static 1/N of total drawdown, remaining daily allowance if the account has a daily cap) → contracts from stop distance → cap at max position size → reduce if the target would exceed the remaining daily profit cap → drop the pair if < 1 micro; then rank the remaining pairs by EV (by expected dollars whenever any sizing cap binds — max position or scaling tier, remaining daily loss allowance, daily profit cap — §6.3) and send the best.
- Because dollar risk is fixed, every trade is measured in **R** (1R = the dollars risked). The model optimizes expected R, not points. **Account R:** in every simulator result, money score, gate and report, R = realized net dollars ÷ the account's nominal risk (`risk_usd_per_trade` × profile multiplier, capped at drawdown ÷ N; `default_risk_usd` in default-cost runs), never ÷ the trade's own capped risk, so a trade the caps shrank counts for what it really made. Labels keep per-trade R for the scorer.

### 6.2 Stops (structural)
- Candidate stops, all beyond something that could support the trade:
  1. beyond the **swing extreme** of the approach — the highest high (shorts) / lowest low (longs) from the event's last re-arm to `decision_ts` (area events: over the last `swing_lookback_sec`, start 300) — + `stop_buffer`
  2. beyond the **far edge of the entry zone** + `stop_buffer`
  3. beyond the **next structure** behind the entry (another node, POC, LVN, single print, band) + `stop_buffer`
  4. the gameplan's `stop`, when the trade comes out of a gameplan zone
  5. beyond the **absorption range** (owner): when absorption (§5.7) was detected within `abs_stop_window_sec` (start 120) at or before `decision_ts`, inside the event's zone ± `zone_proximity` or its VAH/VAL area, the stop goes beyond the highest price (shorts) / lowest price (longs) at which the absorbed aggressive volume traded, + `abs_stop_buffer` (owner: about 5 NQ pts, the start value; frozen with the other execution parameters on months 1–4, §7.1). Available for **every** entry style and structure type, not only absorption entries. Built at step 19 (it needs the step-17 detectors); events are relabelled with it then.
  6. golden zone only: beyond the opening range's extreme (the fib 1.0 level, §5.8) + `stop_buffer`.
- **No stop guardrails (owner):** `min_stop_pts` and `max_stop_pts` are null; the only limit is that at least 1 micro fits the risk (§6.1). Claude Code proposes the starting `stop_buffer`; a stop beyond a mapped level uses at least `stop_buffer_floor` of the level's source (§5.1; candidates 2 and 3: the zone member that sets the far edge, or the structure the stop sits behind; candidates 1 and 6 sit beyond prices, not mapped levels, and have no floor). The model chooses the stop for each trade.
- Learned: the buffer (from stored path data, §6.5 — e.g. how often stopped trades overshot the stop by only 1–3 points and then hit target), and which stop type works best per regime/level type.

### 6.3 Targets (structural, min 1:1, no max)
- **Exact prices (one rule for backtest and live):** a target candidate's limit price = the near edge (the edge price reaches first) of the target zone's band as of `decision_ts`, plus the zone's centre as a second candidate so the model learns which works; stop candidates 2, 3 and 6 sit `stop_buffer` beyond the far edge of their band as of `decision_ts` (§6.2). Prices are then rounded as in §5.1.
- Candidate targets = every registry level (§5.4) and confluence zone in the trade's direction, plus fib extensions for golden-zone trades (§5.8) and the gameplan's T1/T2 when the trade comes out of a gameplan zone. The same structures used for entries are used for exits.
- In selection, discard candidates below **1:1 net RR** at the account's own costs (owner rule; one exception, only inside the step-15 farming challenger on Lucid Flex funded farming days, §13 Q-flexfarm (2)); labels are built down to `pair_build_min_rr` (0.8) at default costs so every account's true 1:1 candidates exist (§7.1, step 13). **RR is net of costs:** net RR = reward_usd ÷ risk_usd, with risk_usd = `risk_per_micro` (§6.1) and reward_usd = (|target − reference price| − entry_slip_ticks × 0.25) × $2 − `commission_per_micro_rt` (a target is a limit, so it has no exit slippage; minis likewise at $20/pt), so a tight stop isn't flattered by ignoring costs. The same net RR is used in EV and the 1:1 floor.
- **The farthest target is not assumed to be best.** The pair scorer (§7.1) learns, from every past trade path, which structures price actually reaches before the stop and which ones it stalls at. Distance only wins when the odds of getting there hold up.
- **The pair scorer** predicts, for each candidate, the probabilities of three outcomes — target first, stop first, timeout (neither by `session.flat_by` or the news flatten, §9, closed at market; the prop simulator re-cuts per account) — plus `E[R | timeout]` (a regression on the realized R of past timeouts). Pick the one with the highest expected R: `EV = P_target × RR − P_stop × 1 + P_timeout × E[R | timeout]`. Timeouts are not counted as wins or full losses; in the simple 2-outcome case this is `P × RR − (1 − P)`. A far target with high P wins; a far target with low P loses to a closer one.
- **Per account:** probabilities are account-independent (horizon `session.flat_by`, cut short by a news flatten, §9). Each account applies its own costs, 1:1 floor, λ, thresholds, caps and lives and picks its own pair (so one setup can trade on one account and be skipped on another), subject to the cross-account direction lock (§10.3); the entry notice lists each account's pair.
- **Stop and target are chosen as a pair**, not separately: a wider stop changes which targets are reachable and the RR of each. The model scores every pair (entry style × stop candidate × target candidate, §7.1 dataset) and, at each entry style's `decision_ts`, picks the best stop × target pair; across styles, §7.1 "Which entry style trades" applies (the first eligible style trades; EV only compares styles eligible at the same moment).
- **Prop-account aware objective.** Plain expected R favors rare big winners, which means long losing streaks that can breach a prop drawdown. The selection objective is `expected R − λ × risk penalty`, where the penalty is the pair's variance of R (from P_target, P_stop, P_timeout and E[R | timeout]). With breakeven on, EV and the variance use the four outcomes of §6.6 (`R_be` includes `exit_slippage_ticks`). `λ` is **not tuned**: it is 0 by default (pure expected R; the `max_loss_run_rate` limit, §6.5b, already keeps losing streaks in check), and the owner can set it per account (`lambda_risk_penalty`) — consistent with his view that losing an account is an accepted, bounded cost.
- **Time matters** only through P_timeout and E[R | timeout] (a target that usually takes hours carries more timeout risk). Time-to-target is predicted and reported, not added to EV.
- **Probability floor:** a pair is only eligible if P ≥ `min_target_prob` (tunable), so a huge-RR, low-odds target can't win on paper alone.
- **Winner's curse (must handle):** picking the best of many stop × target pairs systematically picks pairs whose P was *over*-estimated, so the chosen trade's stated EV is too optimistic. Order: (1) at each decision point (event × enabled entry style × firing n) pick the pair with the highest raw EV among that point's pairs passing the 1:1 floor at default costs — the **decision-point candidate**; (2) fit calibration on the filled decision-point candidates in the fold's tuning months (§7 rule 2), weighted 1 ÷ (decision points in the event × events in its touch group) (the same rows and weights serve ECE, quantile cutoffs and live checks; row minimums count the sum of weights); (3) apply EV and probability thresholds to the calibrated values; (4) live ranks each account's sized pairs by **raw** EV at the account's costs (raw expected dollars when a cap binds) and applies the calibrator only to the chosen pair before the thresholds, since the calibrator was fitted on chosen pairs. The policy and prop simulators re-run this per-account selection over all pairs at each decision point, in §6.1's order of operations, rather than re-sizing a fixed trade list; gate 16 also checks ECE on the traded pairs. Every reported result uses the **realized** R of those selected trades, never the model's predicted EV.
- **When any sizing cap binds** (§6.1: max position or scaling tier, remaining daily loss allowance, daily profit cap), pairs are re-ranked by **expected dollars after cap-limited sizing**, not by R: a nearer target at full size can beat a far target that had to be cut to a few micros.
- **Proof:** compare target-selection policies on walk-forward test months 7–9 (chosen once there, §7 rule 2 Choices vs scores; later runs only report) — nearest structure, farthest structure, max expected R, and risk-adjusted max expected R (identical to max expected R while λ = 0, so skipped then) — each run as the full per-fold pipeline (style, breakeven, calibration, threshold sweep on the tuning months) with only the target choice restricted by its rule — on expected R, win rate, max drawdown, longest losing streak, and simulated prop-account pass rate. Keep whichever wins by the §6.5b objective. The fixed 1:2.5 policy (the target at 2.5 × the chosen structural stop's distance, the stop picked by EV) is run the same way but only as the gate-16/20 baseline, never selectable; its target rows train the scorer but are never a candidate for any other policy.
- Learned per candidate: distance in ATR/expected-move units, target type, confluence at the target, **velocity of the target node** (a growing node pulls harder — owner's read: accumulation in a direction strengthens targets there), number of structures **in between** (obstacles, including SPY/SPX nodes in the path on ES, §5.2), regime, day type.
- v1 bootstrap before the pair scorer is trained: stop candidate 2 (far edge + `stop_buffer`; candidate 1 for area events, which have no far edge) with the nearest structural target ≥ 1:1, plus a fixed 1:2.5 baseline for comparison.

### 6.4 Breakeven (optional)
- If the execution bridge can't move the stop on Tradovate, breakeven is simply turned off (`breakeven_enabled: false`); trades run to stop or target. The backtest reports results with and without it so the owner knows what is lost.
- Move stop to entry (+ fees, `be_offset_ticks`) once the fold's chosen `be_trigger` rule fires.
- Owner's rule (owner: his view and starting point, not a must-use; the re-simulation below decides by the money score, §6.5b): when the target is **more than one structure away**, move to breakeven once a **1-minute bar settles past the first structure** between entry and target (open and close both beyond its band); when the target is the first structure, no breakeven. "Structures" here = active NQ registry zones (§5.4) strictly between entry and target as of `decision_ts`, excluding containers, VAH/VAL areas and ES-only levels; the first one's band is **frozen at its `decision_ts` value** for that trade, in backtest and live alike, and the labeler stores, per pair, the first 1-minute settle time past that frozen band. The `breakeven` signal is sent at that bar's close and takes effect `order_latency_sec` later.
- Learned: the trigger is chosen by **re-simulating every stored trade path** under the four pre-declared rules (none, +0.5R, +1R, first structure; `be_trigger`, owner-approved 2026-10-05, `docs/feature_spec.md` F16.2) and picking what scores best on the §6.5b objective on each fold's tuning months (§7 rule 2); a per-regime split counts as separate grid entries and must be within the ≤ 4 rules approved at step 6. BE often raises win rate but cuts winners that retest entry — the stored paths show the true tradeoff.

### 6.5 Path data stored for every candidate trade (taken or not)
So any stop/target/BE rule can be re-simulated later without rebuilding events:
- Full price path after entry: read from the shared per-session 1-second bars (Databento `ohlcv-1s`, never copied per candidate) from entry to 16:00 ET; the timeout label uses `session.flat_by` (anything still open then is closed at market, exactly as live), and the prop simulator re-cuts at each account's own `flat_by`. Events don't belong to an account: the prop simulator also re-applies the 1:1 floor with each account's commissions.
- **MAE** (max adverse excursion) and **MFE** (max favorable excursion), and the time each happened.
- **If stopped:** how far price went beyond the stop (overshoot), and whether the target was reached afterward (stopped-then-target) and how long after.
- **If target hit:** how far price extended past the target (extension), and whether it returned to entry afterward.
- Which structures were reached in order, with timestamps.
- Reports every run: stopped-then-target rate and overshoot distribution by stop type (tight stops show up here), extension past target by target type (targets left too close), BE simulation results.

### 6.5b Execution threshold (when the bot takes a trade)
- The threshold is on **expected R**, not raw probability, because every trade has a different RR. Breakeven probability for a trade = `1 / (1 + RR)` (1:1 → 50%, 1:2 → 33%, 1:3 → 25%). A trade is taken only if EV (the §6.3 formula, net RR, including the timeout outcome) `≥ min_ev_r` **and** `P ≥ min_target_prob`.
- **Pre-declared grids:** every per-fold choice uses a grid fixed in `settings.yaml` before step 15 (`threshold_grid`: ≤ 5 × 5 values of `min_ev_r` × `min_target_prob`; ≤ 4 breakeven rules; `last_life_grid` [0.10, 0.20, 0.33] (fixed, owner); and, fixed, `profile_ev_quantiles` {conservative: 0.67, protect: 0.90}). The menus (which values are on each grid) are proposed by Claude Code at step 5 and approved by the owner at step 6, before any result is seen; the data then picks from them. With few tuning days the top average is partly luck, so every per-fold choice picks the setting with the highest **money score** = mean total R per month − one standard error (day-block bootstrap), subject to the `max_loss_run_rate` limit: it rewards making the most money and discounts settings whose result rests on a few lucky days. The number of settings tried is printed with each fold's result.
- **How the values are chosen (never guessed):** sweep `min_ev_r` and `min_target_prob` on the out-of-fold tuning months of §7 rule 2 (months 5…m−1 for test month m); for each setting record trades/month, expected R per trade, total R per month, max drawdown, longest losing streak, and prop-simulator pass/breach rate per account. Pick the setting that maximizes the owner's objective (scored by the money score above, which applies to every per-fold choice of §6.4, §6.5b and §6.8) — **total R per month**, subject to winning often enough that a run of L straight full losses stays uncommon, L = the full losses the account's risk allows (the lives at a full drawdown, floor((`drawdown_usd` − `lives_dust_usd`) ÷ risk) + 1: 2, 3 or 4 at 1/2, 1/3 or 1/4; owner 2026-10-14): the bootstrapped share of L-trade runs that are all full losses ≤ `max_loss_run_rate` (full loss = a stop outcome with account R ≤ −0.9; runs = overlapping consecutive L-trade windows in the account's chronological trade sequence, across days, averaged over day-block bootstrap paths; account-independent choices and gates 16/20 (and the step-21/25/27 re-checks) use the `gate_account` block in settings.yaml — `default_risk_usd`, default commissions, `session.flat_by`, 2 minis / 20 micros, `default_max_three_loss_rate` (0.15; the gate account stays at 1/3, so its runs are 3 trades), no daily caps, no lives, no `stop_at_target`, no drawdown or inactivity rules (per-account and preset results are reports); if no setting meets the limit, the fold takes no trades and the report says so) (per account `max_loss_run_rate`, 0.15 for every N (owner 2026-10-14): roughly a 61%+ win rate at 1/2, 47%+ at 1/3, 38%+ at 1/4) — then confirm once on the sealed months at step 20. Both the win probability and R per trade count: this limit (and λ, if the owner sets one, §6.3) stops the bot from chasing rare far targets. Breach rates are reported, not used as a limit (owner: hitting the DLL or drawdown is acceptable as long as each trade risks the right amount). **Money first (owner):** what he wants is the most money made, which also passes accounts fastest; trade count matters only through that (more +EV trades raise total R per month, so the money score already favours them). Report trades/day and the share of days with no trade; if even the loosest +EV setting gives < 1/day, the report says so, with which setups would add volume.
- Requires **calibrated** probabilities (§7): a stated 0.65 must win ~65% of the time, or the threshold is meaningless. Live calibration is monitored and the threshold re-checked at each retrain.
- **"Lives" mode (owner's idea):** thresholds depend on how many full losses the account can still take: `lives = floor((drawdown_remaining − lives_dust_usd) / risk_per_trade) + 1`, with `risk_per_trade` = the all-in risk after the profile multiplier and `lives_dust_usd` (start 25) so a few leftover dollars don't count as a life — **drawdown only** ($1,000 at $333 → 3; after two full losses ~$334 left → 1, the last life; at 1/2, $500 → 2 and one full loss leaves the last life; at 1/4, $250 → 4). Lives ≤ 1 (including 0, when only dust is left) is the last life. The daily loss limit is not counted as lives (on a $600 DLL with $333 risk, any single loss would put the bot on its last life and it would barely trade); it is handled by the daily cap (§6.7). **It only changes anything on the last life** (owner's rule): while 2 or more lives remain, the normal thresholds apply. With **1 life left — one full loss from failing the account** — only the best setups are taken: the cutoff is the (1 − `last_life_top_pct`) quantile of calibrated EV among the decision-point candidates that pass the account's Normal thresholds in the fold's tuning months (if fewer than 30 rows lie above a cutoff, the next looser option is used — 0.33, then the Normal thresholds — and the report says so) (`last_life_top_pct: auto` by default: in per-fold step (4) of §7 rule 2 the prop simulator compares 0.10 / 0.20 / 0.33 on the fold's tuning months and keeps the one with the highest money score computed in the prop simulator in account dollars (account fees are not modelled, §6.7; after a breach the simulator starts a new evaluation); expected profit per evaluation started is reported; a number the owner types overrides it). It is written to `profiles.yaml` as the `last_life` thresholds, and live applies those. Risk per trade stays static.
- The prop simulator compares "lives mode on vs off" per account: pass rate, average days to pass, expected profit per evaluation started. Tradeoff to watch: on the last life the bot may wait days for a qualifying setup (the simulator counts a 7-day gap as an inactivity close, §6.7), and very high P setups usually carry lower RR. The on/off comparison is report-only; the owner's `lives_mode` flag decides.
- Per account in `config/accounts.yaml`: `lives_mode`, `last_life_top_pct`, and `min_ev_r` / `min_target_prob` for the Custom profile. The other profiles read their thresholds from `models/profiles.yaml` (§6.8).

### 6.6 Labels and costs
- **Breakeven-aware labels:** pairs get 4-outcome labels for **every** candidate breakeven rule of §6.4 (a cheap re-simulation from the stored paths), and each fold trains on the labels of the rule its own tuning months picked (§7 rule 2), so no fold uses a rule chosen with later data. The four outcomes are target / stop / breakeven exit / timeout, where `R_be` = the MNQ breakeven offset at default costs (§3.5 test 2: ceil(commission ÷ tick value) + `be_offset_ticks`) net of costs and `exit_slippage_ticks` (a breakeven exit is a stop fill); the pair scorer is retrained and EV = P_target·RR − P_stop + P_be·R_be + P_timeout·E[R | timeout]. A no-breakeven scorer is kept for accounts with `breakeven_enabled: false`; per-fold step (4) calibrates and sweeps it too and writes its profiles, so it is validated by the same folds and the sealed score. If step 21's test 2 fails, months 7–9 are re-run with breakeven set to none and must meet the step-16 criteria, and the production fit re-runs before step 24. Live checks compare hit rates under the same exit rule as the prediction.
- Costs in every label: commission + slippage per the order-type fill rules (§5.5b). Unfilled entries are recorded as "no trade" (with what would have happened), never as wins. The pair scorer trains and calibrates **only on filled rows** (its probabilities are conditional on a fill); unfilled rows feed only the fill-rate and adverse-selection report (§5.5b) and are never labelled with their hypothetical outcome.
- Labels stored per pair (event_id, entry style, firing n, stop candidate, target candidate) for every order type (the frozen order type per style, §7 rule 1, trains the scorer; the others feed only the §5.5b order-type report), plus the path data above. After each freeze (step 13 for (c)/(d), step 19 for (a)/(b)) the non-frozen order types are built only for months 1–4, where that report runs, and pairs whose `decision_ts` is outside the setup windows or in a blackout only for months 1–6 (the out-of-window report, rule 2b), which keeps every later rebuild about a third the size. Time limit = `session.flat_by`, or the moment the next news flatten takes effect if earlier (§9; that exit is a timeout at market) (no max-hold exit: v1 trades end only at stop, target, flat-by, the news flatten or an owner exit, §10.1); costs use the default commissions in `settings.yaml`, expressed per micro (`risk_per_micro`, §6.1, at the default micro commission — the costliest mix), and the fill simulation uses the size the default risk (`default_risk_usd`, start 333) gives; the prop simulator and live re-cost each trade with the account's actual NQ/MNQ mix and commissions.
- Keep the fixed 1:2.5 label (a target at 2.5 × each structural stop's distance) as a baseline to prove structural targets beat it.

### 6.7 Per-account prop rules (each account is different)
Every account carries its own rules. The owner edits them in the **Control Panel UI** (§9b) — no YAML editing needed — and the UI saves them to the panel's accounts file (`<data_dir>/panel/accounts.yaml` on the SSD, §9b; `config/accounts.yaml` is the committed example; owner 2026-11-15). Both the live risk module and the backtest's **prop-account simulator** enforce them:
```yaml
- name: mffur1                 # lowercase letters and digits only (the UI checks); this example = the MFFU Rapid EOD 25k evaluation preset (below)
  active: true                 # false once passed, failed or replaced: only active accounts trade and get lives and caps
  firm: MyFundedFutures
  phase: evaluation            # evaluation | funded
  demo: false                  # true = Tradovate demo account (step 21–25 validation)
  min_trading_days: 4          # firm rules for the prop simulator's pass test (null = none); Rapid EOD eval: 4 days
  max_inactive_days: 7          # calendar days (weekends and holidays included) with no trade before the firm closes the account (MFFU: 7, every account; Lucid: 30)
  payout_min_days: null        # funded: trading days needed in a payout cycle (Builder: 2, which its 50% consistency rule forces anyway)
  traderspost_webhook_env: TP_WEBHOOK_MFFUR1   # name of the .env variable holding this account's webhook URL
  # --- profit cap: the owner picks a mode and types numbers ---
  profit_cap_mode: consistency # consistency | none (Rapid EOD eval: 30% consistency → $450/day)
  profit_target_usd: 1500      # the eval's profit target (consistency mode: also the base of the cap)
  stop_at_target: true         # evaluation: no new entries once balance ≥ start + profit_target_usd and consistency and min_trading_days are met (the eval is passed)
  consistency_pct: 0.30        # consistency mode: daily cap = this share of (the target + the lowest EOD dip below the base) (owner 2026-10-26, §13 Q-consistdip)
  payout_target_usd: null      # funded: profit above payout_baseline_usd at which the owner requests a payout (default payout_min_usd)
  funded_buffer_usd: null      # funded: profit above start balance to clear before any payout (Rapid EOD / Builder: 1100)
  payout_min_usd: null         # funded: smallest payout (Rapid EOD 500, Builder 250)
  payout_profit_since_last_usd: null # funded: net profit needed since the last payout before the next (Rapid EOD 250)
  payout_max_usd: null         # funded: largest payout per cycle (Rapid EOD: null = no cap; Builder 1000; Lucid Flex 1000)
  payout_pct_of_profit: null   # funded: withdrawable = min(payout_max_usd, this × (balance − payout_baseline_usd)) (Lucid Flex: 0.50); null = balance − baseline
  payout_profit_days_min: null # funded: days in the payout cycle with net P&L ≥ payout_profit_day_min_usd needed for a payout (Lucid Flex: 5); restarts after each payout
  payout_profit_day_min_usd: null # funded: the net profit that makes a day count for payout_profit_days_min (Lucid Flex: 100)
  payouts_taken: 0             # funded: set by the "Payout taken" button (count and last payout date)
  after_first_payout: null     # funded: cap fields that replace the current ones after the first payout (Builder: {profit_cap_mode: consistency, profit_target_usd: 1000, consistency_pct: 0.50} → $500/day)
  payout_wait_calendar_days: null # funded: calendar days before each payout may be requested, counted from the first funded trade for the first payout and from the previous payout after that; runs alongside the buffer, both must be met (Pro: 14; Rapid EOD / Builder: null)
  payout_clock_hold: "off"     # quoted: YAML reads a bare off as false; funded, with payout_wait_calendar_days: what the bot does once the payout is reachable but the clock is still running: off (trade normally; default) | pause (no new entries until the clock runs out) | protect (trade on the Protect profile until then); the share and cap stops of the payout cap group apply whatever this says
  profit_split_pct: null       # funded: the trader's share of each payout, for reports only (Pro: 0.80; Lucid Flex: 0.90); null = reports show the gross payout only
  firm_balance_from_zero: false # true = the firm shows the account from $0 (Lucid funded): typed balance and floor get start_balance_usd added
  flip_hold_max_sec: null      # flipping rule, report only: trades held ≤ this count as short (Lucid: 5)
  flip_profit_share_max: null  # the firm's limit on profit from those trades (Lucid: 0.50)
  flip_profit_share_warn: null # warn above this share (Lucid: 0.25)
  payout_cap_group: null       # funded: accounts with the same group share one cumulative payout cap (Pro: mffu_pro); null = no shared cap
  payout_cap_group_usd: null   # cumulative gross payouts allowed across every account of the group, all funded runs, active or not (Pro: 100000)
  copied_accounts: 0           # how many accounts copy this one through TradeSyncer (0 = none); the owner changes it any time, e.g. to 0 when he cuts a copy connection; used for the group payout cap
  auto_protect_within_usd: null # funded: switch to Protect this close to the payout target (§6.8)
  # --- loss side ---
  drawdown_usd: 1000
  drawdown_type: eod_trailing  # static | eod_trailing (owner never trades intraday-trailing accounts)
  drawdown_lock_usd: null      # eod_trailing: the floor value at which the floor stops rising; null = never locks (every MFFU evaluation, this example included: the floor trails continuously; MFFU Rapid EOD and Builder funded: start + $100 = 25100, reached when the $1,100 buffer is; Pro funded: 50100)
  drawdown_lock_trigger: floor # eod_trailing: floor = the floor stops rising when it reaches drawdown_lock_usd (Rapid EOD and Builder funded); first_payout = the floor trails with no cap until the first payout, then is fixed at drawdown_lock_usd for good (Pro funded: 50100); floor_or_first_payout = the floor stops rising at drawdown_lock_usd, or is fixed there at the first payout if that comes first (Lucid Flex funded: 25100)
  start_balance_usd: 25000     # account size at the start
  # daily state (balance, floor) is NOT stored here: it lives in <paths.live_dir>/account_state.json (below)
  dll_enabled: false           # false = this account has no daily loss limit (Rapid EOD has none; Builder: true, 600)
  firm_daily_loss_limit_usd: null  # used only when dll_enabled (Builder: 600)
  bot_daily_loss_cap_usd: null # null = use the firm's DLL (no daily cap if dll_enabled is false); set it to stop earlier, or to add a cap on a no-DLL account
  max_losses_per_day: null     # optional, e.g. 2
  max_trades_per_day: null     # optional hard cap
  max_loss_run_rate: 0.15      # §6.5b: share of L-trade runs (L = lives at a full drawdown: 2 / 3 / 4 at 1/2 / 1/3 / 1/4) that are all full losses must stay at or below this; 0.15 for every N (renamed from max_three_loss_rate, owner 2026-10-14)
  # --- sizing ---
  max_risk_fraction_divisor: 3 # 2, 3 or 4: risk ≤ 1/N of TOTAL drawdown (static, never shrinks; the owner sets it per account; raising risk (N down) needs the typed confirmation and waits for the sweep, lowering risk (N up) applies from the next trade, §9b; owner 2026-10-14)
  risk_usd_per_trade: 333      # follows max_risk_fraction_divisor: floor(drawdown_usd ÷ N); the owner may type lower
  custom_risk_usd: null        # Custom profile only (≤ drawdown ÷ N); other profiles use risk_usd_per_trade × multiplier
  max_minis: 2                 # firm's max position in NQ (owner's MFFU 25k accounts: 2 minis)
  max_micros: 20               # firm's max position in MNQ (2 minis = 20 micros)
  micro_mini_ratio: 10         # how the firm counts micros vs minis (usually 10:1)
  max_contracts_total: null    # set when the firm counts every contract the same, mini or micro: minis + micros ≤ this, replacing the micro_mini_ratio rule (Pro 50k funded: 5, so 5 minis, 4 + 1 micro, 3 + 2, … 0 + 5); null = the micro_mini_ratio rule
  scaling_tiers: null          # optional: firm scaling plan, a list of {min_profit_usd, max_minis, max_micros, max_contracts_total (optional)}
  scaling_tier_update: intraday # intraday = the tier follows the working balance | eod = the previous session's end-of-day profit (Lucid Flex funded)
  allow_micros: true
  allow_mixed: true           # one trade may be an NQ leg + an MNQ leg (§6.1, §10.3)
  commission_per_mini_rt: 4.68    # round-trip $ per NQ, all fees included (MFFU)
  commission_per_micro_rt: 1.90   # round-trip $ per MNQ, all fees included (MFFU)
  flat_by: "15:59"             # owner: trade 09:30–16:00, flat just before 16:00 (firms liquidate ~16:00–16:15)
  # --- risk profile (§6.8): quick dial the owner changes in the UI ---
  risk_profile: normal         # aggressive | normal | conservative | protect | custom
  # --- thresholds: min_ev_r / min_target_prob apply only with risk_profile: custom; other profiles read models/profiles.yaml ---
  min_ev_r: 0.20
  min_target_prob: 0.40
  lives_mode: true
  last_life_top_pct: auto      # last life: only the top X% by calibrated EV; auto = the prop simulator's pick of 0.10/0.20/0.33, or type a number
  lambda_risk_penalty: 0      # §6.3; not tuned; 0 = pure expected R, a positive number penalises high-variance pairs
  breakeven_enabled: true
  sim_fills: true              # Tradovate sim account: a resting limit fills on a touch (step 21); false for a real-money account
```

  **Pro 50k, which rules the simulator already models and which are new (2026-10-08):** already modelled by existing fields: start balance, profit target, EOD-trailing drawdown, no DLL, minimum trading days, the consistency cap (same rule type as Rapid EOD's 30%: cap = `consistency_pct` × (`profit_target_usd` + the dip below the base), owner-confirmed 2026-10-26, §13 Q-consistdip), per-stage max minis / micros for the evaluation (each preset stage fills its own), no scaling (`scaling_tiers: null`), `stop_at_target`, the buffer (`funded_buffer_usd` → `payout_baseline_usd`), the minimum payout, no per-payout maximum, inactivity, the Tier-1 news rule (§9) and fees (not modelled in trading; the plan fees, owner 2026-11-16: `evaluation_fee_usd` one-time per evaluation started, `activation_fee_usd` 0, are in the presets for reports and the plan lifecycle, §13 Q-perf-fees). **New rule types** (fields above; each defaults to null / `floor` / `off` / 0, so the Rapid EOD and Builder presets don't use them):
  1. **Payout clock alongside the buffer** (`payout_wait_calendar_days`): each payout also needs that many calendar days (weekends and holidays included), counted from the account's first funded trade for the first payout and from the previous payout for every later one (the clock restarts at each payout; owner 2026-10-09); the simulator counts from the first trade of each funded run and then from each simulated payout, live from the first funded fill the bot records and then from each recorded payout date (the owner can correct either date on the Accounts page). It and the buffer run at the same time: a payout waits for whichever is met last. **Hold while the clock runs** (`payout_clock_hold`, default `off`): once balance − `payout_baseline_usd` ≥ max(`payout_min_usd`, `payout_target_usd`) but the clock has not run out, `pause` blocks new entries (a Discord note gives the date trading resumes) and `protect` trades on the Protect profile until then; the owner switches it on when he feels the account holds enough, and manual Pause / Resume still works. The share stop and the cap stop (new rule type 3) apply whatever the hold is set to; `off` never overrides them (owner 2026-10-09). The prop simulator reports off, pause and protect side by side.
  2. **Drawdown that trails until the first payout, then locks** (`drawdown_lock_trigger: first_payout`): before the first payout, floor = `eod_high_water − drawdown_usd` with no cap (it can rise above `drawdown_lock_usd`); from the first payout on, floor = `drawdown_lock_usd` for good, even when the trailing floor was higher (owner 2026-10-09). Live floor check (§6.7 Account state): before the first payout the typed floor must lie in [start − `drawdown_usd`, balance); after it, it must equal `drawdown_lock_usd`. Lives and breaches use this floor; the per-trade risk cap stays `drawdown_usd` ÷ N.
  3. **Cumulative payout cap shared by a group of accounts** (`payout_cap_group`, `payout_cap_group_usd`, `copied_accounts`): gross payouts (before the profit split) recorded on every account of the group, past and present, active or not, plus TradeSyncer followers, count toward one total. **Copied accounts:** each account carries `copied_accounts`, the number of accounts copying it through TradeSyncer, which the owner sets and changes on the Accounts page (e.g. to 0 when he cuts a copy connection); followers are not in `accounts.yaml`, so this number is how the bot counts them. **Trading to the cap (owner: always trade up to it):** the bot keeps trading every account of the group until the cap is used. Each account's share = what is left of the cap ÷ the number of group accounts trading (each active account counts 1 + its `copied_accounts`), never less than `payout_min_usd`. Once an account's withdrawable profit (balance − `payout_baseline_usd`) covers its share, it takes no new entries and a Discord note says so; this share stop applies whatever `payout_clock_hold` says. **Near the cap** (owner 2026-10-09): a payout request still needs `payout_min_usd` of withdrawable profit, but the amount paid is min(requested, what is left of the cap); e.g. with $99,500 already paid out, the account must build $1,000 above $52,100 to request a payout and is paid $500. **The payout that reaches the cap** must be taken; it takes the account back to `payout_baseline_usd` ($52,100 on the Pro 50k) with the floor static at `drawdown_lock_usd` and nothing left to withdraw (profit beyond the cap is not paid), so recording it (Payout taken / `!payout`) sets the confirmed balance to `payout_baseline_usd` instead of subtracting the amount. A payout is never recorded above what is left of the cap. **Cap stop (final):** once the cap is used, entries stop on every account of the group. What is left of the cap is recalculated from the recorded payouts plus the current balances and `copied_accounts` every time the owner edits a balance or a copy count (e.g. he stopped copy trading but forgot to update the count), so such an edit can reopen trading. After the $100,000 payout MFFU moves the account to a live account, which is out of scope for now (owner 2026-10-09; §13 Q-Pro-6): there is no next $100,000 count, and working toward another payout applies only before the cap. Live: **Payout taken** / `!payout` asks how many accounts the payout was taken on (default 1 + `copied_accounts`, editable) and records amount × count toward the group; Live Status and the Accounts page show the group's total and what is left, and a Discord note goes out at 75% and 90% of the cap. Simulator: payouts are min(withdrawable, what is left of the cap); a funded run whose group reaches the cap is reported as `payout_cap_reached` with the days it took; since copy trading multiplies every payout, the report shows payouts and days to the cap for 1, 2 and 3 Pro accounts in total, leader included (MFFU's limit of 3 sim-funded at once, below), and for the owner's `copied_accounts`; each simulated payout follows the same share rule, so amount × count never exceeds what is left of the cap, and the simulator ends the funded run once the cap is reached.
  4. **Contract limit counted per contract** (`max_contracts_total`, owner 2026-10-08): the funded stage allows 5 contracts in total, a micro counting as a full contract, unlike the 10:1 mini-equivalent rule (`micro_mini_ratio`) of the evaluation and the 25k accounts. Sizing (§6.1) searches minis + micros ≤ the total; the Accounts page offers it for any account or stage (e.g. a later Pro 100k funded preset), and a scaling plan row may carry its own total.
  5. **Profit split** (`profit_split_pct`): reports only. The simulator and Live Status show both the gross payout and the trader's share; nothing in trading or sizing reads it.
  **Lucid Flex 25k, which rules the simulator already models and which are new (owner 2026-11-04; docs only, built with the prop simulator at step 15):** already modelled by existing fields: start balance, profit target, EOD-trailing drawdown with no evaluation lock, the soft $600 DLL (as Builder's), the 50% consistency cap with the dip rule (§13 Q-consistdip), `stop_at_target`, the 10:1 mini / micro limits, the scaling plan list (`scaling_tiers`), the minimum and maximum payout, the payout target (as Builder's), commissions and flat-by. **New rule types** (fields above; each defaults to null / `floor` / `intraday` / false, so the MFFU presets don't use them):
  1. **Drawdown lock by the floor or the first payout, whichever comes first** (`drawdown_lock_trigger: floor_or_first_payout`): floor = min(`eod_high_water` − `drawdown_usd`, `drawdown_lock_usd`) until the floor reaches `drawdown_lock_usd` (the Lucid Flex 25k funded: an EOD high of $26,100) or the first payout is requested, whichever comes first; from then on floor = `drawdown_lock_usd` for good. Live floor check (Account state below): before the lock the typed floor must lie in [start − `drawdown_usd`, min(`drawdown_lock_usd`, balance)); after it, it must equal `drawdown_lock_usd`. At each session end the simulator updates the floor before it tests a payout. **Step-15 check (owner):** the prop simulator reports how many payouts were requested before the floor had locked; under the max-payout policy it must be 0 (the first payout needs an end-of-day balance ≥ start + $2,000 = $27,000, so the EOD high is already ≥ $26,100 and the floor locks at that same session end); any other count is a bug, shown to the owner.
  2. **Payout eligibility by a count of profitable days per cycle** (`payout_profit_days_min`, `payout_profit_day_min_usd`): a payout needs at least `payout_profit_days_min` session days in the current payout cycle with net P&L (after commissions) ≥ `payout_profit_day_min_usd` (Lucid Flex: 5 days of ≥ $100); the cycle starts at the first funded session and restarts after each payout; days without a trade or with less profit neither count nor reset the count. Live Status and the Accounts page show the count; the simulator tests it with the other payout conditions at session end.
  3. **Payout amount as a capped share of profit above start** (`payout_pct_of_profit`): withdrawable = min(`payout_max_usd`, `payout_pct_of_profit` × (balance − `payout_baseline_usd`)) (the Lucid Flex has no buffer, so the baseline is the start balance); a payout needs withdrawable ≥ max(`payout_min_usd`, `payout_target_usd`) with the other conditions, and the simulator takes the withdrawable amount and subtracts it from the balance. With the owner's max-payout policy (`payout_target_usd` = `payout_max_usd` = 1,000) the first payout comes at $2,000 profit and each later one after $1,000 more (after a $1,000 payout $1,000 of profit is left, half of what the next maximum needs). The payout distance on Live Status and auto-protect (§6.8) use the profit still needed for that withdrawable amount; recording a payout (Payout taken / `!payout`) checks the amount against the withdrawable. With `payout_pct_of_profit` null, withdrawable = balance − baseline as before.
  4. **Scaling tier updated at end of day** (`scaling_tier_update`: `intraday` (default: the working balance, as before) | `eod`; added while recording, since Lucid updates its tiers at end of day): with `eod` a session's tier comes from the previous session's end-of-day profit (balance − start) and doesn't change intraday; live, the morning balance confirmation sets it (the owner confirms the tier from the firm dashboard, as now).
  5. **Flipping rule, report only** (`flip_hold_max_sec`, `flip_profit_share_max`, `flip_profit_share_warn`; owner 2026-11-04, §13 Q-Lucid (d)): Lucid allows at most `flip_profit_share_max` (50%) of profit from trades held ≤ `flip_hold_max_sec` (5 s). The bot never changes a trade for it: the share = the net profit of trades held ≤ 5 s (from the first leg's fill to the last leg's exit) ÷ the period's total net profit, over the evaluation run and over each payout cycle (not shown while total profit ≤ 0), is shown on Live Status and the Accounts page with a Discord warning once it is above `flip_profit_share_warn` (25%); the prop simulator reports its distribution per run and the runs above 25% and 50%. Nothing in trading or sizing reads it.
  The owner's accounts are MyFundedFutures and, from 2026-11-04, Lucid (the Flex 25k preset); MFFU allows up to 3 sim-funded at once (the UI warns at a 4th MFFU account, counting each account's `copied_accounts`); Lucid allows up to 5 accounts at once, copiers permitted (owner 2026-11-04; the UI warns at a 6th Lucid account, counting `copied_accounts`); the direction lock spans every account of both firms (§10.3). **What the owner does on the firm's site, and records in the UI:** activating a funded account after a pass (then he switches `phase` in the UI) and requesting each payout (then he clicks "Payout taken" and types the amount, or sends `!payout`, which records `payouts_taken`, the date and amount in `accounts.yaml`, subtracts the amount from the confirmed balance (the payout that reaches a payout cap group's cap sets it to `payout_baseline_usd` instead, new rule type 3) without touching today's P&L tallies, and is refused while the account has an open, pending or maybe-filled leg; the bot applies `after_first_payout`, with that stage's `profiles.yaml` entry, once `payouts_taken` ≥ 1, and measures profit since the last payout from the first confirmed balance after it). `payout_baseline_usd` = start + `funded_buffer_usd`, fixed from the day the funded phase starts (payout distance is simply negative until the buffer is cleared), and `payout_target_usd` defaults to `payout_min_usd`. The bot never requests a payout or activates an account. `stop_at_target` applies only while `phase: evaluation`. **Prop simulator stages:** an evaluation that passes or breaches starts a new evaluation the next session (account fees are the owner's and are not modelled); a funded account walks buffer → payouts, taking min(balance − baseline, `payout_max_usd`, what is left of the `payout_cap_group_usd`) at session end whenever balance − baseline ≥ max(`payout_min_usd`, `payout_target_usd`), the profit since the last payout is ≥ `payout_profit_since_last_usd`, the cycle has ≥ `payout_min_days` trading days, at least `payout_wait_calendar_days` calendar days have passed since the run's first funded trade (first payout) or the previous payout (later payouts) and the cap rules hold, then applies `after_first_payout` and, with `drawdown_lock_trigger: first_payout`, fixes the floor at `drawdown_lock_usd`. A funded account that breaches ends that funded run (payouts already taken are kept) and the stage restarts from its preset start balance the next session; payouts and breaches per funded run are reported. **Inactivity in the simulator:** an account with no trade for `max_inactive_days` calendar days (weekends and holidays included) is closed by the firm: the prop simulator counts it as a failure (`inactive_close`), reported separately from drawdown breaches, and lives mode, profile and `last_life_top_pct` comparisons include it (the bot never places a trade just to stay active). **Inactivity alert:** after `inactivity_alert_days` (start 5) calendar days without a trade on an account with `max_inactive_days`, a daily Discord alert gives the days left. **Stops that outlast the inactivity limit** (owner 2026-10-09): when a `payout_clock_hold: pause`, a share stop or a cap stop (new rule types 1 and 3) would keep an account from trading past `max_inactive_days`, the owner gets a warning only: a Discord warning live, and the simulator flags it (reported apart from `inactive_close`). The bot never trades just to stay active.
  **MFFU Tier-1 news rule** (no positions or orders 2 minutes either side of FOMC, FOMC minutes, the Employment Report and CPI on funded accounts): covered on every account by §9 News blackouts. Lucid allows news trading (owner 2026-11-04); the bot's §9 blackouts still apply, as on every account.
  **Larger accounts later (owner):** the owner may move to MFFU 50k or 100k accounts of the same types (Pro 50k is a preset since 2026-10-08). They need **no retraining**: the models work in R, and only the account's rules change. **Larger Lucid Flex sizes** (50k, 100k, 150k; owner 2026-11-04) the same way: entered on the Accounts page and kept with **Save as preset** (§9b), no retraining. He enters each new account on the Accounts page (start balance, drawdown and lock, target, consistency, DLL, buffer and payouts, max minis / micros and how the firm counts them (10:1 mini-equivalent or a total contract count), risk per trade); the presets are only starting points and every field stays editable. A new account whose rules match no existing account or preset stage is stale and refuses entries until its overnight threshold sweep has run (§6.8), so it can trade from the morning after it is entered.
- **Daily profit cap, by mode** (`profit_cap_mode` = how the bot limits how much it makes in one day; the UI labels it "Daily profit limit"):
  - `consistency` (owner: MFFU Rapid EOD evaluation 30% × $1,500 = **$450/day**; Builder funded 50% × $2,100 = **$1,050/day** until the first payout, then 50% × $1,000 = **$500/day**; Pro 50k evaluation 50% × $3,000 = **$1,500/day**; Lucid Flex 25k evaluation 50% × $1,250 = **$625/day**): cap = `consistency_pct` × (`profit_target_usd` + max(0, base − the lowest end-of-day balance so far)), where base = the start balance in an evaluation, or the balance the current payout cycle is measured from in a funded stage (owner 2026-10-26, §13 Q-consistdip; `risk.consistency_cap_usd`): a dip below the base raises the cap, a loss that stays above the base does not; the figures above are the caps with no dip (e.g. Rapid EOD eval with a lowest EOD of $24,500 → 30% × $2,000 = $600; Builder funded first cycle after a $300 dip below the base → 50% × $2,400 = $1,200). MFFU measures consistency on net daily P&L, so the cap applies after commissions and fees, and gross can run slightly above it. This caps the **day**: a single trade can make at most the cap, and once the day reaches it the bot stops.
  - `none`: no profit cap.
- **Daily loss limit (DLL):** a cap on how much the account may lose in one day, separately from max drawdown. The owner's DLLs are MFFU Builder's $600 and the Lucid Flex 25k's $600 (evaluation and funded), all **soft**: a breach ends the day, it doesn't fail the account; Rapid EOD has none (`dll_enabled: false`). With `dll_enabled: true` the bot:
  1. **Size to what's left:** `risk = min(normal risk, remaining_allowance)`, costs included, where `remaining_allowance = daily cap + today's net P&L` and the daily cap is `bot_daily_loss_cap_usd`, or the firm's DLL when that is null. Example: DLL $600 (Builder), risk $333 → after a $333 loss the next trade risks ~$267; if it also loses, the day is over. A winning trade earlier in the day adds room, but risk never exceeds the normal amount. If not even 1 micro fits the stop, skip.
  2. **Not counted as lives:** lives mode (§6.5b) counts drawdown only.
  3. **Daily stop:** no new entries once the remaining allowance is used up (or after `max_losses_per_day` losses); the allowance resets each session date (trading ends at 15:59).
- **No DLL (`dll_enabled: false`):** trades are never sized down for the day and there is no daily stop, unless the owner sets `bot_daily_loss_cap_usd` (then it works exactly like a DLL, items 1 and 3) or `max_losses_per_day`.
- **Account state and the drawdown floor:** live, the owner types the firm's balance and current drawdown floor (liquidation level, shown on the MFFU dashboard) on Live Status each morning, or from his phone with `!balance <account> <balance> <floor>` (§9c), confirming the account has no open position or working order; **entries stay blocked until he does**. He may enter them again on any account at any time the account is flat (e.g. after a trade of his own; owner 2026-10-09); the new values replace the bot's working balance and floor from then on. An entry is refused and asked again if the floor is below start − `drawdown_usd`, above `drawdown_lock_usd` (when the account has a lock, except with `drawdown_lock_trigger: first_payout`, which uses the range in new rule type 2 above; with no lock, as on every MFFU evaluation, there is no upper limit but the balance) or ≥ the balance, or the balance differs from the bot's estimate by more than that account's largest possible day (a typo check; typing the same values again confirms them, for a day with the owner's own trades). These daily values (`current_balance_usd`, `current_floor_usd`, P&L adjustments) are stored in `<paths.live_dir>/account_state.json`, not in the committed `accounts.yaml`; the prop simulator keeps its own `eod_high_water`. The bot shows its own estimate for comparison but never accepts it by itself. **Lucid funded balance basis** (`firm_balance_from_zero: true`, owner 2026-11-04, §13 Q-Lucid (e)): Lucid shows a funded account from $0; on such an account the owner types the balance and floor as Lucid shows them (Live Status or `!balance`), the bot adds `start_balance_usd` (a floor shown as −$1,000 → $24,000) and stores and checks only the $25,000-based values; the form shows both. Intraday, the working balance = the confirmed morning balance + today's estimated net P&L (a maybe-filled or unknown leg counted at its full stop loss); lives, `stop_at_target`, auto-protect, the scaling tier and the breach check use it, and the next owner confirmation replaces it. `drawdown_remaining = balance − floor` (used by lives); profit = `balance − start_balance` (scaling tiers; the owner confirms the active tier from the firm dashboard); payout distance and auto-protect use `balance − payout_baseline_usd`. The owner doesn't trade the leader manually during the session, or, if he does, enters a P&L adjustment on Live Status or, once the account is flat, its new balance and floor. The prop simulator computes the floor itself: `start_balance − drawdown_usd` (static) or `min(eod_high_water − drawdown_usd, drawdown_lock_usd)` (eod_trailing, updated each session end; when `drawdown_lock_usd` is null, floor = `eod_high_water − drawdown_usd`; with `drawdown_lock_trigger: first_payout`, `eod_high_water − drawdown_usd` until the first payout, then `drawdown_lock_usd`). A breach (simulator and live) = equity ≤ floor, counting open P&L.
- **Phase-aware:** when an account passes, the owner flips `phase` and its cap mode in the UI. With `stop_at_target: true`, an evaluation stops taking entries once it reaches its target and the bot's own tally also meets the consistency rule and `min_trading_days` (more trading only adds risk); if not, it keeps trading under the daily cap until they are met.
- **Backtest:** the prop simulator replays each account's own per-account selections (§6.3) through its rules (pass = profit target reached, consistency held (best day ≤ the cap above, with the dip rule; owner 2026-10-26), ≥ `min_trading_days`, no breach) and reports pass rate, days to pass, breach rate, and payout-eligibility. It also **bootstraps** with whole days/weeks as blocks (losing streaks cluster in bad regimes, so shuffling single trades would understate them): resamples whole days of decision points (with all their pairs) and re-runs the per-account selection and state machine on each path (`bootstrap_paths`, start 500), since which trade an account takes depends on its lives, caps and phase; gate-account bootstraps (no state) resample daily trade results. One historical path is a single draw. The thresholds (§6.5b) are tuned by the money score (§6.5b) subject to the bootstrapped L-loss-run rate ≤ `max_loss_run_rate`; breach probability is reported only. **Risk fraction report** (owner 2026-10-14): per account and stage, 1/4, 1/3 and 1/2 side by side, each with its own threshold sweep: pass rate, days to pass (median and 75th percentile), payouts (count and dollars per funded run, dollars per evaluation started), breach rate (drawdown breaches; `inactive_close` apart), expected profit per evaluation started, and the share of trades whose size a cap cut below the full risk (by cap: max position or scaling tier, daily profit cap, daily loss allowance), from out-of-fold months as the §6.8 profile figures.

### 6.8 Risk profiles (owner's quick dial)
Each account has a **risk profile** the owner can switch any time in the Control Panel (applies from the next trade). A profile moves two things together: **how much is risked** and **how picky the bot is**.

| Profile | Risk per trade | Selectivity | Typical use |
|---|---|---|---|
| Aggressive | multiplier 1.0 (full risk, capped at drawdown ÷ N) | base thresholds, slightly looser | fresh evaluation, wants to pass fast |
| **Normal** (default) | multiplier 1.0 | backtest-chosen thresholds (§6.5b) | everyday |
| Conservative | multiplier 0.5 | stricter: only roughly the top third of setups by calibrated EV | after a drawdown, or a choppy week |
| Protect | multiplier 0.33 | strictest: only roughly the top 10% of setups by calibrated EV | funded account close to a payout |
| Custom | owner types the risk $ directly (`custom_risk_usd`, ≤ drawdown ÷ N) | owner sets `min_ev_r`, `min_target_prob` directly | anything else |
