- The exact thresholds for each profile come from the **threshold sweep** (§6.5b), not guesses — Normal: the §6.5b objective; Aggressive: among `threshold_grid` points with both thresholds at or below Normal's, the one with the most trades, kept only if its paired total-R-per-month difference against Normal on the tuning months is > 0 within `max_loss_run_rate` (else Aggressive = Normal); Conservative and Protect: the `profile_ev_quantiles` (67% Conservative, 90% Protect) quantile of calibrated EV among the decision-point candidates that pass the account's Normal thresholds in the fold's tuning months (same method as the last-life cutoff, §6.5b). The sweep runs per account (with that account's rules, risk fraction and `max_loss_run_rate`) and writes `models/profiles.yaml`, keyed account (or preset stage) → stage (current / after_first_payout) → profile → {`min_ev_r`, `min_target_prob`, EV cutoff, last-life cutoff}, each per-account sweep covering both stages; an account added later, or one whose sweep-relevant fields (rules, caps, commissions, `max_risk_fraction_divisor`, `max_loss_run_rate`, `last_life_top_pct`, λ, `risk_usd_per_trade`, flat-by, `breakeven_enabled`, `sim_fills`, `lives_mode`) are edited, is marked stale. A stale **new** account refuses entries until its sweep has run, unless its sweep-relevant fields (above) equal an existing account's: then it copies that account's `profiles.yaml` entry and is not stale (a replacement leader can trade the next morning); an edited account keeps its previous thresholds until the sweep finishes. Edits that reduce exposure — lower risk, lower max size or caps, a DLL turned on or lowered, an earlier flat-by — apply from the next trade with the previous thresholds; editing `copied_accounts`, `payout_clock_hold` or `profit_split_pct` is not sweep-relevant, needs no threshold re-run and applies from the next trade (owner 2026-10-09); only threshold changes and edits that raise risk wait for the sweep and take effect at the next session after it. The sweep runs overnight through `nqbot run sweep --account <account>` ("Re-run sweep" button queues it; `nqbot setup-tasks` also registers `nqbot run queue --until 07:30` at `archive_time` + 30 min daily to run queued sweeps, and saving an account that becomes stale outside 08:30–16:15 on a trading day starts the queue at once; the step-15/19 sweeps and the production fit also write `profiles.yaml` entries for every §6.7 preset stage, so a new account matching a preset stage copies that entry and is not stale (preset entries exist at the default 1/3 only: an account at 1/2 or 1/4 is stale for one sweep night, owner 2026-10-14); Custom-profile edits never need one), and the UI shows what they mean, from out-of-fold months only (months 5–6 before step 20; months 7–9 plus the sealed set after it; the page shows the period) — e.g. "Protect: ~2 trades/week, 71% win rate, avg 1.4R, chance of a losing day 18%."
- **Auto-protect (optional, per funded account):** `auto_protect_within_usd` — when the account is within that many dollars of its payout target, the bot switches to Protect by itself and posts a Discord note; it switches back after the payout is requested (owner clicks "Payout taken" or sends `!payout`).
- Risk never goes **above** drawdown ÷ N (the account's setting, at most 1/2) in any profile; the static 1/N rule (§6.1) is the ceiling. The profile multipliers apply to the account's own base risk (drawdown ÷ N).
- Lives mode (§6.5b) still applies on top: on the last life, the stricter of the two rules wins.
- The prop simulator reports each profile's pass rate / payout rate per account, so the owner can see the tradeoff before choosing.

---

## 7. Training and backtest rules (non-negotiable)

1. **No lookahead.** Every feature at time t uses only data with timestamp ≤ t. A touch at second t is tested against the levels from the latest snapshot live would have had by then (applied at τ + `map_latency_sec`, §3.1 Ingest; the unit test uses τ), and bar-based features use only bars closed by t; a unit test shifts the data by one second and one bar and asserts the features at t don't change. Anything chosen from outcomes (chart-level zone widths, the NDXP band, detector grids, and the execution parameters `entry_ttl_sec`, `stop_buffer` (a stop beyond a mapped level uses at least `stop_buffer_floor`, §5.1), `abs_stop_buffer`, `abs_stop_window_sec`, and the order type per entry style) is frozen from development months 1–4 (reaction-distance report run at the start of step 13; owner confirms the NDXP band there; `dp_band` only if dark-pool features enter the model, §13 Q-step13plan (6)) and never refit. Profiles "so far", not full-session. Dark-pool prints count only from the next session's pre-open (prints with `timestamp` ≤ the previous session's 16:00 ET, or earlier under `dp_lag_sessions`, §3.1). Unit-test this. Every order, `exit` and `breakeven` takes effect `order_latency_sec` after the decision (§5.5b).
2. **Walk-forward**: train on older months, test on the next unseen month, roll forward. Never tune on test months. **Schedule (the 9 unsealed months: development months 1–6 + walk-forward test months 7–9, ledger in rule 2b):** the lower models' (structure, trigger, day type) predictions for months 1–4 are **cross-fitted**: each month 1–4 is predicted by lower models trained on the other months 1–4 only (day-blocked, purged); these predictions only ever train the pair scorer, never score anything. From month 5 on, month k is predicted by lower models trained on months 1…k−1 only (expanding window). The pair scorer that predicts month k (5 ≤ k ≤ m−1) trains on months 1…k−1; test month m reuses the scorer that predicted month m−1 (trained on months 1…m−2); calibration / thresholds / breakeven are tuned on the out-of-fold predictions for months 5…m−1 (the **tuning months** of fold m), and month m is scored. *Folds for test months 7, 8, 9:* scorer on 1–5 / 1–6 / 1–7, tuning on 5–6 / 5–7 / 5–8.
   - **Sealed set:** scored by fold 10: lower models through month 9, scorer on 1–8, tuning on 5–9, applied unchanged to every sealed and top-up month. That leaves about 3 walk-forward test months (~60+ trades at ≥ 1/day) plus the sealed set (3 months + the top-up months); confidence intervals will be wide and are always reported.
   - **Per fold, in order** (every choice except step (1)'s searches is made on the tuning months 5…m−1, by the §6.5b objective, re-simulated from the stored paths, §6.5): (1) `recency_halflife_months` (it weights the pair scorer's training rows only) and LightGBM's small parameter grid (§7.1), searched **once per scorer** (for the scorer trained on months 1…k−1) inside its own training months — the LightGBM grid by day-blocked cross-validated log-loss, and `recency_halflife_months` by forward validation (train on months 1…k−2, score month k−1 by log-loss), since random-day CV can't reward weighting recent months, then cached and reused by every later fold; then the enabled entry styles, chosen from the pre-declared `style_set_grid` (≤ 6 sets, approved at step 6) by re-simulating the trades that the no-breakeven (3-outcome) scorers for k = 5…m−1 (each trained on months 1…k−1, cached across folds) select with each set (a selection filter, no retraining). Trades are selected with `settings.yaml → tuning_start` (start `min_ev_r` 0.20, `min_target_prob` 0.40, applied to raw scores at default costs, λ = 0); (2) choose the breakeven rule by re-simulating the trades those same no-breakeven scorers select at the start thresholds; (3) relabel with this fold's rule and train the 4-outcome scorer for every k in 5…m−1 (each on months 1…k−1, so every tuning month has an out-of-fold prediction under the fold's rule; cached and reused whenever an earlier fold chose the same rule); the k = m−1 scorer scores month m; (4) recalibrate on those out-of-fold predictions, then sweep thresholds and set the last-life cutoff (`last_life_top_pct: auto` picks 10/20/33% here) and the risk-profile EV cutoffs (§6.5b, §6.8).
   - **Choices vs scores:** everything above is chosen inside each fold; test month m is only scored. The only decisions made on the months 7–9 scores are a few whole-system yes/no choices — ablation cuts (rule 5, §7.2 item 2), the target-selection policy (§6.3 Proof) and the step-20 order-flow decision — and the sealed set checks them. The gameplan check (§5.6) runs on all scored months after the sealed score; a production fit that adds gameplan inputs is validated only live (steps 24–25).
   - **Training order inside every fold:** day-type model first; the structure model's day-type input is the day-type model's own out-of-fold prediction (cross-fitted for months 1–4, nested: when month h ∈ 1–4 is held out, the day-type inputs of its training rows come from day-type models trained without both h and the row's own month; expanding after); then the structure and trigger models; then the pair scorer.
   - **Fold j** scores month j (for a sealed set, every month of that set, unchanged) = lower models through month j−1, pair scorer on months 1…j−2, tuning months 5…j−1; folds 7–9 are the walk-forward test folds, fold 10 scores the sealed set, and the production fit is fold N+1 (it first trains the expanding-window scorers for months 10…N that its tuning months need).
   - **Production fit** (runs after the step-20 sealed score passes, and again after a new sealed set or a round-2 re-check passes, after the step-21 and step-25 re-checks, after a `map_latency_sec` raise (§3.1) and at each §7.1 Retraining refit — not after step 16): fold N+1 over all N scored months, sealed and top-up months included — out-of-fold lower-model predictions through month N train and tune the scorer, while the deployed lower models are trained on months 1…N; pair scorer on months 1…N−1, per-fold steps (1)–(4) on the tuning months 5…N — and write `models/` and `models/profiles.yaml`. This fit is not re-scored; steps 24–25 validate it live.
   - **Purge and embargo:** drop events whose outcome window overlaps the train/test boundary and leave a gap of one session, so overlapping trades can't leak information across the split.

2b. **Locked final test set and the month ledger.** The ledger is written into `settings.yaml` (`months`) on the day the SSD is set up, just before step 7 (§12 step 4b), and every step uses it:
   - **Development months 1–6:** the only months for hand-made work — detector or definition fixes, sample days, plots, review cards, regression tests — and for every report that can lead to a hand-made rule or feature change (SHAP and importance, win rates by regime/day type/confluence, hold rates, zone-width reports (no gameplan reports before step 20: months 1–9 have no gameplan days; from step 20 on scored months, §5.6), flip and near-cutoff node reports): tables built from labels use months 1–6; model explanations use the fold-7 models (lower models through month 6, pair scorer through month 5) on months 1–6; review cards show out-of-fold scores only (months 5–6 from the expanding-window scorers; months 1–4 show the cross-fitted lower-model scores and no pair score). Outcome-based choices that are frozen (rule 1, §7.1 detector tuning) use months 1–4.
   - **Walk-forward test months 7–9** (after a re-seal or a round-2 fix: every scored month from 7 up to the month before the new sealed set or re-check month, excluding live months used to justify round-2 fixes; gates and the pass interval then use all of them): scored only by the automated walk-forward (rule 2), never looked at by hand; reports on them show only gate metrics and walk-forward scores. A test month may be trained or tuned on (as a later fold's tuning month) only after it has been scored. All gate numbers before step 20 come from these months.
   - **Sealed months 10–12, plus the top-up months** (every complete month after the window, pulled at step 18 and again at step 20 for months completed since): kept out of **all** development work, reports, charts and summaries until step 20 (exceptions, without outcomes: pull and data-coverage reports — days served, `meta.resolution`, credits, costs, verification, no prices or outcomes; the step-9 gameplan parsing and validation; the step-7 and step-22 live-feed measurements — stream cadence and latency, history parity, Tempest availability time; the step-8b mapping measurements on recorded session days (owner 2026-10-19, §13 Q-atlas-oct: `recorded-day`, `ndxp-select`, latency and content offsets, Atlas tables): levels, times and mapping errors only, no labels, events, trades or outcomes, nothing that feeds a model, and nothing chosen from October price outcomes (it also covers the October `recorded-day` / `ndxp-select` work done before it was written); the step-7 `top-prints` cross-check uses development-month dates), then scored **once**, together, by fold 10 of the model that will go live (rule 2). **Pass** = the sealed set's mean R per trade after costs is > 0 and not below the lower bound (10th percentile) of the months 7–9 day-block bootstrap interval. The production fit (rule 2) then adds them to training, so every later model is judged on months it has never seen (step 27, §7.1 Retraining).
   - **Re-seal (if the sealed score fails at step 20 and they are used to justify a fix):** they become walk-forward test months (still never used for hand-made work), and the new sealed set is the first ≥ `min_new_sealed_months` (start 2) complete months after the fix is frozen, pulled when they exist and scored by fold j, where j is the first new sealed month.
   - **Months completed after step 20** are not in the production fit; they are pulled at step 27 (as in step 18). Live months used to justify round-2 fixes are training data only, never walk-forward test months.
   - **Live months** (step 24 onward) are new unseen months. Review round 2 (step 24) shows cards from them but only produces proposals. Fixes are applied after the step-25 gate is scored, those live months then count as development months (hand-made work allowed), and the fixed model must pass the re-check in step 27.
   - *Example — ledger written 1–20 October 2026:* months 1–6 = Oct 2025–Mar 2026 (October 2025 starts at the first day still inside Skylit's 365-day reach), 7–9 = Apr–Jun 2026, sealed 10–12 = Jul–Sep 2026, top-up = Oct 2026 onward (complete months only).
   - The sealed score is the only truly honest score; if it disagrees with the walk-forward results, the walk-forward overfit.
3. Models: start with win-rate tables, then **LightGBM**. Calibrate probabilities on the fold's tuning months (rule 2): isotonic when the calibration rows' weights (§6.3) sum to ≥ ~1,000 (filled decision-point candidates, §6.3), otherwise sigmoid (Platt) — isotonic overfits small samples. The 3- or 4-outcome pair scorer is calibrated per outcome (one-vs-rest, same method and rows), then the calibrated probabilities are renormalised to sum to 1 per pair; reliability is reported per outcome; `E[R | timeout]` is not calibrated. Training rows are weighted so **each touch group counts once** (weights in §5.4 Overlapping events; an event with 40 pairs must not outweigh one with 4), and folds are split by whole days so rows from the same session never sit on both sides.

3b. **Every reported number comes from a sequential policy simulator** that replays the days in order with the exact live rules: one trade per account (NQ and/or MNQ legs), pending-entry time-to-live, setup windows, blackouts, daily profit/loss caps, lives mode, flat-by, slippage and commissions. Scoring every event independently would overstate trades per month and profit (overlapping setups can't all be taken live).
4. Report per run (`reports/`): **total R per month (account R, §6.1) and the money score** (primary), expected R per trade after costs (secondary), win rate vs the breakeven win rate for each trade's RR, calibration plot, trade count, max drawdown in R and in dollars at the configured risk, results by regime and day type, feature importance. Breakdowns (regime, day type, confluence, stack count, entry style, stop/target type) and feature importance / SHAP cover development months 1–6 only (rule 2b); on months 7–9 only the gate metrics, the rule-5 ablation and the §6.3 policy comparison.
5. **Ablation**: remove one pre-declared **group** at a time — trigger model, Trinity, confluence features, day-type input, area events, stop candidate 5, map shape (owner 2026-10-05: the six Skylit concepts beach ball, magnets, air pockets, King dominance/migration, rainbow road and whipsaw), order-flow extras and approach (owner 2026-10-16: the trigger-model sequence and short-range inputs, from 2026-10-17 also the multi-window order flow and volume baselines (B1), and the structure model's approach features; memberships of every group in `docs/feature_spec.md` F16.8), post-cross retests (owner 2026-10-23: the §5.2 post-cross retest events, their pairs and the F2.6b cross features; without them the system is the 5-minute rule alone), (the Trinity group is every cross-market feature, including the area events' `es_at_level` and `area_sp_*` columns, owner 2026-10-26: cutting it removes them; cutting area events removes the events), AMT previous-session profile and multi-expiration context (owner 2026-10-24: the §5.3 previous-session levels and context features; the §5.2 multi-expiration features) — each arm running the full per-fold pipeline (reusing the full system's per-scorer LightGBM grid point and half-life), and cut by backward elimination: remove the group with the lowest point estimate of the total-R-per-month difference (with − without, paired day-block bootstrap on months 7–9) if it is ≤ 0, re-run, repeat; then the all-cuts system is compared with the full system (paired), and lower bounds are reported. Cuts of structure groups (Trinity, confluence, day-type, map shape, approach) apply to the fallback system too. The ablation runs once, at step 19, over all twelve groups (not at step 15). The list is fixed before step 15 (groups added at step 19 included) and the number of comparisons is printed with the result; the sealed set then checks the cuts. Individual features are never kept or dropped by their months 7–9 score (§7.2 item 2). Gameplan inputs are not in this ablation (months 7–9 have no gameplan days): the §5.6 gameplan check and the monthly-retrain ablation handle them.
6. Minimum sample: don't trust a setup bucket with < ~100 events; report confidence intervals.

### 7.1 How the training works

**The dataset.** The backtest replays history tick by tick (Databento trades) from the first backtest on. `data/processed/events.parquet` has one row per touch (§5.4 touch rule), with features as of the touch — what the structure model uses. Values measured after the touch (`swept`, sweep depth and time beyond, audit depth and time inside, the fib level reached) are pair columns computed as of each pair's `decision_ts` (null for the structure model and for style (d)) and never stored in `events.parquet`. `data/processed/pairs.parquet` has one row per (event_id, entry style, firing n, order type, stop candidate, target candidate); a **pair** = (event_id, entry style, firing n, stop candidate, target candidate) executed with the frozen order type for its style, and only those rows train the scorer. Each entry style has its own `decision_ts` (one per firing for styles (a) and (b), §5.7) (when its absorption fires (a), its order-flow trigger fires (b), the audit or sweep reclaims (c), or at the touch (d)); pair features are computed as of `decision_ts`, and labels start at the fill after it, exactly as live. Columns = inputs + what happened afterward (labels). Pair rows run to millions per year, so event features are stored once in `events.parquet` and joined at training time; `pairs.parquet` holds only pair columns. Targets per event are capped at `max_target_candidates` (start 10 nearest distinct zones with net RR ≥ `pair_build_min_rr` at default costs) plus the 1:2.5 baseline, and stop candidates within `stop_merge_ticks` (start 2) of each other are merged. This table is the only thing the models learn from.

**Algorithm (decided).** Start with simple win-rate tables by stack count, member type and entry style (readable, sanity check). The production model is **LightGBM** with conservative settings (shallow trees, strong regularization, a small parameter grid searched inside the training folds only). A **logistic regression baseline** is trained alongside on the same features — about ten lines of code — purely as a cheat detector: if LightGBM can't beat it on unseen months (total R per month, paired day-block bootstrap), the extra complexity is fitting noise. Report-only: the owner is alerted, and it never switches the model class. No CatBoost, no large hyperparameter searches. Good with mixed inputs, missing values (no gameplan that day), and interactions (regime × confluence). Fast to train on a laptop; < 1 ms to predict.

**Each model, separately:**

| Model | Rows | Inputs | Label | What it learns |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Structure | every level-zone touch | zone makeup (stack count, member-type flags, each member's node size and `tests_so_far` / `holds_so_far`, and the proximity features of §5.4: zone width, member spread, each type's distance from the centre, distance to the next zone above/below), velocity lean, daily vol context (§5.2), regime, rug/pika/Trinity flags, day-type P(trend) as of the latest snapshot ≤ t, the trade's direction against the day: `with_day_direction` (trade direction × the sign of `new_highs` − `new_lows` at t) and `vwap_side_vs_trade` (price on the trade's side of the 09:30 VWAP: +1 / −1, 0 within its band) (owner 2026-10-27, §13 Q-trenddir, feature_spec F11.7), distance to VWAP/bands (ATR-scaled), daily vs weekly profile, day memory, time of day, calendar, approach (how price came into the level: move and range over 1 and 15 minutes, straightness over 15, feature_spec F5.18; owner 2026-10-16), NQ vs ES relative strength over 5 and 15 minutes (F5.16), today's relative volume `rvol_today` at the touch (F9.10; owner 2026-10-17), gameplan features (§5.6; only once the gameplan check enables them, else omitted) | **held** = after the touch, price moves ≥ `hold_reaction_pts` (20 now; 30 from the step-13 rebuild, owner 2026-11-06, §13 Q-density (c)) in the hold direction, measured from the touch's extreme (the deepest price reached into or past the zone) with a target-style trade-through (§5.5b), before the zone is crossed; **broke** = the zone is crossed, i.e. every active member crossed by its own rule (§5.4: GEX nodes trade past the band; chart levels a settled 1-minute candle beyond the band, single prints against the band frozen at the touch; highs/lows after the sweep window — a sweep that reclaims is not a break, and is labelled held only if the hold reaction follows before a cross or `hold_window_min`; golden zone a settled 1-minute candle past 0.79, §5.8; gameplan zones price trading past the far edge (their `invalidation` is a feature only, F8; owner 2026-10-23); a post-cross retest (§5.2) is judged in the level's new role, broke = crossed back toward its original side by its own rule); neither within `hold_window_min` = timeout | which Skylit + chart structures actually hold, and under which conditions |
| Trigger (Databento) | order-flow events inside a structure zone or VAH/VAL area, at every structure type (absorption, exhaustion, delta divergence, imbalance) | big trades (count, size, side, price progress after them), delta, CVD slope, aggressive volume vs no-progress, refill counts (mbp-1), top-of-book features (mbp-1, §5.7), imbalance ratios, seconds since touch, sequence inputs (seconds since each detector last held and since the last big trade with its side, distinct detectors fired, absorption followed by an imbalance within `seq_window_sec`) and the last 5 minutes' range in ticks, also vs today's median 5-minute range (owner 2026-10-16, feature_spec F9.9); time of day (`minutes_since_open`), today's relative volume `rvol_today` and the 5-s and 60-s order-flow windows with volume vs its time-of-day normal (owner 2026-10-17, F9.10) | +`trig_fav_ticks` in the trade direction before −`trig_adv_ticks` within `trig_window_sec` (start 120 s; a label parameter, fixed), measured from the best ask (longs) / bid (shorts) at `decision_ts` + `order_latency_sec`, the window starting then | which order-flow signals mean "now," and how much they add on top of structure |
| Day type | one row per session per `daytype_snapshot_min` (start 15) from 10:00, inputs as of that time (no full-day inputs) | IB width, VWAP and POC crosses so far, time above/below VWAP, new highs/lows made so far, rotations, regime, daily vol context (§5.2), realized range so far vs the expected move | range vs trend day (owner): **range** = price keeps cutting through the midpoints (VWAP, POC); **trend** = price stays on one side of them, bouncing off them, while making consistent new highs (up) or new lows (down). EOD label (RTH only; a cross = a clock-aligned `daytype_cross_bar_minutes` (5) bar settled on the other side (F0.5: open beyond the level as of the bar's open, close beyond it as of its close; margin 0), not a 1-minute close (owner 2026-10-27, §13 Q-dtcross), for the 09:30-anchored VWAP, judged as of the bar's open and close; owner's reason: on trend days the pullback often pokes back through VWAP on 1-minute closes, which miscounted trend days as range days; the `vwap_crosses` input and the challenger's `poc_crosses` use the same rule): trend if crosses of the 09:30-anchored VWAP ≤ `trend_max_vwap_crosses` (start 2) and the close is in the outer `trend_close_pct` (start 20%) of the RTH range in the direction of the new highs/lows; else range. POC crosses are not part of the label (owner 2026-10-27, §13 Q-dtcross follow-up: the developing POC can jump across price on trend days without price crossing anything, which would miscount trend days as range); `poc_crosses` stays on the LightGBM day-type challenger list as a candidate input only. The owner may refine this at step 6. Known only at EOD; predicted from partial-day inputs. Few labels (one per session), so: rows are weighted so each session counts once, and it is a regularised logistic regression on at most `daytype_max_features` (start 8) inputs chosen at step 6 (fold 7 has too few sessions for anything richer); a LightGBM day-type model is added later as a §7.1 Retraining code change (the candidate must beat the live model). The report prints its out-of-fold log-loss vs the base rate. The 8 inputs stay as they are, `ib_width_ratio` included (owner 2026-10-24: the owner trades it as a day-type signal; no swap test); the AMT candidates are on the LightGBM challenger list (§7.1 Retraining) | when levels get pushed through (heavy ranging/trending days) |
| **Pair scorer (combiner)** | pair (event × entry style × firing × stop candidate × target candidate, §6.2, §6.3, dataset above) | the models above (out-of-fold; trigger inputs = the trigger probability of this pair's own firing for styles a/b, plus, for every style, the maximum and the latest trigger probability of this event's firings with ts ≤ `decision_ts`, null if none; same-second firings of different style-(b) detectors form one decision point whose trigger input is their maximum), zone makeup (same features as the structure model), entry style, stop type/distance, target type/distance (ATR and expected moves), confluence at target, daily vol context (§5.2), target-node velocity, obstacles in between, time of day, NQ vs ES relative strength at `decision_ts` (F5.16; owner 2026-10-16), `rvol_today` at `decision_ts` (F9.10; owner 2026-10-17), gameplan features (§5.6; only once the gameplan check enables them, else omitted) | **target first / stop first / timeout** (+ breakeven exit when breakeven is on, §6.6), plus `E[R \| timeout]` and time-to-target | the final probability per candidate pair; which stop/target pairs work where |

**Area-event inputs (owner 2026-10-26, §13 Q-areasp; area events from step 19, not in the step-16 model):** an area event has no level zone, so its pairs get: **null** (they describe a level zone's own members): stack count, member-type flags, members' node type (King / Gatekeeper / significant, F2.5), node sign (pika/barney, F2.2), node size, % of King and freshness (F2.3, F5.11), rug and reverse rug (F2.13), `pika_cloud_overlaps_zone` (F2.14), beach-ball and cross/flip state of members (F2.6, F2.6b, F2.7), zone width, member spread, distance from the zone centre, and the zone's Trinity (`trinity_count` / `trinity_aligned`); **computed as for level events** (map-wide or price-based): gamma regime (F2.12), velocity lean and bias (F2.9, F2.18), whipsaw, rolling ceilings/floors (F2.11), King dominance and migration (F2.16), rainbow road (F2.17), magnets (F2.10), air pockets (F2.15), `in_pika_cloud` (F2.14), S&P path features (F2.20), `smt_div` / `rel_delta` / `rs_nq_es` (F5.16), day type, vol context, VIX, calendar, time, `rvol_today`, VAH/VAL depth, previous-session value context and all order flow; the distance to the next level zone above and below is not null: measured from the price at `decision_ts` (pts, ATR, EM); **its own cross-market features:** `es_at_level` and the `area_sp_*` columns (§5.4 Area events). Features the owner's list doesn't name, placed by the same test (a level zone's own members → null; map-wide or price-based → computed): computed: `map_age_sec` and `map_resolution_1m` (F2.1, map-wide), multi-expiration context (F2.23, map-wide; wall distances from price), day memory (F5.12, session-wide), the per-map `lean` (F2.19 output = F2.9), and the pair's target-side features (target-node velocity, confluence at target: they describe the target, not the entry zone); `es_stack_count` and the ES member types (F5.16) come from the ES level found by the area rule (they describe the ES zone, not an NQ zone); null: members' relevance and velocity (F2.4, F2.8), the rest of the F5.13 makeup (`king_present`, `gatekeeper_present`, largest pct_of_king, container makeup, distance to the near edge, `distance_to_level_center`), zone widths (F5.14, a level's band), `maps_with_node_here` and the aligned-node stats of F2.19 (they need the Nasdaq side); not an input: the approach features (F5.18: structure-model inputs, reaching the pair scorer only through the structure probability, which is null here); not features: F5.15 (a report) and F5.17 (simulator ties) (feature_spec F5.8).

There is **no separate target or stop model**: the pair scorer *is* the target/stop decision, because the probability of reaching a target only makes sense for a given stop. Stop behaviour (overshoot past stop, stopped-then-target) is reported from the stored paths (§6.5) and used to tune `stop_buffer`, not modelled separately. Breakeven is chosen by re-simulating stored paths (§6.4). Final trade = entry + the pair with the highest risk-adjusted expected R (min 1:1, P ≥ floor), sized by §6.1.

**Stacking without cheating.** The pair scorer must never see a lower model's prediction for data that model trained on. The schedule (cross-fitting for months 1–4, expanding window after) is in §7 rule 2. The three lower models stay separate because each learns its own label from far more rows than there are trades.

**Tuning detector parameters.** Structure parameters (e.g. `zone_proximity`, `confluence_tol`, `sweep_max_depth`) at step 13, after the reaction report, and order-flow parameters (`abs_*`, `imb_ratio`, …) at step 19: for each grid value, rebuild events on months 1–4 and pick by day-blocked cross-validated **total net R per month** on months 1–4, using the bootstrap policy (stop candidate 2, or candidate 1 for area events; nearest target ≥ 1:1, §6.3) on events whose cross-validated predicted hold probability (trigger probability for order-flow parameters) is above the fold median; report the trade count. At step 13 (owner 2026-11-09, §13 Q-step13-0 (c), `config/settings.yaml` `tune_13`): the provisional structure model at one `lgbm_grid` point (`num_leaves` 15, `min_data_in_leaf` 500, with `lgbm_grid.fixed`, no search), day-blocked cross-validation with 4 folds (one per ledger month of months 1–4), 2,000 day-block bootstrap replicates for every interval and keep-current test, and the detector and variant rows scored on one combined (c)+(d) sequential run (each style's execution parameters still frozen on its own single-style policy). Entry-trigger parameters (`abs_*` except `abs_stop_*`, `imb_*`, `exh_*`, `div_*` at step 19; `sweep_reclaim_bars` at step 13 on style (c) trades) are scored on their style's trades only; `sweep_max_depth` and `flip_margin` (searched in three groups, F16.4) are structure parameters scored on all events at step 13; `abs_stop_buffer` and `abs_stop_window_sec` are frozen at step 19 on all entry styles' trades with stop candidate 5 in place of candidate 2 (candidate 1 for area events). Log-loss is used only to compare model settings on a fixed event set with fixed labels; the feature-only parameters `roll_switch_min_sec` and `king_switch_min_sec` are compared that way (owner 2026-10-25): they change features only, so events and structure labels stay fixed; the feature rows are recomputed by engine runs on months 1–4 (≈ 24 h for the row; owner 2026-11-09, §13 Q-dryrun13 (4)), and if `node_relevance_top_n` = 5 won its row the two windows also change which nodes become NQ levels, so they are scored as event-changing variants (total net R per month, each fold's bar on the shared events) instead of by log loss; grid {30, 60, 120, 300} s each (under 60 behaves as 60 on 1-minute days), scored by the day-blocked cross-validated log loss of the provisional structure model on months 1–4, `roll_switch_min_sec` first (King at 60), then `king_switch_min_sec` (rolls at the winner); pre-declared: a value replaces 60 only if its log loss is lower and the 95% day-block bootstrap interval of the difference excludes 0; the report lists each value's log loss and difference vs 60 with its interval (`node_switch_incumbent_max_share` 0.25 is an owner rule, never tuned). `roll_switch_min_sec` and `king_switch_min_sec` are searched last, after the execution parameters, on the frozen months 1–4 events (§12 step 13 order; owner 2026-10-28). **Event-changing variants (owner 2026-11-04, §13 Q-step12a (5)):** every variant below changes the event set (golden-zone, previous-VA, `node_relevance_top_n`, `node_role_at_formation`, `retest_lookback_sessions`), so (a) each is decided on the total net R per month over **all trades of the sequential run**, its own subset (named with each variant) reported beside it with the trade counts: an extra trade can displace a trade at another zone (one trade per account at a time), which the subset alone doesn't see, and trades that are the same in both runs cancel in the day-paired difference; (b) each fold's trading bar (the fold median of the cross-validated predicted hold probability) is set on the events both values share and applied to all of that value's events, so extra low-scored events don't lower the bar for the shared ones. **One rule everywhere (owner 2026-11-08, §13 Q-step13plan (3)):** (b) also applies to the detector grids (`zone_proximity`, `confluence_tol`, `sweep_max_depth`, `sweep_reclaim_bars`, `flip_margin`), each challenger value against the start value. **Detector-grid pick (owner 2026-11-08, §13 Q-step13plan (8)):** each challenger's difference in total net R per month against the start value, both runs' bars set on the events that pair shares, decides, the start value counting 0; the largest difference wins; if none is above 0, the start value stays; ties go to the value closest to the start value; if two values are equally far from the start value and have exactly the same difference, the one with the larger 95% day-block lower bound wins, and if that is also equal, the start value stays (owner 2026-11-08); each difference is reported with its 95% day-block bootstrap interval for information only (the detector grids keep "best value picked" with no interval test). **Shared events (owner 2026-11-08, §13 Q-step13plan (4); declared before any result):** an event of one run and an event of the other are the same event if they come from the same touching trade, in the same direction, with at least one founding member `level_id` in common (the members the zone was formed with, F5.5); if a touch matches more than one event, the pair with the most founding members in common is taken; if that is still tied, the events are unmatched (exact founding-member equality would count a touch as a different event whenever a variant such as `node_relevance_top_n` drops one weak member). **Golden-zone variants (owner 2026-10-26, §13 Q-fibvariants):** four variants, `golden_zone_flip` {on, off} × `golden_zone_redraw_1030` {on, off} (§5.8); for each, rebuild the golden-zone events on months 1–4 and score day-blocked cross-validated total net R per month with the bootstrap policy over all trades of the run, golden-zone trades reported beside it, with the trade counts; pre-declared: the current design (flip on, redraw on) stays unless a variant has a higher total net R per month with the 95% day-block bootstrap interval of the difference excluding 0; if several do, the one with the largest lower bound; searched after the detector parameters, at their winners. **Previous-VA variants (owner 2026-10-26, §13 Q-vaedge):** `va_edge_sweep` {off, on} (subset: `va_edge` trades), then `prev_profile_anchor` {`globex_1800_1700`, `rth_0930_1600`} at its winner (subset: all events touching the previous POC/VAH/VAL); each value rebuilds the events on months 1–4 and is scored the same way (day-blocked cross-validated total net R per month with the bootstrap policy, trade counts reported); pre-declared: the current rule stays unless the other value has a higher total net R per month with the 95% day-block bootstrap interval of the difference excluding 0; searched after the golden-zone variants. **Top-5 node relevance (owner 2026-11-06, §13 Q-step12 (2)):** `node_relevance_top_n` {off, 5}: with 5, on QQQ and NDXP only the 5 largest-|value| nodes among those relevant by F2.4 (with the debounce) are relevant, the confirmed King, ceiling and floor at any rank, a node leaving only below 7th or below 20% of King, rank joins and leaves through the same 60-s board-time debounce as the % test; it decides only which nodes become NQ levels (zone-book members and events), the map state and every map feature keep the full 25% / 20% set; each value rebuilds the events on months 1–4 and is scored the same way (subset: trades at zones with a QQQ/NDXP node member; trade counts reported); pre-declared: off stays unless 5 has a higher total net R per month with the 95% day-block bootstrap interval of the difference excluding 0; searched after the previous-VA variants. **Node role at formation (owner 2026-11-04, §13 Q-step12a (4)):** `node_role_at_formation` {`approach`, `strike_side` + formation touch}; each value rebuilds the events on months 1–4 and is scored the same way (subset: trades at zones with a QQQ/NDXP node member; trade counts reported); pre-declared: `approach` stays unless the other value has a higher total net R per month with the 95% day-block bootstrap interval of the difference excluding 0; searched after `node_relevance_top_n`. **Retested-extreme lookback (owner 2026-11-05, §13 Q-density (d)):** `retest_lookback_sessions` {3, 5, 10} (it sets both which sessions' highs/lows are F3.8 retest candidates and when a retested extreme ends); each value rebuilds the events on months 1–4 and is scored the same way (subset: trades at zones with a retested-extreme member; trade counts reported); pre-declared: 10 stays unless another value has a higher total net R per month with the 95% day-block bootstrap interval of the difference excluding 0; if both do, the one with the larger lower bound; searched after `node_role_at_formation`. Label parameters (`hold_*`, `trig_*`) stay at the owner-approved start values (`hold_reaction_pts` 30 from the step-13 rebuild, owner 2026-11-06, §13 Q-density (c)). Write the winners to `features.yaml` marked `frozen_from: months 1-4`. Test months are never used. **Execution parameters** (`entry_ttl_sec`, `stop_buffer`, `abs_stop_buffer`, `abs_stop_window_sec`, the order type per entry style) are frozen the same way at step 13 (order-flow styles: step 19), with the same single-style policies (step 13), re-simulated from the stored paths, so the per-fold pipeline never has to retrain for them. **Stop floor (owner 2026-11-08, §13 Q13-vol):** decided with `stop_buffer` by the data: the fixed `stop_buffer_floor` stays unless the volatility-scaled floor (§13 Q13-vol, re-fitted on months 1–4) has a higher total net R per month with the 95% day-block bootstrap interval of the difference excluding 0. **Step 13c as run (owner 2026-11-14, §13 Q-step13c):** every row is scored on the **excess net R per month** over the start value ((R_value − R_start) − the start's mean net R per trade × (N_value − N_start), declared before any result: under the losing start-value policy the total net R rewarded random thinning, Q-step13c (5)), total net R, net R per trade, the trade-count change and the (c)-only / (d)-only excess reported beside it; rows decided on breakeven none (`fs` reported and flagged), row 4 on a style-(c)-only run, row 13 on the full `stop_buffer` × floor grid (the vol-scaled floor on QQQ stops only), the provisional model on held = 1 vs broke / timeout = 0; the driver job `step13_tune`.

**Which entry style trades.** `enabled_entry_styles` (settings.yaml) is chosen inside each fold from the styles built so far (Phase D: (c) and (d); step 19: all four; §7 rule 2), and the production fit writes the final set; if step 20 fails, it is limited to (c) and (d). The model learns which entry style works best (the pair scorer has entry style as an input). Owner's ranking, used only to break near-ties: order flow (a, b) > audit/sweep at the zone edge (c) > plain entry at the structure (d). When several enabled styles are eligible at the same moment, the highest EV wins; the ranking (a = b > c > d) decides only when their EVs are within `style_tie_ev_r` (start 0.05R). If a lower-ranked style is eligible first, it trades (no waiting for a higher-ranked one). While a sent entry is pending or "maybe filled" the other styles for that event wait; after any fill they are skipped for the rest of the event; if it is removed cleanly and unfilled, they become eligible again. The rule applies per account, and the simulator does the same.

**Calibration.** Raw model scores are mapped (method per §7 rule 3, fitted on the filled decision-point candidates in the fold's tuning months, §6.3, §7 rule 2) so that "0.70" really means ~70% of such trades hit target.

**Checking what it learned.** Every training run writes a report: feature importance and SHAP plots (which inputs drive predictions and in which direction), win rates by regime/day type/confluence level, calibration plot. (Months 1–6 only; months 7–9 show only gate metrics, rule 2b.) Owner reads these: if the model learned something that contradicts experience, either a feature is mis-defined (fix it) or it's a real finding (keep it).

**Retraining.** Live engine logs every event in the same format. New months' rows are rebuilt from the pulled history with the backtest code, as at step 18; live logs are used only for live-vs-backtest checks, drift monitoring and fill/slippage measurement. Each monthly retrain also re-runs the gameplan ablation (§5.6) and drops the gameplan inputs if they hurt (part of the declared retrain procedure, not a "change" below). Every `retrain_every_months` (start 1; owner: every 1–3 months is fine; data top-ups still run monthly so Skylit history never ages out), for the newest complete month M: if no code or feature changed, the live model is simply refit through M (production-fit offsets). If anything changed (new feature, fix, new data source), the months that motivated the change (review cards, drift alerts) are not used to judge it: the candidate and the unchanged model are both re-run with the same fold offsets and scored on the next complete month(s) after the change is frozen (paired, like step 27's fold j); the candidate must have the higher money score and meet the step-16 criteria, and any engine or feature code change re-passes the gate-23 replay; only then is it refit through M and put live. **Recorded challenger, not built (owner 2026-10-16):** an LSTM trigger model on per-second order-flow sequences, tried as such a code change once live and top-up months add enough firings (the count is set then); it must beat the live trigger model under this rule. **Recorded challenger, not built (owner 2026-10-18): a LightGBM day-type model.** Its candidate inputs, fixed now before any results: the current 8 day-type inputs (`daytype_inputs`), `rvol_today` (F9.10; §13 Q-dtrvol overlap numbers: it tracks `ib_width_ratio` at +0.57 at the same snapshot time and every other input at |ρ| ≤ 0.36), `poc_crosses` (settled 5-minute bars, owner 2026-10-27, §13 Q-dtcross; a candidate input only, not part of the EOD label), `rotations` (F3.10) and the Tempest fields of QQQ's row (F2.21, as `rv_iv_ratio` uses): the SVX levels (1d, 9d, 30d, 3m, 6m), the term slope and the skew slope (the other F2.21 fields, ATM vol, risk reversal, butterfly and tilt, are not on the list). Added before any results (owner 2026-10-24, §13 Q-amt, Q-multiexp): `open_outside_prev_va_em` (the open's unsigned distance outside the previous session's value area in expected-move units, 0 if inside), `prev_va_overlap`, `dpoc_drift_em`, |`poc_migration_em`| (unsigned: the label has no direction), the multi-expiration net gamma signs `mx_net_sign` and the 0DTE shares `mx_0dte_share` (Nasdaq and S&P sides, F2.23). **Added before any results (owner 2026-10-28, §13 Q-er):** `er_so_far`, Kaufman's Efficiency Ratio of the RTH session so far on clock-aligned 5-minute bars (|price_t − RTH open| ÷ Σ |5-minute close-to-close moves| from the open, the first move measured from the RTH open; null while the sum is 0), `er_<w>` over the same trailing windows as the Q-v2 recent-window idea (§13 Q-v2 Idea 3; e.g. {30, 60, 120} minutes, the window set fixed when that idea is built), and `vr_prior`, the Lo–MacKinlay variance ratio of 5-minute log returns at q = 6 over the prior 10 full RTH sessions (returns within each session only, no overnight return; computed only from completed sessions, so it is known at the open). Nothing else is added after results are seen. It must beat the live day-type model on unseen months under this rule.

### 7.2 Further accuracy practices
(Labels with full paths, realistic fills, costs, expected-R thresholds, the sequential simulator and bootstrapped prop simulation are covered above.)
1. **Recency weighting:** weight recent months more (`recency_halflife_months`, tuned) because markets drift.
2. **Prune:** no per-feature pruning search; LightGBM's regularisation handles weak features, and only the pre-declared groups of §7 rule 5 are cut.
3. **Live drift monitor:** track live calibration monthly; alert when a stated 0.7 bucket wins far from 70%. Retraining follows §7.1 Retraining.
4. **Review rounds** (§8) after every retrain that changes features or code.

Every extra tweak evaluated on the same months is a chance to fit noise; the locked test set (§7 rule 2b) is the defence.

---

## 8. Review loop: owner judges scenarios

Purpose: make sure the detectors see what the owner sees, and capture judgment he didn't think to write down.

**What the owner sees (one card per setup, from development months 1–6 only, §7 rule 2b; round 2 uses the live demo trades):**
- NQ chart around the moment with VWAP + bands, daily/weekly VAH/VAL/POC, IB high/low and fib, ON levels, mapped Skylit nodes, gameplan zone if any
- Skylit maps (QQQ, NDXP, SPY, SPX) at that minute
- Detector readings: node type/freshness, rug/pika/Trinity, regime, confluence, day-type inputs so far (P(trend) from step 15; never the end-of-day label), order-flow trigger (once step 17 has built them)
- The proposed trade: entry, stop, target, entry style
- The outcome stays **hidden** until he answers, so he judges the setup, not the result

**What the owner answers:**
- **Take / Skip**
- **Why** (one or two sentences, in his own words)
- Optional: how he'd enter (order-flow trigger / audit / plain at the level), where he'd put stop and target, confidence 1–5

Saved to `data/reviews/*.parquet`. Cards are a spread of examples for the owner to judge, so the detectors can be checked against what he sees: high, low and borderline scorers once a model exists; in step 14, before any model exists, a stratified sample across stack counts, level types, entry styles and times of day (entry style (c) if an audit or sweep occurred, else (d); stop = candidate 2, far edge + `stop_buffer`; target = the v1 bootstrap, §6.3), plus valid setups that lost (losing is normal when targets are far) and a few setups the owner would never take (to test the filters).

**How answers become changes:**
| Owner's answer | Becomes | Who decides |
|---|---|---|
| "That's not a real rug, the purple node is too small" | Fix to a detector definition/threshold | Claude Code proposes, owner approves (hardcoded rule) |
| A reason that repeats and isn't captured ("already ran 80 pts into it", "lunch chop") | New feature | Owner names it; the model learns its weight from outcomes |
| Outcomes revealed after answering | Comparison of owner's calls vs model's odds | Shows where discretion beats the model and vice versa |

Claude Code reads all reasons after each round, groups repeats, and proposes concrete changes ("6 of 9 skips mention lunch hours → add time-of-day feature"). Approved changes → spec updated → events rebuilt → retrain.

Rounds: 50–100 cards in step 14 (backtest setups), a second round in step 24 (live demo trades). The outcome model is **never** trained to copy owner labels; it trains on what price actually did.

---

## 9. Runtime (live)

- The engine (one asyncio process, plus the guard, §9 Supervision) during session hours: Skylit stream (`/v1/stream`) + Databento Live → incremental feature updates (microseconds) → model called only when price reaches a qualifying zone → decision.
- **Data is always tick-level.** Every NQ/ES trade is processed all session (cheap); 1-minute bars are built from those ticks for the bar-based rules (audits on 1-minute bars, GEX flips on 5-minute bars). What switches on near a level is not finer data but the **work**: order-flow detectors (§5.7) and the models run only while price is inside a zone ± `zone_proximity` or a VAH/VAL area (§5.3).
- Latency budget: data in ~tens of ms; model < 1 ms; Skylit map as soon as the stream pushes it; Discord push 1–3 s.
- **Pre-open warm-up and restarts:** live needs state from before the session (the 18:00 ET VWAP and daily profile, ON high/low, PDH/PDL, the weekly profile, unfilled single prints, multi-day freshness counts, ATR, realized vol, the big-trade threshold, the opening-range averages, the trailing `dp_lookback_days` of dark-pool prints, Tempest's previous close). It starts from the latest evening checkpoint (`state_<date>.parquet`, §11) plus any sessions since. A pre-open job (a Windows Task Scheduler task registered by `nqbot setup-tasks` at `preopen_start` (08:30 ET; re-registered if the Windows time zone isn't America/New_York) with "Run with highest privileges", "Wake the computer to run this task" and "Run task as soon as possible after a scheduled start is missed", and both AC-power conditions turned off (`archive_live` is registered the same way); it exits at once on non-trading days from `calendar.yaml`; done by 09:00 ET, and the checklist turns red if not) first kills any `nqbot run` still running, runs `archive_live` for any day not yet archived (allowed inside the job lock when the pre-open job calls it), starts the Windows Time service and forces a time resync, refreshes the news calendar (§9 News blackouts), pulls the overnight and any missing sessions' Databento trades, Tempest `/v1/vol/history` and the dark-pool prints, and rebuilds that state with the backtest code. The Tempest row is pulled here, at the pre-open, and only that copy is used; no evening job stores a Tempest row pulled after the close (a draft, §3.1 Tempest; owner 2026-10-09). **Machine check and restarts (owner 2026-10-14, after a Windows Update restart at 04:50 ET on 10-14 left Windows at the sign-in screen and the 09:27 recording never ran):** `nqbot preflight` runs at 08:20 ET on weekdays and warns (exit 5; on Discord from step 22) if a restart is pending, Windows Update isn't paused through the end of the session (or the pause ends within 2 days; on Windows 10 past end of support, the build PC, an info line instead, owner 2026-10-31, §11 Two machines), the SSD isn't writable, the laptop is on battery, or the SSD dropped since the last check; every nqbot scheduled task runs whether the user is signed in or not (S4U logon, no password stored; registered from an admin shell with `scripts/register_tasks.ps1`), wakes the computer and starts when available if a run was missed. In session, the engine writes a journal (`<paths.live_dir>/journal.jsonl`, fsynced on every write) **ahead** of every action: one grammar for every signal — `sending entry <account> <ticker> <order_id>`, `sending exit <account> <ticker> <event_id>`, `sending breakeven <account> <ticker> <event_id>`, each followed by `ack|error_sent|not_sent <same fields>` (`error_sent` = the request went out and failed, so an entry becomes "unknown"; `not_sent` = it never left) — plus every state change (pending, maybe filled, unknown, filled, paused, killed, flat) and every cap update. On restart, an unacknowledged `sending entry` marks that account "unknown" (as does any uncleared entry, below) (an unacknowledged exit is simply re-sent, since exits may retry; an unacknowledged breakeven is re-sent after the rebuild only if price is still at least 1 tick on its favourable side, else skipped and logged), and the set of sent order ids is rebuilt from the journal so no id is ever sent twice. On any restart it rebuilds in order: (1) the pre-open state, (2) today's data via Databento Live intraday replay from 18:00 ET, (3) today's Skylit boards: those logged live today, plus `/v1/historical/range` only for spans that weren't logged (§9 Supervision), with entries and `breakeven` disabled (protective exits due meanwhile — news flatten, flat-by, `!flat`, kill — are still sent); breakeven resumes when the rebuild ends (not gated by `!confirm`), and for any assumed-filled leg whose breakeven trigger fired meanwhile, `breakeven` is sent at once if price is still at least 1 tick on the favourable side of the breakeven stop, otherwise it is skipped and logged; the engine first sends `exit` on the ticker of every uncleared entry leg (as the guard does after a crash) and each such account becomes "flat-unconfirmed" once those exits are acknowledged, or "unknown" if they aren't (§10.3 Account states), and entries stay blocked until the rebuild finishes and the owner confirms the state. Step 22 verifies intraday replay and that `/v1/historical/range` serves the current day.
- Must run through the **same code path** as the backtest (replay historical data through the live engine to test it).
- Everything logged (features, prediction, decision, outcome) → becomes new training data. Each evening after 17:00 ET, `nqbot run archive_live` moves the day's live events and paths and the engine and guard journals into `paths.data_dir/live_archive/<date>/`, writes the day's `state_<date>.parquet` with the backtest code from Databento Historical data for the full 18:00–17:00 session and the day's Skylit RTH boards pulled from `/v1/historical/range` at archive time (cached, so the monthly top-up reuses them: no net extra credits) — never from what the engine received live; if either isn't complete yet, the checkpoint is marked provisional and the pre-open job rebuilds it, verifies the copy, and only then trims `paths.live_dir` to today's working files. If the engine was not running at flat-by and wrote no `carry_state` for that day, the next engine start first derives it from the engine and guard journals of the last session the engine ran (days with no engine journal are skipped) (in `live_archive/<date>/` once archived): every account with an entry not shown cleared, a position not shown closed, an unknown or maybe-filled state, or no acknowledged flat-by exit starts blocked until `!resolve`. After the last flat-by retry (`flat_by` + `flat_retry_min`) the engine writes `carry_state` into `account_state.json` (any account still unknown, maybe filled or holding a position, and any pause not yet followed by `!resume`), closes the journal and exits. The guard exits after it (unless an account is "unknown" after a failed flat-by exit: then the guard keeps retrying that account's `exit` and alerts every `unknown_alert_min` (start 5) minutes until it is acknowledged or the owner sends `!resolve`; an acknowledgement after `carry_state` was written is applied at the next start), or, if the engine isn't running, at `flat_by` + `flat_retry_min` + 1 min once its own flat-by backstop exits are acknowledged or the account is journaled unknown. The pre-open job also kills any engine or guard left from an earlier session before `archive_live`. `archive_live` runs from a Task Scheduler entry at `archive_time` (17:05 ET), never writes `account_state.json`, and waits (retrying) while any engine or guard process is alive. `account_state.json` is never trimmed, and the next session starts with any carried account blocked until `!resolve`; before anything else, the pre-open job (or engine start) sends `exit` to every carried account's tickers, including any carried in the previous contract (`carry_state` stores each leg's ticker, quantity and direction, and the direction lock uses it until `!resolve`). `!resolve <account> position` is refused for a carried account unless test 4 showed attached brackets survive past the session.
- **Databento Live gaps:** on any disconnect the engine resubscribes with intraday replay from the last processed `ts_event` inclusive (trades and `mbp-1`), drops records already processed (same instrument, `ts_event` and sequence number) and processes the gap in sequence — bars, profiles, VWAP, CVD, fill inference — before marking the feed healthy. If replay is unavailable, the session is flagged `live_gap`, entries stay blocked for the rest of the day and the owner is alerted. The step-23 replay test includes a simulated 30-second drop.
- **Reconnects never end a live session (owner 2026-10-17; built with steps 21+, not yet):** the live engine keeps reconnecting to Skylit `/v1/stream` and Databento Live (backoff, `Last-Event-ID`, intraday replay) for the whole session however many times a connection drops or a reconnect fails, with no reconnect cap; meanwhile the stale rules (`skylit_stale_sec`, the feed-health rule, §10.3) and the per-map freshness rule (`skylit_map_stale_sec`) block new entries and remove pending ones, brackets stay at the broker, and entries resume only by the normal recovery rule (healthy data for `feed_recovery_sec`, a fresh board, the gap filled). Only the flat-by, a kill or the owner ends the session. The step-7 recordings (`stream-session`, `SESSION_MAX_RECONNECTS` 3) stay as they are: on 2026-10-16 the recording stopped at 13:55 ET when the owner's Wi-Fi went out, which a live engine must ride through.
- **Mapping dislocations (owner 2026-10-14, §5.1, §13 Q-dislguard):** like a feed failure, but per chain: while a chain's mapping is flagged (the per-board trigger or the bot's-R trigger, 0.2% within 2 minutes, + 60 s hold), no new entries on zones with a member from that chain, pending entries at those zones removed with `exit`, open trades keep their brackets; the other chains and chart-only zones trade normally. Each fire is logged (trigger, chain, size) and sent to Discord as a note (not an alert; nothing for the owner to do). The engine feeds every applied board to the same `ChainGuard` the labels use; a restart rebuild replays today's boards through it, so a block in force before the restart stays in force.
- **Clock:** feature, bar and event logic use Databento `ts_event`. Every wall-clock deadline — flat-by, `entry_end`, time-to-live expiry, blackout start, exit retries, the heartbeat — runs on the local clock, so a stalled feed can't freeze them. A wall-clock jump of more than `sleep_detect_sec` (start 30) between loop iterations, or the Windows resume event, triggers the reconnect gap fills (Databento intraday replay from the last `ts_event`, the Skylit gap pull, §3.1) and fires every deadline that passed meanwhile (time-to-live, blackout start, flat-by), with entries blocked until the fills finish and `feed_recovery_sec` has passed (the full restart rebuild runs only when the process itself restarted); shorter stalls count as processing lag. At startup and every `clock_check_min` (start 10) minutes the engine measures the local clock's offset against an NTP time server (an SNTP query; not against `ts_event`, which includes feed latency); if |offset| > `clock_max_offset_ms` (start 250), entries are blocked and the owner alerted; while blocked it re-checks every minute and entries resume after one passing check + `feed_recovery_sec`. An unreachable `ntp_server` counts as a failed check (entries blocked, checklist "clock unverified"); step 22 confirms it answers on each network the owner trades from.
- Notifications and remote control: Discord (§9c); at the desk, the Control Panel's KILL button. If the bot or computer itself is down, flatten in Tradovate web/mobile (§10.1).
- **News blackouts (owner, 2026-10, all accounts):** `config/calendar.yaml` holds **dated** events `{date, time_et, name, blackout: true|false}` for the whole backfill window and as far ahead as the official schedules are published (usually to the end of next year). **Only the owner's high-impact list has `blackout: true`** (his ForexFactory high-impact list, the same for backtest and live): Non-Farm Employment Change (with the Unemployment Rate and Average Hourly Earnings), the FOMC rate decision and statement, the FOMC press conference, FOMC minutes, scheduled Fed Chair testimony and speeches (plus, owner 2026-10-05, the Fed Chair nominee's confirmation hearing and the Senate confirmation vote, listed as `Fed Chair` events), CPI, Core PCE, PPI, Advance GDP, ISM Manufacturing PMI, ISM Services PMI and Retail Sales; other releases are listed with `blackout: false` (calendar features only; owner, 2026-10-04: only the extra lines of the listed releases — Unemployment Rate, Average Hourly Earnings, Core CPI, Core PPI, Core Retail Sales, PCE Price Index, Personal Income — no other releases). The list ignores ForexFactory's impact rating (e.g. the feed rated ISM Services PMI Medium for 2026-10-05) and includes every scheduled Fed Chair testimony and speech. **Live-only red-folder extra (owner, 2026-10-04):** any **other** US event that ForexFactory marks High (red folder) in the feed as known at the `preopen_start` (08:30) refresh also gets a blackout window, live only: it is written to `calendar.yaml → live_extra` (date, time, ForexFactory title, time seen), posted on Discord with its window, and logged so the step-24/25 reviews can count how often it happens. Past red ratings aren't available, so the backtest, labels and both simulators use the listed releases only: live is deliberately a little stricter than the backtest (it can only remove trades, never add them). Comparisons of live with the simulator (the step-23 replay, steps 24–25 and 27–28) run the simulator with the same `live_extra` windows the live run had, so those trades aren't counted as mismatches. Claude Code builds past dates from each agency's release archive (BLS, BEA, Census, ISM, and the Fed's calendar for FOMC and the Chair's testimony and speeches; the October–November 2025 federal shutdown postponed or cancelled several BLS and Census releases inside development months 1–2), each release at the time it was scheduled at 08:30 ET that day (a postponement or cancellation counts only if announced before then), so the backtest never knows a schedule change before live would. **Daily refresh:** the pre-open job (below) downloads ForexFactory's free weekly calendar feed (`calendar_feed_url`; it covers the current week), matches US events to the list **by title** through `news_title_map` (settings.yaml, approved by the owner at step 6, 2026-10-05; ForexFactory's impact rating is ignored, so a re-rated release can't drop out), compares them with `calendar.yaml` from today through the last day the feed covers, writes any added event and, for a moved one, adds the new window while keeping the old one until `!calendar remove` (logged with the time it was seen), and posts a Discord alert listing the change; a non-clock time ("Tentative", "All Day") never moves an event, and same-name events on one day are matched one-to-one by nearest time, keeping any unmatched one. A listed event missing from the feed is **never removed automatically**: the bot alerts and keeps its blackout until the owner confirms the cancellation (`!calendar remove <date> <name>`). A US event ForexFactory rates high-impact that isn't on the list becomes a live-only blackout as above (from the 08:30 refresh only; a rating seen later in the day only alerts). If the feed can't be reached or doesn't parse, the job keeps the file as it is and alerts (the official schedules are already in it months ahead). Feed (checked at step 4a, 2026-10-04): `https://nfs.faireconomy.media/ff_calendar_thisweek.json`, a JSON list of `{title, country, date, impact, forecast, previous}` with `date` in ET carrying its UTC offset (e.g. `2026-10-05T10:00:00-04:00`); it covers the current week only, Sunday to Saturday (there is no next-week feed); the XML twin gives GMT times and is not used. The server answers 403 to a client without a browser-like `User-Agent` (Python's default is refused; curl works), so the refresh sends one. Live and the backtest use the same cut-off: the calendar as known at `preopen_start` (08:30 ET); a change seen later in the day only alerts: the engine re-fetches the feed every `calendar_poll_min` (start 15) minutes during the session and posts any added or moved listed event, and any US event newly rated High, with its window, suggesting `!pause` / `!flat`; blackouts are never changed automatically mid-day. Unscheduled events, and testimony that runs long, are the owner's call: he watches the news and uses `!pause` / `!flat` (§9c). Gameplan blackouts are not used (owner, 2026-10: this rule replaces them). **News flatten (owner):** at release − `blackout_before_min` (30) min − `news_flatten_lead_sec` (start 30 s; must exceed `order_latency_sec` + `webhook_timeout_sec` + 1 s) new entries are blocked and the bot sends `exit` (`event_id` `news-<UTC ts>`) to every account and ticker, then once more `order_latency_sec` + 2 s later, so nothing is open and no order (entry, stop or target) rests from 30 minutes before the release; no new entry is sent until `blackout_after_min` (60) minutes after it (owner's rule, a hard filter, not tuned). Blackout window = [release − `blackout_before_min` − `news_flatten_lead_sec`, release + `blackout_after_min`), used identically by the tradeable filter (§1.2), labels, both simulators and live (live also applies the live-only red-folder windows above). Each listed event has its own window, and overlapping windows merge (an FOMC day: decision 14:00 and press conference 14:30 → no trades 13:30–15:30). This covers MFFU's Tier-1 rule (FOMC meetings, FOMC minutes, the Employment Report and CPI: no positions or orders from 2 minutes before to 2 minutes after). News-flatten exits retry and fail like flat-by exits (still unacknowledged when retries end → "unknown"). In session the rule matters for 10:00 releases (ISM: no trades 09:30–11:00) and 14:00 Fed events (08:30 releases end at 09:30, before the first entry, so they block nothing). Labels, the policy simulator and the prop simulator close any trade still open when that exit takes effect (flatten decision + `order_latency_sec`) at market (a timeout), so the backtest never counts a trade the owner wouldn't hold. The same file lists exchange holidays and early closes (§1.2).
- Runs on the owner's laptop during sessions (one machine, §11; the owner must be watching anyway — MyFundedFutures requires active oversight). On trading days from `preopen_start` (08:30) to 16:15 the laptop stays plugged in, with sleep and the lid-close action disabled, on one network, and the external SSD connected to a USB-C port (§11 Storage). Windows active hours are 08:00–17:00 and updates are installed on weekends (pause them for the maximum and resume on a weekend); the pre-open job warns on Discord if a restart is pending. Before moving it, send `!pause` (blocks new entries and removes pending unfilled ones; open brackets stay at the broker); a network change goes through the reconnect path, a sleep or restart through the journal rebuild. `nqbot live` runs the pre-open job (including `archive_live`) to completion first if it hasn't run, and only then starts the engine and guard; if started after 09:00, entries stay blocked until that job finishes. **External SSD during the session:** all live files are on the SSD (§11). The engine and the guard each hold an exclusive OS lock file in `paths.live_dir` for their lifetime and exit if it is taken (a failed port bind is fatal too), so two engines can never trade at once; `nqbot live` refuses while either is alive, and `nqbot live --restart` kills a hung engine (by its PID) and restarts it as a crash. `nqbot live` refuses to start unless the SSD is mounted (marker file `.nqbot_drive`) with ≥ `min_free_gb_live` (start 20) free. If the SSD disappears or a journal write fails mid-session, the engine at once blocks new entries, keeps its in-memory state, alerts on Discord, and keeps sending protective signals without journaling them (pending-entry removal, `breakeven`, the after-trade `exit`, the news flatten, flat-by, `!flat`, kill; sending an `exit` twice is harmless). Open brackets stay at the broker. The guard does not restart a crashed engine while the SSD is missing; it alerts and still serves KILL / `!flat`. When the SSD is back (marker file found on any drive), the engine, which kept its in-memory state, appends that state and every signal and acknowledgement sent meanwhile to the journal, backfills the board log and resumes entries (no rebuild; if the engine itself restarted meanwhile, the normal restart rebuild runs).
- **Power during a session (owner 2026-10-19, §13 Q-power):** every recording and live session (and every `nqbot run` job) holds `ES_DISPLAY_REQUIRED` with `ES_SYSTEM_REQUIRED` from start to end (per thread, cleared however it ends), so the display idle timeout can't start Modern Standby (on 10-19 the laptop ran on battery, the screen timed out, Windows entered standby with only `ES_SYSTEM_REQUIRED` held and cut the network an hour later); `nqbot preflight` warns unless closing the lid on battery is Do nothing. A power-source watch (`power_poll_sec`, 5 s) logs a warning, also to the event log (Discord from step 22, §9c), on the switch to battery, at each further `battery_warn_step_pct` (10) points below the level at the switch, at the `battery_entry_floor_pct` (30 %) line and when AC returns; the session report records each battery stretch. Live (built with step 22): on battery above `battery_entry_floor_pct` warnings only; at or below it no new entries (open trades keep their brackets; pending entries are removed as on a feed failure, §5.5b), and entries resume when AC returns, or the battery is back above the line while on AC. Built 2026-10-19 for recordings and jobs (the display hold, the lid check, the watch's log lines and battery stretches).
- **Supervision:** `nqbot live` starts two processes: the engine and a small guard (`nqbot guard`). The guard is the only Discord and KILL listener: it forwards every command to the engine over a localhost-only channel (127.0.0.1), checks every `guard_check_sec` that the engine process is alive (if the engine's heartbeat stops while the process is alive, the guard only alerts on Discord), restarts a crashed engine through the journal rebuild at most `max_engine_restarts` (start 3) times per session (after that it stays down, alerts, and still serves KILL / `!flat` and the flat-by backstop; a rebuild reuses the boards logged live today and pulls from `/v1/historical/range` only spans that weren't logged), immediately sends `exit` on the ticker of every **uncleared** entry leg (the journal shows `sending entry` followed by `ack`, `error_sent` or no reply, with no later removal, time-to-live or after-trade `exit` acknowledged and no assumed fill) (acknowledged → "flat-unconfirmed", otherwise "unknown", §10.3 Account states), posts that it did, and sends `exit` to every account and ticker itself on KILL / `!flat` / `!kill` (shared `clients/traderspost.py`), so a hung engine can't block a flatten. The engine owns the paused/killed state; the guard posts the heartbeat (marked "engine down") while the engine is not running. The engine checks the guard every `guard_check_sec` (start 10); if it is gone, the engine alerts through the Discord webhook and restarts it. On KILL / `!flat` the guard first journals the block, forwards it to the engine (which re-checks the guard's block state right before every **entry** POST and sends no entry if it can't confirm; `exit` and `breakeven` are never held back by this check), sends `exit` itself, and sends it once more `order_latency_sec` + 2 s later. If the UI can't reach the guard within 2 s, it sends `exit` to every account and ticker itself through `clients/traderspost.py`, shows that it did, and sends the KILL / Flatten block to the engine on `engine_port`; if the engine doesn't answer either, it shows that the block must be repeated (`!kill` / `!flat`) once the engine or guard is back (the UI writes no journal). The guard also sends `exit` to every account and ticker at each account's `flat_by` + `guard_flat_grace_sec` (start 30) always (sending an `exit` twice is harmless, so it never skips an account because the journal shows another account's exits acknowledged), and likewise at every news-flatten moment + `guard_flat_grace_sec`, whatever the engine's state (from `calendar.yaml` as of the pre-open job), so a hung engine can't skip a news flatten. If a guard journal write fails or the journal can't be read (SSD missing), it sends its exits anyway, unjournaled, treats every journal check as "not acknowledged" and alerts. While it runs, the engine is the only writer of `account_state.json`, Discord-made `accounts.yaml` changes and the journal; the guard listens on `guard_port` and the engine on `engine_port` (both 127.0.0.1); the UI sends KILL and Flatten to the guard (with the same 2-s self-send fallback) and other edits and commands to the engine; settings and account edits are made only while the engine is running or before it starts, and the guard journals its own exits and every KILL / `!flat` block (fsynced, before sending) in `<paths.live_dir>/guard_journal.jsonl`. Every engine start and every rebuild restores the paused/killed state from the engine and guard journals. Optionally the guard pings a dead-man URL (`deadman_url`, empty by default; the ping carries no data) every minute, so the owner's phone is alerted if the laptop dies.
- **Daily checklist (Live Status shows it, green/red):** charger in, SSD mounted, Wi-Fi up, clock offset OK, pre-open job done, news calendar refreshed (today's blackout windows listed), Skylit credits OK, balance and floor typed, gameplan confirmed or "none today", heartbeat seen, TradersPost subscription settings unchanged (§3.5), and after flat-by: every account flat with no working order in Tradovate, followers in TradeSyncer included (§10.1). Backstop if the laptop dies mid-trade: the brackets at the broker protect the position, the heartbeat stops (§9c), and the owner flattens in Tradovate web/mobile; step 27 records, on the first prop account, whether the firm auto-flattens at its session close.

### 9b. Control Panel UI (owner-facing)
One local web app, **CreamFlow** (`nqbot ui`, opens in the browser at `localhost`; nothing exposed to the internet) with six pages (the sixth, Performance, added by the owner 2026-10-26) and a Home start page (item 0, owner 2026-11-14). **Stack (owner 2026-11-14, §13 Q-step14a; replaces Streamlit):** a React + TypeScript single-page app (Vite, Tailwind, shadcn/ui on Radix, Motion, TradingView Lightweight Charts) built to static files and served, with a JSON API over the existing Python code and server-sent events for live data, by a small Starlette + uvicorn server in `src/nqbot/ui/`. It binds only to 127.0.0.1: `nqbot ui` starts uvicorn with host `127.0.0.1` and port `<ui_port>` (whatever folder it is started from) and checks the bound address at startup, refusing to start otherwise, so nobody else on a campus network can open it. Every request must carry a Host header of `127.0.0.1:<ui_port>` or `localhost:<ui_port>` (against DNS rebinding); every POST must carry a matching Origin and the per-launch token the server issues to its own pages, so another website open in the same browser can't send kill, flat or edit requests to it. Every response carries `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self'`: the panel loads no remote code, fonts, images or scripts at runtime (fonts are self-hosted, free-licence woff2 files in the bundle).

**Look and feel (step 14a, owner 2026-11-14, §13 Q-step14a; the source of truth is `design-system/MASTER.md` and `design-system/pages/*.md`, made with the ui-ux-pro-max skill, with the 21st.dev components the owner picks; the font pairing, components and showpieces are proposals until the owner's picks at the end of step 14a part 0).** The owner's references (`docs/design/ui_reference.png`, `ui_reference_2.png`, and `pnl_calendar_reference.png` for the P&L calendar's layout) were a starting point, not a target: the design departs from them wherever something else looks better, and their content, names and logos are never copied.
- **Brand:** the app is **CreamFlow** (one word, capital C and F): browser-tab title `CreamFlow · <page>`, favicon, sidebar header with the logo mark, splash/loading screen, Home's forming title and the dot-matrix footer wordmark (`design-system/brand/`).
- **Colours:** the 21st.dev theme **Cocoa Amber** (owner 2026-11-14, as shown in the preview): near-black background `#111111` with very dark neutral greys for raised surfaces (not navy or slate; overrides the references), cream `#FFE0C2` as the one accent (it also fills the role of gold), a cocoa `#393028` secondary fill, warm cream and gold accents (accents never signal state), green only for healthy status and profit, amber for warnings, red for KILL, breaches and losses; profit and loss always carry a sign and an arrow as well as the colour (colour-blind safe). No light theme.
- **Type:** a display face for headings and the wordmark, a clean UI face, and a monospace with tabular figures for every price, P&L, clock, timestamp and eyebrow label (owner 2026-11-14: Instrument Serif, Geist and Geist Mono, all OFL, self-hosted; specimen `docs/design/type_specimen_1440.png`).
