- **Layout (kept from the references):** a collapsible left sidebar with grouped pages (TRADING / SETUP / RESEARCH) and the date and engine state at its foot; a slim top bar with the breadcrumb eyebrow, account switcher, engine status pill, ET clock and the always-visible **FLAT ALL** control; each page opens with an eyebrow, a two-line title (second line in cream italic), a one-line subtitle, KPI cards, a tab strip and the body; context tags and state pills. New: a ticker tape of the day's key levels under the top bar, the Home scene, animated navigation, and an Effects setting (Full / Calm; Calm forced by `prefers-reduced-motion` and while the live engine runs on the same machine in market hours).
- **Departures from the references and why** (`design-system/MASTER.md` §14): near-black (Cocoa Amber `#111111`) instead of navy (warm accents and glows read better, charts gain contrast); cream/gold instead of steel blue (the brand; blue now means only "info"); a display serif for headings (a signature look; numbers stay mono); fewer borders, depth from surface steps (borders read heavier on black); a Home page instead of none; KPI cards with delta badges and sparklines; the P&L calendar's colours backed by signs and arrows; the network graph dropped as a chart, its idea kept in Home's linked page nodes.
- **Where it applies:** Live Status = one KPI-card row per account (est. P&L vs daily cap, drawdown left, lives, distance to payout) with tabs per account; Review = the reference's replay pattern (Restart / Play / Minute → / Next card, a replay clock and slider that reveal the chart minute by minute up to the decision, and an "at this point" side panel with the card's facts); Accounts and Settings = forms in bordered panels; Gameplan = the parsed table in a panel next to its chart.
- **Risk first:** the kill/flat control, account states and alerts are the most prominent things on every screen; they never animate in a way that delays or hides them, and no decorative effect (glass, tilt, glow, flip-disk digits) is used on them.
- **Front end and build (owner 2026-11-14, §13 Q-step14a):** sources in `ui/web/`, npm versions pinned by the committed `package-lock.json`. `nqbot ui build` runs `npm ci` only (never `npm install`), then `npm audit` (the build fails on a high or critical advisory and lists the rest), then the Vite build into the gitignored `ui/web/dist/`, recording the sources' digest beside it. Each machine builds its own copy (the PC now, the laptop before step 21); Node is needed at build time only. `nqbot ui` refuses to serve a missing or stale SPA build (digest mismatch) and says why, but still starts and serves the fallback below. Step 14 shows the owner a screenshot of each page before the review loop starts.
- **Kill/flat fallback (owner 2026-11-14, §13 Q-step14a): kill and flat never depend on the React front end.** Three layers, each enough on its own: (1) a **no-JavaScript emergency page** `/emergency`, rendered by the Python server from a template in `src/nqbot/ui/` (not from `dist/`), so it works when the SPA build is missing, stale, failed its audit or crashes in the browser: plain HTML form buttons **KILL**, **FLATTEN ALL** and **PAUSE / RESUME** that post to the same endpoints the SPA's controls use (one action path), plus a read-only table of the engine status and each account's state, refreshed by `<meta http-equiv="refresh">`, no script; when the SPA can't be served, `/` shows the reason with these controls inline; the SPA's HTML shell carries a `<noscript>` block and a small load watchdog (inline, allowed by its CSP hash) that links to `/emergency` if the app hasn't mounted within a few seconds, and the top bar's kill/flat control links there too; the same Host, Origin and token checks apply; (2) the **CLI** `nqbot kill` and `nqbot flat` (the same actions over 127.0.0.1 to the engine; with the engine down, they send `exit` to every account and ticker as the guard does; built with the engine at step 22); (3) **Discord** `!kill` / `!flat` (§9c), for when the machine's browser or UI server is unusable.
0. **Home** (owner 2026-11-14, §13 Q-step14a; the start page) — the whole content area is one full-screen CreamFlow scene: the title forms when the panel opens (the full intro on the first open of a session, a click or key skips it), then idles and reacts to the cursor; one node per page (Live from step 21, Gameplan, Accounts, Settings, Review, Performance) placed in the scene around the title, linked to it by thin flowing lines, each with its icon and name and, on hover, a small card with its live number (closest drawdown margin, next setup window, review cards left, this month's P&L, risk per trade); click, keys 1–6 or Ctrl+K open the page. The sidebar, top bar (engine status, FLAT ALL) and ticker tape stay; it fits one screen at 1280 × 800 and up; in Calm the wordmark is still and fully formed with the nodes in place (`design-system/pages/home.md`).
1. **Accounts** — add/edit/delete accounts with plain form fields, grouped:
   - **Account:** name (lowercase letters and digits), firm, phase (evaluation / funded), active yes/no, demo yes/no, TradersPost webhook variable name, sim fills (Tradovate sim).
   - **Targets & caps:** a **preset** picker (§6.7: MFFU Rapid EOD 25k / Builder 25k × evaluation / funded / after first payout; Pro 50k × evaluation / funded; Lucid Flex 25k × evaluation / funded) that fills every field, and **Save as preset** (owner 2026-11-04), which keeps the account's fields as a named preset in the picker (e.g. larger Lucid Flex sizes; a new preset's stages get `profiles.yaml` entries from the next overnight sweep, so the first account on one is stale for a night like any unmatched account, §6.8); profit target; stop at target (evaluations); funded buffer, payout min/max, profit needed since the last payout, payout wait (calendar days before each payout, counted from the first funded trade, then from the previous payout; both dates editable), profit split, payout cap group and its cumulative cap (shows the group's payouts so far and what is left), copied accounts (how many accounts copy this one through TradeSyncer, 0 = none, changeable any time), hold while the payout clock runs (off / pause / protect, default off), the drawdown lock trigger (floor / first payout / whichever comes first), the payout share of profit (capped by the payout max), the profitable days per payout cycle and their minimum profit, scaling tiers and when they update (intraday / end of day), the firm balance shown from $0 (Lucid funded), the flipping report (hold seconds, the firm's share limit, the warning share) and the after-first-payout cap; **daily profit limit**: *Consistency rule* (enter consistency %, the bot computes target × % — e.g. MFFU Rapid EOD eval, Builder funded) or *None*; payout target and auto-protect distance (funded).
   - **Drawdown & losses:** start balance, max drawdown ($) and type (static / EOD trailing), drawdown lock level and when it locks (EOD trailing: when the floor reaches it / at the first payout); **daily loss limit on/off** — when on: $ (a breach ends the day); bot daily loss cap (optional; blank = the firm's DLL, or none when the DLL is off); max losses per day (optional).
   - **Firm rules:** minimum trading days, max inactive days, payout minimum days, commissions per NQ and MNQ round trip.
   - **Position limits:** max trades per day, max minis, max micros, how the firm counts contracts (*mini-equivalent*: micro-to-mini ratio, or *total contracts*: one limit for minis + micros counted alike, `max_contracts_total`), micros allowed, mixing allowed, and an optional **scaling plan** table (profit level → max size) for firms whose limit grows with the account.
   - **Sizing & trade rules:** risk fraction N (2, 3 or 4 = 1/2, 1/3 or 1/4 of the drawdown; default 3) and risk per trade (follows it: floor(drawdown ÷ N), may be typed lower), flat-by time, breakeven on/off, lives mode, last-life cutoff (auto or %), max loss-run rate. **Raising risk** (owner 2026-10-14): a change that raises risk (N goes down, e.g. 3 → 2 = 1/3 → 1/2) opens a dialog that states the new risk in dollars, the lives it leaves and the full losses that fail the account, with the effect of the caps at that fraction (the effective risk at a typical stop and RR, where the max position, the consistency cap or the DLL limits size anyway); it saves only after the owner types the account name and the new fraction (e.g. `mffur1 1/2`), then waits for the account's sweep and takes effect at the next session after it. **Lowering risk** (N goes up, e.g. 3 → 4 = 1/3 → 1/4) needs no confirmation and applies from the next trade. Discord commands cannot change N.
   - Shows live as you type: daily profit limit, risk per trade, lives (from drawdown), the **smallest stop that still gets full risk** before the size cap binds, and the **largest stop that still fits 1 micro**. Validates (a fraction other than 1/2, 1/3 or 1/4, risk > drawdown ÷ N, missing fields, two active accounts whose `traderspost_webhook_env` resolves to the same URL) and blocks saving until fixed; a bot daily loss cap above the firm's DLL only shows a warning. Saves to the panel's accounts file on the SSD (`<data_dir>/panel/accounts.yaml`, below; owner 2026-11-15).
2. **Strategy settings** — per account: **risk profile selector** (Aggressive / Normal / Conservative / Protect / Custom, §6.8) showing each profile's backtested trades per week, win rate, average R and losing-day odds; a **risk $ slider** (capped at drawdown ÷ N) and threshold sliders when Custom is selected; auto-protect distance for funded accounts; lives mode on/off. Global: `gates.gate_pass_rate_min`; session and setup windows and the news blackout (`blackout_before_min` / `blackout_after_min`, owner's 30 / 60) shown read-only (owner's fixed rules: they set the labels, the tradeable filter and `minutes_to_horizon_end`, so a change goes through Claude Code — rebuild events and labels, re-run the walk-forward and the production fit, mark every account's sweep stale; neither value may go below 2, MFFU's Tier-1 rule). Per account: λ (`lambda_risk_penalty`, default 0) and a **Re-run sweep** button (§6.8).
3. **Live status** — one panel per active account (each TradersPost connection) (inferred values are labelled: "assumed filled" / "maybe filled", "est. P&L since the morning balance", "exit sent" until the next import confirms them): today's est. P&L vs daily cap, drawdown remaining, lives left, distance to payout, **current risk profile with a one-click switcher**, trades taken, open position with stop/target, last alerts; plus a big **KILL** button (same action as the Discord `!kill`), **Flatten** (same as `!flat`), **Pause / Resume**, **Resolve** (account + flat, or position with its legs; same as `!resolve`), **Confirm state** (same as `!confirm`) , **Payout taken** and **New evaluation** (demo) buttons, the balance and floor entry (each morning, and any time the account is flat), a P&L adjustment field, **Followers flat** (§10.1) and the Orders/Fills report upload (steps 24 and 27). If the balance and floor aren't entered by 09:40, the bot posts a Discord reminder. Discord commands `!profile <account> protect` etc. do the same from the phone.
   - **Levels (owner 2026-10-23):** a level ladder, one row per level: a role chip (ceiling red, floor green), the state (armed / break undecided with the sweep countdown / crossed, provisional new role / flipped ×n settled 5-minute bars / reclaimed), since the cross its time, depth, volume vs normal and, for nodes, the % of King change and sign (F2.6b), and live the structure model's P(held in the new role) at the next touch. On the chart (owner 2026-11-14, §13 Q-step14a (6)): a single level is a thin line and a zone (merged levels, §5.4) a shaded band between its edges; either is solid in the original role, dashed (a line) or hatched (a band) while provisional, coloured by the current role, with a marker at each cross, flip and reclaim; hovering shows the level's role timeline for the day. Gameplan zones the same, with the stated flip (F8) when the text gave one; the golden zone keeps its own orientation badge.
4. **Gameplan** — drag-and-drop today's gameplan file (.md, .pdf or .docx; a .docx is converted to Markdown first, §3.4). The parser (§3.4) extracts it in a few seconds and shows the result as an editable table (zones, side, stops, targets, ES gates, HVL, scenarios) next to a small NQ chart of today's pre-open with the zones drawn on it (the chart appears from step 22, when the pre-open data path exists). Flagged fields are highlighted; the owner fixes anything wrong and clicks **Confirm**, and only confirmed gameplans reach the live engine. Only a gameplan confirmed before 09:30 is used for that day's live trading; a later upload or parse correction is saved for training but doesn't change today's trading. Status line: "Today: confirmed 09:12" or "No gameplan today — bot runs without it."
5. **Review** — the setup-card review loop (§8).
6. **Performance** (owner 2026-10-26, §13 Q-perfpage; built at step 14, its data filling in as each phase exists; reads only stored results, no new pulls):
   - **Equity curves** per account and for the model, in R (account R, §6.1) and in $ (each account's own sizing, costs and caps, from the prop simulator and, live, from fills), on one time axis, each phase coloured with a vertical marker where it starts: development months 1–6, walk-forward months 7–9 never drawn (totals and gate metrics only, below), sealed months not before the step-20 record, demo (from step 24), each prop account (from step 26).
   - **Stats panel** for the selection (account, phase, date range): net R and $, trades, win rate, average win and loss, largest win and loss, profit factor, max drawdown (close-to-close, and intraday from the stored paths, §6.5), longest win and loss streaks, average streak lengths.
   - **Drawdown view:** each prop account's equity against its own drawdown limit (§6.7: static or EOD-trailing floor, with its lock), the closest approach marked.
   - **Monte Carlo:** 1,000 reshuffles by day blocks (whole sessions, never single trades: same-day trades are linked); the base, smallest- and largest-drawdown curves, a 5–95% band, and the probability of reaching the account's drawdown limit, computed with the same lives and floor rules as §6.5b / §6.7 so it agrees with the lives shown elsewhere.
   - **Live vs expected:** the demo / live curve drawn over the band the backtest predicts for the same number of trades.
   - **Ledger guard (§7 rule 2b), enforced in code, not by convention:** development months are always viewable; walk-forward months 7–9 only once the step-16 gate is recorded as passed, and then only as totals and gate metrics, never trade- or day-level (owner 2026-11-09, §13 Q-dryrun13 (16)); sealed months only once the step-20 sealed score is recorded (both read from the gate record `config/gate_record.json` (committed in the repo, small, so it is not lost with the SSD; owner 2026-10-26): one entry per gate with the gate id, the date passed and the commit the gate was judged on (the parent of the commit that writes the entry, since a commit cannot hold its own hash); every gate approval appends its entry in the same commit as the CLAUDE.md Status "Last gate passed" line, so the two never disagree, and `tests/test_gate_record.py` checks the file parses and its latest gate matches that line; gate 6 (2026-10-05) back-filled); otherwise the page refuses those months and says why (which gate is missing). A unit test checks the guard (the same idea as the `tests/conftest.py` real-data guard): a request for months 7–9 before the step-16 record, or for sealed months before the step-20 record, or for months 7–9 at trade or day level at any time, is refused, including through the CSV export.
   - **P&L calendar** (owner 2026-11-04; layout reference `docs/design/pnl_calendar_reference.png`, its layout only: none of that app's name, logo or buttons): a real month calendar, Mon–Fri columns plus a Week column, previous / next month and a month picker. Each day box: the date, net P&L, trade count and wins / losses; green background for a positive day, red for a negative one, blank for a day with no trades (a day at exactly $0 net: no colour). Week column: the week's P&L, days traded, trades, wins / losses and win rate. Above the calendar: the month's P&L, trading days (green / red counts), trades (W / L), best day and worst day (with dates). Selectors: one account or all accounts combined; $ or R (combined R = the sum of the accounts' R). Always net of commissions; days are New York trading dates. A win = a trade with net P&L > 0, a loss < 0; win rate = wins ÷ (wins + losses). Clicking a day lists that day's trades (time, account, direction, entry style, size, entry / exit, net $ and R). Data: the live trade journal ("no live trades yet" until step 24), for the live, demo and prop ledgers, and the backtest ledger of development months 1–6, labelled as backtest; months 7–12 never appear in it (the same holds for every other day- or trade-level view; owner 2026-11-09, §13 Q-dryrun13 (16); after step 20: §13 Q-ledger-after20). It is the page's P&L summary (there was no plainer one to replace).
   - **Data:** the per-run trade ledgers of the backtest and simulators, the live trade logs and the imported fills (step 25); exportable as CSV (the same ledger guard applies).
- Every `accounts.yaml` write goes through the engine while it runs (the UI sends edits over 127.0.0.1); when no engine is running for the session (before `nqbot live`, or after the engine's end-of-day exit), the UI writes only its own untracked local file (accounts, presets, UI settings; on the SSD in `<data_dir>/panel/` (`accounts.yaml`, `presets.yaml`, `ui.yaml`), so it moves with the SSD to the laptop, each save keeping the previous version in `panel/history/`, backed up to Drive `NQ bot backup/panel/` by `nqbot ui backup` (and the hourly `nqbot status-push` once the build pin moves after 13c); it never holds a secret: only the webhook's `.env` variable name, never a URL, key or login (owner 2026-11-15); committed example and schema; settings the build reads stay in git and are read-only in the UI; owner 2026-11-09, §13 Q-dryrun13 (10)), under a lock file; while a session's engine is down mid-session it refuses edits (KILL and Flatten still work); every writer re-checks the file hash before saving and reloads on a mismatch.
- Settings changes apply **between trades only** (the engine reloads config when flat), never mid-trade; an open trade keeps the size and exits it was entered with. Config files are written atomically (temp file in the same folder, then rename) and validated with pydantic on reload; an invalid file keeps the last good config and alerts.
- Every change is logged with a timestamp so backtest-vs-live comparisons know which settings were active.

### 9c. Discord (phone remote, optional but recommended)
The Control Panel is the main interface at the desk. Discord is the **phone remote** for a bot that executes on its own: notifications of what it did, P&L checks, and commands to pause, flatten or kill. Free, private server, one channel.

**What it sends (via the webhook):**
- **Entry notice** (sent as the order goes out) — zone (what's stacked there; golden-zone flag), entry style, gamma regime, trigger, entry / stop / target with order type, size per account, P, EV in R, and the account's lives left.
- **Trade events** — assumed filled / target hit / stopped / breakeven sent / exit sent, with estimated P&L (confirmed by the daily import).
- **Calendar** — the pre-open job's news-calendar changes and today's blackout windows (§9).
- **Account events** — daily cap reached, daily loss stop hit, last life reached, auto-protect switched on, flat-by exits sent.
- **Health problems** — data feed dropped or lagging, Skylit stream stale or credits low, SSD missing, clock offset, engine restarted, webhook rejected or timed out (broker rejections are not visible), bot paused; plus a heartbeat every `heartbeat_min` (start 15) so silence means trouble. These matter most: they are how the owner learns something broke while away.
- **End-of-day summary** — trades, R, P&L per account.

**What it listens for (via a small Discord bot, `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN`):**
| Command | Action |
|---|---|
| `!status` | Reply with each active account's est. P&L, lives left, profile, and open position as the bot believes it |
| `!pause` / `!resume` | Stop / restart new entries; `!pause` also removes pending unfilled entries (`exit` on tickers with no filled leg); open trades untouched |
| `!flat` | Send `exit` to every account and ticker (this also removes pending entries), and pause new entries until `!resume` |
| `!kill` | Send `exit` to every account and ticker, then block entries until the next session. The process keeps running (exit retries, heartbeat reads "KILLED", commands still work) |
| `!balance <account> <balance> <floor>` | Enter the balance and drawdown floor from the phone (same as Live Status): each morning, and any time the account is flat |
| `!profile <account> <profile>` | Switch risk profile (§6.8) |
| `!calendar remove <date> <name>` | Confirm that a listed news event was cancelled, so its blackout is dropped (§9 News blackouts); the bot alerts first and never drops one by itself |
| `!resolve <account> flat` or `!resolve <account> position <ticker> <qty> [<ticker> <qty>]` | Clear an "unknown" or "maybe filled" state after checking Tradovate (§10.3); `flat` first sends `exit` to both tickers of that account (so no resting entry survives) and clears only when both are acknowledged; with `position`, the bot tracks only the stated legs and quantities, with their sent brackets, and first sends `exit` to every ticker of that account not listed (clearing once those are acknowledged) |
| `!confirm [<account>]` | Show the account's state (all accounts if none named), then confirm it after a restart rebuild or a webhook error; clears a "flat-unconfirmed" account; refused for an account that is unknown or maybe filled (use `!resolve`) |
| `!payout <account> <amount>` | Same as the "Payout taken" button: records the payout (`payouts_taken`, date, amount) and subtracts the amount from the confirmed balance (the payout that reaches a payout cap group's cap sets it to `payout_baseline_usd` instead, §6.7), leaving today's P&L, DLL and cap tallies untouched; on an account with a payout cap group it also asks how many accounts the payout was taken on (default 1 + `copied_accounts`); refused while the account has an open, pending or maybe-filled leg |
| `!followers <leader> flat` | Confirm that a TradeSyncer leader's followers are flat (§10.1), releasing the leader's direction from the lock |

- **Security:** the bot obeys only the owner's Discord user ID (`DISCORD_OWNER_ID`) in that one private channel; everything else is ignored.
- **Always auto-execution** (owner): the bot executes; Discord reports and is the remote control. There is no alert-only or confirm mode; validation runs as auto-execution on a Tradovate demo account (step 24).
- Without Discord the bot still works fully from the Control Panel; you just lose phone notifications and remote control.

---

## 10. Execution and risk (Phases F–G)

### 10.1 Chosen path: Python bot → TradersPost → Tradovate (MyFundedFutures)
Why: works with the owner's firm (MyFundedFutures); TradersPost officially supports webhooks from custom code; supports brackets (stop + target on entry), a built-in **breakeven** action, cancel and exit; no TradingView needed.

Firm rules the code must respect. MyFundedFutures confirmed (2026-10) that a TradersPost bot is allowed if it is supervised; that check covered MFFU firm-wide, not each account type, so re-read the Pro terms on automation before going live on a Pro account (step 26). Re-check the terms before going live, since they change:
- **MyFundedFutures:** semi-automated trading with tools like TradersPost and custom scripts is allowed on evaluation, sim-funded and live, **but only with active trader oversight** — fully autonomous set-and-forget bots are prohibited; no HFT (200+ trades/day); **no hedging across accounts** (§10.3 direction lock); sim-funded accounts follow the Tier-1 news rule (§6.7, §9).
- Therefore the bot runs **supervised**: the owner is at the screen during trading hours, and the kill switch (Control Panel KILL button or Discord `!kill`) is one tap away.

Order flow per trade:
1. Entry signal with `stopLoss` and `takeProfit` attached (bracket). Never a naked entry.
2. If the breakeven rule fires (§6.4): one `breakeven` signal.
3. v1 has **no model-driven early exits**: a trade ends at its stop, its target, flat-by, the news flatten (§9 News blackouts) or an owner `!flat` / kill (one `exit` signal). Labels and the simulator assume exactly this.
That is 1–3 webhook calls per leg — far below the 60/min limit.

Several accounts (owner's setup, 2026-10): each account with its own rules or stage (a Rapid EOD eval, a Builder eval, a funded account) gets its own TradersPost connected account (~$10/month each beyond the plan's first; TradersPost counts every Tradovate account separately even under one login, and a connection can be re-pointed to the next account when one passes or fails). The bot computes each decision once and then applies **each account's own** thresholds, profile, lives and caps, so the same setup can trade on one account and be skipped on another (e.g. an account on its last life with a stricter cutoff). Accounts with the same preset **and** stage may instead share one connection through TradeSyncer: TradeSyncer copies the leader's trades **without** the bot's per-account checks, so only identical accounts may follow (the owner sets TradeSyncer's size multipliers, daily loss limits and max position caps there). `!flat` / `!kill` exit every connected account and TradeSyncer copies the exits. An `exit` sent while a leader is already flat copies nothing, so a follower that drifted out of sync (failed copy, rejection) is not flattened by the bot: at flat-by the bot posts "check followers flat in TradeSyncer", the owner confirms it on Live Status (checklist), and flattens any out-of-sync follower in TradeSyncer or Tradovate. After every leader trade end or removal the bot posts "check followers flat", and the leader's last direction stays in the direction lock until the owner taps **Followers flat** on Live Status (or `!followers <leader> flat`). Live Status and `!status` show every connected account. When an account passes, fails or is replaced, the owner marks it inactive (`active: false`), re-points that TradersPost connection to the new account and enters it on the Accounts page; the bot blocks entries on it until its state is confirmed. The UI refuses to save two active accounts whose `traderspost_webhook_env` resolves to the same URL, and flat-by, `!flat` and kill exits go once per distinct webhook URL. If the bot or computer is down, flatten the leader in Tradovate web/mobile (TradeSyncer copies the exit), then `!resolve <account> flat`.

Bridge risks and mitigations:
- Extra hop (TradersPost cloud): sub-second, but it matters for seconds-scale order-flow entries, so labels and simulators delay every order, `exit` and `breakeven` by `order_latency_sec` (§5.5b).
- **Broker rejections:** a webhook reply only shows TradersPost received the signal, not that Tradovate accepted the order (max position, account locked, failed account). Unless step 21 shows rejections are visible, Live Status and Discord show entries as "sent – broker acceptance unconfirmed"; the owner checks TradersPost's signal log for the first 10 demo trades; every assumed fill is flagged for reconciliation and overridden by the next orders/fills import (step 24).
- Bridge or Tradovate outage: bracket orders already sit at the broker, so open trades stay protected; the bot stops sending new entries if a webhook returns an error or times out, and alerts the owner on Discord.
- No fill feedback: the bot logs what it sent; the owner reconciles with Tradovate; if TradersPost offers a trade log/API, poll it.

### 10.2 Not chosen (don't propose these)
PickMyTrade (TradersPost covers Tradovate), Topstep/ProjectX (owner declined), Rithmic direct API (needs firm permission and conformance testing), Quantower/NinjaTrader strategies (C#; would duplicate the Python feature code).

### 10.3 Hard limits in code
- Per-trade dollar risk from `config/accounts.yaml`; skip if the stop doesn't fit (§6.1). Size never exceeds the account's max minis/micros or current scaling tier.
- Daily loss cap where the account has one (§6.7; tracked by the bot from its own fills estimate, plus the firm's own enforcement); max trades/day; max position; flat-by time; news/data blackouts; kill switch (Discord `!kill` + Control Panel KILL button) that sends `exit` and stops new entries. At flat-by, on `!flat` and on kill, `exit` is sent to **every** connected account and ticker, whatever the bot believes its state is (a harmless no-op when flat).
- If the data feed or the bridge fails: no new entries; existing brackets stay at the broker; Discord alert.
- **Direction lock across accounts (MFFU bans hedging across accounts; owner):** at any moment every account's open positions and pending entries (NQ and MNQ together) point the same way. An entry opposite to any position, pending entry, or "maybe filled" / "flat-unconfirmed" / "unknown" state (which counts as the direction of that account's last sent entry, kept in `carry_state` for a carried account) on any account is **skipped for that account, never queued** (a removed pending entry keeps its direction until its removal `exit`, sent after the entry's own reply, is acknowledged + `order_latency_sec`, plus `tradesyncer_copy_lag_sec` (measured by test 9 (h)) for a TradeSyncer leader); the account can take a later decision. Decisions in the same second are processed in one fixed order (highest calibrated EV first, then account name), and the first direction sent locks the rest. `demo: true` accounts are outside the lock. Threshold sweeps, preset-stage sweeps, per-fold choices and gates 16/20 run per single account without the lock; the policy and prop simulators then replay the active account set together with the lock for the per-account reports (§6.7 Backtest, §6.8), showing the trades the lock removed per account.
- **One trade per account at a time.** A trade is one NQ leg and/or one MNQ leg, sent together with identical entry, stop and target prices; breakeven and exit go to every filled leg; a leg whose entry is still pending is removed with `exit` on its own ticker. After the trade's **last** open leg has ended (stop or target inferred on each filled leg's own instrument) and after every time-to-live expiry with no leg filled, the bot also sends `exit` to both tickers of that account (a no-op when flat, test 3), so no stale resting entry or leg can fill into the next trade. Live fill inference reads each leg's own instrument (Databento Live subscribes to MNQ trades for the contract month resolved from `NQ.v.0`, same dataset). If only one leg fills before the time-to-live, the other is removed with `exit` on its own ticker and the trade runs smaller (less risk, never more). No new entry is sent while a position is open, an entry order is pending, or any `exit` / `breakeven` for that account is still unacknowledged (being retried); an acknowledged one never blocks; `!resume` after `!flat` waits only for `!flat` exits still being retried (one whose retries ended has already made its account "unknown", which `!resolve` handles). Exits never flip into a reverse position (`exit`, never `reverse`).
- **Retries and data health** (keys in settings.yaml): `exit` / `breakeven` retry with backoff (1, 2, 4… s) until acknowledged or `exit_retry_window_sec` (start 120) has passed (before every `breakeven` send or retry, price must still be at least 1 tick + `exit_slippage_ticks` on its favourable side; otherwise retries stop, it is logged and the original bracket stays); flat-by exits keep retrying for `flat_retry_min` (start 11) minutes after that day's flat-by, kill and `!flat` exits for `flat_retry_min` after the command, and news-flatten exits for `flat_retry_min` after the flatten moment, and alert every `flat_alert_sec` (start 30). `breakeven` goes only to legs assumed filled (never "maybe filled"); a "no open position" reply ends its retries, marks the leg for fill re-inference and is not a webhook error; `breakevenOffset` is omitted when the converted offset is 0. Webhook calls time out after `webhook_timeout_sec` (start 5). Any `exit` for an account (either ticker) still unacknowledged when its retries end puts the account in "unknown" until `!resolve`. Each live schema (NQ/ES trades, NQ `mbp-1`, MNQ trades) has its own staleness check and a processing-lag check (local receive time − `ts_event` > `feed_lag_max_ms`, start 500); if any schema an enabled model or fill inference uses (MNQ trades whenever micros are allowed) is stale or lagging, new entries are blocked (inputs are never passed to a model as missing). New entries are also blocked while an NQ/ES connection is stale (no record of any kind, data or Databento heartbeat (heartbeat interval as low as Databento allows), for > `feed_stale_sec` (start 5); a quiet market is not stale), or while the Skylit connection has been silent (no event or ping) for > `skylit_stale_sec` (start 45 s, i.e. three missed pings), and on any webhook error; they resume after `feed_recovery_sec` (start 60) of healthy data plus a fresh Skylit board (after a Skylit gap, also the gap filled from history, §3.1), or (webhook errors) when the owner confirms. **Per-map freshness (owner 2026-10-14, §13 Q-mapstale; live only):** the stream drops board updates per map, so the connection-level check above can stay quiet while one map is silent. A map is stale while its newest board arrived more than `skylit_map_stale_sec` (start 5 s) ago: no new entries on a zone with a level from that map (zones of other maps and chart-only zones trade on); a pending entry on such a zone stays (it was placed on a fresh map and is bracketed) unless the map stays stale past `skylit_stale_sec`, when the feed-failure rule above applies to that map (its pending entries removed). After a block the map stays blocked until a second board arrives within `disl_guard_confirm_sec` (10 s) of the first (owner 2026-10-17, §13 Q-dislguard-2 (b)(ii): the R right after a gap rests on one board the dislocation guard cannot yet check; a first board not confirmed in time is replaced by the next as the new first). Every block and every confirmation case is logged (map, start, length) and counted per session (`live/map_freshness.py`; the session-recording report counts them with the same code). History has a board every second, so the backtest never sees a stale map: **a known live-vs-backtest difference** (measured: NDXP stale 1.19% of 10-12's session and 0.14% of 10-09's; QQQ and SPY ≈ 0), not modelled in labels or simulators. A data outage, the start of a news blackout or `!pause` removes pending entries (a mapping-dislocation block removes those at its chain's zones and blocks new ones there, §5.1), and the news flatten (§9) also exits open positions, (`exit`, §3.5). The simulator does the same for outages and blackouts.
- **Account states (one table; §3.5, §9, §9c and CLAUDE.md rule 8 follow it):** *flat*; *pending* (entry sent, unfilled); *filled* (assumed, §3.5); *maybe filled* (§3.5); *flat-unconfirmed* — an `exit` sent after the entry's own reply was acknowledged on every uncertain leg's ticker: the worse case is booked, the account takes no new entry and its last direction stays in the lock until the owner checks Tradovate and sends `!confirm <account>` (or Confirm on Live Status); *unknown* — an `exit` still unacknowledged when its retries end, or an entry whose fate can't be told: cleared only by `!resolve`. An `exit` sent while that leg's entry POST is still unanswered doesn't count for any of this: it is re-sent as soon as the entry's reply arrives. Step 21 records what a webhook reply shows when the broker rejects an `exit`.
- **Never auto-retry an entry.** If an entry webhook times out or errors after the request was sent, the bot does not resend it (a resend could double the position if the first one actually went through); it marks the account "unknown state", pauses new entries, alerts the owner to check Tradovate, and at once sends `exit` (`cancel: true`) on that leg's ticker, again after `webhook_timeout_sec` + `order_latency_sec` (in case the entry was still in flight); once acknowledged, the leg is "flat-unconfirmed" (worse case booked; `!confirm <account>` clears it after the owner checks Tradovate). An account still "unknown" (an exit unacknowledged after its retries) leaves that state only when the owner resolves it (Live Status **Resolve** or `!resolve <account> flat|position`); "maybe filled" also clears by itself per §3.5. An entry that failed before anything was sent (DNS or connection error) is logged as "not sent" and is not retried either, but does not set "unknown". Exits may retry, since sending `exit` twice is harmless.
- Every entry leg carries a unique `extras.order_id` (`<event_id>-<entry style><n>-<account>-<leg>`, §5.4) plus its `extras.event_id`; the bot refuses to send the same `order_id` twice. `exit` and `breakeven` signals carry the `event_id` and are exempt; blanket exits carry `event_id` = `flatby-<UTC ts>` (flat-by), `flat-<UTC ts>` (`!flat`), `news-<UTC ts>` (news flatten) or `kill-<UTC ts>` (kill). The journal logs every exit and breakeven too (`sending exit <account> <ticker> <event_id>`, `sending breakeven <account> <ticker> <event_id>`, §9).
- Start: one account, normal sizing (§6.1), owner watching.

---

## 11. Repo layout and coding rules

```
nq-signal-bot/
  CLAUDE.md             # working rules, read every session
  .env                  # SKYLIT_API_KEY, DATABENTO_API_KEY, ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL, DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN, DISCORD_OWNER_ID, DISCORD_CHANNEL_ID, one TP_WEBHOOK_<ACCOUNT> per TradersPost connection (never commit)
  .gitignore            # .env, config/local.yaml, data/, models/, reports/ (a safety net only: these folders never exist inside the repo)
  pyproject.toml
  config/
    settings.yaml       # sessions, anchors, symbols, thresholds
    features.yaml       # every tunable detector parameter, zone widths
    accounts.yaml       # per-account risk, max minis/micros, scaling tiers, caps, thresholds
    calendar.yaml       # release times (past dates: as scheduled that morning; refreshed by the pre-open job), holidays and early closes, OPEX, quarter-end, owner-entered event dates
  docs/
    SPEC.md             # this spec
    skylit/             # academy docs (owner provides)
    feature_spec.md     # approved definitions
    owner_notes.md
  data/                 # under paths.root on the external SSD, not in the repo
    raw/{skylit,databento,gameplans}/
    processed/          # parquet: bars, profiles, mapped nodes, events
    reviews/
    live/               # paths.live_dir: journal, account_state.json, today's state
    live_archive/
  src/nqbot/
    clients/  skylit.py  databento_client.py  discord.py  traderspost.py
    gameplan/ parser.py
    features/ vwap.py  profile.py  tpo.py  volctx.py  opening_range.py  session_levels.py  nodes.py  mapping.py  confluence.py  zones.py  darkpool.py  regime.py  orderflow.py  daytype.py
    detectors/ rug.py  pika.py  trinity.py  audit.py  absorption.py  exhaustion.py
    events/   builder.py  labeler.py  paths.py
    models/   structure.py  trigger.py  daytype.py  pair_scorer.py  calibrate.py  compare.py
    backtest/ walkforward.py  report.py  exit_sim.py  prop_sim.py
    live/     engine.py  guard.py  risk.py  sizing.py  execution.py  archive.py
    ui/       app.py  pages/{accounts,settings,live_status,gameplan,review}.py
  tests/
  reports/              # paths.reports_dir, on the SSD
  models/               # paths.models_dir, on the SSD
```
- **Path convention:** every `data/…`, `models/…`, `reports/…` path in this spec means a folder under `paths.root` on the SSD: `paths.data_dir` = `<root>/data`, `paths.raw_dir` = `<root>/data/raw`, `paths.live_dir` = `<root>/data/live`, `paths.models_dir` = `<root>/models`, `paths.reports_dir` = `<root>/reports`, `paths.log_dir` = `<root>/logs`, `paths.tmp_dir` = `<root>/tmp`. Code always builds paths from `paths.*`, never repo-relative.
- Python 3.12 (pinned in `pyproject.toml` and `.python-version`; `uv` manages it, `uv.lock` is committed, every command runs as `uv run nqbot …`, step 4a), pandas/polars, pyarrow, lightgbm, scikit-learn, shap, plotly, starlette + uvicorn (the Control Panel server; its React front end is built with Node at build time only, §9b, owner 2026-11-14), httpx + httpx-sse, databento, anthropic, python-docx (gameplan .docx → Markdown), discord.py, pydantic, pytest, tzdata (Windows has no time-zone database of its own).
- **Where settings live:**
  - **Per account → `accounts.yaml`** (the owner's file is `<data_dir>/panel/accounts.yaml` on the SSD, written by the Control Panel; `config/accounts.yaml` is the committed example; owner 2026-11-15, §9b; §6.7 fields, plus `lambda_risk_penalty` §6.3, `max_risk_fraction_divisor`, `max_loss_run_rate`, `lives_mode`, `last_life_top_pct` (`auto` or a number), `sim_fills`, `custom_risk_usd`, `stop_at_target`, `funded_buffer_usd`, `payout_min_usd`, `payout_max_usd`, `payout_profit_since_last_usd`, `payouts_taken`, `after_first_payout`, `active` and the Custom profile's `min_ev_r` / `min_target_prob` §6.5b). Daily account state lives in `<paths.live_dir>/account_state.json`, not here (§6.7).
  - **Global trading/execution → `settings.yaml`:** `session.flat_by`, `min_minutes_before_flat`, the month ledger `months` (§7 rule 2b), `paths.root` (settings.yaml stores the folder relative to the drive, e.g. `nqbot/`; at startup the drive is found by scanning mounted drives for the marker file `.nqbot_drive`, never by drive letter) with `paths.data_dir` = `<paths.root>/data`, `paths.raw_dir`, `paths.models_dir`, `paths.reports_dir`, `paths.log_dir`, `paths.tmp_dir`, `paths.live_dir` under it, `min_free_gb` (50), `min_free_gb_live` (20), `max_fold_mem_gb` (10), `ops.job_lock_window` (08:30–16:15) with `ops.job_lock_window_enforced` (false until step 22 sets it true), `preopen_start` (08:30), `tempest_draft_cutoff_et` (09:25) and `tempest_draft_retry_min` (5) (§3.1 Tempest),
