`settings.yaml` holds only their start values.
- All times stored in UTC; session logic in America/New_York.
- **Two machines (owner 2026-10-28):** the **home PC** (RTX 3060, 48 GB RAM) is the build machine: steps 11–20 and later retrains, top-ups and every long `nqbot run` job, holding the external SSD. The **laptop** (Dell 16 Plus, 16 GB) is the live-trading machine from step 21 and never runs long jobs while it trades; the PC never trades: its `.env` holds no `TP_WEBHOOK_*` (`scripts/register_tasks_pc.ps1` refuses otherwise). **The PC is a dedicated build box on Windows 10 (owner 2026-10-31):** Windows 10 Home 22H2, past end of support (support ended 2025-10-14, the consumer Extended Security Updates year on 2026-10-13) and unable to upgrade to Windows 11, so Windows Update can't be paused and delivers no updates and no update restarts (Defender's definitions still update); it is kept to the build work with Defender on and no browsing. `nqbot preflight` reports this as an info line there (build below 22000, after 2026-10-13 ET) instead of the "not paused" warning and exit 5; Windows 11 (the laptop) keeps the pause check. How the laptop reaches the data it needs for live trading is decided before step 21 (§13 Q-livedata). Code lives in a private GitHub repo (`git pull` before working on a machine, `git push` after; `data/`, `models/` and `.env` never committed); `.env` is recreated on each machine from the owner's password manager, and after a key rotation `nqbot keys-check` makes one free authenticated call per service on each machine before the old keys are revoked (owner 2026-10-28). Both install from the committed `uv.lock`, so models trained on the PC load and behave identically on the laptop; LightGBM runs on the CPU with fixed seeds and `deterministic: true` (no GPU build). Machine resources (`max_workers`, `max_fold_mem_gb`, `low_memory`, and `rclone_path` and `test_parallel` (owner 2026-10-30: `nqbot test` runs parallel only where it is true, the PC; absent = serial, so the live laptop never does by default); no other key) go in an uncommitted `config/local.yaml` (format `config/local.example.yaml`); nothing that changes results may go there. Both machines keep Windows on Eastern time with automatic time sync; `nqbot preflight` and `nqbot status` check the time zone, the sync setting, that the Windows Time service is running (owner 2026-10-31) and the offset against `ntp_server` (`clock_max_offset_ms`), measured from the best of 4 SNTP exchanges by round trip with the name resolved first (owner 2026-10-31: a slow DNS lookup inside the first exchange read as +500 ms on a clock 60 ms off); a best round trip over `clock_max_rtt_ms` (200) warns "couldn't measure the clock precisely" instead of reporting an offset, and the round trip is printed beside it. **Time sync (owner 2026-10-31):** on the PC `w32time` was stopped (start type Manual, started only on a work domain or by "Sync now"), polled every 9 h, and drifted to +535 ms after a restart while the setting read "on"; `scripts/fix_time_sync.ps1` (admin PowerShell, the same on both machines; `-ShowOnly` prints the state and changes nothing) sets the service to Automatic (Delayed Start) and starts it, sets the peers `time.cloudflare.com,0x9 time.windows.com,0x9` with a 1024-s poll (Windows clamps the poll to 2^`MinPollInterval` .. 2^`MaxPollInterval`, 1024 .. 32768 s, so the first version's 900 s ran every 1024 s; a value outside is refused; the 2048 s "Poll Interval" of `w32tm /query /status` is the host's clock-discipline poll, not the peer poll) and resyncs, and registers the task `nqbot time resync` (SYSTEM, `w32tm /resync /force` 2 min after every startup, 3 retries), printing the service, peers, poll interval, task and `w32tm /query /status` before and after. A job interrupted by unplugging the SSD resumes on the other machine from its last chunk (`nqbot run` is resumable), and only one machine runs jobs on the SSD at a time (the job lock files are on the SSD; **two locks, owner 2026-10-05:** the Skylit lock (step-7 checks and Skylit pull jobs; the short step-7 checks that run alongside the step-10 backfill don't take it, owner 2026-10-06, §12 step 7) and the Databento lock (`databento plan` and Databento pull jobs), so the step-10 and step-8 backfills run in parallel on the laptop; every other job holds both; each lock records its machine, so a second machine is still refused; a lock left by a run whose SSD was unplugged can't remove itself, so `nqbot unlock [--skylit | --databento]` shows the holders and removes them with `--yes` after the owner confirms that job has stopped (never a lock whose holder is still running on this machine); `nqbot status` shows both locks; a dead process's lock on the same machine is reclaimed automatically. The two backfills fit the 16 GB laptop side by side: neither uses worker processes (`nqbot status` shows each job's measured peak memory), and the `min_free_gb` check reads the drive's free space before every chunk, so it counts both). Unattended runs on the PC: a scheduled resume task (§11 Overnight jobs) restarts the queued job after a reboot, power loss or crash; the job lock prevents a second copy.
- **Overnight jobs (owner):** every long job (data pulls, feature/event rebuilds, detector grids, walk-forward folds, sweeps, monthly retrains) runs through `nqbot run <job> --until HH:MM` (default `--until 07:30`). Jobs are split into resumable chunks (one session, one month or one fold at a time) that save their progress; a chunk starts only if its measured 90th-percentile duration fits before `--until`, work is checked against the clock between work units, and the next `nqbot run <job>` resumes where it left off. Folds are split into sub-chunks (one lower model or one grid point each). Every output is written to a temp file, fsynced and renamed, so a killed chunk leaves nothing partial and loses at most that chunk. A rename that Windows refuses (an antivirus scan holding a fresh file) is retried with backoff for about 30 s (presd_fixes D5). `nqbot stop <job>` asks a running job to stop after the call or window it is in, its answer saved (status `stopped`; unlike Ctrl-C, which can throw a paid answer away; owner 2026-10-06); `nqbot run <job>` resumes it. Overnight runs need the machine running the job plugged in with sleep (and, on the laptop, the lid-close action) off; `nqbot run` also asks Windows to keep the system awake while a job runs (`SetThreadExecutionState`, owner 2026-10-05; this doesn't stop lid-close sleep, so the lid action on AC stays "Do nothing") and warns (changing nothing; `nqbot setup-ssd` too) if `powercfg /qh` shows AC sleep or hibernate after a timeout, a lid action on AC other than "Do nothing" (skipped on a machine with no battery, so no lid, as in `nqbot preflight`; owner 2026-10-31), or USB selective suspend on AC, or can't read one of them (read by position, so a non-English Windows works too). The owner's laptop uses Modern Standby (S0 low-power idle, no S3): before the first night, check once that the keep-awake request holds there: start `nqbot run dummy`, let the screen turn off, and see `python.exe` listed under SYSTEM in `powercfg /requests`; the next morning `powercfg /sleepstudy` should show no standby during the run. From step 22, the pre-open job kills any `nqbot run` still running when it starts (`preopen_start`, 08:30). `nqbot run` refuses any pull or job that would leave less than `min_free_gb` free on the drive it writes to, and checks the SSD is mounted before every chunk (it stops cleanly, and resumes later, if the SSD is unplugged). Jobs use at most `max_workers` cores (start: all but 2) so the machine stays usable, and, once live trading has started (step 22 on), **never run 08:30–16:15 ET on trading days on the live machine** (the pre-open job and live trading own the machine then; `nqbot run` refuses to start or stops in that window, except `archive_live` when the pre-open job (`nqbot preopen`) calls it). Before that, jobs may run any time, and the time-critical Phase C pulls run as soon as they are approved. `nqbot status` shows each job's progress and estimated nights left, the resume queue and the clock check, and marks a job lock whose holder on this machine is no longer running as stale (the next job run takes it over and writes its own pid and start time; owner 2026-10-31). **Unattended runs (owner 2026-10-28):** the job queue `<data_dir>/jobs/queue.txt` (on the SSD; `nqbot queue show|add|remove|release`, written only by the owner or by Claude Code with his OK) lists the jobs `nqbot resume` runs in order, each with `--until` (default 07:30), the next once the previous is complete. The PC's task `nqbot resume` (`scripts/register_tasks_pc.ps1`, S4U, wakes the computer) starts it at startup (+2 min), daily at 07:35 and every hour, with no task time limit; one instance at a time, and the job lock refuses a second run. A job is resumed only if its last run ended at `--until`, on an SSD drop, inside the job-lock window, or never recorded an end (the process died: reboot, power loss, crash; its lock is reclaimed as a dead process's); every other end (`stopped` by the owner, `credits`, `failures`, `api_error`, `low_space`, a refusal before it started) waits for the owner, who resumes it by hand with `nqbot run <job>`. A job whose last `ops.resume_max_crashes` (3) resume attempts each died without finishing a chunk is held until `nqbot queue release <job>`. The first resume after a restart runs `nqbot verify` over the last `ops.resume_verify_days` (2) days first and holds the whole queue if anything is damaged (owner 2026-10-31: the hold names when that check ran and the files it flagged, and `nqbot status` shows a hold even with the queue empty). Resume never bypasses an approval: it only calls `nqbot run`, which refuses a paid job whose plan lacks the owner's approval (CLAUDE.md rule 2). The resume drill job `dummy_drill` (`nqbot drill setup --chunks N --sleep S`, `nqbot drill check`) proves it on a new machine. Remote progress: the hourly task `nqbot status push` writes `nqbot status`, the queue, the clock check and the latest preflight warnings to `<log_dir>/status_<host>.txt` and copies it with rclone to `<backup.drive_folder>/<backup.status_folder>/` on Google Drive (which rclone, owner 2026-10-30: `rclone_path` in `config/local.yaml`, else `rclone` on PATH, else winget's `WinGet\Links\rclone.exe`, else its `WinGet\Packages\Rclone.Rclone_*\rclone-v*\rclone.exe` (highest version), so a task or terminal started before winget installed it still finds it; `status-push`, `backup`, `preflight` and `keys-check` print the path used, and none found is a clear "rclone not found" with the fix); the task logs are `<log_dir>/resume_<yyyyMMdd>.txt`, `preflight_…`, `status_push_…`. `nqbot test` prints its wall time and the peak memory of the pytest process tree above its exit line. A long job (`nqbot run`, `nqbot backup`, `nqbot resume`, `nqbot queue add`) runs only a commit that passed the full suite (owner 2026-10-29, CLAUDE.md rule 14 (f)): `nqbot test` with no pytest arguments records a pass on a clean commit in `<data_dir>/jobs/test_passes.jsonl`; only `.md` changes since a passed ancestor still count; otherwise the job refuses, and it warns if git or the record can't be read.
- **Low memory (the default; the PC may set `low_memory: false` once a test shows identical outputs):** never hold all pair rows in memory. Each fold's LightGBM Dataset is built with `lgb.Sequence` readers that stream parquet row groups (event features joined per batch), `free_raw_data=True`, then saved with `save_binary` for reuse; features are float32; grid searches use all pair rows by default (`grid_sample_frac: 1.0`, owner prefers quality over speed; lowering it only shortens the runs and is the one 16 GB shortcut that could change results slightly); SHAP runs on ≤ 50k sampled rows; `mbp-1` is streamed per session. A fold whose estimated peak memory is over `max_fold_mem_gb` is refused and re-chunked. `max_workers` (setting, default all cores but 2) is capped at run time at floor((free RAM − 10 GB) ÷ the measured peak per session) (owner 2026-11-14: was 4 GB; the feature-build pool keeps the same 10 GB free besides its sessions, one reserve for the whole machine and every build together, and logs the free memory at each session start and once per held stretch; a per-machine `min_free_ram_gb` in `config/local.yaml` comes with the next build-key change, since `config.py` is in the build key, §13 Q-step13-review). Live trading needs only a few GB.
- **Storage:** convert every Skylit range response to zstd parquet as it is pulled — a per-strike table (`asOf`, symbol, strike, value as float32) and a per-board table (`asOf`, symbol, `spot`, `previousClose`, `meta.resolution`; `priceChange` = `spot` − `previousClose` when needed), since mapping needs each board's spot and aged-out history can't be pulled again (a step-7 unit test rebuilds R from the stored sample). Layout (`clients/skylit_store.py`): `raw/skylit/range/<date>/<HHMM ET>_{boards,strikes}.parquet` per 15-minute window, with the request parameters in the parquet metadata (a window is reused only with identical parameters, never silently re-pulled); `fullchain/` (step-7 strike-window calls), `historical/`, `darkpool/`, `vol/`, `stream/`; the CLAUDE.md rule-2 re-pull exceptions go to `repull/<reason>/…`, never over the original; `ledger.jsonl` logs every call the client makes (paid or failed) with its credits (each step-7 check that runs alongside the backfill without the lock writes its own `ledger_<check>.jsonl`; readers merge them all); a charged answer that can't be decoded or converted is kept in `failed/` and never retried. A window interrupted between its two file writes has its orphan file moved to `repull/partial_write/` and is pulled again (25 credits, rare; a CLAUDE.md rule-2 exception, owner 2026-10-05). Dark-pool dates pulled fewer than 5 sessions after their trade date are marked `provisional` in the file metadata (first received live, so the later top-ups may re-pull them); a recent trading day with no prints yet is never cached as empty. The month ledger (`settings.yaml → months`) maps each month number to one `YYYY-MM` or a list of them (the §12 step-4b month-1 merge) (≈ 15–25 GB per year instead of ~150 GB of JSON); Databento `trades` for NQ, ES and MNQ plus NQ `mbp-1` are ≈ 50–100 GB, no depth data is stored. Databento layout (`clients/databento_store.py`, step 8): `raw/databento/<schema>/<raw contract>/<CME trading day>.dbn.zst`, one request per file as delivered (zstd DBN, lossless), reused only if the file's own DBN metadata (dataset, schema, symbol, start, end) matches the request; `raw/databento/symbology/` (the contract resolutions) and `ledger.jsonl` (every resolve, cost and download call); 1- and 5-minute bars in `processed/bars/<NQ|ES>/<day>_{1m,5m}.parquet`, roll spreads in `processed/rolls/<root>_<roll day>.json`. About 230–280 GB is used at the start, growing ≈ 15–25 GB per month with top-ups and live logs. **Retention:** keep only the current and previous features version of processed files and caches, and delete LightGBM binaries of superseded folds after each production fit; `nqbot status` shows SSD use and the months left at the current growth (about 2.5 years on 1 TB with this rule). **Where data lives (owner):** **all** project data — `data/` (raw history, processed files, caches, the live folder and its archive), `models/`, `reports/`, logs, LightGBM and SHAP temp files and downloads — sits on the owner's **1 TB external USB-C SSD** under `paths.root`, so the machines' internal drives stay clean. Plug it into a USB-C port (the 10 Gbps USB-C or the Thunderbolt 4 port), never the 5 Gbps USB-A port. Format it NTFS once before use (journaled, so an unplug can't corrupt the file system) and turn off USB selective suspend while it is plugged in. Temp files are created in the destination folder (same drive), so the rename is atomic. Live trading needs the SSD (§9 says what happens if it disappears). **USB drops (owner 2026-10-11):** every atomic write and ledger append whose device vanishes waits up to `SSD_DROP_WAIT_SEC` (30 s) for the drive and is retried once, only after the drive is back and stable: a freshly opened probe file can be written, fsynced and removed `SSD_STABLE_PROBES` (4) times in a row 0.5 s apart, never sooner than 2 s after the drop (owner 2026-10-13: on 10-12 the retry ran 3 ms after a drop while the old volume still looked present, and failed) (a ledger line already written is never appended twice; a Databento download is never re-sent, since a stream is billed for the data sent); every drop is logged to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\nqbot\ssd_events.log` on the internal drive and, once the SSD is back, to `<root>/logs/ssd_events.log`. Every `nqbot` command holds Windows awake while it runs (with the screen off, Modern Standby powered down the Type-C controller and the SSD reset 1–2 minutes later). `nqbot verify --since D` reads back every file written since D (parquet in full, `.dbn.zst` decompressed frame by frame and its size checked against the download ledger, json parsed (a leading UTF-8 BOM accepted), text decoded as UTF-8 or Windows-1252 with no run of 16 zero bytes, owner 2026-10-31) and lists anything damaged in `<reports_dir>/verify/verify.md`. **Recording fallback (owner 2026-10-11, the only exception to "all data on the SSD"):** `step7 stream-session --fallback-local` records on the internal drive (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\nqbot\fallback\<root>`, same layout) if the SSD isn't reachable or writable at the start, or from the first part it can't take mid-session; after the close it moves the recording to the SSD itself (copied, fsynced, checksum-checked, then deleted locally; ledgers merged line by line; a differing file stays put and is listed), or `nqbot fallback-move` does it later. Nothing else ever falls back. **Console and log output (owner 2026-10-16):** a pulled or re-mounted SSD never ends a run through its own output: every command wraps stdout and stderr (`console.py`), so a write to a dead task-log handle carries on in a freshly opened copy of that log (`NQBOT_TASK_LOG`, set by `scripts/nqbot_task.ps1`) or, while the SSD can't be opened, in a file of the same name in the fallback logs folder (tried back on the SSD every 60 s, merged by `fallback-move`); the daily log file reopens after a failed write; the task wrapper writes its `TASK EXIT CODE` line to the fallback folder if the log is gone; a scheduled task that starts while the SSD is missing waits up to `SSD_DROP_WAIT_SEC` for it (from a terminal a command still refuses at once). Found 2026-10-15: the recorder survived three short drops, then the SSD stayed out past the 30-s wait, the recording switched to the internal drive as designed, and the next line it printed (to the task log on the SSD) killed the process (exit 1). **Backup (once, when step 10 finishes; owner 2026-10-06, presd_fixes D12):** Claude Code copies `data/raw/skylit/` and the dark-pool prints (≈ 15–25 GB of Skylit parquet plus the prints; size measured at step 7) to the owner's Google Drive folder (owner, 2026-10; §13) with **rclone straight from the SSD** (not Drive for desktop; Google One 100 GB bought; owner 2026-10-05; the owner authorises the rclone Google Drive remote once with `rclone config`) and verifies checksums (`rclone check`) — a copy on the same SSD is not a backup, and that history can never be pulled again. Command: `uv run nqbot backup [--until HH:MM]` (= `nqbot run skylit_backup`; `backup.py`, session C): checks that rclone runs and that the remote `backup.rclone_remote` (`gdrive`) exists, then per folder of `raw/skylit/` (one chunk per month under `range/`) `rclone copy --checksum` to `<backup.drive_folder>/raw/skylit/` (`NQ bot backup`) and `rclone check --one-way` (MD5); atomic-write temp files are never copied; the `range/` months go first and the folders a running check may still write (the `ledger*.jsonl` files, `vol/`, `stream/`, `repull/`) last. Each chunk id carries a fingerprint of its folder's files, so a re-run copies and checks only folders that changed; a failed check stops the job loudly (`backup_failed`). It holds the Skylit job lock (never while the Skylit backfill writes; the Databento backfill may run alongside); report `<reports_dir>/backup/backup.md`, rclone logs in `logs/`. The Drive copy never deletes: a file later moved aside on the SSD (a `repull/partial_write/` orphan, a superseded sample-day window) stays in its old Drive folder too, so a restore uses the SSD layout's rules (a window counts only with both files and matching parameters).
- **Expected run time on the laptop (rough; the PC is expected to be faster, `nqbot status` measures it):** a full 12-month feature/event rebuild ≈ 1 night; the step-13 detector grid ≈ 1–3 nights; the first full walk-forward (step 15) ≈ 4–8 nights with full-data searches, re-runs with cached searches ≈ 1–2 nights; step 19 ≈ 3–6 weeks of nights (F16.10: ≈ 16–26 nights before any cut, each cut adds a round of arms) (the order-flow detector grid, a rebuild, the walk-forward and the full-pipeline arms: seven ablation arms in the first backward-elimination round, one fewer in each later round, plus structure-only and the logistic baseline once each; arms reuse each scorer's cached grid point and half-life, which are re-searched only when features change, not on a relabel); the production fit ≈ 2–4 nights; a monthly retrain ≈ 1–3 nights (weekends are used when needed, since nothing runs 08:30–16:15 on trading days). Backtest replay runs in two passes: pass 1 replays session by session the full zone and structure-label engine (with the Skylit boards, no feature or pair output), since freshness depends on whole zones, to carry the multi-day state (freshness counts, unfilled single prints, weekly profile, yesterday's day type, dark-pool lists, rolling statistics) and writes `state_<date>.json` (JSON, owner 2026-11-01, §13 Q-step11f; before the first session, the full warm-up build writes `state_warmup_<date>.json`, named by that session; every state records the digest of the state it was built from, §13 Q-step11h); pass 2 replays sessions in parallel, each starting from its checkpoint, and asserts that its end state equals the next checkpoint. Live writes the same checkpoint each evening (§9), including the rolling statistics (ATR, realized vol, big-trade threshold, opening-range averages) so warm-up never needs more than the sessions since the last checkpoint. Mapped-level and node-type timelines are cached per features version, and grid values that don't change them reuse the cache.
- Money and pulls: CLAUDE.md rule 2.
- Small, tested modules. Each detector has a unit test with a hand-built example.

---

## 12. Build steps (in order)

**Legend:** 🛠 setup · 💳 buy · ✅ GATE (must pass before continuing)

### Phase A — Setup (no new cost; steps 1–4 should be done within days, see Phase C)
1. 🛠 Install Python 3.11+, git, VS Code, and Claude Code. Create a **private** GitHub repo `nq-signal-bot`, clone it, put `CLAUDE.md` at the root and `SPEC.md` in `docs/`.
2. 🛠 Create `.env` with `SKYLIT_API_KEY` (existing). Sign up for **Databento** (the $125 signup credit covers cost estimates; the Standard subscription starts at step 8) → add `DATABENTO_API_KEY`. Create a Discord server + channel → Integrations → Webhooks → add `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL`; also create a Discord bot (Developer Portal) with message permissions and the **Message Content Intent** enabled (needed for `!` commands) → `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN`, and add your own Discord user ID → `DISCORD_OWNER_ID` and the private channel's ID → `DISCORD_CHANNEL_ID`.
3. 🛠 Format the external SSD as NTFS and plug it into a USB-C port (§11 Storage). Put the Skylit academy docs in `docs/skylit/`, the owner's notes in `docs/owner_notes.md` (done 2026-10), the archived gameplans (Google Drive folder "Jeron gameplans"; all `.md`, owner 2026-10-05) ready to download from the Drive website into `data/raw/gameplans/` once the SSD is set up (step 4b). Account rules (§6.1, §6.7: profit target, consistency %, drawdown size/type, daily loss limit, max minis/micros, scaling plan, risk per trade, commissions) are needed by step 14, when they are entered on the Control Panel's Accounts page (§6.7, §9b); flat-by is 15:59 on every account (owner). Zone widths and stops need no owner input (§5.4b, §6.2).
4. **4a (can be done before the SSD arrives).** Claude Code: scaffold the repo (layout in §11), `pyproject.toml` with a committed lock file, configs (plus an uncommitted `config/local.yaml` for machine resources), logging, pytest, `.gitignore`, a private GitHub repo, and the overnight job runner (`nqbot run <job> --until`, `nqbot status`, §11) that every later long job uses. Build `config/calendar.yaml` from 14 months before today through the coming months, listing the sources: past release times as scheduled at 08:30 ET on each day (from the agencies' release archives and schedule announcements; a postponement or cancellation counts only if announced before 08:30 that day; list each change with its announcement date, §9), published schedules for future dates (FOMC, BLS, BEA, Census, ISM, the Fed's calendar for the Chair's testimony and speeches), and CME equity-index holidays and early closes (§1.2); the owner confirms it at step 6; Claude Code refreshes it at the start of every month from then on and checks it at steps 18, 20 and 22. Write `session.flat_by` (15:59, owner) into `settings.yaml`.
   **4b (on the day the SSD is set up, just before step 7).** Set `paths.root` on the SSD and write its marker file `.nqbot_drive` with `nqbot setup-ssd` (built 2026-10-05, `docs/presd_fixes.md` issue 6): it uses the drive already carrying the marker, else the one named with `--drive E:` (a first setup always names it, since an internal second partition looks like any other drive letter; without it the drives are listed with their sizes); refuses a drive that isn't NTFS or has less than `min_free_gb` free; prints the power warnings (§11 Overnight jobs); computes the window and the month-1 merge below; prints the `settings.yaml` diff (only the `paths.root` and `months` lines change, comments kept, checked by loading it back) and writes nothing without `--yes` (then: the marker at the drive root, the `paths.root` folder, `settings.yaml`, which is committed). It never changes an already-set `paths.root`, and changes an already-set ledger only with `--rewrite-ledger`. Every read of the ledger checks it strictly (month numbers 1–12, quoted `"YYYY-MM"` strings, a list only for month 1, calendar months consecutive). Fix the 12-month window as the 12 complete calendar months ending with the last full month before the step-7 pull starts, and write the month ledger (§7 rule 2b: development months 1–6, walk-forward test months 7–9, sealed months 10–12) into `settings.yaml`; every later step uses it. If fewer than `min_month1_sessions` (start 10) sessions of the first month are still inside Skylit's 365-day reach when step 10 starts, that remainder is merged into month 1 together with the next calendar month (the window then ends one month later; that new month 12 is pulled with the step-18 top-up once complete, and the ledger in `settings.yaml` is rewritten when step 10 starts, before any data is looked at). A session counts as inside the reach if its 09:30 window still is. `nqbot setup-ssd` applies the merge on SSD day if it already applies; `nqbot setup-ssd --rewrite-ledger [--yes]` re-checks it when step 10 starts (it keeps month 1's calendar month, prints where each calendar month and the step-7 sample days move, and refuses once the step-10 Skylit backfill has a plan), and `nqbot skylit plan` / `nqbot databento plan` refuse while step 10 has no plan yet, the merge applies and the ledger isn't merged; once step 10 has a plan the ledger is fixed (a Databento plan made earlier is simply run again after a rewrite: its window follows the ledger). A ledger is merged once at most; if even the merged month 1 has fewer than `min_month1_sessions` sessions in reach (step 10 starting more than about a month after the SSD day), the command refuses: the whole window must move, a hand edit done with Claude Code. With the merge, the new month 12 is not complete when the plans are written, so they include only its days up to yesterday (sealed, never looked at); the rest of it comes with the step-18 top-up.

### Phase B — Spec
5. Claude Code: read `docs/skylit/`, `docs/owner_notes.md` + §5 and write `docs/feature_spec.md` — every concept, its measurable definition, tunable parameters with defaults, and a list of open questions. Also propose a start value for every backticked parameter anywhere in this spec that doesn't have one yet (e.g. `zone_proximity`, `confluence_tol`, `entry_ttl_sec`, `hold_reaction_pts`, label windows, `lean_window`, the rolling parameters, `regime_band`, `abs_*`, `exh_*`, `div_*`, `imb_ratio`, `abs_push_ticks`, `be_trigger`, `recency_halflife_months`, and the day-type model's ≤ `daytype_max_features` inputs), and propose every pre-declared grid (`threshold_grid`, the ≤ 4 breakeven rules, `style_set_grid`, the step-13/19 detector and execution-parameter grids, the `recency_halflife_months` and LightGBM grids, and the §7 rule 5 ablation groups), listed in §13 for the owner to approve at step 6.
6. ✅ **GATE: owner reviews and approves the spec.** The owner answers the step-6 open questions in §13.

### Phase C — Data (Skylit credits + Databento Standard $199/month)
**Time-critical — runs right after step 4b (SSD day), before step 6 if step 5 isn't done yet.** Before the SSD arrives Claude Code may already do step 5 (no data needed; the owner's step-6 review too) and write and unit-test the step-7, 8 and 9 code against mocked APIs (no pulls, nothing written to `data/`). Skylit range history only reaches 365 days back and Databento's included L1 history is the last 12 months, so the window's oldest days drop out of reach (Skylit) or become paid (Databento) every day. Order: step 7 → owner OKs the parameter check and the backfill credits → step 10 (Skylit, oldest day first) and step 8 (Databento) start the same day → steps 5–6, 8b and 9 start while step 10 is still running (5–6 and 9 need no pulled data; 8b needs only the step-7 sample days and their step-8 trades) → step 11 starts once gates 6 and 8b have passed, steps 8 and 10 cover the warm-up plus months 1–6, and the step-7 live-session results (`map_latency_sec`, stream parity) and `tempest_lag_sessions` are in (the dark-pool late-print check may still be running: if it changes `dp_lag_sessions`, the dark-pool levels are rebuilt before step 12) (months 7–9 must be in before the step-13 full event build and step 15; sealed months may arrive later, at step 18 or 20). Nothing in these pulls depends on the spec definitions (symbols, `maxExpirations=1`, `maxStrikes`, gamma and RTH are decided), and no outcome is looked at.
7. Claude Code: Skylit client (auth, retries, rate limits, credit tracking from `X-Credits-Remaining`). Read the OpenAPI spec; call `/v1/symbols` and confirm QQQ, NDXP, SPY, SPX are served and how far back each goes, whether SPX includes the 0DTE (SPXW) expirations, and that NDXP's front expiry is each day's 0DTE; confirm one `/v1/historical/range` call covers them all. First set the strike window: one 15-minute `/v1/historical/range` call on a busy development day and one per sample day, each with the API's largest `maxStrikes` (25 credits per call); `skylit_max_strikes` = the smallest window in which every strike ≥ `node_drop_pct` × the full-chain King lies at least `strike_edge_margin` (start 5) strikes from either edge on every board. Then pull **5 days from development month 6** (≈ 3,250 credits, enough for the step-8b check; the owner looks up the same dates on Atlas replay — it reaches back far enough) and report actual credits used and payload size. Cache them so the step-10 backfill doesn't re-pull them. Check that `maxStrikes` = `skylit_max_strikes` covers each sample day's strongest nodes. During one live session (this doesn't hold up step 10), record `/v1/stream` (all 4 symbols: QQQ, NDXP, SPY, SPX; `maxExpirations=1`, `maxStrikes`) for 15 minutes: measure the gap between boards, the delay from `asOf` to arrival (`map_latency_sec`), one reconnect with `Last-Event-ID`, and credits used (`stream-record` refuses a start whose 15 minutes plus 60 s would run past the close or `early_close_et`, stops at the latest 60 s after its 15 minutes even if nothing arrives (nothing received: refused, nothing written), and allows at most 3 reconnects, the forced one included; an incomplete recording is kept and marked for a re-record); then, at least one session later, pull the same 15 minutes from `/v1/historical/range` and compare board by board (the §3.1 Ingest parity and revision check), and confirm the 15-second `: ping` (`skylit_stale_sec` = 45 s). Read the Flowseeker dark-pool reference (Skylit docs; not in `/v1/openapi.json`), pull the 45 calendar days of QQQ/SPY prints before one development-month date (cached for step 10), confirm coverage, history depth (ideally ≥ 45 days before month 1; otherwise `dp_partial`, §3.1) and credits per call, and check that the bot's top-3 list matches `/v1/dark-pool/top-prints` for that date and a few later development-month dates. The same check pulls the last ≤ 31 days of those dates once more as one `date_start`/`date_end` call and compares it print by print with the one-date answers; step 10 uses 31-day calls only if they match (§3.1 Dark pools). **Late-print check:** on ≥ 5 mornings save the pre-open pull of the previous session's QQQ/SPY prints and the computed top-3, re-pull those dates ≥ 5 trading days later and compare print sets and top-3 lists before any events are built; if late prints change a list, `dp_lag_sessions` (features.yaml, start 1) makes backtest and live both use prints through session T−2. **Done 2026-10-22:** 5 of 5 mornings (10-07, 10-08, 10-09, 10-12, 10-16) re-pulled ≥ 5 trading days later: no print added, changed or removed, no top-3 list changed (QQQ, SPY), so `dp_lag_sessions` stays 1, now measured; nothing is rebuilt. Check `/v1/vol/symbols` and `/v1/vol/history` (does it serve VIX, how far back); on two or more mornings record when the previous session's row (and VIX) first appears and whether re-pulled rows change, and set `tempest_lag_sessions` (if in doubt, 2; §5.2) once the finality check has compared the pre-open copies with a later re-pull (owner 2026-10-09: `step7 tempest-morning` stores a row from the pre-open of a later day, `step7 tempest-finality` re-pulls and compares). Pull 40 `/v1/historical` instants (5 credits each, ≈ 200 credits) spread over development months 1–6 to compare against the bot's node-type classifier once step 11 builds it (no outcomes are used). **A full-chain call that times out (owner 2026-10-06)** is replaced by consecutive shorter calls that together cover the same full 15 minutes (3 × 5 minutes; a 5-minute piece that still times out, 5 × 1 minute; each priced and printed first), so the proposal rests on exactly the same boards; a manifest records the pieces, and the strike window is never measured on less than the full 15 minutes (a piece that fails otherwise stops the check with no report). Once the strike-window check passes, `step7 oldest-window` pulls one 15-minute window (25 credits) of the oldest day still in reach (at least `skylit_reach_margin_min` + 1 h inside it) and reports whether its answer converts (the backfill starts with those days, which may be 1-minute data); the window is kept for the backfill (presd_fixes D1). Show the owner the parameter check (symbols, SPX and NDXP 0DTE, strikes window, resolution, the oldest-window test; it says NOT READY while the strike-window check fails) and the backfill estimate, with the credit shortfall and the pack that covers it (§3.1); on his OK (and the purchase), step 10 starts the same day. **Owner 2026-10-05:** this OK comes as soon as the strike window and the sample days check out; the stream test, parity, the late-print mornings and the Tempest mornings run alongside and never hold up step 10. **Owner 2026-10-06 (decision 2):** these short checks don't take the Skylit lock: each writes only its own files (`stream/`, `repull/<reason>/`, a new `vol/` file, its own ledger file; the late-print check keeps lookback dates the main cache lacks in `repull/late_print/lookback/`, which the backfill copies for free instead of paying again), and the backfill leaves `skylit_alongside_reserve_credits` for them.
8. 💳 Owner subscribes to **Databento Standard ($199/month)**. Claude Code: Databento puller → NQ + ES `ohlcv-1s` and `trades` for the 12 backfill months, NQ `mbp-1` (RTH 09:25–16:05 only; no ES `mbp-1`: ES features use trades only) for the same months, plus the warm-up (NQ and ES `trades` + `ohlcv-1s` for `warmup_sessions` sessions before the first backfill day, §3.2; any session lookback approved or tuned later is capped at the warm-up bought here, or the gap is bought after asking), and MNQ `trades` for the window (only to tell whether MNQ orders filled; no feature uses MNQ — every feature comes from NQ and ES); confirm the plan's live data covers NQ, ES and MNQ trades and NQ `mbp-1`. Estimate with `metadata.get_cost` first; the pay-as-you-go items are the warm-up and any Skylit-pulled day that has left Databento's included window: print the amount and ask the owner before buying. Decide one raw contract per session by the previous-session volume rule from the candidates' `ohlcv-1h` bars (§3.2; owner 2026-10-30: re-planned, the moved days' new-contract files pulled and their bars, the roll spreads and the dataset condition rebuilt). Build 1-minute and 5-minute bars from the trades. If the build later stalls for months, the owner may cancel the subscription and resubscribe before the step-18/20 top-ups and step 22 (newer months stay inside the included window).
8b. ✅ **GATE: level-mapping precision** (§5.1): using the step-7 Skylit day(s) and the step-8 NQ/ES trades, each chain meets the §5.1 pass conditions (owner 2026-10-08: (a) bias, (b) the chosen estimator within 10% of the best possible R, the alignment check in place of spot lag; the F1.6 step change and spot lag are diagnostics, and spot lag on a 1-minute day still decides the §3.1 delay), and the owner's Atlas replay check matches (the automated Atlas match is a regression check only, §13 Q8b-5). Must pass before step 11. (The backfill is already pulled for time reasons; a mapping problem changes only how the raw strikes are converted, never the pull.) The step-8 Databento job pulls the step-7 sample days first (their trades only: 8b needs no warm-up, since its realized vol uses the 15 minutes before each board from the same CME day), so this runs after the first night: `nqbot step8b precision` (per chain: median / p95 / p99 / max, by 30-minute time-of-day bucket and by tercile of trailing 15-minute realized vol of 1-minute futures log returns (owner 2026-10-05), the F1.2 rejection rate and `ratio_shift_day`s, and the stop floors per level source, `stop_buffer_floor`), `nqbot step8b spot-lag` (a diagnostic; on a 1-minute day it gives the §3.1 recommendation), the gate conditions `nqbot step8b bias`, `estimator` and `alignment` `--dates D1,…` (QQQ, SPY, SPX on development days; each day's crossings kept on disk; NDXP pending its method), `nqbot step8b recorded-day D [--with-prev]` after each owner-approved session recording (refuses until Databento has published the CME day, prints the price and pulls that day's NQ/ES trades at $0 only, fetches the free NDX/QQQ closes, fits every map's live content offset and alerts past floor(`asOf`) + 1 s, and scores both NDXP methods against the recording's real NDX at every board, plus (report only, owner 2026-10-11) at NDXP's relevant-node crossings; `--with-prev` also pulls the previous session's NQ trades for the basis method's settlement, after the owner's OK; owner OK for 2026-10-10: 10-08's NQ trades), then `nqbot step8b ndxp-select` (the leave-one-day-out NDXP choice, §13 Q8b-1), `nqbot step8b atlas --date D --minutes HH:MM,…` (the owner's Atlas table: relevant QQQ/SPY strikes with % of King, mapped level and band, and each time spot crossed the strike in the next 30 minutes, the futures price then next to the mapped level from the R in force), then `nqbot step8b report` → `<reports_dir>/step8b/mapping_gate.md`; the owner's Atlas check is the last condition. **✅ Passed 2026-10-28** (owner: Atlas replay check all match on the rebuilt sample-day and recorded-day tables; QQQ, SPY and SPX pass (a), (b) and the alignment check on the 22 development days with the frozen values; NDXP QQQ × k N = 20 by `ndxp-select`, floor 6.50; `config/gate_record.json`).
9. Owner adds `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` to `.env`. Claude Code: gameplan parser (file → Claude API → JSON per §5.6, §3.4). Validate on every archived gameplan; flag anything it can't parse. Report gameplan days per month. When gameplan zones and inputs are used: §5.6. 
10. Skylit backfill (runs right after step 7, §12 Phase C), RTH only on every weekday of the window; on a `calendar.yaml` holiday one 15-minute probe call is made and the day is pulled only if it returns boards, and early-close days are pulled 09:30 to `early_close_et` plus one probe window (days with no data are logged; holidays and early closes filter sessions after the pull), `maxExpirations=1`, `maxStrikes` = `skylit_max_strikes`, gamma only: all 12 months, **oldest day first** (≈ 163k credits: the plan's balance plus a top-up pack bought on SSD day, so it runs straight through, §3.1). Report any day already beyond the 365-day limit (wholly, or partly: its lost windows) and the share of sessions served at 1-minute resolution (`<reports_dir>/step10/coverage.md` and `.json`, rewritten after every chunk: counts per month, and dates listed only for development months and the days before month 1 (§7 rule 2b); the run commands and credit stop are in §3.1 Backfill); back it up as in §11 Storage. Also pull QQQ/SPY dark-pool prints for the same months plus `dp_lookback_days` before month 1 (oldest first; 31-day `date_start`/`date_end` calls once the step-7 darkpool check has matched them print by print, else, and always within a day of the 365-day reach, one date per call in case the same 365-day limit applies (§3.1 Dark pools); a date the API refuses or answers empty on a trading day is reported, never cached as an empty day, and the affected sessions are flagged `dp_partial`, §3.1), and Tempest daily vol history (`/v1/vol/history`, QQQ and SPY, plus NDXP/SPX and VIX if step 7 found them served, from `warmup_sessions` + 2 sessions before month 1 (covers either Tempest lag)).

### Phase D — Structure engine and backtest (1-second data, structure-only entry styles (c)/(d))
11. Claude Code: features — VWAP/bands, daily (18:00 ET) and weekly developing profiles (POC and LVNs as levels, VAH/VAL premium/discount context, §5.3), TPO single prints carried across sessions until filled, IB high/low and highs/lows with sweep/cross/flip and retested extremes, freshness, Tempest daily vol context and the expected move (§5.2, §5.4), adaptive opening range + fib golden zone (§5.8), overnight range, mapping, node tracker (King, % of King and node types computed from the per-strike values, §5.2; touch counts; velocity rebuilt from snapshots), regime, rug, pika, Trinity, rolling ceilings/floors, dark-pool levels, zone widths (§5.4b), confluence clustering, day type. With unit tests and a no-lookahead test.
12. ✅ **GATE: visual check (owner 2026-10-23: chart levels against Deepcharts; the Skylit side is gate 8b).** Atlas replays only from 2026-04-21 and the Skylit plan is paused after gate 8b, so the Skylit node mapping is not re-checked here: it is covered by gate 8b (the owner's Atlas check of the recorded-day tables, QQQ/SPY in full and NDXP/SPX as spot checks, plus the gate numbers). Step 12 keeps the node-type agreement report (the bot's types vs Skylit's `nodeType` on the step-7 sample, stored data), and its plots draw the Skylit nodes (NDXP included) as display only.
    - **Days (development months 1–6 only, §7 rule 2b):** 2026-03-06, 03-11, 03-17, 03-24, 03-26: the step-7 sample days with 2026-03-17 as the roll-week day (§3.2 Roll days) in place of 03-18 (owner 2026-10-30, §13 Q-frontroll: under the previous-session volume rule 03-17 is the first session of NQM6; 03-18 was v.0's). If none of the five has a 10:30 redraw or an orientation flip (§5.8), one day for each is added from months 1–6, picked by the calendar and the fib rule only (the earliest development day on which the step-11 code draws a redraw, or a flip), never by outcomes.
    - **Levels compared against Deepcharts:** VWAP (18:00 and 09:30 anchors) with its ±1σ/±2σ bands; daily and weekly POC, VAH and VAL; LVNs; single prints; IB high/low; the opening-range fib (anchors, 0.5, 0.79, orientation); PDH/PDL, overnight high/low, prior-week high/low.
    - **Readings (the fewest clock times that cover every level and the redraw/flip; values from data ≤ that time):** on each of the five days **09:46** (the fib at its 09:45 start) and **10:01** (every level: the static ones, IB and the fib just locked, and the developing VWAP, profiles, LVNs and single prints); on a redraw day **10:31** (IB high/low and fib after the redraw); on a flip day the first minute after the flip candle settles (fib). The previous session's POC, VAH and VAL (owner 2026-10-24) are static levels read at 10:01 against Deepcharts' previous-session profile, within the POC/VAH/VAL tolerance. An added redraw or flip day is read for IB and the fib only (09:46, 10:01, and 10:31 or the flip minute). About 12–16 readings in all, roughly 230–270 values, most of them in the five 10:01 readings.
    - **How:** for each day the bot writes the plot and a table of every level's value at those times (a CSV with an empty Deepcharts column); the owner reads Deepcharts at the same times and fills it in; the bot computes the differences.
    - **Tolerances (owner 2026-10-23):** Deepcharts' volume profile is read on Manual grouping, 4 ticks (1 pt) (owner 2026-11-07: Automatic changes the row size with the range and the zoom; §13 Q-step12 (3)). IB, the fib and the prior-day/overnight/week levels exact (within the 0.25 rounding); VWAP within 1 tick, its bands within 2; the daily, weekly and previous-session POC within ±2 pts (8 ticks; owner 2026-11-07: price chops around the POC rather than reacting at an exact price), VAH/VAL within ±4 ticks (owner 2026-11-07: one Deepcharts row at the Manual 4-tick grouping the readings use, so up to 3 ticks is row rounding); LVNs: the same LVN present within 4 ticks (kept, owner 2026-11-08); single prints within one TPO bin (`tpo_tick_pts`, 4 ticks at 1 pt; owner 2026-11-02: bin-edge conventions differ by one bin; `sp_min_ticks` counts these 1-pt bins, so 8 = 8 points, confirmed). A miss is either a Deepcharts setting (session hours, value-area %, tick grouping) or a definition difference; a definition difference goes to §13 for the owner.
    - **LVN lifetime (owner 2026-10-31):** the step-11 smoke run (2026-03-20, `lvn_smooth_ticks` 8) showed LVNs flickering: most qualify at only 1–2 profile instants (1–2 minutes), a few for 30–160 minutes. On 2–3 of the five days, the bot lists every daily and weekly LVN with when it first qualified, when it stopped, its lifetime and its price; the owner compares those with the LVNs Deepcharts draws over the same hours (are the long-lived ones the ones Deepcharts shows; do the 1–2 minute ones appear there at all). Report only, no parameter changes here: a mismatch goes to §13 for the owner (e.g. `lvn_smooth_ticks`, `lvn_max_pct` or a minimum lifetime are step-6 definitions).
    - **Gate:** every level within tolerance, or each miss explained and settled by the owner; and the node-type agreement report reviewed.

Row-size check: rule (i) (POC / VAH / VAL on the row low, clipped to the traded range) passes 34 of 39 POC / VAH / VAL outside the roll day (30 of 32 without the likely-typed readings), against 26 of 39 now. The 2026-03-11 golden zone is confirmed (§13 Q-step12 (1)); the owner's held/broke marker spot check on all five days: the events look right; the node answers became the step-13 variant `node_relevance_top_n` (§13 Q-step12 (2)); Q-density is closed (§13). Step 13 is next.

The ±0.5 bands stay fixed: nothing is frozen from it unless the owner changes them after reading it. Then the event builder. Before labeling, measure `exit_slippage_ticks` on months 1–4: for every stop candidate (§6.2) that price later traded through, a stop order of the default size (NQ-equivalent contracts, §5.5b) fills against the best bid/ask size from `mbp-1` at the first touch and any remainder at the prices of the following trades in sequence until filled (no depth data); slippage = fill − stop in ticks, against the trade; take the 95th percentile. Then the labeler (every approach and touch → features → labels for entry styles (c) and (d) (order-flow styles join at step 19) × every structural stop candidate × every target candidate with net RR ≥ `pair_build_min_rr` (start 0.8) at default costs, plus the fixed 1:2.5 baseline; each account's true 1:1 floor is applied in selection, the simulators and live). Build months 1–4 only first (13b; the slippage measurement and tuning need nothing else); output `events.parquet`, `pairs.parquet` (one row per event × entry style × firing n × order type × stop × target) and `paths.parquet`, each in its build key's own output folder under `data/processed/` (owner 2026-11-09, §13 Q-dryrun13 (7)). §7.1 Tuning; owner 2026-11-05, §13 Q-density (d)), then the execution parameters on the frozen events (style (d)'s order types include the passive limit at the zone's centre, `limit_center`, for a single Skylit node the mapped strike, scored like the others with the same time-to-live grid; if `node_relevance_top_n` = 5 won, scored as event-changing variants on the shared events instead; §7.1 Tuning; owner 2026-10-25, 2026-11-09, §13 Q-dryrun13 (4))), and then build events, labels and paths for the full window once, with the winners. **Flip checkpoint (owner 2026-10-23, §13 Q-flipretest):** with the months 1–4 events built, a count-only report (events only, never outcomes): crosses, reclaims, 5-minute flips, and post-cross retests (§5.2) before vs after a flip, per level-type group (GEX nodes, chart lines, highs/lows, gameplan zones), with the post-cross bucket sizes per group; any bucket under 100 is flagged. **Golden-zone count report (owner 2026-10-26, §13 Q-fibvariants; months 1–4, counts and structure-label hold rates only, no model change):** golden-zone events with their structure-label hold rates (§7.1 Structure label) and counts, split by `fib_flips` at the touch (0 vs ≥ 1) and by drawing (`fib_drawing`: running before 10:00 / locked at 10:00 / redrawn at 10:30) and by zone (`golden_zone_part`: shallow / deep; owner 2026-10-27, §13 Q-gzsplit), with the current design (flip on, redraw on); any bucket under 100 is flagged. **Counts on development months 1–6 (owner 2026-10-24; counts only, no outcomes, no model change; once the full window's events are built):** (a) **S&P-only node touches** (§13 Q-v2): each time ES touches a relevant SPY or SPX node (ES price inside the node's band ± `es_zone_proximity`, re-armed by the F5.6 rule with the ES-side values, F0.11) while no active NQ zone is within `zone_proximity` of the NQ price at that moment; reported by map (SPY, SPX), by node type (King / Gatekeeper / significant) and per session; (b) **pika clouds at touches:** how often a pika cloud (F2.14) is present at event touches, per map (QQQ, NDXP, SPY, SPX), with the number of touches; "present" is the F2.14 feature itself, read from its column so the report and the feature can't disagree: on QQQ/NDXP the cloud band overlaps the NQ zone (`pika_cloud_overlaps_zone`); on SPY/SPX the cloud band ± `es_zone_proximity` contains the ES price (`in_pika_cloud`, the Trinity rule; owner 2026-10-24). **Map-role conflicts (owner 2026-10-28, §13 Q-maprole; development months 1–6, counts and distributions only, no outcome-based change, no model change; once the full window's events are built):** on QQQ and NDXP, a case starts at the applied board (its `t_apply`) at which a relevant node is the confirmed ceiling held only by the F2.11 inside-band exception (the board spot above its strike, NQ has not traded beyond its band's upper edge since it was approached from below) while, by the F2.11 Q6 rule on the floor side (W = `roll_switch_min_sec`, the incumbent floor led less than `node_switch_incumbent_max_share` of the trailing W and doesn't lead the current board), it is the floor switch's choice, i.e. it becomes (or would become) the confirmed floor; the mirror for a floor held by the exception that becomes the confirmed ceiling. Under F2.11 as written the floor side does make that switch (§13 Q-maprole answer 0), so the case is the switch itself. For each case, what NQ did next, from the case start: left the band in the new role's direction (first trade beyond the upper edge for a new floor, below the lower edge for a new ceiling), with the time to leave and the furthest excursion beyond that edge within 30 minutes of the case start in EM (F0.8); crossed back out the old role's side (first trade beyond the other edge); or still inside the band at 30 minutes. Reported per map and per session, with the counts per outcome and the excursion distribution; it sizes the Q-v2 map-role events idea (§13 Q-v2 Idea 4). **King dominance (owner 2026-10-26; development months 1–6, counts and hold rates only, no model change):** the structure-label hold rate (§7.1 Structure label) of touches of King-labelled levels (a zone member that is the confirmed King at the touch, F2.16) split by `king_dominance` at the touch (< 1.2, 1.2–2, > 2), next to the same split for touches of relevant non-King nodes, with the count per bucket. **Day-type crosses (owner 2026-10-27, §13 Q-dtcross; development months 1–6, counts only, no outcomes, no model change):** the share of trend vs range days by the EOD label (§7.1) under three rules side by side, so the effect of each change is visible: (a) the old rule (a cross = a 1-minute close on the other side; VWAP crosses ≤ 2 and POC crosses ≤ 2), (b) settled 5-minute crosses with the POC condition (VWAP ≤ 2 and POC ≤ 2), (c) settled 5-minute crosses without POC (the rule now used); each with the day counts and the VWAP and POC cross distributions (1-minute and 5-minute), so the owner can revisit `trend_max_vwap_crosses` (2) before step 15. The POC limit 2 in (a) and (b) is the former `trend_max_poc_crosses`, a report constant only. **Day-type label vs Efficiency Ratio (owner 2026-10-28, §13 Q-er; development months 1–6, report only, no model or label change):** each session's full-day ER (RTH open to close on clock-aligned 5-minute bars, the `er_so_far` formula at the close) next to its trend/range EOD label under the current rule (§7.1), with the ER distribution per label (quantiles and a histogram), the overlap (how many range days have an ER above the trend days' median and how many trend days below the range days' median, and the AUC of ER for the label) and the day counts, so the owner can see how well the label agrees with ER before step 15. **Pairs and the event's own outcome (owner 2026-11-01, §13 Q-step11h):** no pair is built whose `decision_ts` is at or after its event's own structure-label resolution (the setup is over once the level held or broke); and an event's rows never see a retested extreme made by any event of its touch group (the group is the event's own, as for day memory), built with the pair builder and tested. **Step-12 zone corrections (owner 2026-11-03/04, §13 Q-step12a, Q-step12b):** built at the start of step 13, before tuning, in its single rebuild with a `features_version` bump, together with Q-step11h: level jumps end the old level and start a new one (the build reports level jumps by type, months 1–6); merged zones keep the older `zone_id` and the larger touch count; a crossed member's retest needs only its own re-arm (`rearm_dist` 10); the zone-book candidate-time fix (each pair also checked at both levels' step times between trades; the build reports how many sessions of months 1–6 had any zone difference from it); the zone hysteresis (`zone_join_buffer` 5, `zone_qualify_buffer` 5, `max_zone_width` unchanged as the hard cap); the relevance debounce (`node_relevance_min_sec` 60, `node_relevance_fast_pct` 0.40). `node_role_at_formation` is a variant (§7.1 Tuning); so is `node_relevance_top_n` {off, 5}, both of its values on the debounced relevance (§13 Q-step12 (2)). **Budget (owner 2026-11-06, §13 Q-step12):** re-estimated from the first months 1–4 rebuild; no variant is dropped or shortened for time without asking the owner; the re-estimate sets out, each with its cost, running around the clock on the build box, two rebuilds at once if memory and CPU allow, and screening on fewer months with the survivors confirmed on months 1–4. **Chosen (owner 2026-11-08, §13 Q-step13plan (7)):** around the clock on the build PC; two rebuilds at once not now (a code change for about 20%); screening rejected (noisier, changes §7.1, saves about 9%); reusing pass-1 checkpoints for variants that don't change carried state is measured after the first rebuild and proposed only with pass 2's end-state check. **Hold distance (owner 2026-11-06, §13 Q-density (c)):** in the same rebuild the structure label's `hold_reaction_pts` becomes 30 (the 30-minute window and the cross rule unchanged); the outcome-history features follow it through `history_hold_pts` 30 (owner 2026-11-06): freshness `holds_so_far` and `pending_tests` (F5.11), day memory `held_today` / `broke_today` / `timeout_today` / `held_rate_today`, golden-zone history `gz_prev_held` / `gz_prev_broke` / `gz_prev_pending` / `gz_last_run_pts` (F10); the pair cut at label resolution (Q-step11h (1)) and F3.8 retested extremes keep 20 through `pair_end_hold_pts` and `retest_hold_pts` (20 each); the build report gives the held / broke / timeout shares at 30 beside 20 (months 1–6). **Months 1–6 build counts (owner 2026-11-08, §13 Q-step13plan (2)):** the level jumps by type, the sessions with a zone difference from the candidate-time fix and the shares at 30 beside 20 come from the 13d full-window build; there is no extra months 5–6 build.

Claude Code: structure and day-type models, the zone-width report (§5.4b; no gameplan report before step 20: months 1–9 have no gameplan days), then the pair scorer over every stop × target candidate (§7.1); the fold schedule of §7 rule 2 (cross-fitted lower-model predictions for months 1–4, folds 7–9); LightGBM with logistic baseline; breakeven re-simulation (§6.4); purged walk-forward; calibration; prop simulator with bootstrap (§6.7); threshold sweep (§6.5b); report (including stopped-then-target and extension-past-target tables). It passes if its pooled held-out log loss is lower, with a by-day bootstrap 95% interval of the difference excluding 0, and it is no worse on the retests after a flip. The sealed months are **not** scored here: they are scored once, at step 20, by the model that will go live. If not passing: improve features/definitions (hand-made work on development months 1–6 only) — do not buy anything else yet.

### Phase E — Order flow (L1 only: trades and `mbp-1`, already pulled at step 8)

Real-data checks and plots of the detectors use development months 1–6 only. **Side-N checklist (owner 2026-10-29, §13 Q-sideN; none may be skipped):** the tests (1) a 200-lot side-N print at 10:00 is not a big trade and leaves the 10:00 slot threshold unchanged (F9.2), (2) `delta_ratio_w` with B 6, A 2, N 4 = 0.5 (F9.10), (3) F9.3's B 5, A 2, N 4, B 1 → delta +4 (the bar test, B 5, A 2, N 4 → volume 11, delta +3, exists since 2026-10-29); (4) the F9.8 top-of-book reader skips `mbp-1` records with action N (standalone F_LAST records from Databento's 2026-08-08 normalization; the completeness reader already does, with a test); and in the order-flow report each session's side-N share of front-contract RTH volume, NQ and ES, with sessions above `side_n_max_share` flagged for the owner, and each session's unexplained-outside share (report only, no threshold; owner 2026-10-30).

These months join the sealed set (§7 rule 2b): they are built into events and labels but appear in no report until step 20.
19. Claude Code: trigger model; add entry styles (a) and (b), VAH/VAL area events (§5.4) and the absorption stop candidate (§6.2 item 5) to the events and labels (features version bump, §5.0); compare all four entry styles (a)–(d); retrain the pair scorer with trigger inputs. Order-flow detector parameters and the order type for styles (a)/(b) are tuned and frozen on months 1–4 first (§7.1); the trigger model follows the §7 rule 2 schedule (cross-fitted for months 1–4). Then re-run the full step-15 pipeline (breakeven choice and relabel, calibration, ablation, prop simulator, threshold sweep → `models/profiles.yaml`). Entry styles, breakeven and thresholds are chosen inside each fold (§7 rule 2); the ablation is reported on months 7–9 and the entry-style comparison on months 1–6; the sealed and top-up months are not scored here. This applies to whichever model goes live, with or without order flow.
20. ✅ **GATE: order flow adds lift over structure alone.** Ablation = full system (per-fold style choice from (a)–(d), plus order-flow inputs) vs structure-only = exactly the fallback system (per-fold style choice from (c)–(d), no area events, no stop candidate 5, no trigger model or order-flow inputs). Pass if the one-sided 90% lower bound of the **total-R-per-month** difference (paired day-block bootstrap: both systems resampled on the same days) is > 0, and the three-loss rate stays ≤ `default_max_three_loss_rate` (at `default_risk_usd`, §6.5b). If not, set `enabled_entry_styles: [c, d]` and drop area events, stop candidate 5, the trigger model and the order-flow inputs, keep Databento Live for NQ/ES trades, NQ `mbp-1` (fill simulation and step-24 scoring) and MNQ trades (fill inference) only (no order-flow features); the NQ/ES trades feed the structure features, which the live engine needs. Either way, every step-16 criterion must also hold on months 7–9 for the model that will go live. **Then top up any month completed since step 18 and, once, score the sealed set** (months 10–12 plus all top-up months, together, with fold 10 of that model, §7 rule 2; report the total and each block; fold 10 also scores the structure-only and fixed 1:2.5 arms, and their sealed differences are reported). Pass rule: §7 rule 2b. If it fails, go back and apply the re-seal rule. After a pass, run the gameplan check (§5.6) on the now-scored months; then the production fit runs (with the gameplan inputs if the check passed and ≥ `gameplan_min_days` exist).

### Phase F — Live auto-execution on a Tradovate demo account
21. 💳 Owner subscribes to **TradersPost Starter (~$49/month)**, connects a **Tradovate demo (sim) account as a broker connection** (TradersPost's own paper mode can't use Tradovate data, so use a real Tradovate demo login), creates a strategy + webhook (strategy subscription: **Allow signal override** on, adding off, no default stop-loss or take-profit, no order-type override), and adds its URL to `.env` as `TP_WEBHOOK_<ACCOUNT>`. Claude Code builds `clients/traderspost.py` (build and POST signals: entry bracket, breakeven, exit; retries for `exit` and `breakeven` only — entries are never resent (§10.3); an `extras.order_id` already sent (journal, §9) is refused; `exit` / `breakeven` are exempt; error handling), `live/sizing.py` (§6.1), `live/execution.py` (send entry, watch price for breakeven, log everything), the hard limits from §10.3 and the kill switch, with unit tests on a mocked webhook. Then it runs the nine verification tests in §3.5 (the owner also sets up a second demo account as a TradeSyncer follower for test 9), measures end-to-end latency (§3.5) and sets `order_latency_sec` to its 95th percentile. If it is above the 1.0 s placeholder, the **execution-change re-run** follows: labels are rebuilt, the execution parameters are re-frozen on months 1–4, per-fold steps (1)–(4) are re-run for folds 7–9, which must still meet the step-16 criteria and the step-20 full-vs-structure-only test (else the fallback system; if the step-16 criteria fail, stop before step 24 or 27 and ask the owner), and the production fit re-runs (not re-scored; steps 24–25 validate it, §7 rule 2); the same applies if step 22 has to raise `tempest_lag_sessions` (§5.2). It also records whether the demo fills a resting limit on a touch: if so, for sim accounts (per account: `accounts.yaml → sim_fills`, default true; all of the owner's accounts are Tradovate sim) the live fill inference treats a touch as filled (no "maybe filled"), and the simulator used for the step-25/28 comparisons does the same (training labels keep trade-through). The owner enters the demo account with the same rules, caps, commissions and flat-by as the prop account planned for step 27. On a `demo: true` account, reaching the profit target or the floor starts a new simulated evaluation at the next session: the bot posts it, and the owner clicks **New evaluation** on Live Status (it records the current balance and new floor as the evaluation start in `account_state.json`; not a sweep-relevant edit). Demo trades keep counting toward step 24, and the step-25 simulator interval uses the same restarts. ✅ **GATE: all nine §3.5 tests pass before step 24, and the SSD passes a sustained-load test with zero disconnects before any live trading (owner 2026-10-11):** at least 2 hours of continuous mixed reads and writes on the SSD on the live machine, on AC and with the screen off, through the live port and cable; pass = no `disk` 51, no `Ntfs` 140, no new mount of the SSD's volume (`Ntfs` 98) in the System log and no line in `ssd_events.log` for the whole run (§11 Storage; the 2026-10-08..11 drops, §13).
22. Claude Code: live engine (asyncio), with the pre-open warm-up and restart journal of §9: Skylit `/v1/stream` (QQQ, NDXP, SPY, SPX; hourly reconnect with `Last-Event-ID`) + Databento Live (NQ and ES trades, NQ `mbp-1`, MNQ trades; intraday-replay reconnect, §9) → same feature code → models → chosen entry/stop/target/size → orders through TradersPost (auto). Risk module, logging, reconnect handling, session schedule, the guard process, the evening archive job and the Task Scheduler pre-open entry (§9). Also record when Tempest's previous-session row first appears (§5.2). Live Status page in the Control Panel; Discord notifications, health warnings, end-of-day summary and all §9c commands. Runs on the owner's own computer.
23. ✅ **GATE: replay test.** Claude Code — feed a historical day through the live engine (orders to a mocked webhook); it must produce the same event ids and decisions as the backtest, with features equal within 1e-9; a second run drops the Databento feed for 30 seconds and a third for 10 minutes mid-session (intraday replay mocked from the cached files; each drop run is compared with the policy simulator replayed with the same outage injected — blocked from drop start + `feed_stale_sec` to recovery + `feed_recovery_sec` — and features, events and decisions must be identical, with no entries inside the blocked span); two more runs mock a Skylit silence longer than `skylit_stale_sec` filled from `/v1/historical/range` (§3.1) and an engine killed mid-session and rebuilt from the journal (§9): features must be identical to the backtest from fill/rebuild + `feed_recovery_sec`, with no entries before that; the engine's clock is injectable, so in replay local time = `ts_event` + the measured receive lag and every wall-clock deadline (time-to-live, `entry_end`, flat-by, blackout starts) matches the backtest; a fourth replays the busiest development-month session (e.g. a CPI day, 09:30–10:30) at real-time speed on the laptop with the UI open, and the 99th-percentile processing lag must stay below `feed_lag_max_ms` (else the order-flow detectors move to their own process).
24. Run auto-execution on the demo account during the session until 100+ live events are scored and 30+ demo trades are done (typically 4–8 weeks). Every decision-point candidate, traded or not, is scored on live ticks with the training-label (trade-through) fill rules whatever `sim_fills` says; only the demo-trade comparisons use `sim_fills`. Owner does review round 2 on the live trades. Fill and order reconciliation is **automatic where possible**: step 21 checks whether TradersPost offers a trade log/API; if not, the owner uploads Tradovate's daily **Orders** report (all orders, including cancelled) and **Fills** report on Live Status, only during validation (steps 24 and 27; no automated web download, no Tradovate login stored); afterwards it is optional, since the morning balance confirmation already corrects the bot's estimates. Claude Code also compares the live pre-open dark-pool top-3 lists with the backtest's reconstruction for the same days (§3.1) and, once those days are pulled at the step-27 top-up, the Skylit boards logged live with that history. Claude Code builds the importer: it checks that every entry leg had a stop and a target created with it, measures slippage and send-to-fill latency (send log vs order/fill time), and imported results override the bot's inferred fills, P&L and caps. No prop account trades before gate 25 passes (owner: demo first).
25. ✅ **GATE: over those live events and demo trades, calibration-in-the-large holds (the mean predicted P of the scored best pairs lies inside the two-sided day-block bootstrap 90% interval (5th–95th percentile) of their realized hit rate; the full step-16 test once ≥ 500 live events exist); the mean R of the demo trades stays inside the policy simulator's two-sided 90% interval for that trade count (built from the out-of-fold trades of months 7–9 and the sealed set); no missing brackets; entry slippage ≤ `max_entry_slip_ticks`; stop slippage: if the measured 95th percentile (from the step-24 imported fills; needs ≥ 20 stop fills, else stop fills plus market exits — flat-by, news, owner — never target fills) exceeds `exit_slippage_ticks`, set it to the measured value, run the step-21 execution-change re-run at the new cost (net RR, `R_be`; an execution-only fix: steps 24–25 don't repeat); daily caps never breached (no entry sent after a daily stop and none sized above the remaining allowance at the configured slippage; overshoot from slippage beyond that is reported, not a failure); `order_latency_sec` and `map_latency_sec` re-checked against live (§5.5b, §3.1).** If it fails: execution-only fixes → repeat steps 24–25 with no refit; fixes to features or model code → the step-27 re-check, then the production fit, then repeat steps 24–25.

### Phase G — Prop accounts
26. 🛠 Done 2026-10: MyFundedFutures confirmed a TradersPost bot is allowed if supervised. That check covered MFFU firm-wide; the Pro 50k type (added 2026-10-08) was not checked on its own, so re-read the Pro terms on automation before going live on a Pro account. Before going live, re-read MyFundedFutures' current automation rules for the exact account types. Lucid (owner 2026-11-04): automation and trade copiers are explicitly permitted (the owner has the screenshot); re-read Lucid's terms for the Flex account type before going live on it, including the eight Q-flexfarm items (owner 2026-11-10, §13 Q-flexfarm (5)): payout consistency on the funded Flex, what counts as a profitable day, prohibited practices, the flipping rule, the payout formula, the floor-lock triggers, the scaling-tier timing and soft DLL, automation / copiers and the 5-account limit; the farming challenger's $110 day stop is revised to Lucid's confirmed counting rule.
27. First pull every complete month since step 20 (as in step 18). If round-2 fixes were applied and not already re-checked after step 25, re-check first: the fixed model's walk-forward over every scored month from 7 onward (excluding live months used for the round-2 fixes) must meet the step-16 criteria, and the first complete month after the fixes are frozen (the demo keeps running meanwhile) is scored once by fold j, where j is that month, with the §7 rule 2b pass rule. A round-2 fix to features or model code repeats steps 24–25 on demo first (as on the gate-25 fail path), and any feature or engine code change after step 23 — here or at a later retrain — re-passes the gate-23 replay before it trades. Then connect **one** prop account; auto-execution with the normal sizing (§6.1: NQ and/or MNQ, whatever fits the stop and risk best), owner supervising, for 2+ weeks; record whether the firm auto-flattens at its session close. Daily orders/fills reconciliation as in step 24, and every limit the bot counted as filled on a touch (`sim_fills`) is checked against the fills import: gate 28 requires them to have filled, else that account gets `sim_fills: false` and its sweep is re-run; compare fills, slippage and R against the demo and the backtest.
28. ✅ **GATE: prop execution matches the demo results (no missing brackets, the 95th percentile of prop stop fills ≤ the configured `exit_slippage_ticks` (with fewer than 20 stop fills, gate 25's fallback set: stop fills plus market exits, never target fills; judged once ≥ 20 such fills exist, and scaling waits until then), every entry fill ≤ `max_entry_slip_ticks`, daily caps never breached as defined at step 25).** Then scale to more accounts (before the first prop TradeSyncer follower copies live trades, repeat test 9 on it): TradeSyncer followers are sized by their TradeSyncer multiplier; only extra TradersPost connections are sized by their own risk.

### Phase H — Ongoing
29. Retrain every `retrain_every_months` (§7.1) on the new months rebuilt from pulled history (live logs feed the fill, slippage and drift checks), judged as in §7.1 Retraining; refresh `config/calendar.yaml`; review rounds as needed; re-run breakeven simulation; pull each new complete month as in step 18 (Skylit, dark-pool prints, Tempest, Databento NQ + ES `ohlcv-1s`/`trades`, NQ `mbp-1`, MNQ `trades`). The home PC remains the only fallback if the laptop proves too slow (§11).
