## 13. Open questions

**Checked by Claude Code during the build (no owner input needed):**
- **Q-step11d (step 11 part 2, answered by the owner 2026-10-31; feature_spec F0.1, F2.6, F2.23, F5.14, F5.16; `features_version` 31):** (1) S&P nodes have their own F2.6 state on ES (read by Trinity and `es_at_level`); their `flip_margin` is the Nasdaq chain of the same kind: SPY nodes use `flip_margin.qqq_node`, SPX nodes `flip_margin.ndxp_node`, each × ES ÷ NQ at the open (F0.11; before 09:30 the previous session's ratio); no new keys. (2) Multi-expiration walls (F2.23) are re-chosen at every t: the strikes come only from the snapshot in force at t, and above / below and the `mx_wall_reach_em` reach are judged with the price and R at t; a side is null when no strike qualifies (a wall fixed at the in-force time could end up on the wrong side of price after a run). (3) `rel_delta` (F5.16) divides each side's delta by its aggressor volume (B + A): side-N trades are left out of both, as for `delta_ratio_w` (Q-sideN); null when a side has no aggressor volume. (4) NDXP's spot for every per-board rule (F2.1 two clocks: node types, ceiling / floor candidates, rug, lean sides, the regime band) is the spot of the QQQ board in force × k (the frozen QQQ × k, N = 20), in history and live alike (rule 4): history's NDXP `spot` is an NQ futures price (Q8b-1). (5) An NDXP board's R = the QQQ R in force at the NDXP board's own `t_apply` ÷ k; with no QQQ board applied within min(`skylit_map_stale_sec`, `ratio_gap_reset_sec`) of it, the NDXP board is not applied (no NDXP content offset is needed). (6) Trinity's Q19 entry rule applies on every map: a QQQ / NDXP node with no role yet counts in ρ when NQ entered its band (± `zone_proximity`) from ρ's side, as a SPY / SPX node does with ES. (7) Trinity's overlap search skips the moments τ inside the chain's dislocation block (they never count as an overlap; moments outside it count normally); a map blocked at t is null (F1.8); and if no overlap is found at any unblocked moment but the chain was blocked for part of [touch, t], the map's result is null, not false (the blocked stretch could have hidden one): false only when the whole window was unblocked, the three-way rule of `es_at_level`. **Follow-ups after the independent review (owner 2026-10-31, `features_version` 32):** (8) an SPY / SPX overlap needs NQ inside a QQQ / NDXP node at the same moment, so the (7) null also applies when the Nasdaq leg's chain (QQQ, so NDXP) was blocked for part of [touch, t] and no overlap was found. (9) A multi-expiration root is stale (null) when its `asOf` is before the previous session's RTH close − 1 minute (15:59; 12:59 after a 13:00 early close), not a fixed 15:59 (which would have nulled 2025-12-01 and 2025-12-26). (10) Air pockets (F2.15): 'the next relevant node ahead' is the nearest relevant node whose band lies entirely beyond price in the trade direction (behind: the mirror), so the event's own node doesn't cap the gap; the gap runs to that band's near edge. (11) Node freezes (F2.4): a 1-minute or flip bar that formed partly while the node was frozen (not relevant or missing) is not judged; the next full bar is.
- **Q-step11e (step 11 part 2 review, 2026-10-31; an independent agent checked the step against CLAUDE.md and this SPEC):** **Answered by the owner 2026-11-01 (Trinity and relevance):** Trinity needs a relevant node in the role: an overlap found at a moment when the node was relevant keeps counting at a later t (the [touch, t] rule, Q18), but a frozen node (not relevant, or missing from the board, F2.1 / F2.4) never creates an overlap at moments while it is frozen. The build already did this (`SkylitSession._inside` drops frozen moments for the Nasdaq and S&P nodes and the `es_at_level` node zones); now pinned by `test_owner_frozen_moments_never_create_trinity_overlaps`; no output changed, `features_version` stays 32. **Note for step 22 (live engine memory):** the Skylit engine peaks at 3.24 GB for one day (`step11 skylit-day 2026-03-20`, mostly the per-strike row arrays of the four maps); at step 22 check the live engine's memory on the 16 GB laptop and cut the per-strike arrays (float32, fewer kept columns, or a trailing window) if needed. The review's findings: fixed and tested (`tests/test_step11_part2_review.py`): the rolls driver took transitions inside a dislocation block that started between two of the map's boards (NDXP's blocks come from QQQ's board times); the beach-ball outputs weren't null inside a block; a reclaim judged at a block end recorded depth 0; a stretch with the strike missing from the board counted as 'back inside' in `bb_seconds_beyond`; a roll's `new_growth_pct` was lost when the new node was below spot on the base board (the rolls side now sees the whole board's values); `minutes_since_relevant` restarted on a board that just didn't carry the strike (F2.1 keeps the state); `in_pika_cloud_member` and Trinity's `aligned_has_pika` meant any positive relevant node, now membership of a cloud of at least `pika_cloud_min_nodes` (F2.14); Trinity returned false, not null, for a map with no applied board yet (F0.7: e.g. SPX / NDXP in the opening skip, or NDXP all day without k, which also makes `maps_with_node_here` null then); `pocket_ahead` was 0 where Q15 says null (no relevant node ahead and no pocket within the reach). Also: every R-dependent output is null on the closed block span [start, end], the guard's own convention (`disloc_guard.in_block`, used for `tradeable`); an S&P map refuses a missing ES ÷ NQ ratio instead of defaulting it to 1. Built as read, kept unless the owner says otherwise: `pika_cloud_edge_dist` is 0 inside a cloud; `sp_path` is null while either S&P chain is blocked; every pika output is null in a block (choosing the nearest cloud needs R); a tie of first-overlap moments in `_aligned_stats` goes to the lower strike; `above_mass_change_roll` is also given for a paused episode; a side's multi-expiration walls are null while any chain mapping a root of that side is blocked; `fomc_day` is the rate-decision day; `pocket_behind` with no node behind uses the same reach as ahead.
- **Q-step11f (step 11 part 3, built 2026-11-01). ✅ Answered by the owner 2026-11-01 (`features_version` 33):** (1) day memory keeps the overnight touches (consistent with levels tracked from their creation); (15) changed: the feature build builds the warm-up sessions fully (`features build-warmup`, the job's chunk `p0`, `state/state_warmup.json`), so month 1 doesn't start with every level falsely fresh; only 17 warm-up sessions are stored before month 1 (2025-09-08 to 09-30: the Databento warm-up was sized when the window started on 10-07), so the first is folded carry-only (nothing before it) and the other 16 are built fully, without Skylit maps (boards start 2025-10-07; maps are null there, as on 2025-10-01 to 10-06, flagged `skylit_missing`); the smoke command keeps the carry-only replay unless `--full-warmup`; (3) the release rule and (4) the zone losing its last member: approved as built; (16) the checkpoint stays JSON and §11 says so; every other reading (2), (5)-(14): approved as built. **Gaps (owner 2026-11-01):** the Q-er inputs are built now in the day-type rows (and every event row as `dt_*`), LightGBM day-type challenger candidates only, never in the 8 inputs or the label: `er_so_far`, `er_<w>` and `vr_prior` (`features/daytype_rows.py`, tests and the no-lookahead check; `carry.rth5` keeps each session's RTH 5-minute log returns, never roll-shifted); the `er_<w>` windows are `er_windows_min` [30, 60, 120] (the Q-v2 Idea 3 example set, fixed now so this run needs no rebuild; `er_bar_minutes` 5, `vr_q` 6, `vr_sessions` 10, all owner rules in features.yaml, never tuned); `er_<w>` is null until w minutes of 5-minute bars have closed since the open. Dark pools: the feature build refuses to run while `dp_in_model` is true (dark-pool levels are not wired into the zones; they are added and tested at the later retrain that turns them on). `features/block_state.py` is no longer used by any engine (`level_state` blocks and freezes replaced it): left in place, a cleanup after the step-16 gate. The readings as built were: part 3 (zones F5.3-F5.5, touches F5.6-F5.7, groups F5.10, structure label F11.4, freshness F5.11, day memory F5.12, makeup F5.13, `es_at_level` on merged ES zones F5.16, day-type rows F3.10/F11.6, gameplan features F8, the feature-build job) is built with these readings where the text leaves a choice: (1) **day memory** (F5.12) counts the CME trading day's events (overnight touches included), not RTH only; (2) a level with **no role yet** (F2.6 step 1) is an active zone member (F5.3: 'not crossed'); a provisional role crossed back is not; (3) the **merge release** (F5.3 (b)) is literal: on a width or qualify failure the member farthest from the founding anchors goes first, whichever member failed; when no founding member is active any more, the distance is from the centre of the zone's current members; (4) a zone that **loses its last member** counts as broke if a departing member left by a cross against the event's role at that instant, else the label is a timeout (F5.4/F11.4); (5) the **primary member** of F2.6b's zone forms = the member in a post-cross role with the most recent cross; (6) **next zone above / below** (F5.13) is the gap from the event's zone edge to the nearest other level zone's edge (containers not counted); (7) gameplans are known from 09:30 (their zones exist and invalidations are checked from then); with several gameplan zones holding the price, the features read the event's own gameplan zone, else the first one whose side matches the trade, else the first; (8) **rotations** (F3.10): the first move of `rotation_min_pts` × ATR from the first RTH trade sets the swing direction and is not counted; (9) `new_highs` / `new_lows`: the first RTH bar counts for neither; `time_above_vwap_frac` is over the seconds since the first RTH trade; (10) **touch vs a zone moving onto price** (§5.4): a touch needs the previous trade, judged against the same current edges, outside the touch band; if it was inside too the zone moved onto price: disarmed, no touch; (11) two touches of one zone in the same second (possible only in a 15-point swing inside one second) give the second `event_id` a `.1` suffix so order ids stay unique; (12) labels of touches at or after the flat-by (and overnight) time out at min(touch + `hold_window_min`, the CME day's end); (13) F3.8 retest candidates are each session's own registry highs/lows (ON, PDH/PDL, prior RTH, prior week, IB, price = the band centre), kept `retest_lookback_sessions` sessions and roll-shifted with retested extremes; (14) `tradeable` (F5.7): golden zones from 09:45, all others from 10:00, before `entry_end`, outside news blackouts and outside a dislocation block of a chain with a node in the zone (containers: their confluence nodes); (15) the feature build's first session starts from a carry-only warm-up replay, so its multi-day touch history (freshness of weekly POC/LVNs, prior-week levels, single prints) starts empty at month 1 (the alternative, a full part-3 build of the 21 warm-up sessions, adds about 40 minutes); (16) the pass-1 checkpoint is JSON (`state/state_<date>.json`), the §11 text says parquet. Smoke run `step11 build-day 2026-03-20`: 294 events (292 level, 2 golden), 129 tradeable, labels held 220 / broke 55 / timeout 19, built twice from the same state with identical end states; 105 s build, 3 s rows, 4.29 GB peak for one build.
- **Q-step11g (independent review of step 11 part 3, 2026-11-01; fixes built, `features_version` 34). ✅ Answered by the owner 2026-11-01 (`features_version` 35):** **(1) one retested extreme per price:** candidates within `retest_tol_ticks` of each other are one extreme (the tolerance that defines a retest, not the exact tick; owner's lean, recommended: two highs a tick or two apart are the same extreme for traders, and an exact-tick rule would still make several levels from one held touch), and the most recent session's occurrence is kept, so its age limit runs from the latest session; taken newest first, a candidate within the tolerance of one already kept is dropped (`session_levels.dedupe_candidates`, which also settles chains of near prices the same way every time); no retested extreme is made within the tolerance of a high/low level (ON, PDH/PDL, prior RTH, prior week, IB) or a retested extreme that exists at its creation time, nor of one accepted at the same instant; tested (`tests/test_step11_part3_review.py`); the number of sessions that made the extreme at that price is a Q-v2 idea. **(2) freshness and day memory at any decision time** count only the touches before the event's own touch (and their labels if resolved by t), never the event's own touch or label; the golden-zone history likewise. A retested extreme made by the event's own held label could still reach its rows (it can merge the event's zone with another and change later labels), so from that level's creation the event's rows are read from the session built without it (`SessionFeatures(..., exclude_source=event)`, made only when such a row is asked for: the feature build's rows are at the touch, always before it; step 13's `decision_ts` rows use it when needed). Tests (`tests/test_feature_lookahead.py`): every event's rows at its touch and at later decision times (also after its own label resolved) are unchanged when its own label is replaced, and a source event's rows equal those of a build where its retested extreme is never made. **(3)** `es_at_level` stays null on the 4 sessions without Skylit boards. **The review:** a fresh reviewer checked commit 93bc481 against CLAUDE.md and the step-11 sections. **Fixed, each with a regression test (`tests/test_step11_part3_review.py`, `tests/test_feature_build.py`, `tests/test_feature_lookahead.py`):** (a) the touch files kept only the columns of each day's first row (pyarrow takes the schema from row 0, and the first event is usually overnight), so the golden-zone history and nodes inside (F10), the expected-move lines and other RTH-only columns were lost: rows now take every column any row has, null where a row lacks it; (b) a VWAP line (a moving band) was a zone member only from its first role event, sometimes hours after it existed (its anchor instant has no trade, and activity was checked only then): it is active from its first value, as Q-step11f (2) approved; (c) `zone_tests_so_far` counted carried touches of an earlier session's zone with the same name (zone ids restart at z1 each session, F5.5): it counts today's touches only; (d) while a far-side break was unresolved, the touch scan jumped over the near side too, so a near-side re-arm and the touch after it were missed (F5.6 holds back only the far side); (e) a count whose values were all whole one day (an int column) and fractional the next would not concatenate: numbers are stored as float32 (the time columns stay int64) and day files are read with type promotion; (f) the F3.8 retest loop accepted every retest a pass found at once, so a later retest whose touch would not exist once an earlier retest was in force could stay (not what one pass in time order, live, would do; rule 4): each pass now accepts only the earliest new retest(s) (one pass per distinct creation time; 2025-10-01: 3 passes, about 20 s each on the busy 2026-03-20), and a retested level keeps its id across a roll (the candidate's id is fixed when it is made); (g) the plan could chain states across a gap (months `1-4,7`, or a plan starting later reusing month 1's warm-up): plans must be consecutive ledger months, the warm-up file is named by the plan's first session (`state_warmup_<date>.json`), and every session checks its starting state is the state after the planned session before it; (h) pass 2's resume took the first parquet file as done, so a stop between files left the day without its day-type or outcome file: done = the summary's pass-2 entry, written last; (i) at a pair's `decision_ts`, the golden-zone touch history counted the event itself and later touches (F10 says earlier touches): earlier touches only; (j) the zone book could miss a merge at the first trade after a level's span started (a relation change from the state just before it); (k) smaller: ES zones and the golden zone's S&P node count use ES ÷ NQ at the open (F0.11) also on days without Skylit boards, not 1.0 / the ratio at t; `king_present` / `gatekeeper_present` are null without Skylit (unknown, not 'no King'); two gameplan entries with one label stay two containers; the keep lengths of the carried 5-minute returns and minute volumes follow `vr_sessions` / `vol_baseline_sessions` (twice each, unchanged at today's values); children write their output to a log file per session (`logs/` in the build folder) instead of a pipe; a memory check before the warm-up chunk; the per-session memory estimate 4.5 GB (4.29 GB measured); the dark-pool refusal is a clean refusal at the plan. The no-lookahead test now carries touch records under a zone id today's book reuses, and retest candidates that create retested extremes at several times, and a planted lookahead (day memory counting unresolved labels) must fail it. **The questions as asked:** (1) **duplicate retested extremes (F3.8):** every session lists its own highs/lows as candidates, so one extreme appears several times (the prior-week high once per session of the week; PDH and the prior RTH high often the same price), and one held touch creates a `retested_extreme` per copy (on synthetic data 7 at one price), inflating `stack_count`, `n_retested_extreme` and freshness. Proposal: one candidate per price (rounded to the tick), keeping the newest session (the longest life), and no candidate at a price that is already a registry high/low that day; as built until answered; (2) **freshness and day memory at a pair's `decision_ts`** (F5.11, F5.12): as built they count every touch before t and every label resolved by t, so the event's own touch (and its label, if resolved by then) counts at its later pairs. Proposal: count only touches before the event's own touch, and labels resolved by t of those, so `decision_ts` rows describe the same history as the touch row plus what resolved since; as built until answered (step 13 calls it first); (3) **`es_at_level` without Skylit boards** (2025-10-01 to 10-06, 4 sessions): null as built (the overlap search runs on the board apply times), although ES chart levels exist; proposal: keep it null there (flagged `skylit_missing`).
- **[13-0 items built 2026-11-09, §13 Q-step13-0] Q-step11h (second independent review, of a10816a and 72e62f7, 2026-11-01; fixes built, `features_version` 36). ✅ (1) and (2) answered by the owner 2026-11-01 (both for step 13; neither changes the feature build, whose rows are at the touch), confirmed again 2026-11-02 after the feature build finished, in the owner's words: (a) step 13 builds no pairs for an event after its own structure label has resolved; (b) the whole touch group counts as the event's own (same rule as day memory), so no touch-group mate can reach an event's later rows:** **(1) no pair after its event's own structure label has resolved:** once the level has held or broken the setup is over (live, the bot wouldn't enter it then either), and live trading never needs a mid-session rebuild; the step-13 pair builder drops every pair whose `decision_ts` is at or after its event's own label resolution (the session built without the event's own retested extreme stays only as a guard); **(2) the whole touch group is the event's own:** events touched by the same trade are one setup (weighted as one, traded at most once, §5.4), and day memory already counts only touches before the event's own touch; so a retested extreme made by the held label of any event of the touch group never reaches the event's rows, like its own (`SessionFeatures.world` / `_own` over the group, built at step 13 with a test). **Fixed, each with a regression test:** (a) **pass 2 could not write any session's outcomes file:** a10816a's float32 cast of integer columns met `t_resolved` (a nanosecond time, beyond float32's exact range) and raised, so the run would have stopped at the first pass-2 session after the whole of pass 1; integers now stay int64 (only floats become float32), the test job's fake outcome rows have the real shape, and the smoke run writes the three pass-2 files of its day the same way (`write_day_files`, a temporary folder on the SSD) and reads them back; (b) **a re-plan could reuse checkpoints and rows of another chain** (e.g. months 4-12, then 1-12: months 4-12 kept states descending from the warm-up before month 4): every chunk id now names the chain's first session and every pass-2 chunk its first and last session (the runner skips ids it has finished), every checkpoint and pass-2 summary records the digest of the state it was built from, and a stale one is built again (pass 2 refuses a checkpoint from another start); the build key now also covers the code that makes the outputs (`code_digest`: `features/`, `session_build`, `feature_build`, the calendar, the stores and the gameplan parser, line endings normalised), so a code change needs a re-plan like a config change; (c) **the candidate merge depended on the order sessions arrived in** (A, B, C each 3 ticks apart: merged at once {A, C}, merged at each checkpoint {C}): the checkpoint now keeps the candidates as they come (within the lookback) and they are merged within `retest_tol_ticks` only when used, so the result is the one Q-step11g (1) defines whatever the order; (d) an event's zone edges and members after its own retested extreme now come from the same session as its rows (`SessionFeatures.world`), and at most `MAX_ALT_BUILDS` (2) such sessions are kept; (e) a moving band's relation flips after a parameter change are judged with the parameters then in force (a latent case: no book has both today); (f) the own-label test also replaces the label in the session the later rows are read from. Checked and found right by the reviewer: the vectorised zone book against a10816a's on 60 random sessions (77 runs: equal timestamps, spans starting between trades, NaN rows, `end_ns`, VWAP before its first value, % of King gaps, a parameter switch, the 2,048-instant block boundary; identical), `es_at_level` old vs new on 360 calls (a QQQ block, an SPY block, the ES ÷ NQ ratio changing at 09:31:35; identical); building the session without an event's retested extreme leaves the main build, its end state and every other row unchanged. **The questions as asked:** (1) **rows after an event's own retested extreme:** a pair whose `decision_ts` comes after its own event's held label made a retested extreme is read from a rebuild of the session without that level; live (step 22) would have to run that rebuild at the decision (a full session build on the laptop). Such a pair is rare: a held label needs a `hold_reaction_pts` (20) move away, beyond `rearm_dist` (10), so price would be back at the zone only as a new event. Proposal: step 13 builds no pair of an event after its own structure label resolved (the event is over then), so the rebuild is never needed and stays only as a guard; (2) **touch-group mates:** a retested extreme made by the held label of another event of the same touch group (same touch trade, another zone) can reach the event's later rows, while day memory already leaves those labels out (only touches before the event's own touch count). Proposal: treat the whole touch group as the event's own (its mates' retested extremes also excluded), the same rule as day memory.
- **[13-0 items built 2026-11-09, §13 Q-step13-0] Q-step12a (zone churn on the five step-12 sample days, RTH; read-only reports `<reports_dir>/step12/zone_edges/zone_edges.md` and `zone_edges/flicker/flicker.md`, 2026-11-04). ✅ decided by the owner 2026-11-04: (1), (2) and (4) first, then (3) as the fast variant and (4) redefined after the read-only check (`zone_edges/relcheck/relcheck.md`); (5) proposed and accepted by the owner the same day. Docs only now: (1)–(4) are built at the start of step 13, before tuning, together with Q-step11h and Q-step12b (the owner's level-jump, zone and post-cross decisions), in step 13's single rebuild with a `features_version` bump:** **(1) Zone-book candidate-time defect (approved):** `ZoneBook._flip_times` (`features/zones.py`) compares each pair's relation code (qualify; pair hull ≤ `max_zone_width`) only from one trade to the next, so a relation that flips at another level's band step between two trades (a board's `t_apply`, a profile instant) and flips back at the next trade never becomes a candidate time: on 2026-03-17 `qqq_node-2026-03-17-601` stepped at the 09:35:09.160 board, z11 + `vwap18` was 60.002 wide at that candidate (`vwap18` became its own zone, z108) and 59.998 at the next trade (09:35:09.236, where recomputing at every trade joins it to z11); from there 170,031 of the session's 255,090 trades saw different zones. The other four sample days match the every-trade book at every trade. Fix: each pair is also checked at both levels' step times between trades; a hand-built regression test from the 03-17 case, beside the existing parity test (`tests/test_zones.py`); the step-13 build reports how many sessions of months 1–6 had any zone difference from the fix. **(2) Zone hysteresis (approved):** `zone_join_buffer` = 5 pts (a level joins a zone, or two zones merge, only if the result is ≤ `max_zone_width` − 5) and `zone_qualify_buffer` = 5 pts (a kept member stays while it still qualifies with another kept member within 5 pts more: bands at most 5 pts apart, or centres ≤ `confluence_tol` + 5; joining still needs the plain F5.3 rule). The release rule and the hard cap `max_zone_width` (60) are unchanged, so no zone is ever wider than 60. Tests for the boundary cases (a merged width of exactly 55 and of 55.25, a kept zone at exactly 60 and 60.25, a band gap of exactly 5 and 5.25, centres exactly `confluence_tol` + 5 apart). Evidence (five days, RTH; a round trip = a member back in the same zone within 60 s): zone-width/merge round trips 6,531 → 431 pts (−93%; 496 → 24), within 300 s 7,992 → 1,038 (−87%); releases were triggered by the qualify test 572 times and by the width 410 times, so the join buffer alone removes 73% and the qualify buffer alone nothing; zones born 897 → 605, edge movement −12%; a release-side buffer let zones reach 64.9 pts and a minimum persistence time 459 pts (both rejected). Cross/re-arm churn is left as is. **(3) Node-relevance debounce (approved 2026-11-04 as the fast variant):** `node_relevance_min_sec` = 60 and `node_relevance_fast_pct` = 0.40: a node whose `pct_of_king` is ≥ 0.40 becomes relevant at once (at that board); a marginal join (≥ `min_node_pct_of_king` 0.25, < 0.40) and every leave (< `node_drop_pct` 0.20) count only once they have held 60 s of board time; boards up to t only (no lookahead); tests including a King strike switch (every node's % of King moves together on that board), a marginal join falling back within 60 s (never relevant), a fast join, and a leave recovering within 60 s (stays relevant). Causes on the five days: QQQ 860 relevance changes, 63% after a board within 2 pp of the threshold and 73% moved by the node's own value (values hovering at the thresholds); NDXP 1,585, 56% moved by the King's size and the King strike itself changed on that board in 43% (every node's % of King jumps together); board noise (one-board |Δ % of King| p90 ≤ 1.2 pp) and nodes missing from boards (6 boards) negligible. Effect: relevance round trips within 60 s 7,348 → 520 pts (−93%), within 300 s 14,807 → 7,406 (−50%); node member-seconds −0.2%, every lasting change 60 s later. Read-only check (owner 2026-11-04; five sample days exact, months 1–6 from the boards, the NQ tape and the stored touch rows, 121 sessions with boards; `<reports_dir>/step12/zone_edges/relcheck/relcheck.md`): (a) rolls, rollbacks, price switches and King / ceiling / floor switches read every strike on the board, never relevance (`map_state.py`, `node_levels.py` `MapRolls`, `rolls.py`), so no switch is delayed; only the newly confirmed node's relevance can be: of the 585 switches (five days, RTH) whose node becomes relevant, the plain 60 s debounce delays 53 (9%; delay p50 23 s, p90 64 s, max 170 s), the fast variant 24 (4%; p50 60 s, p90 68 s, max 170 s); (b) quick node bounces (a relevance onset with price within 10 pts of the mapped strike, then 20 pts away on its own side within 5 min, before a trade beyond the band): months 1–6 846 of the 1,770 onsets within 10 pts (411 crossed first, 513 neither), median 72 s to the 20-pt point; at most 14 fire at or before the bounce today (five days: 0 of 43, 8 fire later as retests); the cause is F5.6, never the debounce or the node's role: the zone formed on price or moved onto it and starts unarmed, and price never got `zone_proximity` + `rearm_dist` (15 pts) outside it before the bounce (all 581 months 1–6 cases without an event); (c) relevance round trips within 60 s (five days) 7,348 pts → 520 (plain debounce) / 973 (fast 0.40) / 722 (fast 0.50); with the zone buffers of (2), all 60 s round trips 2,270 / 2,524 / 2,408; bounce nodes relevant by the bounce (months 1–6, of 846) 428 / 534 / 493; (d) see (4). **(4) Step-13 variant `node_role_at_formation` (redefined by the owner 2026-11-04 after the check):** (a) `approach` (current: F2.6 step 1, no role until price is `rearm_dist` outside the band; no formation touch); (b) `strike_side` + formation touch: when a QQQ/NDXP node becomes relevant (by (3): at once at ≥ 0.40 of the King, else after its 60 s) with price inside its touch band (band ± `zone_proximity`, the same rule as a normal touch; owner 2026-11-04, (5) (c)), that moment counts as a touch of its zone in the strike-side role (price below the strike → ceiling, above → floor), an event, instead of disarming the zone; price exactly on the strike → the approach rule (no formation touch; the zone starts unarmed as today). Check (five days, 43 bounce cases): under the current rule 12 nodes had no role at the onset (still none at the bounce) and 31 a role carried from before a drop (10 opposite to the strike side, 7 of them crossed); a role rule alone fires none of them, since F5.6 touches and directions come from the zone, not the node's role; a formation touch inside the touch band (band ± `zone_proximity`) would fire all 846 months 1–6 bounces and every other onset inside it: 4,356 (≈ 36 a session against a median 250 events a session), of which the 1,770 within 10 pts end bounce 846 / crossed first 411 / neither 513. Judged on months 1–4 with the same criterion as the other step-13 variants (§7.1 Tuning: each value rebuilds the events and retrains in the same day-blocked folds; total net R per month with the bootstrap policy over all trades of the run, trades at zones with a QQQ/NDXP node member reported beside it, each fold's bar set on the shared events, (5); trade counts reported); pre-declared: `approach` stays unless (b) has a higher total net R per month with the 95% day-block bootstrap interval of the difference excluding 0; winner frozen (`frozen_from: months 1-4`); searched after the previous-VA variants; months 7–12 are never used. **Fairness with different event sets (owner asked 2026-11-04):** the comparison is like for like on what it measures: both values run on the same months 1–4 days and the same day-blocked folds, each rebuilding its events and retraining inside them; the score is a total, not a mean or a hit rate, so a value that adds events gains only if the extra trades add net R after costs and slippage, and losing extra trades count against it; the difference is paired by day in the day-block bootstrap; every trade comes from the sequential simulator (§7 rule 3b). Two effects could still have credited (b) for something other than its own events; (5) removes them. **(5) Scoring of event-changing variants (proposed 2026-11-04; ✅ accepted by the owner the same day for every event-changing step-13 variant: golden-zone, previous-VA, `node_role_at_formation`; §7.1 Tuning):** (a) decide on the total net R per month of all trades of the sequential run, with the node-member subset reported beside it: a formation-touch trade takes the account's one slot (§10.3) and can displace a trade at another zone, which the node-member subset doesn't see; on paired days the trades that are the same in both runs cancel, so this adds only the trades the variant changed; (b) set each fold's trading bar (the fold median of the predicted hold probability) on the events both values share and apply it to all of that value's events: with the bar set over all events, many extra low-scored events lower it and let more of the shared events trade, which would credit (b) for a threshold shift; (c) "price inside its band" in (4) means the touch band, band ± `zone_proximity`, the same rule as a normal touch (owner 2026-11-04; what the check counted).
- **[13-0 items built 2026-11-09, §13 Q-step13-0] Q-step12b (level jumps, zone identity, post-cross retests; from the step-12 checks of 2026-11-03: `<reports_dir>/step12/post_cross/post_cross.md` and the 2025-11-18 weekly POC jump). ✅ Answered by the owner 2026-11-03 (recorded 2026-11-04). Docs only now: built at the start of step 13, before tuning, with Q-step11h and Q-step12a, in step 13's single rebuild with a `features_version` bump:** **(a) Level jumps:** crosses and flips come only from price trading through a level's band, never from the level moving. When a level's new band doesn't overlap its previous band (a jump, e.g. a POC / VAH / VAL switching to another volume peak), the old instance ends (it leaves its zone as a member that ended without crossing, F5.4) and the level continues as a new level at the new price: a new `level_id`, fresh state (tests and holds 0, no cross), its role from the approach rule (F2.6 step 1). A drift whose new band overlaps the previous one keeps the same level. Applies to every level whose value is recomputed. Regression test from the 2025-11-18 case: at 13:19:00 the weekly POC moved 25,150 → 24,750 (24,750's volume 1,151 → 1,179 contracts against 25,150's 1,152) and zone z20, founded by the weekly POC at 18:00, moved 400 pts with it onto price. The step-13 build report counts level jumps by type (months 1–6). **(b) Zones:** a zone never follows a member through a jump ((a) removes it). When zones merge, the merged zone keeps the older `zone_id` and the larger of the two zones' touch counts (F5.11; no reset, no summing). **(c) Post-cross retests:** a crossed member's retest needs only its own re-arm (price `rearm_dist` beyond its own band on the crossed side, F2.6 step 5), not the zone's re-arm (`zone_proximity` + `rearm_dist` beyond the zone's edges, F5.6); this replaces Q23's reading for post-cross retests. Evidence (months 1–6): of 9,833 crossed chart levels that came back within `zone_proximity` of their band within 60 min, 2,898 fired; of the 6,935 that didn't, 2,647 were held only by the zone's re-arm (2,395 in merged zones, 252 single-level), the cases (c) addresses, and 4,247 never got `rearm_dist` beyond their own band. **(d)** `rearm_dist` stays 10 for post-cross retests (no separate value). **(e)** Fix the legend of the 2025-11-18 jump plot (`<reports_dir>/step12/post_cross/plots/more/2025-11-18_weekly_poc_jump.png`), redrawn with the step-13 regression report: the band entry says "shown while an active zone member" although the band is drawn over the whole window, and no entry says why z20's edges stop at 13:38 (owner confirmed 2026-11-04).

- **[13-0 items built 2026-11-09, §13 Q-step13-0] Q-step12 (gate-12 visual check: the owner's answers, 2026-11-06; ✅ decided by the owner 2026-11-06, both follow-ups included; docs only, the variant is built at step 13; read-only count from the stored boards).** (1) **2026-03-11 golden zone:** the owner confirms it is drawn right (flips at 09:50 and 10:25, redraw at 10:30); no change. (2) **Node levels:** the owner trades both positive and negative nodes; King, ceiling and floor matter most; Gatekeepers matter when there is confluence on SPY / SPXW or NDXP; his preferred rule is the 5 largest-magnitude nodes above 25% of King. **Recorded as the step-13 variant `node_relevance_top_n` ∈ {5, off} (off = now; §7.1 Tuning):** per map, on QQQ and NDXP only (the maps whose nodes enter the zone book), the nodes that pass F2.4 (25% in / 20% out, with the step-13 debounce of Q-step12a (3)) are ranked by |value| on each applied board and only the top 5 are relevant; the confirmed King (F2.16) and the confirmed ceiling and floor (F2.11) are relevant at any rank; rank hysteresis: a node joins when it is in the top 5 and leaves only when it falls below 7th or below 20% of King (`node_relevance_top_n_leave_rank` 7), so a map has at most 7 ranked plus the three protected nodes relevant; the rank changes go through the same 60-s board-time debounce as the % test (follow-up (ii) below). Scored like the other event-changing variants (Q-step12a (5)): decided on the total net R per month over all trades of the sequential run on months 1–4, trades at zones with a QQQ/NDXP node member reported beside it with the trade counts, each fold's bar set on the events both values share; pre-declared: off stays unless 5 has a higher total net R per month with the 95% day-block bootstrap interval of the difference excluding 0. **With the debounce:** the relevance debounce (Q-step12a (3), the fast variant `node_relevance_min_sec` 60 / `node_relevance_fast_pct` 0.40) is a correction built into the step-13 rebuild, not a searched variant, so both values of `node_relevance_top_n` run on the debounced relevance and the rank applies to the nodes it passes. **Order:** after the previous-VA variants, before `node_role_at_formation` (which nodes are levels comes before how a node level's role is set, and `node_role_at_formation`'s subset, trades at zones with a node member, depends on it), then `retest_lookback_sessions`. Gatekeeper confluence stays a model matter through the existing features (`gatekeepers`, `maps_with_node_here`, `trinity_count`, `es_at_level`); no new rule or feature. **Follow-ups (✅ owner 2026-11-06):** (i) **approved:** `node_relevance_top_n` decides only which QQQ / NDXP nodes become NQ levels (zone-book members and events); the map state and every map feature (King, confirmed King / ceiling / floor, Gatekeepers, rolls, pika clouds, Trinity, `maps_with_node_here` and the other F2 features) keep the full 25% / 20% set, so those features are unchanged and the protected King, ceiling and floor are chosen as now (no circularity); (ii) **approved:** the rank test goes through the same 60-s board-time debounce as the % test: a rank join (into the top 5) counts at once at ≥ `node_relevance_fast_pct` 0.40 of King, otherwise after 60 s of board time held, and a rank leave (below 7th) counts only once it has held 60 s; reason: one test and one clock for every relevance change, as the debounce already has; ranks 5–7 swap on the same board noise the debounce absorbs (two nodes of similar size swapping order), which the 2-place buffer (5/7) alone doesn't absorb; a King strike switch doesn't move ranks (the |value| order doesn't depend on the King), so the rank part is steadier than % of King there. **Read-only count (2026-11-06, months 1–6 RTH, the 121 sessions with boards; relevance per applied board from the stored boards with the build's own `MapState` relevance, equal to the build's on every session; before the step-13 debounce):** QQQ 2,799,489 boards: more than 5 relevant on 48.9% (more than 7 on 27.0%), median 5, p90 10, p99 15, max 20; NDXP 705,484 boards: more than 5 on 47.5% (more than 7 on 33.5%), median 5, p90 14, p99 25, max 48; the per-session share above 5 has a median of about 48% (QQQ 0–100%, NDXP 0–96%). **Budget:** one more months 1–4 rebuild (it changes the node levels, so the cached node timelines are not reused): about 0.13 night by F16.4's scale (19 rebuilds ≈ 2.5 nights), about 5 h on the PC if a months 1–4 rebuild costs per session what the step-11 build did (15.5 h for 251 sessions, two passes). It is the 28th rebuild run of step 13 (19 detector runs, 8 variant runs before it), so it adds about half a night rather than pushing the step out; at the step-11 speed the whole set is ≈ 28 × 5 h ≈ 6 days of PC time, past F16.4's 3–4 nights, so step 13 re-estimates the budget from its first months 1–4 rebuild (owner noted 2026-11-06). Owner 2026-11-06: no variant is dropped or shortened for time without asking the owner; the re-estimate sets out, each with its cost, running around the clock on the build box, two rebuilds at once if memory and CPU allow, and screening on fewer months with the survivors confirmed on months 1–4. **(3) Deepcharts profile readings and tolerances (owner 2026-11-07/08; step-12 compare only, `checks/step12.py`, outside the build key):** Deepcharts' volume profile is read on Manual grouping, 4 ticks (1 pt), on all five days (on Automatic the row size changes with the range and the zoom; "Shifted Poc Tick Grouping = 4" is the POC-shift alert only); the 03-06 POCs first read on Automatic were replaced by Manual readings (owner 2026-11-08). Tolerances (§12 step 12): POC ±2 pts, VAH/VAL ±4 ticks, LVNs 4 ticks kept (on the Manual readings 8 of 10 LVNs match at 0 ticks and none is one row away; the 2 misses are the roll day's weekly LVNs). A POC miss is a near-tie when the bot's volume in the 1-pt row holding Deepcharts' POC is ≥ 90% of its largest 1-pt row; rows start at whole points (owner 2026-11-08: Deepcharts shows the price under the cursor, and where the bot's price looked closer than a tick-level reading allowed the owner typed the bot's price, so the readings can't settle the alignment). Result 2026-11-08 (`<reports_dir>/step12/compare.md`, 535 values): 514 ok, 21 misses, none unread; 8 POC misses: 2 on the roll day (below), 5 near-ties (all 100% at 1-pt rows), 1 other (2026-03-24 daily, 78%). **Roll day (owner 2026-11-08: a known Deepcharts difference; the evidence accepted, no Databento pull):** on roll days Deepcharts appears to use the new contract's own data. On 2026-03-17 its weekly POC / VAH / VAL are within tolerance of NQM6's own week (03-16 plus 03-17 to the 10:01 instant; method A: 0 / +2 / −4 ticks) and its previous-session values are close to NQM6's own 03-16 (method A −1 / −9 / −4, method B −11 / −3 / +1 ticks), against +160 / −136 / +259 and −17 / −27 / +45 for the bot's definitions. The match isn't exact because only NQM6's 1-s bars are stored for 03-16, not its trades (method A = each bar's volume at its close, B = spread evenly over its low–high; on 03-17, where trades exist, B came within 1 tick on VAH, VAL and the LVNs, while A moved a near-tie POC 600 ticks); of Deepcharts' two weekly LVNs one is 5 ticks from a method-A LVN and the other is unmatched. The bot keeps its definitions (the roll-week weekly drops pre-roll days, F3.2; the previous session as built, F3.2b) because the new contract's own pre-roll history is thin. The six 03-17 weekly and previous-session POC / VAH / VAL misses and the two weekly LVN misses (same cause: the roll-week weekly drops pre-roll days; owner 2026-11-08) are marked "roll-day definition" in compare.md. **(4) Step-13 correction: volume-profile row size (✅ decided by the owner 2026-11-08, the row prices and the edge clip included; docs only, built at the start of step 13 in its single rebuild with Q-step11h, Q-step12a and Q-step12b, with a `features_version` bump; the build key changes with the code):** every volume profile (daily, weekly, and the previous session's under either `prev_profile_anchor` window) builds POC, VAH and VAL on 1-pt rows of 4 ticks starting at whole points (a row = [⌊price⌋, ⌊price⌋ + 0.75]), summed from the same per-tick volume (the stored and carried volumes stay per 0.25 tick). **POC row** = the busiest row; ties by the F3.2 rules applied to rows (the row nearest the midpoint of the profile's row range, then the row nearer the volume-median row, then the lower row). **Value area** = from the POC row outward one row at a time, adding the larger of the next row above / below, both on a tie, only the other side once one side reaches the profile edge, until ≥ 70% of the volume (F3.3 on rows). **Prices (owner 2026-11-08):** POC, VAH and VAL each sit on their row's whole point (the row low), as Deepcharts labels its rows on Manual 4 ticks (its POCs read as whole points; the off-point readings are where the owner typed the bot's price). **Edge clip (owner 2026-11-08):** POC, VAH and VAL = min(max(x, profile low), profile high), i.e. clipped to the profile's traded range (with row lows only the low side can bind: the top row's low is at or below the high); this keeps VAL ≤ POC ≤ VAH when the POC row is a partly traded edge row. **Invariant test, built with it:** VAL ≤ POC ≤ VAH on every profile instant of a build, and on hand-built profiles (ties, a one-row profile, an edge row only partly traded); the row lows and the clip on all three give it by construction. **LVNs stay as now:** `profile.stats` hands the same per-tick array to `poc_index` / `value_area` and, Gaussian-smoothed, to the LVN finder; LVNs read no POC/VAH/VAL, so grouping only the POC/VA path leaves the LVN levels and features unchanged. **Evidence (step-12 compare 2026-11-08, five days at Manual 4 ticks, the 39 POC/VAH/VAL values outside the roll day's 6 weekly / previous-session values):** 26 pass their tolerance at the current single ticks; Deepcharts is within tolerance of the bot's 1-pt row for 36 (inside the row for 23); the 5 POC near-tie misses are 100% at 1-pt rows. At exact prices, same tolerances, the edge clip applied (the owner's check before recording, 2026-11-08): (i) all three on the row low 34 of 39 (POC 13, VAH 11, VAL 10 of 13 each); (ii) the earlier rule (busiest tick / top tick / bottom tick) 32 (13, 9, 10); (iii) the current single ticks 26 (7, 10, 9); the clip changes none of the 39. Typing bias: 7 readings are off-point values equal to the bot's current price (most likely typed by the owner); (iii) passes all 7 by construction, (i) and (ii) 4 each; without them (i) 30, (ii) 28, (iii) 19 of 32. (i) is better than (ii) overall and on VAH, so it is recorded. Closed (owner 2026-11-08): clipping VAL alone could break VAL ≤ POC when the POC row is the profile's bottom row and only partly traded; the clip now applies to all three. **What it changes (all at the step-13 rebuild):** the registry levels `poc_daily`, `poc_weekly`, `prev_poc` (type `poc`) and `prev_vah`, `prev_val` (`va_edge`), so their bands, zone merges and identities, touches, events, structure labels, the touch-row features of every zone they are in, the level-jump counts (Q-step12b (a)) and the step-12 reading tables; F3.3 per profile `in_vah_zone`, `in_val_zone`, `va_depth`, `dist_vah` / `dist_val` with their ATR and EM versions, `va_direction_fit`; F3.2b `prev_poc_naked` (the POC tick); F3.12 `dist_prev_poc_em`, `dist_prev_vah_em`, `dist_prev_val_em`, `prev_va_overlap`, `dpoc_drift_em`, `poc_migration_em` / `poc_migration_em_abs`, `open_vs_prev_va`; the day-type challenger candidates `poc_crosses` (F3.10) and `open_outside_prev_va_em`; F5.8 area events (step 19: the VAH/VAL zones that create them and their Q-areasp features); the step-13 variants `va_edge_sweep` (which previous VAH/VAL are sweep-eligible and the `va_edge` trades it is scored on) and `prev_profile_anchor` (both windows on rows; scored on events at the previous POC/VAH/VAL); ablation groups 5 (area events) and 11 (`amt_prev_session`). Not changed: LVNs, single prints / TPO (F3.5), VWAP, the EOD day-type label (no POC since Q-dtcross).
- **[13-0 items built 2026-11-09, §13 Q-step13-0] Q-step13plan (the step-13 plan: the owner's decisions, 2026-11-08). ✅ Decided; docs only, written into §7 rule 2, §7.1 Tuning, §12 step 13 and Q13-vol; built from step 13-0.** (1) **Order and sub-steps:** the corrections code first, then the reaction-distance report on the corrected levels (the retested extremes in it at the start widths). **13-0** the corrections with their tests, no build: Q-step11h (1) the pair cut at the event's own label resolution (at `pair_end_hold_pts` 20) and (2) the touch group as the event's own; Q-step12a (1) the zone-book candidate-time fix, (2) the hysteresis (`zone_join_buffer` 5, `zone_qualify_buffer` 5), (3) the relevance debounce (`node_relevance_min_sec` 60, `node_relevance_fast_pct` 0.40), (4) both `node_role_at_formation` values with the formation touch; Q-step12b (a)–(e); the profile rows of Q-step12 (4) (1-pt rows, the row low, the clip, the invariant VAL ≤ POC ≤ VAH; LVNs unchanged); the hold distance of Q-density (c) (`hold_reaction_pts` 30, `history_hold_pts` 30, `pair_end_hold_pts` and `retest_hold_pts` 20); the `node_relevance_top_n` code (Q-step12 (2)); feature_spec (F2.4, F3.2/F3.3, F3.8, F5.11/F5.12, F10, F11.4, F15, the F16.4 rows for `node_relevance_top_n`, `node_role_at_formation` and `retest_lookback_sessions`), config (`tune_grids_13` with its config test) and one `features_version` bump. **13a** a months 1–4 run writing the corrected level timelines at the start widths (§13 Q-dryrun13 (3)), then the reaction-distance report and the frozen widths (item 6). **13b** the event builder, labeler, pairs and paths code; the corrections rebuild on months 1–4 (re-planned under the new build key; also the budget measurement; 13a's run reused if it is the same build and the frozen widths equal the start widths, Q-dryrun13 (3)); the `exit_slippage_ticks` measurement; pair labels and paths; the flip checkpoint and golden-zone count reports. **13c** the tuning, rows 1–14 in this order: `zone_proximity`, `confluence_tol`, `sweep_max_depth`, `sweep_reclaim_bars` (style (c) trades), `flip_margin` (three groups), the golden-zone variants, `va_edge_sweep`, `prev_profile_anchor`, `node_relevance_top_n`, `node_role_at_formation`, `retest_lookback_sessions`, the order type × `entry_ttl_sec` per style ((d) with `limit_center`), `stop_buffer` with Q13-vol, then `roll_switch_min_sec` and `king_switch_min_sec` (log loss on the fixed events, the rows recomputed by engine runs, ≈ 24 h; scored as event-changing on the shared events if `node_relevance_top_n` = 5 won; §13 Q-dryrun13 (4)). **13d** the full-window build with the winners and the months 1–6 count reports. (2) **Months 1–6 build counts** (level jumps by type, sessions with a zone difference from the candidate-time fix, held / broke / timeout at 30 beside 20) wait for the 13d build; no extra months 5–6 build. (3) **The shared-event bar everywhere:** rule (b) of Q-step12a (5) (each fold's bar set on the events both values share) also applies to the detector grids (rows 1–5), each challenger against the start value; one rule everywhere. (4) **Shared-event matching, declared before any result:** the same touching trade, the same direction, and at least one founding member `level_id` in common; if a touch matches more than one event, the pair with the most common members is taken; if still tied, unmatched. Reason: exact founding-member equality would count a touch as a different event whenever a variant such as `node_relevance_top_n` drops one weak member. (5) **Q13-vol** is decided at row 13 by the data with the keep-current rule: the fixed stop floor stays unless the volatility-scaled one wins with its 95% day-block interval of the difference excluding 0. (6) **Reaction report:** confirm the NDXP ±20 band and freeze `em_level`'s half-width from it; `dp_band` is skipped while `dp_in_model` is false and set from the same report if dark-pool features ever enter the model. (7) **Budget:** option 1, around the clock on the build PC (provisional, at step-11 speed ≈ 3.7 min a session: the months 1–4 rebuild ≈ 5.2 h, 28 event-changing rebuilds ≈ 146 h, ≈ 8–8.5 days in all plus pairs, labels, paths and cross-validation; re-estimated from the first months 1–4 rebuild, Q-step12). Option 2 (two rebuilds at once, about 20% faster) not now: it needs a code change (`feature_build` keeps one `plan.json` and holds both job locks). Option 3 (screening on months 1–2, survivors confirmed on months 1–4) rejected: noisier, changes the §7.1 procedure, saves about 9%. The reuse of pass-1 checkpoints for variants that don't change carried state is measured after the first rebuild and proposed only with pass 2's end-state check (a wrong reuse fails the build loudly). (8) **The detector-grid pick (owner 2026-11-08, follow-up):** for rows 1–5, each challenger's difference in total net R per month against the start value, both runs' bars set on the events that pair shares, decides, the start value counting 0; the largest difference wins; if none is above 0, the start value stays; ties go to the value closest to the start value; if two values are equally far from the start value and have exactly the same difference, the one with the larger 95% day-block lower bound wins, and if that is also equal, the start value stays (owner 2026-11-08); each difference is reported with its 95% day-block bootstrap interval for information only (the detector grids keep "best value picked" with no interval test).
- **[13-0 items built 2026-11-09, §13 Q-step13-0] Q-dryrun13 (dry run of steps 13 and 14, 2026-11-08: an independent agent walked through both steps read-only against CLAUDE.md, this SPEC, feature_spec, config and the code; owner's decisions 2026-11-09). ✅ Decided; docs only now, written into §7.1 Tuning, §9b, §12 step 13, Q-step13plan, feature_spec F16.4 and CLAUDE.md rule 13; each item is built in the sub-step named.** (1) **Build worktree (13-0):** builds and every long job run from a separate git worktree pinned to a commit with a recorded full-suite pass (CLAUDE.md rule 14 (f)), at a short path (e.g. `C:\nqb\build`, clear of the Windows long-path limit) with its own uv environment; `scripts/register_tasks_pc.ps1` and `nqbot resume` point there; the pin moves only between jobs; coding continues in the main folder. Reason: every session of a build re-checks the build key against the code and config it runs from (`load_plan`), and `resume` refuses uncommitted tracked changes, so editing the folder a build runs from halts it. "No repo changes while a long build runs" now means no changes to the build worktree (CLAUDE.md rule 13). (2) **Run-time overrides and a tuning driver (13-0 the overrides, 13c the driver):** `features plan` takes run-time overrides, stored in `plan.json`, included in the build key and applied by every session, the committed config untouched; a tuning driver runs one coordinate pass (a 13c row) under one owner approval; the 13d full-window build refuses any override; each winner is frozen by a commit with `frozen_from: months 1-4`. (3) **A months 1–4 run before 13a (13a):** it writes the corrected level timelines at the start widths that the reaction-distance report reads (the build stores none now). If it is the same build as 13b's months 1–4 build and the frozen widths equal the start widths, 13b reuses it; otherwise 13b rebuilds. (4) **Row 14 (13c):** `roll_switch_min_sec` / `king_switch_min_sec` change feature rows that only an engine run can recompute: about 24 h of runs for row 14, approved; scored by structure-model log loss on the fixed months 1–4 events as before, unless `node_relevance_top_n` = 5 won its row: then the confirmed King / ceiling / floor are relevant at any rank (Q-step12 (2)), the two windows change which nodes become NQ levels, and row 14 is scored as event-changing (total net R per month, each fold's bar on the shared events, Q-step12a (5), matched by Q-step13plan (4)). Fixed in §7.1 Tuning, §12 step 13, Q-step13plan and F16.4. (5) **Golden-zone matching (13-0 the schema; used from 13c):** golden-zone touches have no founding members, so the Q-step13plan (4) rule could never match them; two golden-zone events match on the same touching trade, the same direction, both golden-zone events, and overlapping golden-zone bands. The event schema gains the touching trade's index and the founding member `level_id`s (13-0). (6) **Narrower build key (13-0):** the key covers only the config values the build reads; execution costs (e.g. `exit_slippage_ticks`) and the execution parameters (`entry_ttl_sec`, `stop_buffer`, the order types) are applied in the simulator, not stored in pairs; a test fails if the build reads a config value the key doesn't cover. (7) **Output folders and fingerprint (13-0 the folders, 13b the fingerprint):** every build key has its own output folder (events, pairs and paths included, so variant runs never overwrite each other); the 13b code (the event builder, labeler, pairs and paths) goes into the code fingerprint. (8) **Q13-vol (before 13c row 13):** a complete definition is proposed in this SPEC for the owner's OK before row 13: config key, volatility measure, which floors it applies to, fit objective, fitted on months 1–4 with the frozen `map_latency_sec` 1.16 and QQQ `stop_buffer_floor` 4.25. If it can't be defined cleanly, the fixed floor stays and Q13-vol moves to Q-v2. (9) **13d counts coded in 13b:** the old-vs-new zone-book comparison (sessions with any zone difference from the candidate-time fix) and the held / broke / timeout shares at 30 beside 20 are coded in 13b, so the 13d build runs once. (10) **Control Panel machine and files (step 14):** step 14 runs on the PC (the data and the SSD are there). Everything the UI edits (accounts, presets, UI settings) moves to an untracked local file with a committed example and schema; settings the build reads stay in git and are read-only in the UI (so a UI edit never changes a build key or makes a long job refuse). On the PC the Accounts page shows the webhook same-URL check as "not available on this machine" (no `TP_WEBHOOK_*` there); it works on the laptop from step 21. (11) **Review before gate 16 (step 14):** review may only propose corrections to existing definitions; new features go to Q-v2 (the §5.0 item 7 freeze); only what a correction touches is re-tuned; one review round is budgeted, anything bigger is asked of the owner first with its cost. (12) **Review-card regression tests (step 14):** hand-built synthetic reproductions; tests never read real data (the `tests/conftest.py` guard). (13) **Budget numbers (13-0):** the docs' step-13 budget figures are corrected to the Q-step13plan (7) budget (F16.4 and F16.10 say "≈ 3–4 nights", "≈ 1 h per point", cached timelines and golden-zone-only rebuilds, none of which exist), and the execution re-simulation count is 40, not 34 (style (c) 3 order types × 5 lives, style (d) 4 × 5, `stop_buffer` 5). (14) **Smaller items:** (a) 13-0: the stale `tune_grids_13` comments ("golden-zone trades only", "scored on va_edge trades": both are decided on all trades of the run; `features_logloss` is searched last) and F11.1's floors (QQQ 4.25, NDXP 6.50, frozen) are fixed; (b) 13b: a `be_offset_ticks` re-check follows the `exit_slippage_ticks` measurement; the measurement skips the four month-1 sessions before `mbp-1` starts (2025-10-07; never pulled, Databento is cancelled) and its report records the skip; (c) 13-0: proposals for the owner's OK written into this SPEC: the provisional structure model's default LightGBM grid point, the number of CV folds, the bootstrap replicate count, and combined vs single-style scoring (the owner leans to one combined (c)+(d) sequential run, since that's how the bot trades); (d) 13a: the rule mapping NDXP reaction distances to ±10 / 15 / 20 is proposed with the report; (e) step 20: the gate record allows one entry per gate (`tests/test_gate_record.py`) and a re-seal would need a second "20" entry: a note for step 20, no change now. (15) **Operational:** the PC's USB 3 link power management is off (the owner applied it; CLAUDE.md corrected); while a build runs only targeted tests run (CLAUDE.md rule 14 (b)); the full suite runs between jobs, and pushes wait for a full-suite pass. (16) **Performance page and the ledger (step 14; §9b item 6 changed; the owner's answer to the dry run's question 8):** every day- or trade-level view (the P&L calendar included) may show the live, demo and prop ledgers and the backtest ledger of development months 1–6, labelled as backtest; after the step-16 gate, months 7–9 appear only as totals and gate metrics, never trade- or day-level; months 10–12 not at all until the step-20 record (owner follow-up 2026-11-09). What months 7–12 may show after step 20 is open (Q-ledger-after20).

Q-step12 (4) the 1-pt profile rows; Q-density (c) the four hold distances and (d) the `retest_lookback_sessions` grid; Q-step12 (2) `node_relevance_top_n` (`features/node_rank.py`); `tune_grids_13.node_variants` / `retest_variants` with their config test; Q-dryrun13 (1) the build worktree (`scripts/build_worktree.ps1`, `nqbot build-pin set|show|clear|can-move`, `<data_dir>/jobs/build_pin.json`; `nqbot run` / `resume` / `backup` / `queue add` refuse outside the pinned worktree and commit on that machine; `scripts/register_tasks_pc.ps1` starts the tasks there, the owner re-registers once from an admin PowerShell), (2) run-time overrides (`nqbot features plan --set key=value`, stored in the plan, in its key, applied by every session, ledger months 1–4 only), (5) the event schema (`touch_idx`, `founding_level_ids`, `touch_zone_lo` / `touch_zone_hi`) and the shared-event matcher (`tuning/shared_events.py`, Q-step13plan (4) plus the golden rule; ties by the larger band overlap), (6) the narrower build key (`feature_build.NON_BUILD_FEATURE_KEYS` / `NON_BUILD_SETTING_KEYS`: the tuning grids and the settings only the simulators, models, gates and live trading read; a test fails if a build session reads one), (7) per-key output folders (`events/`, `pairs/`, `paths/` and a copy of the plan in each build's folder), (13) and (14a) the budget figures, the 40 re-simulations, the stale `tune_grids_13` comments and F11.1's floors. **(b) As built, for the owner's OK (none changes a decided rule; each is a reading where the decision left a detail open):** (i) **level-jump scope:** every recomputed band is cut at a jump: the daily and weekly POC, LVNs, single prints and the VWAP lines and bands, NQ and ES; a later piece is the level `<level_id>@<UTC time of the jump>` with a fresh machine (no initial or natural role); a multi-day level (the weekly POC) carries its current id and last band in `CarryState.level_alias`, and if a session's first band doesn't overlap the carried one, a new id starts at the session start; GEX node bands keep their (map, strike) identity (F2.4) and their jumps are counted only (none expected: a jump needs R to move by a node band); (ii) **own re-arm:** armed by a member's own re-arm (and not also by the zone's re-arm band), the touch, "onto" and "across" tests read that member's touch band, so a merged zone already holding price doesn't disarm it; repeated retests each need a new own re-arm (as any re-armed zone); an unresolved chart-level break blocks arming only while price is beyond that member's band (always so for the zone's own re-arm band, so the old path is unchanged); (iii) **top-n:** ties in |value| go to the strike nearer the board's spot, then the lower strike; the protected King / ceiling / floor are those confirmed at the board (as of its `t_apply`); a node that stops being relevant is unranked at once (its relevance leave is already debounced); (iv) **formation:** only at a node's relevance onsets (its first relevant board and each return to relevance), so a relevant node entering the top n is not a formation touch; QQQ / NDXP only; the node's machine takes the strike-side role with a fresh state (no cross, armed); (v) **ES zone book:** the two buffers × ES ÷ NQ at the open, like the other NQ-point zone parameters (F0.11); (vi) **previous-session profile:** its POC / VAH / VAL are recomputed from the carried volume when read, so a carry written by an older build gives row values; (vii) **step-12 tools:** they describe the stored v36 build (single-tick profiles) and are unchanged; `nqbot step12 day` now refuses (the build key changed with the code), as intended. **(c) Proposals for the owner's OK before 13c (Q-dryrun13 (14c)):** (1) the provisional structure model (§7.1 Tuning) at one `lgbm_grid` point: `num_leaves` 15, `min_data_in_leaf` 500 (the middle and the conservative end of the F16.7 grid) with `lgbm_grid.fixed`, no search; (2) day-blocked cross-validation with 4 folds, one per ledger month of months 1–4 (each month predicted by a model fitted on the other three, so every month 1–4 event gets an out-of-fold prediction); (3) 2,000 day-block bootstrap replicates for every interval and the keep-current tests of 13c (sessions resampled whole, the same days for both values of a pair); (4) one combined (c)+(d) sequential run for the detector and variant rows (rows 1–11), as the bot trades both styles at once (the owner's lean, Q-dryrun13 (14c)), with each style's execution parameters still frozen on its own single-style policy (rows 12–13, §7.1). **(d) The check (job `step13_check`, `checks/step13.py`, outside the build key; development days 2025-10-01, 2026-03-06, 03-11, 03-17, 03-20, 03-24, 03-26, each rebuilt from the stored v36 build's start state into `<tmp_dir>/step13_0_check/`; report `<reports_dir>/step13/corrections_check.md`):** runs `legacy` (neutral settings, every unparameterised fix off: must equal v36 exactly), `neutral` (buffers 0, every hold distance 20, top-n off, `approach`, no debounce, lookback 10; the unparameterised fixes on), `legacy+<fix>` for each of the five fixes alone, each parameterised correction or variant alone on top of `neutral`, and `start` / `start_again` (the committed configuration, built twice). Smoke on 2026-03-06 before the job: `legacy` = v36 exactly (508 events, every label and resolution time, every row column, the day-type rows and the end state); `neutral` 508 → 650 events, mostly the own re-arm (304 zones armed by a member's own re-arm that day); 22 level jumps (18 daily POC, 3 weekly POC, 1 VWAP band), no node-band jump. **Results (job `step13_check` on commit 9931af4 (the pinned build worktree `C:
qbuild`; 30877cd after it adds only docs and the shared-event matcher, which the check doesn't use), 121/121 chunks, 2026-11-09 03:38–08:08 ET; report `<reports_dir>/step13/corrections_check.md`):** (1) **`legacy` = v36 exactly on all 7 days** (2,178 events: every id, label and resolution time, every touch-row column, the day-type rows and the end state's v36 fields). (2) **`neutral`** 2,178 → 2,773 events (771 same id, 856 renamed, 551 removed, 1,146 added; 14 labels and 18 resolution times changed on kept events); of its 1,715 differing events, 811 also differ with the own re-arm alone, 660 with the 1-pt profile rows alone, 6 with the level jumps alone, 0 with the candidate times or the merge count alone (those change 7 event ids and one column, `zone_tests_so_far`, 395 values), 312 only from interactions.
