(4) **Deterministic:** `start` and `start_again` identical on every day (state digest, events, labels, row columns). (5) **Level jumps** (`start`, per session, median / max): NQ daily POC RTH 6 / 14, overnight 8 / 12; weekly POC RTH 0 / 3, overnight 0 / 5; VWAP 18:00 −2σ band 0 / 1 (2026-03-06 and 03-24, both 17–21 ms after the session's first trade, as the band widens from zero); no other VWAP line or band and no node band jumped, so the stop condition (more than 3 in a session) was not met. (6) **Jump plot** redrawn (legend below the chart, times and prices read from the build): on 2025-11-18 the weekly POC level from 2025-11-16 (25,146–25,154) ends at **13:14:00** (v36's single-tick profile jumped at 13:19:00), and the POC then switches among 1-pt rows ≈ 10 pts apart, five new levels in 30 minutes (13:14, 13:15, 13:19, 13:43, 13:44; the 13:44 piece is back on the 13:19 band but is a new level); zone z20 keeps its other members at 25,146–25,163.75 and does not follow. **For the owner (readings, nothing changed):** (i) a developing POC that switches between near-tied rows makes a new level at every switch (daily POC up to 14 in an RTH session), as the Q-step12b (a) rule reads (a band not overlapping the previous one), including a return to a band it held minutes earlier; (ii) the 18:00 VWAP −2σ band's first-trades jump renames that level for the session after ≈ 20 ms (harmless; could be skipped by not splitting in a band's first second). **(e) Owner's decisions on the check (2026-11-09):** (1) **POC switching (d1): the level id is reused** (built, `features_version` 38; feature_spec F5.x level jumps "Returns"): when a daily or weekly POC, or any recomputed profile level (LVNs, single prints), jumps to a band that overlaps a band one of its earlier pieces held in the same profile period (the session for daily, the week for weekly), it returns to that piece's `level_id` and `LevelState` instead of starting a new level; a jump to a band no earlier piece had is still a new level. The time away doesn't count as a cross. **How the state is brought up to date on return (proposed in 13-0; approved by the owner 2026-11-09 after the re-run's report):** the time away is a freeze of the level's machine (as a node that isn't relevant, F2.4: no cross, flip or stretch beyond is judged; a bar that formed partly while away isn't judged; the stretch beyond restarts at the return); an open sweep window ends without a verdict; then, from price at the return (the last trade at or before it): inside the band, or on the side the state expects (the holding side of an unbroken role, the crossed side of a provisional role, the original side once crossed back), the state carries on unchanged; on the other side, the role is set from price's side (above the band: floor, below: ceiling) as a confirmed role, not crossed, with no open episode, armed only if price is `rearm_dist` beyond the band on that side (else by the usual rule), keeping its counts (flips, crosses today, the last sweep, reclaimed, the cross / flip times); a level that never had a role keeps the approach rule; several earlier pieces overlapping: the most recently active one. A weekly level's piece from an earlier session of the week returns by id, and its machine starts at the session start like every carried level (machines run per session). VWAP lines and bands and node bands keep Q-step12b (a). The affected check runs were re-built (neutral, start, start_again and legacy+jump on the 7 days; the others are on the earlier code) and reported with new daily / weekly POC levels per session before vs after and the event-count change. **Re-run results (2026-11-09, commit b9eac19; report `corrections_check.md` §5–§6):** new NQ daily-POC levels per session (RTH + overnight) median 14 → 8, max 23 → 9; by day, daily POC RTH 4→4, 14→6, 11→2, 3→3, 11→2, 6→3, 3→2 and overnight 8→5, 4→3, 12→4, 5→3, 8→6, 8→5, 8→6 (2025-10-01, 03-06, 03-11, 03-17, 03-20, 03-24, 03-26); in the `start` run daily-POC jumps stay a median 6 / max 14 a session in RTH (8 / 12 overnight) but new levels are 3 / 6 (5 / 6); weekly POC new levels at most 3 a session (RTH and overnight; 03-17 overnight 5 → 3, 03-24 overnight 4 → 1, 03-06 RTH 3 → 2); 57 POC returns per run over the 7 days. 2025-11-18's weekly POC: the 13:14, 13:15 and 13:19 jumps are new levels (the 400-pt jump at 13:14 included), 13:43 and 13:44 return to the 13:15 and 13:19 levels (3 new levels instead of 5; plot redrawn with the returns marked). Events: `neutral` 2,773 → 2,771 (−2), `start` 2,690 → 2,690 (per day −4 to +4); one label changed vs before in each run; `held_today` / `held_rate_today` / `broke_today` change where a returning POC brings back its earlier piece's touches. Touch groups: on all 7 days in both runs the events present before and after share exactly the same touch groups (0 groups differ); `touch_group_id` changes on them are numbering only (owner's check 2026-11-09, report §6). `neutral` vs v36: 1,763 events differ (was 1,715): own re-arm 811, 1-pt rows 678, jumps 9, interactions 341. `start` = `start_again` on every day (deterministic); no VWAP or node band jumps more than 3 times a session. `legacy` re-built on the wrap-up code (eac7146, 2026-11-09): still equals v36 exactly on all 7 days (events, labels, resolution times, every v36 row column, day-type rows, the end state's v36 fields). The eight single-setting runs (join5 … lookback5) predate the return rule (built on 9931af4) and were not re-built (owner 2026-11-09): informational only. (2) **The VWAP −2σ first-trades jump: left as it is, recorded as harmless.** (3) **(b) as-built readings: approved.** (4) **(c) proposals: approved** (written into §7.1 Tuning detector parameters, §12 step 13 and `config/settings.yaml` `tune_13`): the provisional structure model at `num_leaves` 15, `min_data_in_leaf` 500 with `lgbm_grid.fixed`; 4 day-blocked CV folds, one per ledger month of months 1–4; 2,000 day-block bootstrap replicates; one combined (c)+(d) sequential run for the detector and variant rows, single-style for the execution rows.
- **Q-step13a (step 13a, the reaction-distance report, §5.4b, feature_spec F5.14; owner 2026-11-10). ✅ Readings decided; the widths themselves wait for the report and the owner's confirmation.** Measured on the months 1–4 level timelines (Q-dryrun13 (3)) of the corrected step-13-0 levels at the start widths, by `checks/step13a.py` (job `step13a_reactions`: `nqbot step13 reactions-plan`, then `nqbot run step13a_reactions --until HH:MM`; report `<reports_dir>/step13/reactions.md`, `reactions_widths.png`, `reactions_nodes.png`, `proposed_widths.json`). Approved readings: (1) highs/lows, "penetration beyond the sweep window excluded": an approach whose penetration resolves as a break by the sweep rule (F3.9) judged against the line (more than `sweep_max_depth` past before a 1-minute close back, or no close back by the `sweep_reclaim_bars`-th full bar) is not a reacted approach; (2) a level is measured over its band's whole existence except while frozen, not only its active spans (a cross at the start width would otherwise cap the measured penetration at the width being measured); (3) every hour of the CME day counts, the RTH-only figure shown beside; **for each width, if the RTH-only figure differs from the all-hours figure by more than 20% (`RTH_DIFF_SHARE`; both clipped to the cap first, since beyond it both freeze to the cap: approved by the owner 2026-11-10) or rounds to a different width, the report flags it and the owner decides which to freeze** (the NDXP band too); (4) the NDXP band (Q-dryrun13 (14) (d)) = the smallest of ±10 / 15 / 20 NDXP $ that covers the `react_width_q` (0.8) quantile of the NDXP reaction distances in NDXP $ (above 20: 20, flagged); (5) QQQ / SPY node reaction rows (Q-maprole, months 1–6) on months 1–4 in this report, months 5–6 added from the step-13d full-window build; report only, nothing frozen from them; (6) `dp_band` skipped while `dp_in_model` is false. Also in the report: the VWAP lines and bands are read once a second (the line after each second's last trade, the stored timeline), and an approach whose reaction window overlaps a damaged RTH stretch (§3.2) is left out.
- **Q-step13a-2 (2026-11-10; the reaction-distance reading, F5.14; ✅ decided by the owner 2026-11-10: reading B, the return rule, the cap, single prints, the prior_week fallback; the widths, NDXP ±15 and the RTH flags decided by the owner 2026-11-10 after the final run; the cross rule unchanged, a known label limitation).** The step-13a job (months 1–4, 84 sessions, build `58b311aa6985`) measured F5.14 as written and every chart-level width came out **0** (q0.8 = 0 for every type; NDXP q0.8 = 0 → ±10): an approach starts when price comes within cap + `zone_proximity` (12 + 5 = 17 NQ pts for a chart level, ≈ 25 for a node) of the line, and a 10-pt (`react_pts`) reversal from there is a reaction, so 82–93% of reacted approaches turn before reaching the line and max(0, …) records them as 0. Diagnostic (every 4th session, 21 sessions, read-only): reading **B**, the deepest point past the line over reacted approaches that reached the line (near misses left out), q0.8 ≈ vwap 17, vwap_band 14, poc 14–15, lvn 14–21, em_level 17, va_edge 12.5–14.6 (all above the 12 cap → 12), highs/lows (on_extreme, pdh_pdl, prior_rth, prior_week, ib, retested_extreme) 6.75–9.5, single prints 0, NDXP 13.5–15.2 NDXP $ (→ ±15 or ±20), nearly the same under either approach radius; reading **D**, approach radius `zone_proximity` only (5 pts) with near misses still at 0: vwap 11, vwap_band 9.6, poc 9.25, lvn 9, em_level 9.9, va_edge 10.8, highs/lows 4.5–5.25, NDXP 11.1 $ (→ ±15). The owner picks the reading; the job then re-runs on all 84 sessions (≈ 6 min) and the widths are shown again before anything is frozen. **✅ Owner 2026-11-10: reading B** (written into F5.14 and §5.4b): a reaction counts only if price reached the line; its distance is how far past the line price went before the 10-pt reversal; near misses are left out of the width measurement, since the touch band (`zone_proximity`) already covers turns before the line. The report shows for every type the reactions used, the share of measured distances at or above the cap, reading D beside B (information only) and the RTH flags; a type with fewer than 100 reactions over months 1–4 gets no width from its own numbers: proposed fallback (from the SPEC) = the pooled quantile of its F16.4 type group (`tune_grids_13.detectors.flip_margin.groups`, single prints and dark pools left out), clipped to its cap, else its step-6 start value; the near misses' shortfall per type (count, median, q0.8) is reported for the `zone_proximity` grid at 13c (information only). **Single prints come out 0 under every reading** because their edges move: a trade inside the unfilled run fills those bins, so the near edge moves with price (350 of 635 single prints in a sample of sessions had moving edges, 370 ended inside the session) and price can't be measured past the edge by more than a sliver of a bin; nothing is proposed for them from these rows (the start value 0 stays unless the owner picks another measurement). **Hold vs break check (owner's question 2026-11-10; all 84 sessions, read-only):** F5.14 doesn't separate a hold from a break: a run through the line followed by a 10-pt pullback far beyond it counted as a reaction (the five largest: 56–75 pts past, e.g. a prev VAL on 2025-10-07 11:04 broken by 55 pts and never reclaimed). Of the reactions that reached the line, the share that did not come back across the line after its deepest point within `hold_window_min` (30 min): vwap 3%, vwap_band 2%, poc 5%, lvn 3%, em_level 7%, va_edge 4%, highs/lows 0–1%, NDXP 5%; with them removed q0.8 falls by 0.3–1.7 pts (vwap 16.5 → 15.2, vwap_band 14.4 → 13.9, poc 16.3 → 14.5, lvn 13.0 → 12.6, em_level 13.7 → 12.1, va_edge 14.3 → 13.3, NDXP 14.6 → 13.1 NDXP $), so the six chart-line types still sit at or above the 12-pt cap: their lines are genuinely pierced 12–15 pts on the 0.8 quantile before holding. A moving VWAP line can also "come back" to price by itself within 30 min. **Owner's decisions 2026-11-10 (F5.14):** (1) **return rule approved:** a reaction counts only if price comes back across the line to the approach side after its deepest point within the 30-min label window (`hold_window_min`), else it is a break ("no return"), excluded; a moving line such as VWAP makes it a little lenient (the line can drift back to price by itself). (2) **The 12-pt cap stays.** Measured q0.8 above the cap (diagnostic, all 84 sessions, with the return rule) and the share of holds the capped band leaves outside (> 12 pts past the line): vwap 15.2 (29%), vwap_band 13.9 (26%), poc 14.5 (26%), lvn 12.6 (20%), em_level 12.1 (20%), va_edge 13.3 (24%); a touch still counts out to the band edge + `zone_proximity`, which 13c tunes; the report repeats this per type from the final run. (3) **single_print keeps its start value (0)**, for the reason above (its near edge moves as the run gets filled). (4) **prior_week:** the pooled highs/lows fallback is approved if the final run still has fewer than 100 reactions (diagnostic: 69–70). **Final run (2026-11-10, all 84 sessions, commit 5f123cf; `<reports_dir>/step13/reactions.md`) and the owner's decisions the same day:** (5) **widths frozen as proposed, `frozen_from: months 1-4`** (config/features.yaml): vwap, vwap_band, poc, lvn, em_level and va_edge 12 (the cap); on_extreme 9, pdh_pdl 8.5, prior_rth 8, ib 8.25, retested_extreme 8.25; prior_week 8.25 (the pooled highs/lows fallback, 69 reactions); single_print 0 (its start value); the all-hours widths; `em_level`'s half-width 12 (122 reactions, its own width). **NDXP band ±15** (from ±20) by the rule (q0.8 13.14 NDXP $, all hours and RTH only). **RTH flags:** the all-hours widths are kept; the four flagged types (pdh_pdl RTH 8.25, prior_rth 8.5, prior_week 8.25, retested_extreme 8.0) differ by one or two ticks, well inside 20%. (6) **Cross rule: no change and no variant now; a known label limitation.** Check (owner's question 2026-11-10; all 84 sessions, read-only): a chart line is crossed by one 1-minute bar opening and closing beyond the band's far edge (F5.2, F0.5: margin 0, no time condition beyond the bar), and the structure label (F11.4) says held only if the 30-pt hold comes before such a cross, so a hold that dips past line ± 12 for a full bar is labelled broke. Of the 3,756 real holds (reading B + the return rule) at the six line types, 980 went more than 12 pts past the line; 247 of them (25%) are labelled broke by their own dip, **6.6% of all line-type holds** (vwap 7.3%, vwap_band 6.8%, poc 6.6%, lvn 1.5%, em_level 5.7%, va_edge 6.2%); 245 of the 247 reached the full 30-pt hold later in the window (median dip 22 pts past the line, 86% RTH). This is an **upper bound**: each level was treated as a zone of its own, and a merged zone breaks only once every member is crossed. Real breaks (approaches past the line with no 10-pt reaction or no return) are labelled at a median **19 pts past the line**, about 11 min after the touch. Such trades would also have hit a stop placed beyond the zone, so the pair labels are right either way (owner). Options considered, none tested now (the likely gain is small and each tested value costs about one months 1–4 rebuild): (A) as now; (B) a larger cap for the line types only (16: widths would come out at their q0.8, 12.1–15.25, ≈ 5.5–6% mislabelled; 20 / 24: 4.2% / 3.0%, but a line's own band then nearly fills the 55-pt merge limit, the share of line zones merged with a neighbour falls 96% → 89% / 79%, stops widen, real breaks labelled at 29 / 33 pts past); (C) a cross margin for chart lines with the band kept at 12 (+4 / +8 / +12: 5.4% / 4.2% / 3.0%, real breaks at 24 / 29 / 33 pts past, no density or stop change); (D) a time condition (two settled 1-minute bars: 4.1%, breaks at 22 pts; a settled 5-minute bar: 1.1%, but breaks labelled about 6 min later at 29.5 pts past and a third fewer within the window). A cross margin for the chart-line types is a Q-v2 idea (Idea 5). Diagnostic scripts in the session scratchpad (not in the repo). **(7) QQQ node band ±0.375 (owner 2026-11-12; `frozen_from: months 1-4`; was ±0.5):** by the rule that set NDXP ±15 (the smallest of ±10/15/20 NQ pts, ≈ $0.25/$0.375/$0.50, covering the reactions' q0.8, ≥ 100 reactions): 392 QQQ reactions on months 1–4, q0.8 14.97 NQ pts = $0.36 (RTH $0.37), so ±$0.25 falls short and ±$0.375 covers it. The ±0.5 start was an owner value set at step 6 before anything was measured (half the $1 strike step, a band one step wide, ≈ ±20.6 NQ pts); ±0.375 ≈ ±15.5 NQ pts against NDXP ±15 restores the step-6 intent that both Nasdaq maps' node bands are about the same share of price. It changes the QQQ map features (every one built on the band: crosses, pauses, rolls, pockets, pika-cloud widths and edges, beach-ball overshoots, Trinity overlaps, `es_at_level`) as well as the zones. Evidence (read-only, `<reports_dir>/step13/zone_widths.md` part 5, 16 sampled months 1–4 sessions): zones −2.1%, events −2.8%, tradeable −2.7%, lone QQQ-node zones 41.3 → 30.9 pts (median), all tradeable zones 45.4 → 43.8 (p90 55.0 → 54.7); stop medians: swing 4.1 → 4.8, far edge 49.8 → 48.0, next structure 113 → 108, fib 1.0 unchanged. **13-0 style check (2026-11-12, the 7 step-13-0 check days from the v36 start states, every pass-2 file incl. the engine stage):** deterministic (built twice: all 56 files byte-identical, end states equal); against the committed configuration (±0.5): events 2,665 → 2,558 (−4.0%), zones 854 → 796 (−6.8%), tradeable events 1,321 → 1,238 (−6.3%), style (c)/(d) decision points 3,928 → 3,763 (−4.2%); QQQ node levels 103 on both (the same nodes, narrower bands); 2025-10-01 (no Skylit boards) identical; of the events kept, labels changed on 11 and resolution times on 17; the changed row columns are the QQQ map features above and the zone and day-memory columns that follow from other zones (e.g. `qqq_pika_cloud_width` 811 events, `zone_width` 310, `held_rate_today` 844); the day-type rows are unchanged. The no-lookahead tests pass with the new value. `features_version` stays 38 (a frozen value, like NDXP's in (5)); the build key changes.
- **Q-flexfarm (owner 2026-11-10). ✅ Decided 2026-11-10 (answers below); docs only now, the pair change is built in 13b, the challenger in the step-15 prop simulator.** Owner idea, **Lucid Flex funded accounts only:** make one large funded day early (about $1,600: past the $1,100 that locks the drawdown floor at $25,100 and the $1,000 that unlocks 2 minis, §6.7), then "farm" the other four profitable days the payout needs (5 days of ≥ $100, §6.7 new rule type 2) at about $100 each, so total profit reaches about $2,000 and the payout (50% of profit) hits its $1,000 cap; on those days trade only for a winning day and stop for the day once the day's net P&L is ≥ +$100 after commissions. On farming days, negative-RR trades (target smaller than stop) are acceptable if their P(target before stop) makes a winning day likely. **It must be validated, not assumed.** Validation (step-15 prop simulator, Lucid Flex only; the other presets don't reward winning days): the current policy vs this phase-aware policy on expected net cash per evaluation bought, payout rate, days to first payout, payouts over the account's life, and P(account lost before the first payout); keep-current rule: the new policy wins only if its 95% day-block interval of the difference is above 0; designed on months 1–6 and judged walk-forward like everything else (§7 rule 2b). To decide before 13b: (a) whether pairs are built below `pair_build_min_rr` (0.8, F11.2) at 13b, and down to which net RR, so the farming policy's targets exist without a later pairs rebuild; (b) the §6.3 owner rule (selection discards candidates below 1:1 net RR at the account's own costs) would need an exception for farming days; (c) the day-profit stop (+$100 net) is a new account rule type, not one of the `profit_cap_mode` modes. Lucid's site is re-read for this at step 26 (§12 step 26). **Owner's decisions (2026-11-10):** (1) **Pairs:** at 13b pairs are built down to net RR **0.25** at default costs (covers a net RR of 0.30 at Lucid's lower costs: $100 ÷ $333, the smallest RR at which one full-risk win makes a $100 day); the existing pairs stay exactly as now: the `max_target_candidates` (10) nearest zones at RR ≥ `pair_build_min_rr` (0.8) are chosen as today, and the below-0.8 zones with RR ≥ 0.25 are added on top (up to 3 zones, nearest first); the main pair scorer and the 13c tuning policy keep using only RR ≥ 0.8 / ≥ 1:1 pairs, unchanged; the farming challenger gets its own scorer trained on the low-RR pairs too; the 13b builder counts pairs by RR band (< 0.5, 0.5–0.8, ≥ 0.8) so the real cost shows in the budget run, and a check shows the ≥ 0.8 pairs and their labels are identical with and without the extension (§12 step 13, F11.2). (2) **The 1:1 rule (§6.3):** an exception only inside the farming challenger, only on Lucid Flex funded farming days; everywhere else the 1:1 rule stands. (3) **Day-profit stop:** a new rule only for the farming challenger: stop for the day once the day's net P&L (after commissions) is ≥ $100 + a $10 margin ($110), until Lucid's exact counting rule is confirmed at step 26. (4) **Farming-day loss cap:** the challenger tests {the soft $600 DLL as now, stop after the first losing trade}, so the simulator shows which loses less to win $100. (5) **Re-read on Lucid's site at step 26** (§12 step 26): (i) any payout consistency rule on the funded Flex (largest day as a share of the cycle's profit); (ii) what makes a day count toward the 5 profitable days (net of commissions, by session date, whether exactly $100 counts); (iii) any prohibited-practice clause (gambling-style or all-in trading, trading only to meet day counts, minimum trade size or hold time, microscalping); (iv) the flipping rule's exact terms (5 s, 50%) and whether it is judged per payout cycle; (v) the payout formula (50% of profit capped at $1,000, $500 minimum, the 90/10 split); (vi) the floor-lock triggers (EOD high $26,100 or the first payout request); (vii) when the scaling tier updates and how the soft DLL behaves; (viii) automation and copiers for the Flex type and the 5-account limit. Also from the rules check (§6.7 as recorded, nothing forbids the idea): the funded Flex has no consistency rule or daily profit cap, the evaluation's 50% consistency rule ($625/day) rules out such a day there; at $333 risk the 2-mini unlock rarely binds (stops of 40–45 pts are sized in micros); farming days give at most ≈ 20% of a cycle's profit, under the flipping rule's 25% warning even if all are held ≤ 5 s. **Finding for later (report only, nothing changes from it now; owner 2026-11-10):** on the months 1–4 build (v38, 23,503 events), with the stop at the zone's far edge + `stop_buffer` (candidate 2) at default costs, the nearest target zone in the trade direction is below net RR 0.8 for about 7 in 10 events (< 0.33: 32%, 0.33–0.5: 16%, 0.5–0.8: 23%, ≥ 0.8: 29%): zones are wide (median 40 pts, mostly node bands), so stops are wide.
- **Q-step13b (step 13b, the event builder, labeler, pairs and paths; owner 2026-11-10). ✅ (a) decided by the owner 2026-11-10 (plan approved); (b) the as-built readings approved by the owner 2026-11-10, (vii) changed (gapped stops and market exits); (c) the smoke run; the style-(c) firings decision waits for the months 1–4 `counts.md`.** **(a) Decisions:** (1) **two stages:** the keyed engine build (pass 2 of `feature_build`, after its end-state check; package `events/`, in the build's code digest, Q-dryrun13 (7)) writes per session `events/` (one row per event: identity, setup tags, the structure label and the pair cut, level-type groups, `post_cross_retest`, `n_firings_c`, `damage_drop`; its features are the touch row in `touches/`, joined by `event_id`), `decisions/` (one row per decision point = event × style × firing, only where `pair_allowed`: the snapshot as of `decision_ts`, float64 prices, and the F6.5 pair-only columns), `decision_rows/` (the pair scorer's inputs `row(event, decision_ts)` of each style-(c) decision point; style (d)'s are the touch rows) and `level_events/` (every NQ level's and gameplan zone's transitions with its group, for the flip checkpoint); a separate **labeler** (package `labeler/`, job `step13_labels`) with its own key = the build key + every execution and cost setting it reads (`labeler/params.py`) + its code, writes `pairs/<labels key>/`, `fills/<labels key>/` and `paths/<labels key>/` from the decision points and the session's NQ trades and `mbp-1` top of book, so its outputs never collide and 13c's execution rows re-run only the labeler (about 10 s a session, no rebuild); the low-RR extension, its identity check (every session labelled with the extension on and off, the ≥ 0.8 rows and labels compared) and the RR-band counts are in the labeler; tests: the labeler agrees with a trade-by-trade loop on hand-built tapes, the decision snapshot holds only data known at `decision_ts` (the band track, read only to remove a pending entry, is the one exception) and equals the session's own queries, and each stage reads only configuration its key covers; (2) **style (c): every completed audit or sweep reclaim is a decision point** (`firing_n` 1, 2, …; a later firing needs a new trade inside the band since the previous completion), no cap; the firings per event are reported (distribution, the largest, examples) and `nqbot step13 labels-plan` refuses while any event has more than 5 until the owner has read them (`--firings-ok`); (3) **no `mbp-1`** (2025-10-01 to 10-06, and outside 09:25–16:05): a marketable-limit row is `no_book` (no fill, no label, left out of the order-type comparison and of that order type's training); a stop-limit fills at its triggering trade's price (capped at the limit), flagged `entry_no_book`; a market exit uses the trades only, flagged `exit_no_book`; the slippage measurement skips those sessions and lists them; (4) **target counting (F11.2):** each zone gives its near edge and its centre; a zone counts toward `max_target_candidates` if one of its two prices has net RR ≥ `pair_build_min_rr` (only those prices are built), nearest first; golden-zone fib 0 / −0.272 / −0.618 (net RR ≥ 0.8) and the 1:2.5 baseline (`baseline_rr`) are extra; the low-RR extension adds up to `pair_ext_max_zones` (3) more zones, nearest first, with prices in [`pair_ext_min_rr` (0.25), 0.8), not already in the set; per decision point × order type × stop. Owner addition: the `events-day` smoke runs the labeler too and reports decision points, pairs by style × order type × RR band, disk per folder and the labeler time, extrapolated to months 1–4 and the 251-session window, stopping before the long run if pairs or disk look out of proportion (e.g. > about 50 GB, or the labeler slower than the build). **(b) As-built readings (none changes a decided rule; each fills a detail the SPEC leaves open; ✅ approved by the owner 2026-11-10, (vii) as changed by him):** (i) **damage (§3.2 rule 3):** the event's span = its structure-label window (touch to min(touch + `hold_window_min`, flat-by)); a dropped event builds no decision point; (ii) **time-to-live removal, "zone moved":** the order's reference point on the zone (its near edge; the centre for `limit_center`) more than `entry_reprice_tol` from its value at `decision_ts`, read at each NQ trade over the next 300 s (`TRACK_HORIZON_SEC` = the largest `entry_ttl_sec` grid value); vanished = no edges (a golden zone redrawn or flipped away, a zone that lost its members); crossed = the structure label's cross rule; the time-to-live counts from `decision_ts`; (iii) **sweep reclaim:** the same completion as an audit (the sweep trades through the band; a close back on the range side within `sweep_reclaim_bars` completes it, a later one is a break that crosses the level), tagged `sweep_reclaim` when a sweep-eligible member swept since the touch or the previous completion; (iv) **reference prices:** style (d)'s stop-limit trigger = 1 tick beyond the price at `decision_ts`; style (c)'s passive limit = the zone's near edge (a pullback entry); a marketable limit's reference = the best ask / bid at `decision_ts`; (v) **unfilled orders:** the report-only hypothetical outcome is a fill at the reference price at arrival; (vi) **stop candidate 3** = the nearest level zone wholly beyond the entry zone's far edge; containers (golden, gameplan zones) are targets but never stop candidate 3 or the first structure (F11.3 excludes them); (vii) **breakeven:** the r-rules measure k × the distance from the fill to the stop; the breakeven stop (fill + ceil(commission ÷ $0.50) + `be_offset_ticks` ticks) is rounded toward the entry; **gapped stops (owner 2026-11-10, changed from the as-built reading):** a stop or breakeven stop that price gaps through fills at the worse of (a) the first trade at or beyond the stop and (b) the stop ∓ `exit_slippage_ticks`, never better than the actual trade; a market exit (flat-by, news flatten) likewise never fills better than the first trade at or after its time (the book's size then the following trades, or that trade if worse); the labeler records each exit's extra cost from the rule (`gap_ticks_<rule>`) and reports how many stop and market exits it changes and their mean extra cost in ticks (`labels_<key>.md`); a labeler-only change (the build key is unchanged); (viii) **paths:** MAE / MFE from the fill to the exit; overshoot, stopped-then-target, extension and the return to entry from the exit to the RTH close; (ix) **market exits** (flat-by, news flatten): the best bid / ask at the effective time up to its size, the rest over the following trades in sequence (the slippage method); (x) **outside the setup windows** (overnight, after `entry_end`) decision points are labelled too (`tradeable` false), as §1.2 keeps them for the out-of-window report; (xi) **slippage measurement:** at the tradeable decision points' stop candidates (the start `stop_buffer`, from the start order type's reference), the first touch after `decision_ts` + `order_latency_sec` before the RTH close, sized at `exit_slippage_ticks` 1 to 4 (sensitivity); proposal = the p95 rounded up to a whole tick; the `be_offset_ticks` re-check proposes the smallest offset keeping a breakeven exit at or above \$0 after costs. **(c) Smoke run (`nqbot step13 events-day 2026-03-20`, the busy day, 2026-11-10; `<reports_dir>/step13/events_day_2026-03-20.md`):** build 90 s, engine stage 13 s (style (c) 8 s, the 13d zone comparison 5 s), labeler 7 s (+ 7 s identity re-run), peak 4.3 GB; built twice: decision points and events identical; 307 events, 493 decision points ((d) 307, (c) 186); 74,864 pairs, identity check passed (69,524 base rows, 5,340 extension rows); about 16 MB a session (pairs 1.5 MB), ≈ 4 GB for the full window; 6 events with more than 5 style-(c) firings (max 10; 5 of them outside the setup windows, chop along wide zones; one tradeable, 14:42, 6 completions in 6 minutes before its 20-pt resolution), shown to the owner; the labeler waits for `--firings-ok` after the months 1–4 counts. **(d) Months 1–4 results and owner decisions (2026-11-11):** the months 1–4 build (key 876bff98c2ab, 84 sessions, 99/99 chunks, every end-state check passed; one pass-2 chunk, 2025-10-09 to 10-16, sat about 13.7 h after its last session had finished while the PC slept, its sessions' outputs complete and of the same kind as every other); `counts.md`, `slippage.md`, `<reports_dir>/step13/`. (1) **Style-(c) firings, labeler runs with `--firings-ok`, no cap:** 202 of 22,681 events fire more than 5 times (largest 13), 146 of them with no tradeable decision point (overnight or outside the setup windows); **56 tradeable events fire more than 5 times (largest 11)**, mostly chop along the zone's edge (a 1-minute close outside almost every minute; audits, not sweep reclaims), ending in timeouts (labels at 30 pts: 33 timeout, 20 held, 3 broke); they make up 409 of the 5,090 tradeable style-(c) decision points (8.0%; 2.4% of all 16,918 tradeable decision points). Step 15 adds a simulator-only sensitivity, the maximum firings traded per event {no cap, 3}, with no rebuild (§12 step 15). (2) **Gap rule, compared against the triggering sweep (option B; changes (b) (vii)):** the measured stop slippage is almost all the sweep that hits the stop: one aggressor's prints share a `ts_event`, the first at the stop (stop to the first print at or through it: p95 1 tick), the later ones through it (stop to the sweep's last print: p95 10), and the stop fills against the book the sweep left (measured beyond the sweep's last print: p95 1); so a stop or breakeven stop fills at the worse of (a) the **last print of the triggering sweep** (every print at the stop's trigger timestamp) and (b) the stop ∓ `exit_slippage_ticks`, and a market exit (flat-by, news flatten) never fills better than the last print of the first sweep at or after its time (`labeler/outcomes.sweep_end`; a labeler-only change, the build key unchanged). **`exit_slippage_ticks` 4 and `be_offset_ticks` 4, frozen_from months 1–4** (were 1 and 1): 4 is the smallest whole tick at which the labeler's charge, max(4 ticks, the sweep's gap), covers the measured fill on ≥ 95% of the 29,911 tradeable stops (95.4%, mean charge 5.05 ticks; the plain p95 10, and 10 under the first-print rule, would have charged every stop at least 10 ticks against a measured mean of 2.47); offset 4 keeps a breakeven exit at +$0.10 per micro after costs (ceil(1.90 ÷ 0.50) + 4 − 4 ticks × $0.50 − $1.90). The proposal rule is in `slippage.md` (`sweep_rule_proposal`). (3) **Engine speed fix approved** (the repeated rebuild of a session without a touch group's own retested extremes: engine stage 71% of pass-2 process time, 6,108 s on 2025-10-10 against a 663 s build; `MAX_ALT_BUILDS` 2): after the 13b labeler run and before 13c, with a test that its outputs equal the current build's on the seven step-13-0 check days (only the key changes); `step13 budget` uses the sessions' own times (pass 2 = the union of each chunk's session intervals), not the chunk times. (4) **Zone widths:** before 13c, a read-only report, no change: the width distribution of tradeable entry zones by member composition, stop distance by stop candidate (swing, far edge, next structure, fib), the share of trades sized at the minimum, and what a `max_zone_width` of 40 instead of 60 would change (zones, events, stop distances); the owner decides after it (tradeable entry zones: median 44 pts, p10 24, p90 55; 81% of tradeable stops size at one NQ-equivalent). **(e) Engine speed fix (owner 2026-11-12; both changes exact, approved after the diagnosis):** measured on the seven step-13-0 check days (from the v36 checkpoints) and three heavy months 1–4 days (2025-10-10, 10-22, 2026-01-02, from this build's checkpoints): no world is ever built twice (the (d) (3) reading of a repeated rebuild was wrong); every one is the band track's 300-s look past a decision point within reach of its touch group's own retested extreme, one whole-session build per such group (2025-10-10: 13 groups, 6,069 s). (1) the band track reads the part of its window before that retested extreme from the session itself and builds the world without it only if a removal time (`t_move_near`, `t_move_center`, `t_vanish`; each a first instant) is still missing; (2) such a world is built only to the touch group's latest `t_pair_end` + `TRACK_HORIZON_SEC` + a 5-minute margin (`SessionFeatures(horizon_ns=…)`, `world(need, horizon)`; a cached one is never read past its horizon, else the whole session is built as before; it accepts no retested extreme created after its horizon); the build is causal, so everything it answers up to the horizon equals the whole-session build's. **Proof:** every pass-2 file (touches, daytype, outcomes, levels, events, decisions, decision_rows, level_events), the end-state digest and the engine stage's counts equal the old code's on all 10 days, and on the three months 1–4 days equal the stored build's own files; with the hash seed fixed (2026-03-17, `PYTHONHASHSEED=0`) all eight files are byte-identical; tests `test_the_engine_speed_fix_changes_no_output`, `test_a_horizon_world_answers_as_the_whole_one_up_to_its_horizon`, `test_a_cached_horizon_world_is_never_read_past_its_horizon`. **Speed:** engine stage 11,658 → 3,062 s over the 10 days (3.8×; check days 2,241 → 393 s, 5.7×; 2025-10-10 6,124 → 1,492 s); all of it from (2), (1) skipped no build on these days. The build key changes (code), no output does. **Found on the way (not from the fix):** in `levels/` and `level_events/` rows that tie (e.g. two carried single prints with the same start) come out in an order that depends on Python's per-process string hashing; the same rows every build, only their order differs between processes (the old code against itself too). **Owner 2026-11-12:** both files' rows are now sorted by level uid (each level's own transitions in their order), in the same commit as the fix (the key changes anyway); `test_level_files_come_in_a_fixed_order`. **Zone widths (owner 2026-11-12, after `zone_widths.md`):** `max_zone_width` stays 60: a 40 cap adds 66% more events (zones +97%) on the 16 sampled sessions and leaves a lone QQQ node zone (41.3 pts by itself) unchanged, so it isn't the fix; next, read-only: how the QQQ node band width is defined and why, the QQQ node reaction distances from the 13a report against the NDXP rule (the smallest of ±10 / 15 / 20 covering q0.8), and, if a narrower QQQ band is supported, what it changes on the same 16 sessions. Answered (owner 2026-11-12): QQQ `node_band` ±0.375, Q-step13a-2 (7). **(f) Build speed-ups (owner 2026-11-12, after the two-builds-at-once costing; none changes an output):** (1) **now, before 13c: a rolling pass-2 pool**, and the scheduling out of the build key. Pass 2 is one chunk (`p2:<first>~<last>:<key>:<root>`): up to the plan's `batch` sessions run at once as memory allows (free memory less the 4-GB reserve and what each running session may still grow by to the expected peak), the next starting as soon as any finishes, longest first by its pass-1 build time (the fixed batches of up to 6 waited for each batch's slowest session: measured parallelism 1.7 on the months 1–4 build). A stop request, `--until` or a failing session stops new starts; the running sessions finish; the chunk re-runs next time with only the sessions not done. The job, its chunks, the child processes, the pool and the build report are `checks/feature_build_job.py`, outside `CODE_PARTS` (`feature_build.code_files()`; test `test_scheduling_is_outside_the_build_key`): every session is still built by `features build-day D --pass N` from its checkpoint (inside the key), so the pool decides only when a session runs. Tests: the pool (longest first, never more than `batch` at once, rolling), a failure or stop stopping new starts and the resume, the memory headroom. **Proof on real data:** the 7 check days built by the new code equal the e4367ed code's file for file (every pass-2 file incl. the engine stage, byte-identical, end states equal) at the same configuration. (2) **then, in the main folder alongside 13c's first runs: two builds at once** (a lock the builds share while data pulls take it alone, one machine-wide memory and core budget, resume and the queue running both), with tests; it is outside the key, so it can be switched on between jobs without renaming any build; **switched on only with the owner's OK.** **As built (2026-11-12; committed 2026-11-13 after the 13c baseline build and labels finished and the full suite passed, owner OK; the switch stays off until the owner decides after a real-data proof):** build slot 2 = job `feature_build_2` with its own plan (`nqbot features plan --months 1-4 --set k=v --slot 2`, `<data_dir>/jobs/feature_build_2/plan.json`), state, queue entry, children (`--slot 2`) and report (`step11/feature_build_2.md`); slot 1 is unchanged (`feature_build`, its children's arguments identical); two slots never plan or run the same build key, and a running slot can't be re-planned. The switch is `parallel_builds: true` in config/local.yaml (default false; kept out of the settings, so out of every build key; read at the start of each run). On: a build holds the Skylit and Databento locks **shared** (one file per holder beside the lock; a job holding a lock alone is refused while any build holds it shared, and the reverse; each side creates its file before looking for the other, so the two never overlap) plus its own run lock `<data_dir>/jobs/<job>/run.lock`; **one machine-wide budget**: all builds' sessions together within `max_workers` (all cores but 2), the memory headroom counting what every build's running sessions may still grow by, and each build that wants sessions keeps its fair share (cores ÷ builds wanting) free, so one build's pass 1 isn't starved by the other's pass-2 pool (each build publishes its sessions in `<data_dir>/jobs/build_budget/`, starts decided under its mutex; the warm-up goes through the same pool); **`nqbot resume`** runs the first two queued builds at once, each in its own `nqbot run` process, leaves one already running alone, and goes on past them only when both are complete. Off: everything as before (exclusive locks, one build at a time). `nqbot status`, `nqbot unlock` and the build pin show the shared holders and run locks. The build key is unchanged (code digest identical to b2c0f3c's). Tests `tests/test_parallel_builds.py`.
- **Q-step13-review (independent review before 13c, 2026-11-13, owner's request; three fresh agents checked 92153f0..959293a, the commits since Q-step11h: 13-0, 13a, 13b's engine and labeler, the speed fix, the QQQ band, the rolling pass-2 pool, two builds at once and the status fix, against CLAUDE.md and this SPEC). Fixed with regression tests (`tests/test_review_jobs.py`, `tests/test_review_step13b_jobs.py`, `tests/test_review_untested.py`), outside both keys (the build and labels code digests unchanged):** (1) `nqbot stop` found only exclusive locks, so a build with `parallel_builds` couldn't be stopped: it now finds the run lock and shared holds; (2) the pass-2 memory check read the uv venv's launcher process (4 MB; the session is its child), so every running session counted as not yet grown and pass 2 ran about 4 sessions instead of the plan's 6 (e62b6a80d4ad: 4 at once for 97.8% of pass 2): the largest peak in the process tree counts; (3) sessions were left running when the pool raised (Windows doesn't end a dead parent's children) and the next run could start the same sessions beside them: the pool ends its sessions on any exception, a budget-mutex timeout drains the running sessions as a clean stop, and each run first ends any session a dead run of its slot left (children carry a tag with the data root and slot); (4) a pass-2 failure with the SSD gone ended `failures` (waits for the owner): now `ssd_missing` (resumed by itself); (5) pass 2 is one chunk, so restarts during it counted as crashes while sessions finished (held after `resume_max_crashes`): the pool records finished sessions in `jobs/<job>/pool_progress.json` and resume counts them as progress; `nqbot status` shows `pass 2: n/m sessions done`; (6) the queue showed a running build's previous end until its first chunk finished: every queued job is checked for a live holder; (7) §11's next-unit-fits-before-`--until` check now applies to each pass-2 session (p90 of the recorded session times); (8) `nqbot status` names the `local.yaml` it read and warns when the pinned worktree's copy has another `parallel_builds` value; (9) two starters taking over the same dead lock could both hold it: the dead file is renamed away (one rename wins) and put back if it turned out to be a live holder's; (10) the pin could move while a job started (the old one-off check): `scripts/build_worktree.ps1` records the move (`build-pin move-begin --pid`) before the no-job check and ends it after `build-pin set`; `nqbot run` / `resume` refuse while it lives, and resume checks before each job that no move runs and the commit is the one it started on; (11) resume's child output goes to `<root>/logs/` (was the internal drive's temp folder); (12) with two builds, pass 0/1 waits for memory the other build's pass 2 holds instead of ending the night; labeler side: a failed identity check is written beside the day summary (`<day>.identity_failed.json`), never as the summary the job takes as done, and the report lists it; slippage day files carry a digest of the settings they read and are made again when it differs; tests added for the 13d zone comparison, the `mbp-1` reader and the labeler's path summary and structures reached. **Key-changing findings: owner 2026-11-13: L1–L5 and B1–B5 built with tests (`tests/test_review_keys.py`, `tests/test_events_engine.py`), `features_version` 39 (B1–B3 change events and labels), one months 1–4 rebuild, then the labels once on the new build key (no labels re-run on e62b6a80d4ad); B2 counted read-only first, so its change went into the same rebuild:** labels key: (L1) decision points after flat-by (1,149 of 33,468 on e62b6a80d4ad; none tradeable) get an exit before their fill (`timeout_at` = flat-by + latency; 232,769 pairs), owner: such a fill times out at the session end (`end_ns`) or a news flatten before it (`timeout_reason` `session_end`), for the §1.2 out-of-window report; (L2) the top of book is treated as in force after the `mbp-1` file ends (16:05): past its covered end a marketable limit should be `no_book` and a stop-limit fill at its trade (Q-step13b (a) (3)); built: `TopOfBook.at` is None more than `BOOK_END_TOL_NS` (1 s) after the file's last record; 1,892 marketable limits and 1,710 stop-limits filled 16:05-17:00 on a stale book; (L3) a stop-limit can fill better than the trade that triggered it (the book read after the whole sweep at that timestamp; 7 RTH pairs, 1-3 ticks): cap at the trigger trade; (L4) `rr_band` hard-codes 0.5 / 0.8 (should use `pair_build_min_rr` and a named 0.5); (L5) refuse an `entry_ttl_sec` + latency longer than the 300-s band track. Build key: (B1) lookahead in style (c): `rearm_time` follows a merge of the event's zone without checking the merge time, so a merge hours later cuts the audit's firings at the absorbing zone's earlier re-arm; (B2) the band track's cross (and the event's own structure label) is read on the session world including the touch group's own retested extreme, the edges without it; the count on e62b6a80d4ad (read-only, all 84 sessions; 319 events whose touch group made one, every such group a single event): the band track differs at 6 of 33,468 decision points (5 style (d), 1 (c); 5 tradeable; 1 within the 60-s time-to-live + latency, not tradeable), the 30-pt structure label for 4 of 22,121 events (held → broke 2, timeout → broke 1, broke → held 1; 3 tradeable), the 20-pt pair cut for none; owner: fixed in this rebuild: an event's own structure label, its pair cut and its band track's cross read the world its zone edges come from (`SessionFeatures.own_cross` / `own_outcome`: this session's before the touch group's own retested extreme, the session without it from then); the retested extremes themselves (`retest_hold_pts`) and the outcome history that later events and the next session read (`history_hold_pts`) stay as built (the one world live has, so pass 1 and the carried state are unchanged by it); the world without the extreme is built to the group's latest label-window end + 300 s, so one build serves the edges, the band track and the labels; after the rebuild the change is checked against the count (6 band-track readings, 4 structure labels, nothing tradeable within the time-to-live, pair cuts unchanged); (B3) RTH damage that is only excess empty minutes never drops an event (none in the window today); built: a damaged RTH part with excess empty minutes (no minute identity, also beside gaps) counts as damaged over its whole span (`DayDamage.rth_unlocated`, padded like a gap); (B4) the labeler-only settings `order_latency_sec`, `max_target_candidates`, `pair_build_min_rr`, `stop_merge_ticks` are in the build key (a later change would rebuild for nothing); (B5) build-key holes: code that shapes outputs outside `CODE_PARTS` (`checks/step8b.skylit_base`, `clients/skylit_multiexp`, `clients/ndx_sources`, `clients/databento_contracts`, `warmup.py`, `gameplan/overrides|schema|validate`, `config.py`). Also noted: book records ordered by `ts_event` rather than Databento's `ts_recv` (closed 2026-11-14: counted read-only on every stored file of months 1–4 before the labels ran: `mbp-1` 80 files, 692.4 M records; NQ trades 84 files, 31.0 M; ES trades 84 files, 40.9 M; no record's `ts_event` (trades: `ts_event`, then sequence) goes backwards in the file's own order, so the loaders' sorts keep the file order and neither the build nor the labels can be affected; one ES sequence step back with the time going forward, on the degraded day 2025-11-28); `label_one`'s input selection outside the labels key (orchestration only): owner 2026-11-13: moved into the labels key (`labeler/session.py`). **Results (2026-11-14):** months 1–4 rebuild b958fe6e42aa (`features_version` 39, commit 9ba7f88; 84/84 sessions, 86/86 chunks; stopped once at 02:21 in pass 2 by Claude Code's own low-memory stop of its background shell (6 sessions of 3.0–4.1 GB plus other programs; the pool's 4 GB check had passed and Windows logged no low-memory event), resumed 03:45 from a scheduled task, done 04:31); B2 against e62b6a80d4ad exactly as counted: 6 band-track readings (5 (d), 1 (c); the one within the 60-s time-to-live + latency not tradeable), 4 structure labels (held → broke 2, timeout → broke 1, broke → held 1; 3 tradeable), no pair cut; B1: 134 more style-(c) decision points (31 tradeable), none removed, 67 events' firings changed, events with > 5 firings 194 → 201 (max 13); everything else identical: 22,121 events, every shared decision point and style-(c) decision row, `level_events` on all 84 days; labels 8e9cdad94970 (`--firings-ok`; 84/84 sessions, identity check identical on all, 7.24 M pairs, 35 min, 0.16 GB); the gap rule's changed market exits 6.1% → 4.3% (3,719,414 → 3,283,554 exits) traced to L1/L2 only: fills before flat-by on shared decision points identical (3,026,798 exits, 141,448 changed), fills at or after flat-by 692,616 → 246,870 exits, none tradeable (157,352 fewer fills after 16:05 without the stale book, L2; 288,478 now end at their stop or target before the session end, L1; the rest time out at the session end with no book and no trade after, so the rule changes none), B1's new decision points +9,886 exits / +367 changed; `step13 counts` re-run on b958fe6e42aa (the 876bff98c2ab report kept as `counts_876bff98c2ab.md`): golden-zone counts identical, the flip-checkpoint and build-count changes all from the QQQ band (e62b6a80d4ad and b958fe6e42aa agree there). - **Q-step13c (step 13c, the tuning on months 1–4, rows 1–14; owner 2026-11-14). (a) Decided by the owner 2026-11-14 (plan approved): (1) breakeven: every row is decided on the bootstrap policy's `r_none` (stop or target or timeout); the run is repeated on `r_fs` (the owner's first-structure rule) for information, and a row where `r_fs` would have picked a different winner is flagged; nothing is decided on `r_fs`. (2) Row 4 `sweep_reclaim_bars` is scored on a style-(c)-only sequential run per value (§7.1: "scored on their style's trades only"); because the value also changes when highs/lows count as crossed, the (d)-only and combined net R are reported beside each value for information, and the row is flagged if the (c) winner makes (d) clearly worse than the start value (the day-block 95% interval of the (d)-only difference excluding 0); nothing is decided on those numbers. (3) Row 13 is the full 5 × 2 grid: `stop_buffer` {1, 2.5, 5, 8, 12} × {fixed `stop_buffer_floor`, the volatility-scaled floor}; the best buffer per floor by the best-value rule (start 5), then the vol-scaled floor replaces the fixed one only by the keep-current rule (Q13-vol); scored on the (c) and (d) single-style policies summed ("pooled (c)+(d) trades"), at the row-12 winners. (4) The provisional structure model's label: held = 1, broke and timeout = 0 (P(held); the fold bar is its median). (5) **The score of every 13c row (declared before any result; replaces "total net R per month" for step 13c):** the **excess net R per month** of a value over the start value, (R_value − R_start) − r̄_start × (N_value − N_start), R = the run's net R, N = its filled trades, r̄_start = the start run's mean net R per trade; the 95% day-block interval resamples whole days with r̄_start recomputed inside each replicate. It decides the best-value rows (1–5, 12, 13's buffer) and the keep-current rows (6–11, Q13-vol, row 14 when event-changing), whose intervals are on the same score. Each trade counts at its margin over the start policy's average trade: removing average trades scores 0, removing worse-than-average ones or adding better-than-average ones scores positive. **Reason (the thinning test, owner's request, read-only on the baseline b958fe6e42aa / labels 8e9cdad94970, 20 random thinnings per level, the same predictions):** with the start-value policy losing (−31.3 R/month), the total net R rewards trading less: 10% of events removed (trades −1%, the freed slots refill) thinned ahead 7/20; 30% (trades −6%) 16/20, mean +2.6 R/month; 50% (trades −14%) 20/20, mean +5.7 (interval above 0 in 2/20); so rows 1–5 (best value, no interval) would drift to values that trade less. The excess: 7/20, 12/20 (mean +0.8), 14/20 (mean +1.4), its interval never above 0; net R per trade was also neutral (7, 12, 14 of 20) but ignores volume; shared events only was biased the other way (thinned ahead 0/20 at every level, interval below 0 in 12/20 at 50%: the freed slots fill with other shared losing trades). (6) Reported beside each value, for information only: the total net R difference, net R per trade (start → value and the difference), the change in filled trades (count and share), and the (c)-only and (d)-only excess (each style's own single-style run against the start's); flags: any winner with more than 10% fewer trades than the start value, any detector row (1–3, 5) where the (c)-only and the (d)-only excess would pick different winners, row 4 as (2), the `fs` disagreement as (1). (7) Row-12 tie-break: the same rule as every other row (Q-step13plan (8): closest to the start value, then the larger lower bound, then the start stays; §7.1 freezes the execution parameters "the same way" and gives no other rule), with the distance of an (order type, ttl) point from the start point: fewer things changed first (only the ttl, then only the order type, then both), then |ttl − start ttl| in seconds. (8) The vol-scaled floor applies to QQQ-sourced stops only; NDXP keeps its fixed 6.50 in both arms. Reason: NDXP's crossing error barely grows with volatility (Spearman 0.06 between |error| and the trailing 15-minute range on the 1,223 relevant-node crossings of the five October recordings, against QQQ's 0.29, findings R12), and a fit would rest on those 5 recorded days (no months 1–4 data: NDXP's history spot is an NQ price); by the same rule it would be max(4.68, 0.425 × vol15), covering 95.1% like the fixed 6.50. NDXP sets stop 2's far edge on 8.7% of the start policy's trades (QQQ 19.1%, chart 72.2%). (b) As built (owner 2026-11-14: (iii) the left-out day-type and gameplan inputs and the early stopping with the embargo, (iv) strictly above the median approved; the rest for the owner's OK before row 1 runs): (i) the driver is one job, `step13_tune` (`checks/step13c.py`, `tuning/`; `nqbot step13 tune-plan`, `nqbot run step13_tune`), whose chunks are static and resolved at run time from the winners so far (`<data_dir>/jobs/step13_tune/winners.json`); within a row the challenger builds run two at a time in build slots 1 and 2 (child `nqbot run feature_build[_2]` with the same `--until`; a build already complete is skipped); labels by build key (`step13 labels-day D --build-key K --label-key L`, four sessions at once); the driver holds the data locks shared (needs `parallel_builds`) and skips the p90 chunk-fit rule (`p90_fit = False`: its children stop themselves at `--until`); nothing is written to features.yaml / settings.yaml: the winners go there with `frozen_from: months 1-4` after the owner reads `<reports_dir>/step13/tune/tune_summary.md`. (ii) Before row 1 (`r00`): the baseline is labelled again under the current labeler (the Q13-vol code changed the labeler, so its key: 8e9cdad94970 → 1c11656c2474) and must equal 8e9cdad94970 file for file (pairs, fills, paths); the baseline is scored against itself (every event shared, difference 0, the model fitted twice identically); the vol-scaled floor is re-fitted on every months 1–4 session with 1-second boards by the step-8b study's rule (the 95th percentile of |error| in the quietest quartile of the trailing 15-minute volatility as the minimum, the smallest k covering 95% of the crossings), with the frozen mapping values. (iii) Provisional model: the touch-row columns minus ids, times, absolute prices (touch and last price, zone and golden-zone edges, fib prices, VWAP values, King strikes), the day-type model's own inputs (`dt_*`; the structure model gets the day type as P(trend), left out at step 13), the gameplan features (`gp_*`, off until the step-20 check) and all-null columns; strings categorical (codes over the build's values); trained on events tradeable at the touch and undamaged, each touch group weighted 1 / its trained events; 4 folds by ledger month, a one-session embargo each side of the predicted month; early stopping on an inner block, the last 20% of the training sessions (never the predicted month), then a refit on all training sessions with that round count. (iv) The bar: per fold, each build's median P(held) over the shared events (both tradeable at the touch and undamaged); an event trades if its P(held) is strictly above it and it is undamaged. (v) The sequential run: one account (`gate_account`), the days in order, decision points by `decision_ts`, same moment (c) before (d); a decision point trades only if tradeable at `decision_ts` (the labeler's flag: setup windows, `entry_end`, blackouts, dislocation blocks) and the account has no position or pending entry (else skipped, never queued); a touch group trades once (after a fill; an unfilled removal frees it); an entry keeps the account busy to its removal (`removal_t`, latency included) or exit; an unfilled entry counts 0 R; a `no_book` entry is skipped and counted; bootstrap pair = stop candidate 2 (a merged "1,2" counts) with the nearest target at net RR ≥ 1 (not the 1:2.5 baseline, not the low-RR extension), the style's order type (start values `entry_order_type`; after row 12 its winners). (vi) Shared events are matched session by session (a touch index is a session's own). (vii) Per month = the run's total ÷ 4 (months 1–4); intervals: 2,000 day-block replicates (whole sessions, the same draw for both values), percentile 95%. (viii) The lead over the next-best grid point, on the excess (5): the winner's excess over its start run minus the next-best point's over its own (the start counting 0), the same day draw for both, with its interval (the start runs of the two comparisons differ only by their bars); reported for information. (ix) Row 12: one labeler run per ttl sets both styles' ttl (the start ttls of (c) and (d) are both 60); ties as (a) (7). (x) Q13-vol: QQQ-sourced stops only (candidates 2 and 3 beyond a QQQ member's edge), as (a) (8); chart levels 0; the floor's trailing volatility is the mean high-low of the 1-minute NQ bars completed in the last 15 clock minutes before `decision_ts` (the labeler's `trailing_range`, also used for the fit); it is a labeler override (`stop_floor_vol`), not a setting, so the build key is unchanged. (xi) Row 14 by log loss: the per-event weighted log loss summed per day, the difference of the two ratios with whole days resampled; several values clearing the rule: the most negative upper bound; the events and structure labels of each challenger build must equal the start build's (else the row stops for the owner); if `node_relevance_top_n` = 5 won row 9, row 14 is scored as event-changing rows (labels, net R on the shared events, keep-current rule). (c) **Finding for step 15** (read-only, 2026-11-14, the baseline b958fe6e42aa, labels 8e9cdad94970, breakeven none, R per month over months 1–4): the bootstrap policy at the start values, with the provisional model's median filter: combined −31.3 (669 trades: (c) −1.4 on 29, (d) −29.9 on 640), (c) alone +5.5 (255 trades, 49.8% winners, +0.086 R/trade), (d) alone −30.8 (681 trades, 37.7% winners, −0.181 R/trade; median net RR 1.18, break-even 46%: passive limits at the near edge fill when price trades through, adverse selection); without the filter (every undamaged event): combined −26.1 (983 trades: (c) +0.6 on 94, (d) −26.7 on 889), (c) alone +5.6 (416 trades, 47.6% winners), (d) alone −26.4 (1,009 trades, 40.7% winners). Two things to carry to step 15: **the median filter makes (d) worse** (−30.8 with it, −26.4 without; for (c) it is neutral), and **(d) crowds (c) out of the combined run** ((c) trades 255 alone, 29 combined: (d) decides at the touch and holds the account by the time (c)'s audit or reclaim fires, "the first eligible style trades", §7.1). Neither changes a 13c row (the rows compare values on the same pre-declared policy, scored by (5)). **Finding for step 15 (owner 2026-11-16, read-only, no change to any winner):** in r02 (`confluence_tol`) the (c)-only excess for 5 is +4.41 R/month [+0.18, +8.80], the first interval in 13c to exclude zero (none of r01's do), while the combined score keeps 10 (5: −3.38 [−11.50, +4.54]), because (d) crowds (c) out of the combined run (above). It is one of the 24 intervals reported in r01–r02 (2 rows × 3 values × combined, total, (c)-only, (d)-only), so a lead to test, not a result. With the crowding finding above, step 15 considers whether (c) should be tuned or traded separately from (d) (its own detector values, or its own account / turn so (d) can't take the account first); report `<reports_dir>/step13/tune/r02_confluence_tol.md`. (d) **Run note (owner 2026-11-15, job-side, outside both keys):** the first two runs (2026-11-14 20:04 and the 02:05 resume) stopped at 01:42 and 02:27 ET on row 1's builds, not at `--until` (the children had 07:25 / 07:30 EST): the two-builds budget mutex stuck, because Windows refuses to delete a file another process has open (the waiting build reads the holder every 50 ms) and `JobLock.release` ignored the failed delete, so a live build kept it and both stopped after 60 s, recorded as `until`. Fixed: a release retries a refused delete for `RELEASE_WAIT_SEC` (5 s), then logs and raises `LockReleaseFailed`; the budget mutex takes back its own process's leftover (`reclaim_own`; never a lock a JobLock of the process holds); a mutex still locked after 60 s, or one that could not be released, stops with its own status `budget_stall` (passed through the pool and the driver, resumed by `nqbot resume` like `until`). No session output was lost (finished sessions are kept).**
- **Q-step14a (step 14a part 0, the Control Panel's stack and design pass; owner 2026-11-14). ✅ Stack decided by the owner 2026-11-14; written into §9b and §11; docs only, nothing built yet; the design system and component picks wait for the owner.** (1) **Stack:** the 21st.dev components the owner wants are React + TypeScript + Tailwind + shadcn/ui (Radix), which Streamlit's rerun-per-interaction model can't host (no persistent canvas or WebGL, animations reset on every rerun), so the panel is a React single-page app (Vite, Tailwind, shadcn/Radix, Motion, Lightweight Charts) served with a JSON API and server-sent events by Starlette + uvicorn on 127.0.0.1:`ui_port`; Streamlit is dropped (removed from the dependencies when the panel is built). (2) **Builds per machine:** `ui/web/dist/` gitignored, `nqbot ui build` on each machine (the repo guard blocks tracked files over 1 MB, and generated files would clutter every UI commit); `nqbot ui` refuses a missing or stale SPA build.
