    - no native `title` tooltips anywhere: CreamFlow's own styled hint (look of 21st.dev 207);
    - no horizontal scrollbar on any page;
    - `scrollbar-gutter: stable both-edges`, so no page shifts sideways when a scrollbar appears, and every page is
      exactly centred.
  - **Accounts page and dock:**
    - the accounts list and the editor fade-and-slide into each other (220 ms, reversed on close; Calm instant);
    - the dock magnifies with springs on transforms in fixed slots (1.2×).
  - **Home:**
    - the hint shares the date block's line;
    - the wordmark runs at 60 fps idle in Full: an exception to the MASTER §8 CPU budget; Calm is the saving;
    - node headers are fixed on their points (no layout animation);
    - hover cards are overlays towards the wordmark, inside the scene, fading and scaling in over 180 ms and out over
      120 ms, with a lift and cream glow on their own hover;
    - a label lights up as one: every part on one 180-ms transition;
    - a banner arriving or leaving starts a glide in the same frame as the banner's own animation (270 ms,
      cubic-bezier(0.4, 0.55, 0.6, 1)). The nodes move and the lines morph point by point. The wordmark keeps its
      banner-free size while banners show. The layouts and lines of 0–3 stacked banners are prepared ahead of time in
      idle slices.
- **Q-perf-mc (✅ decided by the owner 2026-11-15; step 14 build stage 3):** the Monte Carlo's headline is (a), whole
  days drawn with replacement (the fan, the chance of reaching the floor, the median day). (b), the same days
  reordered, is a secondary "order luck" line. Both draw days from the strategy's backtest or simulator ledger (the
  guarded development days), never from one account's own few trades.
  - **Backtest / simulator phases:** by account type (every preset), each type's rules applied: risk and cap, drawdown
    type, size and lock, daily caps. A comparison strip shows one row per type.
  - **Real-trades phases (demo, prop, live):** one actual account "from today": its current balance and floor (from
    the engine's account state from step 22, else its trades in the ledger), picked by the page's account filter or,
    with All accounts, the focused account.
  - **Drawdown:** the same two modes.
- **Q-perf-plans (✅ owner 2026-11-15; step 14 build stage 3):** the strategy view works by plan and its lifecycle,
  replacing the per-stage account types.
  - **Plans:** MFFU Rapid 25k, MFFU Builder 25k, MFFU Pro 50k, Lucid Flex 25k, plus saved presets, grouped by name.
  - **Monte Carlo, one plan at a time:** every path starts as the plan's evaluation. On the profit target it continues
    under the funded rules, with a marker. A floor ends it.
    - X-axis: 30 / 60 / 120 trading days (default 60).
    - Headline: pass rate, median days to pass, the chance the funded account survives to the last day, and the median
      result at the end (the funded P&L; 0 if never passed).
    - Order luck is kept, with "payouts not modelled".
    - "Compare plans" shows one row per plan, off by default.
  - **Drawdown:** the backtest replayed through the lifecycle, a fresh evaluation after every floor, with the count of
    evaluations used.
  - **My-account mode** is unchanged.
  - **Rework 3 (owner 2026-11-15; built, waiting for the owner's OK):**
    - **Plan selector:** backtest phases price every number by the plan (KPIs, stats, Equity, Drawdown, Monte Carlo,
      Calendar, Trades, CSV; R unchanged); the synthetic backtest is one strategy ledger; real-trade phases tie the
      account filter to the top-bar switcher both ways.
    - **Lifecycle with payouts** (`performance/lifecycle.py`, the step 15-16 simulator's engine too): evaluation →
      target (with `min_trading_days`) → funded → payouts at the maximum whenever the §6.7 payout rules allow → a floor
      fails the account and a new evaluation starts, until 30 / 60 / 120 trading days. Fees: `evaluation_fee_usd`
      (each evaluation started) and `activation_fee_usd` (each pass); in the presets since 2026-11-16 (Q-perf-fees).
    - **Numbers:** evaluations used, pass rate, days to pass, payouts taken and their total, fees, net cash (the
      trader's share after `profit_split_pct` − fees), medians with 10-90% ranges; the fan is the balance.
    - **Readings to confirm:** (1) payouts are taken at the maximum withdrawable as soon as every rule allows, so with
      a capped payout (Builder, Lucid $1,000) the balance can climb above the payout line and the next payout comes
      as soon as its cycle rules allow (Builder: 2 days and the 50% rule; Lucid: 5 days of $100+), not after another
      $1,000 of new profit; (2) the 14-day clock in the Monte Carlo counts 5 sessions a week (the replay uses the real
      dates); (3) a pass or floor on the horizon's last day doesn't start (or charge) a new account; (4) not modelled:
      scaling tiers, the consistency dip rule, `payout_clock_hold`; the payout that reaches a cap group's cap closes
      the account and ends the path.
- **Q-perf-fees (✅ owner 2026-11-16):** what the owner pays (discounted), one-time, no monthly billing, no activation fee:
  MFFU Rapid EOD 25k evaluation $97.20, MFFU Builder 25k evaluation $63.00, MFFU Pro 50k evaluation $159.00, Lucid
  Flex 25k evaluation $50.40. Preset fields (and the account schema): `evaluation_fee_usd` with
  `evaluation_fee_billing: one_time` on each evaluation stage, `activation_fee_usd: 0` on every stage. The plan
  lifecycle charges the price once per evaluation started; the Performance page shows net cash (payouts to you − fees)
  as the real figure. Reports and the lifecycle only; nothing in trading reads them.
- **Q-perf-eval (✅ owner 2026-11-16, rework 4; replaces the 2026-11-15 reading):** the my-account "from today" Monte
  Carlo covers only the account's own stage, from where it stands today (a passed evaluation becomes a new funded
  account with its own ID). Evaluation: each path runs to its profit target (passed, with the minimum trading days) or
  its floor (failed) or the horizon; headline: the chance of passing before failing, the median days to each. Funded:
  payouts as its rules allow (eligibility, minimum days, consistency, thresholds and maximum, the floor lock after a
  payout) until its floor or the horizon; headline: the chance of the next payout before the floor, the median days
  to it, the payouts over the horizon. "P(reaching the floor from here)" is removed everywhere. Built in
  `performance/account_mc.py` on the lifecycle engine (`start=`, `restart=False`). Not known before step 22: where a
  funded account's current payout cycle started after a payout (taken as today until the engine records it).
- **Q-perf-all (owner 2026-11-16; approved and built 2026-11-16):** an "All accounts" view in the real-trades phases
  (demo, prop, live), also in the top-bar switcher (focus `all`): every account's trades together (KPIs, stats, the
  equity curve, calendar, trades with their account), and under the KPIs a per-account breakdown (account, firm, plan,
  stage, status, start / end, net P&L, payouts to the trader, fees paid, net cash; sortable; a row click focuses the
  account) with totals across every account ever. Drawdown and Monte Carlo stay per account, with a note: the last
  single account selected (the page's filter, the top bar or a breakdown row; kept per browser), else the most recently
  active one (latest trade). **Accounts are never deleted once they have trades:** a new account field `status` (active,
  passed, failed, closed; with `started` / `ended`); every finished account (passed, failed or closed) = archived: off the Accounts page, which lists active accounts only
  (owner 2026-11-16: no "Show archived" toggle; a past account is reached from Performance only: each row of the "All
  accounts" breakdown has a small Edit action that opens it in the editor, archived ones included, where its status can
  be set back to active if it was archived by mistake; the editor's back button then returns to Performance; the
  synthetic stand-ins, not in the accounts file, have no Edit), listed under "Archived" in the top bar (Q-perf-lists),
  always in "All accounts" and its totals; deleting is refused while the ledger holds trades for it.
  Finished accounts no longer count toward a firm's account limit (§6.7). Fees per account: the account's own fee
  fields, else its preset's. Saving an evaluation as passed asks "add its funded account?": Add opens a new account
  filled from the same plan's funded preset (its own name, §6.7: a passed evaluation becomes a new funded account);
  Not now closes. A passed evaluation opened later shows the same offer in its editor.
- **Q-home-return, Q-banners (owner 2026-11-16; built):** returning to Home after the session's first-open intro plays
  a short return entrance (about 360-450 ms): the wordmark fades in with a slight scale-up (0.98 to 1, 300 ms), the six
  nodes fade in 40 ms apart in their arrival order and each line draws out to the word (220 ms) as its node arrives;
  the previous page fades out where it is; a click or a key finishes it at once; Calm: Home complete in its first
  frame. Off Home, a banner arriving unfolds its height on the same 270-ms curve as on Home and as a banner leaving,
  so the page below glides down with it, stacked banners included; Calm: one step per banner. A critical banner
  (owner 2026-11-16) is fully visible from its first frame (MASTER §7: it never animates in): it is drawn over the
  content at full height, and the page glides down beneath it on the same 270-ms curve to make room, so nothing is
  covered once the glide ends (on Home the scene's own banner glide already does this).
- **Q-pages (owner 2026-11-16; built):** one rule for every page change (Home, Accounts, Settings, Performance, the
  account editor): the current page stays on screen until the next page's data is loaded, then the next page
  crossfades in already filled (no placeholder, no card filling in) and plays its entrance as it is revealed, every time
  it opens (it rises in; Performance's numbers roll in, its cards stagger in and its equity curve draws in; a switch
  inside a page only glides its numbers; owner 2026-11-16, second round); the page that leaves fades out exactly where
  it is (its scroll never moves; the next page opens at its own top, placed there while hidden; no scroll anchoring),
  and the scrolling pages share one footer that glides into place with the tab switch's height glide (driven
  frame by frame, so a late frame never carries most of the move). Hovering or focusing a dock icon, a Home node or a
  link to a page loads its data ahead, so most changes are immediate. Edit in Performance's breakdown goes straight to
  the filled account editor (never through the accounts list); the editor's back returns to Performance on the phase it
  was on. A change slower than ~300 ms shows a thin progress line under the top bar. Calm: an instant swap. A save
  forgets every loaded answer (the next page change loads fresh).
- **Q-perf-switch (owner 2026-11-16; built):** a switch on the Performance page (account, and so phase, plan and dates
  too) never jumps: the current page stays on screen until everything the new selection needs has arrived (the page's
  data and the open tab's own data: Drawdown / Monte Carlo, the calendar month, the trades), with no loading
  placeholder; then it shows in one step: the KPI numbers roll to their new values, the tab's content crossfades, and a
  section that comes or goes (the "All accounts" breakdown, the stats box) opens or closes with the tab switch's height
  glide (0.25 s). A load slower than 300 ms shows a thin progress line between the header and the page (21st.dev Busy
  37649 pattern). Calm: the swap happens in one frame. Replaces Q-perf-lists' whole-body crossfade on an account switch.
- **Q-perf-lists (owner 2026-11-16; built):** the top bar's "All accounts" row shows its whole description (wrapped)
  and a summary like an account row's: "n active · m archived", today's combined P&L (from the engine, "—" before step
  22) and the all-time net cash (payouts - fees across every account ever, real-trades phases, each account once; the
  status heartbeat, recomputed at most every 30 s). Both account lists (the top bar and the Performance page's menu)
  show at most 10 accounts before they scroll (and never run under the dock), active accounts first, then the
  archived ones under a small "Archived" heading (the top bar now lists archived accounts too); past 10 accounts a
  search field tops the list. An account switch on the Performance page shows its new body (KPIs, stats, breakdown,
  tabs) in one crossfade once its data has arrived, with the same height glide as the tab switch; Calm: instant.
- **Q-perf-ui7 (owner 2026-11-16; built, waiting for the owner's OK; variant A kept):** the Monte Carlo summary tightened
  (smaller padding and gaps, legend values beside their labels, "3 days", the backtest details in the header's
  tooltip), a calmer ring (124 px, 7 px stroke, a 19 px number); on the Performance page uppercase labels at 0.05 em,
  smaller and lighter than the numbers; the Drawdown stats rows light up on hover. **Panel-wide rules:** (1) in every
  list (dropdowns, menus, pickers, the command palette) nothing stays highlighted once the pointer leaves the list,
  the arrow keys still highlight (`tests/test_ui_hover_rule.py`); (2) a table of contents (Settings now): a clicked
  entry is active at once and stays so while the page scrolls to it, and at the bottom of the page the last section
  on screen is the active one (`lib/scrollspy.ts`), with no padding added; (3) every scrolling page has the same 80 px
  from the end of its content to the footer's "CREAMFLOW · NQ".
- **Q-perf-ui6 (owner 2026-11-16; built, waiting for the owner's OK):** profit factor with no losing trade shows "—" again
  (no ∞, no note, no tooltip); the calendar's month picker is our own dropdown (newest first, six visible, keys, prev /
  next) and the calendar follows the page's phase; a real account's Drawdown has one point per trade at its exit time
  from the account's start (the floor in force during each session; the summary's floor is the one set at the last
  close); the Monte Carlo summaries (account and plan) are redesigned in two variants (A: ring + position bar, B:
  partition bar + position bar), each with range-bar stat cards.
- **Q-perf-ui5 (owner 2026-11-16; built, waiting for the owner's OK):** every equity curve starts at $0 / 0R at the start of the
  period or the account (the price scale always includes it); real-trades phases plot one point per trade at its exit
  time, the trading days labelled along the bottom; KPI tiles show the dashed line with fewer than 2 points, no note under
  profit factor ∞. **Approved (owner 2026-11-16):** Drawdown's horizon = the first N days of the strategy replay and the
  last N days of a real account; a funded account's payout cycle is assumed to start today (with a note) until the
  engine records payouts at step 22. **New account field** `last_payout_date` (funded, optional, on the Accounts page):
  when set (or when a payout is recorded in `payouts`), the current payout cycle starts there (its days, profit days,
  calendar clock and starting balance); not a trading input.
- **Q-perf-ui4 (owner 2026-11-16; built, waiting for the owner's OK):** the Performance page has no "All phases"
  (Development by default); every phase uses one layout (the same KPI tiles, stats box, header and chart sizes) and the
  switch crossfades (200 ms) with gliding numbers (Calm: instant); the 30 / 60 / 120-day switch and zoom / pan are on
  every Monte Carlo and Drawdown chart; KPI sparklines are per trading day with the badge on the last 5 trading days
  against the earlier ones (one day: a dot, no badge); profit factor with no losing trade shows ∞; the synthetic
  accounts stay in states their rules allow.
- **Q-step14-review (step 14 build stage 4: the main chart, the GEX radial, the Review page; built 2026-11-16, waiting
  for the owner's OK; `docs/design/build_stage_4.md`):** a review **round** is drawn by `nqbot review round-new
  --build-key K --labels-key L [--n 60] [--seed S]` (read-only on the build, development months only, refused
  otherwise) into `<data_dir>/reviews/rounds/<round>/` (the round and one file per card); the answers go to
  `<data_dir>/reviews/answers_<round>.parquet` (§8), one row per card, final once the outcome was shown. A card holds
  what was known at its decision time T (`pre`: the NQ / ES 1-minute bars with the bar in progress built from the
  trades up to T only, every level's band and role (`review/roles.py`: role, provisional after a cross, settled at a
  flip, back at a reclaim), the zones as they stood at each decision point, the earlier touches with the labels known
  by T, the four maps once a minute over the replay span, the confirmed King's switches) and the rest of the path with
  the outcome (`post`); the server sends `post` only after the answer is stored, and every card read re-checks its
  month (§7 rule 2b). A style-(c) decision at a bar close (e.g. 12:05:00.000) shows the 12:04 bar as its last. The
  maps, the R in force and the King come from the bot's own code (`features/boards.py`, `features/node_switch.py`).
  The preview round `preview-b958` (60 cards, build b958fe6e42aa, labels 8e9cdad94970, months 1-4; drawn with
  `--preview`, Q-review-sample) is for the screenshots and a first look; the owner's round runs on 13d's months 1-6
  build (Q-step14a (16)).
- **Q-review-chart (owner 2026-11-16, stage-4 review; built, waiting for the owner's OK):** (1) each symbol fits its own
  candles: a switch between NQ and ES resets the price scale (a stretched axis never leaves the other symbol blank);
  (2) one Focus | Table switch, exactly one selected, the chart (Focus) when a card opens; the old "every level at full
  strength" view stays on the A key (decided in round 3: the key only, no button); (3) the GEX nodes are no longer level lines
  or a column of orbs at the clock: at every minute from 09:30, one small orb per node at its chart price (strike × the R
  in force), from the board known at that minute only, sized, lit and glowing by its share of that board's largest
  |value| (Pika gold, Barney orchid), the King the brightest with a ring (the reference
  `docs/design/inspiration/gex_chart_1.jpeg`); nothing after the replay clock or the decision; drawn from pre-rendered
  sprites into a cached layer, redrawn only when the view or the clock moves; (4) the card strip has arrows at both ends,
  fades its edges while there is more to scroll and keeps the current card in view, inside its own row; (5) at 1280 px and
  wider the chart card stretches to the height of the column beside it (no gap above the GEX snapshot and the readings),
  the replay bar sits under the chart's toolbar so it never moves under the dock (the plot's lower part may sit behind
  the open dock until scrolled); (6) hovering any drawn object (zone, level line, high / low, orb, touch, crown, the
  trade) shows its facts and price, all objects within 4 px in one card sorted by price; nothing in the empty room right
  of the last bar; no OHLC box; the card never covers the price axis; the axis carries only the last price and the
  trade's entry, stop and target tags (the level names now show on hover and in the Table, no longer in a label column);
  (7) the chart and the replay slider start at the session's open, 18:00 ET the evening before (Restart still goes to
  the decision − 45 min); the overnight follows the same cut; (8) a skip-to-end button after step-forward and the End
  key: the decision before the answer, the end of the revealed path after it.
- **Q-review-chart round 3 (owner 2026-11-16; built, waiting for the owner's OK):** (1) the replay bar is back at the
  chart's bottom (the dock may cover it; the owner scrolls or minimizes the dock); (2) hover cards show only individual
  levels, highs / lows, node orbs and touch markers, each with its price (no zones: the setup's zone is in the panel on
  the right; no crowns or trade lines); (3) the detector readings list the confluences behind "Levels stacked": the
  scorer's own count per level type at the decision (`stack_count`, `n_<sub_type>`), and for each type the level(s)
  whose band at the decision lies in, or nearest, the zone, with the name, price (band centre), role and whether it was
  still provisional, all from the state at the decision only; a member the chart can't find is listed as missing, a
  type the counts don't name as "other"; the facts panel's "Stacked" line names the same members; (4) the GEX orbs are
  vertical ovals (every one taller than wide), the weakest about 80% of a bar wide so their rows read as near-continuous
  lines, both axes growing with the node's share, the glow softer (the King still the brightest), 60 fps at 4× kept.
  **Decided:** the every-level view stays on the A key only (no button), listed in the `?` shortcut sheet with the other
  Review keys; level names on hover and in the Table, the axis keeping only the last price, entry, stop and target
  (approved).
- **Q-review-chart round 4 (owner 2026-11-16; built, waiting for the owner's OK):** (1) every orb 20% smaller, the same
  proportions (vertical ovals; the weakest about 62% of a bar wide); (2) a hover card is measured before it is shown
  and placed right or left, below or above the pointer, wherever it fits whole inside the chart's pane; a card taller
  than the pane shows each level's latest role events with "+n earlier (all in the Table)", then fewer objects ("+n more
  here"); the Table gains a full Timeline column; (3) no King-switch crowns; (4) the stacked levels are a readings group
  like the others (name left, a muted note only for provisional / crossed back / flipped ×n, the price right coloured
  by role); (5) the replay bar on one centre line (buttons, speed, scrubber, clock; measured 0 px apart), the scrubber
  our own track, fill and thumb over a transparent native range, ticks at 09:30 and, after the answer, the decision;
  (7) only the 5 largest nodes (|value|, Pika and Barney together) of each ticker at each minute are drawn, from that
  minute's board only; NQ: QQQ and NDXP, ES: SPX (the SPXW chain) and SPY; the readings, the radial, the Table and the
  hover shares keep the whole board.
- **Q-review-stack (owner 2026-11-16, round 5: the display rule decided and built; exact recording a pre-13d item; step 14 build stage 4, round 4 (6)):** the scorer records which levels it
  stacked only as counts (`stack_count`, `n_<sub_type>`; `founding_level_ids` holds a level zone's founding members at
  its formation, not at the decision). How membership is decided (`features/session_features.py` `members_at`,
  `features/zones.py`): a **level zone's** members are the levels the zone book holds at that instant (two levels join
  when their bands overlap or their centres are within `confluence_tol`, within `max_zone_width`), and the zone's edges
  are the union of the members' bands, so every member's band lies inside the zone; a **container's** (golden zone,
  gameplan zone) members are every active level whose band overlaps it; the event's own retested extreme is left out
  in both. The Review card reconstructs the members per type from the stored level bands at the decision: the current
  rule (bands overlapping, then nearest) gives exactly the counted number on all 10 golden-zone cards of the preview
  round and on 33 of its 50 level-zone cards (17 have more overlapping levels of a type than counted). A read-only
  audit of a stricter rule for level zones (band inside the zone) gives the counted number on all 17 of those and on
  43 of the 50; the other 7 are VWAP lines whose band overlaps but is not inside by a hair (the card samples a VWAP
  band once a second, the scorer reads it at the trade). Proposed, not built (display only, no scorer change): level
  zones "inside, then overlapping to fill the count", containers "overlapping"; the card would then match every count
  in the preview round. Recording the counted `level_id`s in the scorer's rows would make it exact, but that changes
  the build (after 13c, the owner's decision).
  **Decided (owner 2026-11-16, round 5):** (a) the display rule is built (`review/cards.py` `stack_payload`: level
  zones inside first, then overlapping, then nearest; containers overlapping, then nearest; each item carries its
  `fit`; test `test_stacked_members_prefer_bands_inside_a_level_zone`); the stored preview round was updated in place
  by `nqbot review restack preview-b958` (only each card's `stack` field, checked; old files under
  `restack_backup/`; not a redraw, answers untouched): the listed levels are the same on all 60 cards (the old
  nearest-centre tie-break already chose the inside bands), now 105 inside and 7 overlapping on the level zones, 45
  overlapping and 4 inside on the golden zones, none outside. (b) **Pre-13d item (after 13c finishes, before 13d's
  months 1–6 build):** the scorer records which levels it counted at each decision point (their `level_id`s, beside
  `stack_count` / `n_<sub_type>`), so the real rounds show exact members. It adds a record only and changes no
  feature value: proved by building the same sessions before and after and comparing every build file (all
  existing columns equal; only the new column added). Not built now: no scorer or build change while 13c runs.
  (c) **Pre-13d item, retested-extreme age (owner 2026-11-16; a correction that changes features: `features_version`
  bump; not built now, no scorer or build change while 13c runs):** F3.8's "`retest_lookback_sessions` sessions after
  E's own session" counts from the session in which the extreme **printed**, not the session in which it was a
  registry level: a PDH/PDL or prior-RTH extreme printed the session before (card 10 of `preview-b958`: the
  2025-10-23 RTH low at 24,999.00, 09:31:40, a candidate as 10-24's prior RTH low; counted from 10-23, 11-07 is the
  11th session and the level would not exist), a prior-week extreme on its own day of the previous week; ON and IB
  extremes print in their own session (unchanged). Candidates and created levels both age by it. Read-only estimate
  (build b958fe6e42aa, months 1–4, first order only: dedupe and merge knock-ons not rebuilt): 142 of 2,265
  retested-extreme level-days removed (6.3%: PDH 36, PDL 41, prior RTH 45, prior week 20), 127 of 331 retested extremes
  affected on at least one day, 8 never made, 12 with an unresolved print session (prior-week extremes before the
  window or across the roll) left out; 1,284 of the 10,365 touches at zones with a retested extreme had an affected one
  in the zone at the touch (band overlap, an upper bound), of which 43 single-level zones (the touch would go), the rest
  keep the touch with one member fewer.

- **Q-review-chart round 8 (owner 2026-11-16; built, waiting for the owner's OK):** no description under the GEX

- **Q-step11c (step 11 part 1 review, 2026-10-31; an independent agent checked the step against CLAUDE.md and this SPEC):** fixed and tested (`tests/test_step11_review.py`): the IB high/low start as ceiling / floor at the 10:00 lock as F3.6 says (before, a level created near price got its role only from the first `rearm_dist` move, so an IB break from the lock was never recorded; the 10:30 replacement keeps the approach rule); a carried single print kept its `level_id` within a session but not into the next one (Q-step11b (3)); LVN smoothing returned a padded array on profiles narrower than the kernel, shifting LVN prices; LVN identity now rounds halves up; the provisional role ends at a confirmed cross back (F2.6 step 5; `provisional_role` reported false while broken back); today's ES ÷ NQ ratio applies from the later of the two first RTH trades, not 09:30:00.000 (F0.11); a contract change with no recorded roll spread is refused instead of left unshifted, and a spread off the tick grid is refused before anything moves; `rvol_today` / `vol_tod_w` stop at the early close; a level's machine stops at its own end; the global no-lookahead shift test now also covers the F5.18 approach features, `vol_tod_w` and the ES path (F5.18 and F9.10 require it). Nothing was built under the old code, so `features_version` stays 29. **Answered by the owner 2026-10-31 (`features_version` 30):** (1) approved: a high starts as a ceiling and a low as a floor whenever price is on the range side when the level appears (not beyond the band; the last trade at or before its creation, else the first trade after it), for the ON high/low, PDH/PDL, prior RTH and prior week highs/lows (NQ and the ES cross-market ON high/low and PDH/PDL), otherwise the approach rule; written into feature_spec F3.7 and F2.6 step 1, tested like the IB fix; (2) kept as built: a settled 5-minute bar can flip a level whose provisional role was broken back, without a resume; (3) ES levels get no volume-based cross features (`xb_vol_rel`, `xb_delta_rel` null; feature_spec F2.6b); ES volume baselines are a Q-v2 idea (feature freeze). The questions as asked: (1) the ON high/low (from 09:30) and PDH/PDL, prior RTH and prior week highs/lows (from 18:00) still take their role from the first `rearm_dist` move (F2.6 step 1), so a break that starts within `rearm_dist` of the band at creation isn't recorded as a cross; proposed: a high starts as a ceiling and a low as a floor when price is on the range side of the band at creation (the ON range always contains the 09:30 price; PDH/PDL and prior levels can be gapped through at 18:00, then the approach rule as now); built: only the IB (F3.6 states it). (2) A flip can come straight from the broken-back state (a settled 5-minute bar beyond on the crossed side while the provisional role is broken back, F2.6 step 3 "while crossed"), without a resume first; proposed: keep it (a settled 5-minute bar is a stronger confirmation than the resume's settled 1-minute bar). (3) ES cross-market levels get no `xb_vol_rel` / `xb_delta_rel` (F9.10's normals are NQ only); proposed: keep them null on ES (ES levels are cross-market features; the volume normals would need an ES baseline). (4) For the record, no change: the §3.2 damage count adds the long-gap minutes and the excess empty minutes, which can count the same empty minute twice, as the approved rule reads (Q-step11b (5); no session in the window is near the 30-minute line).
- **Q-step11b (step 11 part 1, 2026-10-31; points 1–7 approved as built by the owner 2026-10-31, written into feature_spec F3.8, F0.11, F3.5, F3.3, F2.6b and §3.2; `features_version` 28; point 8 answered the same day, `features_version` 29):** (1) a retested extreme ends only by age (`retest_lookback_sessions` after its own session), crossed or not; (2) ES levels before 09:30 use the previous session's ES ÷ NQ open ratio, today's from 09:30; (3) a single-print sub-run keeps its creation `level_id` while it shrinks; (4) VAH/VAL zones between profile instants are the last instant's, a price beyond its high/low is outside; (5) §3.2 union over series = the long-gap minutes once plus the largest excess-empty count; (6) no separate overnight profile: only the ON high/low are emptied; (7) F2.6b "since the cross" counts from a settled-bar cross's close. **(8) answered by the owner 2026-10-31 (`features_version` 29):** a reclaim needs the cross back confirmed by the level's own rule first (chart levels: a settled 1-minute bar on the original side; highs/lows: the reverse sweep resolved, past the window or beyond `sweep_max_depth`), then the `rearm_dist` trade (in the confirming bar or window, known at its close, or later); resume by the same rule on the crossed side; GEX nodes and gameplan zones unchanged (their own rule is a trade). Written into §5.2 and feature_spec F2.6 steps 4–5, F3.9, F5.2; tests in `tests/test_level_state.py`. A reverse break beyond `sweep_max_depth` (15) is always at least `rearm_dist` (10) past the edge, so it reclaims at once.
- **Q-step11a (step 11 part 1, answered by the owner 2026-10-31; feature_spec F2.6, F2.6b, F3.5; `features_version` 27, nothing built under 26):** (1) every level's state machine (crossed / flipped / reclaimed, post-cross retests, the F2.6b features, `cross_count_today`) runs from the level's creation, overnight included (18:00 for the levels known at the session start, 08:30 for the expected-move lines, 09:30 for the ON high/low and the 09:30 VWAP, 10:00 for the IB), so a cross before 09:30 is a real cross (§1.2: levels are tracked before the open as context); (2) `xb_vol_rel` / `xb_delta_rel` divide by the time-of-day normal over the seconds spent beyond the band (the stretches `xb_sec_beyond` counts); (3) only the RTH part of those seconds counts, volume and normal both (null with no RTH part); (4) a single-print band's upper edge is its top bin's upper bound (b + 1) × `tpo_tick_pts`.

So development months 1–6 and walk-forward month 7's lookback have **no dark-pool data**; full 45-day lists start about mid-May 2026. The step-7 darkpool check's "8/8 top-prints match" and "31-day call matches" compared empty with empty, and its 102 one-date answers (2025-10-31 … 2026-03-18) are cached as empty trading days, which would read as "no prints" rather than "no coverage". - **Step 7, Tempest timing (2026-10-07):** `/v1/vol/history` serves VIX (rows from 2025-08-28 with QQQ). Poll #1, started 16:08 ET 2026-10-07, found that day's rows already out for all 5 symbols, so it only shows "published by 16:08". **Owner 2026-10-07:** poll #2 runs with `step7 tempest-poll --today`, started during the session: it waits for that day's row, polling the free `/v1/vol/symbols` each minute, records whether the row exists before the close, and if so pulls it then (kept in `repull/tempest_intraday/`, a Tempest re-pull check) and again 10 minutes after the close, counting the changed cells (no values). A row that exists and changes before the close is never used for that session (`tempest_lag_sessions` ≥ 1 uses the previous session's row). **Results (2026-10-08/09):** poll #2 (started 15:19 ET 10-08) saw no row before the close; the row appeared at 16:06:56. The morning re-pulls of 10-09 08:48 ET (`tempest-compare`) changed 121 cells of the 10-07 copy pulled at 16:08 and 119 cells of the 10-08 copy pulled at 16:07 (most fields, all 5 symbols): the post-close row is a draft. **Owner 2026-10-09:** only a copy pulled at the pre-open of a later day is stored and used (§3.1 Tempest, §9 pre-open job); the 16:07 copies of 10-07 and 10-08 were moved to `repull/tempest_draft/` and replaced in `vol/` by the 08:48 re-pulls (0 credits); `tempest_lag_sessions` stays at its start value until the finality check below.
- **SSD drops (2026-10-08..11, owner 2026-10-11):** the Fanxiang PS1008 (JMicron JMS583 USB-NVMe bridge, UASP driver `UASPStor`, on the Intel Type-C/Thunderbolt xHCI 8086:A831 root hub port 3, running as USB 3) was surprise-removed and re-mounted 51 times (HarddiskVolume8 → 110, disk device DR1 → DR51) with `disk` 51 and `Ntfs` 140; chkdsk clean. Pattern: every drop came 1–2 minutes after a Modern Standby entry (Kernel-Power 506) while a command that didn't hold Windows awake (step 8b, databento condition) was using the SSD; none during the `nqbot run` backfill and backup nights (no standby entry then), and more on battery (shorter screen timeout, USB selective suspend on). Power plan: Balanced only, no plan change logged (Razer Cortex didn't swap plans; the AC selective-suspend setting hadn't reverted, the battery one was never set); USB 3 link power management and PCIe link-state power management at maximum savings on AC and battery. Fixed in code: every command holds Windows awake; writes retry once after a drop; drops logged; recording fallback (§11 Storage). Owner: power settings (USB 3 LPM off, selective suspend off on battery, PCIe ASPM off), Razer Cortex removed, cable/port/heat; the step-21 soak test is the gate. Confirmed the same evening: `nqbot verify` read all 66.9 GB written since 10-04 with no drop and found nothing damaged (three old task logs were UTF-16, now read as such); then at 22:26:08 the laptop entered Modern Standby with no nqbot command running, a test run started inside it at 22:27:41, and the SSD dropped at 22:29:23. A command that holds Windows awake from its start keeps standby away, but one started after standby began doesn't end it, so long jobs also need the display-off timeout on AC set to never (or the screen kept on) while they run. **Port (2026-10-14):** the drops continued on 10-11 23:14–23:52 with the laptop awake on AC (pairs a minute apart every ~4 min); the owner moved the SSD from USB-C port 3 to port 2 of the same Intel Type-C xHCI (8086:A831) at 23:56, and since then (to 10-14 14:30) there has been no drop at all: a full 10-12 recording, idle nights, two reboots (the two 'remounts' on 10-12 were C: shadow copies, not the SSD). A bridge idle timer would follow the device, so the cause is port 3's path (connector or its cable seating); UASP, the driver and the power settings are the same on both ports. Keep the SSD on port 2; the step-21 soak test (§12) decides whether it, or a replacement, goes live. **Port 2 too (2026-10-16):** drops resumed on port 2 from 10-14 22:23 (8 D: re-mounts that evening, 72 on 10-15 with 14 `disk` 51 errors, e.g. 08:40:01 → back 08:40:05, which made the 08:40 late-prints task refuse); so the port move didn't fix it. The 10-15 recording (`--fallback-local`) rode out three drops (10:57, 11:42, 12:12, back in 4.5–5 s, retried) and kept parts 0–20 (09:27–14:42 ET) on the SSD (`nqbot verify --since 2026-10-15`: 70 files, nothing damaged). At 14:47:43 the SSD and the AC power went away together (Kernel-Power 105 power-source change, and Modern Standby entered the same second) and came back together at 19:14:35–37, so they probably share one hub (to confirm with the owner); part 21's write at 14:54:40 waited 30 s, gave up, switched to the internal drive and the process died on its next print (fixed, §11 Storage). If so, unplugging the hub also takes the laptop off AC.
- **Q-mapstale (answered by the owner 2026-10-14): the stream drops board updates per map, and the stale check is per connection.** Decisions: (a) approved: `skylit_map_stale_sec` 5 s per map (§9), live only, logged and counted per session, a known live-vs-backtest difference; built in `live/map_freshness.py` with tests, counted in the session-recording report. (b) approved in principle, built with the live steps (21+); open until then: history's availability delay after a window closes, whether that copy is final (vs the evening pull on a recording day), and the in-session CPU/memory cost on the laptop; same feature code as the backtest. (c) yes: `step7 stream-drops --session D [--window HH:MM]` (25 credits) on every recording, from 10-15 (Fri 10-16 08:50). (d) checked: our reader was never behind (no `lagged`, post-gap boards at the normal 0.7 s median delay, at most 8 boards of all maps within any 100 ms), so the boards are skipped on Skylit's side; the docs allow NDXP on a separate connection (≤ 10 symbols a connection, ≤ 25 per customer per server, 1 credit per symbol to open) but have no option that sends every board; proposal: one future recording with NDXP alone on a second connection and QQQ/SPY on the first, compared with `stream-drops` (not 10-15); question 4 added to the Skylit draft (`docs/step8b_findings.md` §8). The finding: A `/v1/historical/range` window (25 credits) over the gappiest stretch of the 10-12 session recording (12:30–12:45 ET) has a board for nearly every second (NDXP 896, QQQ 900, SPY 900 of 900); the stream had NDXP 643, QQQ 899, SPY 818, and of the seconds only history has, NDXP 249 of 253 and SPY 82 of 82 differ from the last board the stream sent (`lagged` was never true): the stream drops changed boards, it does not send only on change. Where both have the same second they agree (NDXP 639/643, QQQ and SPY all). Share of the session a map's newest board was older than 5 s: NDXP 1.19% on 10-12 (longest 25 s), 0.14% on 10-09; QQQ and SPY ≈ 0. §9's `skylit_stale_sec` (45 s) counts any event or ping on the connection, so a stalled NDXP never trips it, and live would act on NDXP boards up to ~25 s old while the backtest (history) has every second. Proposals: (a) per-map freshness `skylit_map_stale_sec` (start 5 s): while a map's newest board is older, no new entries on zones with a member from that map (other maps trade on; would have blocked NDXP levels 1.2% of 10-12, 0.14% of 10-09); (b) back-fill each finished 15-minute window from history intraday (25 credits a window, ≈ 650 a session, replacing the evening checkpoint pull §9 already budgets) so velocity, lean and rolling features rebuild on the same every-second boards as the backtest; (c) one history window per future recording to measure the drop rate per map, and the finding added to the Skylit question.
- **Step 7, Tempest finality (owner 2026-10-09). Answered 2026-10-12: final, `tempest_lag_sessions` = 1.** Re-pulled at 08:55 ET 10-12: 10-08's row against its pre-open copy of 10-09 08:48:10 and 10-09's against 10-10 08:31:04, 0 cells changed in either (all 5 symbols); both timing mornings had the row by 09:00. Step 22 re-checks it live (§5.2). The question was: is the pre-open copy final? Sat 10-10 (after 08:30 ET) `step7 tempest-morning` stores 10-09's row from a pre-open pull (1 credit); Mon 10-12 before 09:30 `step7 tempest-finality --dates 2026-10-08,2026-10-09` re-pulls both rows (2 credits) and compares them with their first pre-open copies (10-08: 10-09 08:48; 10-09: the Saturday pull). Unchanged: `tempest_lag_sessions` = 1; anything changed: 2. - **Q8-degraded Databento non-available days (added 2026-10-09). ✅ Answered by the owner 2026-10-09: approved as proposed, the five values unchanged; written into §3.2 (Dataset condition).** `get_dataset_condition` over every UTC date of the step-8 window (warm-up included) lists 10 `degraded` dates, the rest `available`: 2025-09-17, 2025-09-24, 2025-11-28, 2026-01-31, 2026-03-15, 2026-03-16, 2026-03-21, 2026-04-10, 2026-05-24, 2026-08-29 (four are Saturdays and touch no CME session). The measured damage per session is in `<reports_dir>/step8/step8_report.md` (completeness only, for every month: no prices, levels or outcomes, §7 rule 2b). **Proposed rule, fixed 2026-10-09 from completeness only, before any outcome on those days was looked at** (written before the damage numbers were read; parameters in `settings.yaml`, nothing reads them until approved):
  1. A UTC date that is not `available` maps to the parts of the CME days it overlaps: ETH (18:00 ET → 09:30, and the RTH close → 17:00) and RTH (09:30 → 16:00 or the early close). A date touching no CME day (a weekend) needs nothing.
  2. Each part is measured per series (NQ `trades`, ES `trades`, NQ `mbp-1` in RTH) against the same part on the `databento_damage_baseline_sessions` (20) nearest sessions whose UTC dates are all available. A part is **damaged** if its empty minutes exceed the normal maximum by more than `databento_damage_excess_minutes` (5), or a gap is longer than both the normal maximum gap and `databento_damage_gap_sec` (120 s). Its **damaged minutes** are the minutes inside such gaps plus its excess empty minutes. A degraded date whose parts are all undamaged is kept unchanged (listed in the report).
  3. **RTH, small damage** (damaged minutes, union over the three series, ≤ `databento_damage_session_minutes` (30)): drop only the events (training rows, labels, backtest and simulator trades) whose span, from the touch to the end of the label horizon, comes within `databento_damage_window_pad_min` (15) of a damaged minute; the rest of the session stays. Cumulative session features after the damage (VWAP, session profile, CVD) use the stored data as it is.
  4. **RTH, large damage** (> 30 damaged minutes): drop the whole session (no events, labels, scoring or simulator days). The levels it would hand to the next session (prior-day high/low, value area, POC) are left empty there, the way any missing value is.
  5. **ETH only:** RTH events are kept. If the ETH part has > 30 damaged minutes, the overnight-derived levels and features of that session (ON high/low, overnight profile) are left empty; otherwise used as stored.
  6. The rule is applied by code to every month alike; sealed and walk-forward test months are handled without anyone looking at their outcomes (§7 rule 2b), and the report shows completeness only.
  **Measured 2026-10-09 (after the rule above was written; completeness only):** every RTH part of every degraded date is within the normal range (NQ/ES `trades` 0 empty minutes, longest gap ≤ 27 s; NQ `mbp-1` 0 empty, ≤ 1.2 s; 2025-11-28's early-close RTH has one same-length normal session to compare with). The damage is overnight: **2025-11-28 (month 2) ETH is missing 21:44 ET 11-27 → 08:30 ET 11-28** in NQ and ES `trades` alike (647 / 645 of 870 minutes empty; the stored `ohlcv-1s` has the same hole), so the rule leaves that session's overnight levels and features empty and keeps its RTH; small ETH damage, used as stored under the rule: 2025-09-17 (warm-up) NQ 24 empty minutes vs a normal max of 2, longest gap 193 s; CME day 2026-03-16 NQ one 121 s gap; CME day 2026-03-17 (18:00–20:00 ET on 03-16) NQ 4 empty minutes, 132 s gap. Undamaged: 2025-09-24, 2026-03-15, 2026-04-10, 2026-05-24 (Memorial Day Globex). No session is dropped. The Saturdays 2026-01-31, 03-21, 08-29 touch no session.
  Question as asked: approve the rule and its five values as proposed (or change them)?

- **Step 9, gameplan parser v2 (2026-10-07). ✅ All five answered by the owner 2026-10-07** (written into feature_spec F8, F5.9, F5.10, F11.2 and §5.6; new setting `gameplan_ny_am_end` 12:00):
  1. **"Settled bar"** with no timeframe: = the §5.5 settled bar (1-minute); if the gameplan names a timeframe, that one.
  2. **Scenario set:** the set matching the event's time: `ny_am` during the NY AM window (09:30 to `gameplan_ny_am_end`), `session` otherwise; if the gameplan has only one set (`unspecified` included), that one.
  3. **ES gate wording:** "holding" / "reclaiming" X = ES above X; "losing" / "rejecting" / "failing" X = ES below X; anything else stays unclear, and an unclear gate gives an empty feature, never a guess. (Claude Code's reading, in F8: a gate given as a range holds only when ES is beyond the whole range, and is empty inside it.)
  4. **HVL and HVL0** are two separate features (distance to each), empty when not given; never pick one. A target range is a band like any other level: reaching its near edge counts as reaching it; order uses the middle.
  5. **Gameplan invalidations are features only, never trading rules** (rule 6): "invalidation as written has been met", per timeframe: acceptance = a bar of the stated minutes (15 if not given) closing beyond; settled_bar = answer 1; not_stated = a settled 1-minute close (the §5.2 chart-level default); other = empty feature. A gameplan zone is crossed when price trades past its far edge.

Use it as a single-price zone [p, p] at `trigger.nq_price` (its touch by `zone_proximity`, its own stop and targets as candidates), or only as a feature of the zone it breaks out of? Owner 2026-10-07: find the causes read-only and change no gate limit or definition until he decides. Evidence, method and the before/after table: `docs/step8b_findings.md`. Until these are answered, nothing below changes and step 11 waits:
  - **Q8b-1 NDXP spot.** History's NDXP `spot` is an NQ futures last price, not NDX (it equals the NQ trade 1 s after `asOf` and switches between H6/M6/U6; U6 confirmed against NQU6 1-second bars). The live stream's NDXP spot is the index. **Owner 2026-10-07/08: keep NDXP and fix it; choose the method with the lower error.** Two methods, both known before the open (findings R6, R9): (1) basis + carry (previous NQ settlement ÷ NDX official close, per contract, decayed to expiry; day-ahead p95 15.1 NQ, bias +0.2); (2) QQQ × k (NDX ≈ QQQ spot × k, k = NDX close ÷ QQQ official close over the previous N sessions, dividend-adjusted across ex-dates; N = 5 day-ahead p95 13.8, bias +2.2). Sources are free: FRED `NASDAQ100`, Nasdaq's quote API for QQQ closes and ex-dividend dates. Live 2026-10-07 against the stream's real NDX: basis + carry p95 14.4 / 10.0, QQQ × k 2.9–10.8 depending on N. **Pending:** the session recordings decide, chosen leave-one-day-out across the three recordings (owner 2026-10-08): candidates basis + carry and QQQ × k with N ∈ {5, 10, 20}; hold one day out, pick the candidate with the lowest p95 on the other two, score it on the held-out day, rotate; each candidate's held-out p95 and median are reported, the method and N with the best held-out p95 win (every-board scoring stays the main method, owner 2026-10-11); report only, the same candidates' p95 at relevant-node crossings (F2.4 on the NDXP board in force, the gate's crossing rule) is shown next to it, and the proposed NDXP `stop_buffer_floor` is the larger of the winner's every-board held-out p95 and its crossing p95, so stops are never tighter than crossings show; the owner decides on it (owner 2026-10-11; `nqbot step8b ndxp-select`), (owner 2026-10-08: record `--maps NDXP,QQQ,SPY` on all three sessions, ≈ 1,185 credits each (≤ 1,254 with 12 reconnects); `step7 stream-session`, Windows task 09:27 ET 10-09, 10-12, 10-13; $0 Databento NQ and ES trades for each recorded day, priced first). NDXP keeps the ±20 band until the step-13 reaction-distance report. The Skylit question stays open (findings §8). **✅ Method answered (owner 2026-10-19; findings R15):** `ndxp-select` over the recordings 10-09, 10-12, 10-15 (to 14:42) and 10-16 (to 13:55; 10-13/10-14 were not recorded): **QQQ × k with N = 20** (`ndxp_r_method: qqq_k`, `ndxp_k_sessions: 20`): held-out every-board p95 5.43 NQ (abs median 2.51), crossings 5.79 (768); N = 10 6.92, N = 5 8.06, basis + carry 18.80. The close is not harder on the two full days, so the two partial days do not favour a candidate. NDXP's stop floor is proposed after the gate re-run; the 10-19 recording checks it.
  - **Q8b-2 Board effective time (ingest rule). Answered (owner 2026-10-08), written into §3.1 Ingest, §5.1, F0.1 and F1.1 (`board_content_offset_ms`, `skylit_stamp_change_date`; SPY from 03-17 and NDXP pending the recordings):** apply a board at max(arrival, floor(`asOf`) + 1 s + `map_latency_sec`) in every era and live (history: no arrival), and read the futures at the board's measured content time (`board_content_offset_ms` per era and map; medians in findings R4/R10). Measured content times never pass floor(`asOf`) + 1 s (NDXP exactly +1 s). An alignment check (`step8b alignment`, to be built) measures the offset on every recording against the futures and alerts if a content time falls after floor(`asOf`) + 1 s. SPY's live offset (+0.3/+0.5 s on 10-07 vs −0.06 s in March) is re-measured on the next recording that includes SPY. **Owner 2026-10-10: QQQ's live offset joins SPY's in Wednesday's decision (after the third recording):** fitted live QQQ −80 / −140 ms (10-07) and −320 ms (10-09, −220 to −320 by hour) vs the configured −60; SPY +520 / +320 (10-07) and +560 ms (10-09, +440 to +620 by hour). Neither puts a live board's content after floor(`asOf`) + 1 s (the correlation peak's upper edge is at most +620 ms), so the alignment check does not alert; no setting changed (findings R14). Content offsets compare Skylit `asOf` with Databento `ts_event`, so the laptop clock does not enter them. **✅ Answered (owner 2026-10-19; findings R15):** live QQQ and SPY −60 ms, the history value (fitted QQQ −320 / +260 / +40 / +560 and SPY +560 / −200 / −180 / −140 on 10-09 / 10-12 / 10-15 / 10-16: day-to-day scatter, no steady shift; all pass the alignment check); `map_latency_sec` 1.16 s (arrival − τ p99.9, §3.1 Ingest).
