The tables for 10-09, 10-12, 10-15 and 10-16 are generated with `step8b atlas-recorded` (10 fixed minutes per day, spread evenly over the recorded part of the session, not chosen from prices; reports kept apart from the sample-day regression check); 10-19 is added after its `recorded-day`.** **Spot checks (owner 2026-10-22):** each table also carries NDXP and SPX as a light spot check, levels and times only (no crossings, no tolerance): NDXP at 3 clock-fixed minutes (10 minutes after the open, midday, 10 minutes before the recording's end; R = the QQQ R in force / k, the frozen method); SPX is not in the recordings, so it comes from the day's stored history windows (the step-7 parity / stream-drops pulls, one minute 10 minutes into each window: 10-12 12:40, 10-16 10:25, 10-19 09:40; none on 10-09 and 10-15). The owner's Atlas replay reaches back only to 2026-04-21, so he asked for the Atlas tables (QQQ and SPY: relevant-node levels and times only, no outcomes, about 10 times per map per day across the session including the close) on the recorded days 10-09, 10-12, 10-15, 10-16 (and 10-19), and never on a date from April to September. **Check against §7 rule 2b (Claude Code, 2026-10-19): it does not fit as written.** October 2026 is a top-up month, so it is sealed until step 20. The rule's exceptions without outcomes are pull and data-coverage reports with **no prices**, the gameplan parsing, and the step-7 and step-22 live-feed measurements: stream cadence and latency, history parity and Tempest availability time. An Atlas table lists mapped price levels from the bot's mapping, which is none of these. (The October recordings' `recorded-day` / `ndxp-select` mapping errors are the same kind of step-8b measurement, approved by the owner on 2026-10-08 and 2026-10-11 but not written into rule 2b either.) **Proposed:** add to rule 2b's exceptions "the step-8b mapping measurements on session recordings (`recorded-day`, `ndxp-select`, Atlas tables of relevant-node levels and times): no labels, events, trades or outcomes, mapping inputs only", so October recordings can be used for them; the Atlas tables are generated only after the owner decides.
  - **Q-vix Skylit's VIX row (2026-10-20). (1) ✅ Answered (owner 2026-10-20), written into §5.2 and feature_spec F2.21:** use Skylit's VIX row as it is, from the same pre-open Tempest copy and the same `tempest_lag_sessions` lag as the other rows: its vol fields per F2.21 plus its `close`, named Skylit's VIX reference price (`vix_ref`), not the official VIX close; no FRED dependency for backtest or live. Why it is not the official close (check 2026-10-20 on the warm-up and months 1–6 only, §7 rule 2b; FRED VIXCLS used once for this check, never an input): over 148 sessions the row's close lies above the CBOE close by a median of 0.95 (−3.72 to +3.36), within 0.05 on 5 sessions, correlation 0.976. **(2) Roll check (owner 2026-10-20, before (1) is final for the close): it rolls. ✅ Answered (owner 2026-10-20): the proposal below approved, the vol fields kept as they are (`skew_s30` included); written into §5.2 and F2.21, `features_version` 6.** The row's `front_min` (minutes to the front VIX expiry) counts down each session and resets from 1,441–2,881 to 40,321–50,401 on the 7 roll sessions of the sample (2025-09-17, 10-21, 11-19, 12-17, 2026-01-21, 02-18, 03-18; 10-21 is the Tuesday before the 10-22 VX expiry, the others the expiry Wednesday). The close's gap to the official VIX is near 0 at expiry and widens with time to expiry (median |gap| 0.15 with ≤ 3 days left, 0.81 at 3–10, 1.32 at 10–20, 1.80 at 20–40 days) and jumps on each roll session (session before → roll session: −0.23 → 2.05, 0.27 → 1.16, −0.63 → −2.51, −0.04 → 1.25, 0.32 → 1.54, −0.15 → 0.52, 0.01 → −0.49); its day-to-day change minus the official change is 0.39 in median size on other sessions (p90 1.21) and 2.28, 0.89, 1.88, 1.28, 1.22, 0.67, 0.50 on the roll sessions, and its median |change| on a roll session is 4.6× a normal session's. So the level is a front-month forward carrying a term-structure sawtooth, not the VIX level. The vol fields barely move at the roll (median |change| on roll sessions 0.6–2.0× a normal session's, at most 2 of 7 rolls above a normal session's 90th percentile), except `skew_s30` (2.3×, 4 of 7); kept per (1). **Proposed (approved 2026-10-20):** the close is used only as its one-session change, `vix_ref_chg` = ln(close(T−1) ÷ close(T−2)) from rows of the same pre-open copy, null when session T−1 is a roll session (its row's `front_min` is larger than T−2's: the row's own field, known pre-open, no calendar, no new parameter); the level is not an input. Not a ratio to a rolling baseline: a baseline of N closes spans the roll for the N sessions after it, and the roll gap can't be taken out without an outside VIX source. `features_version` 6 (nothing was built under 0–5, so nothing is rebuilt).
  - **Q-power Laptop on battery during a session (2026-10-19). ✅ Answered (owner 2026-10-19), written into §9 (Power during a session) and settings.yaml (`power_poll_sec`, `battery_warn_step_pct`, `battery_entry_floor_pct`):** (1) approved: recordings, jobs and live sessions hold `ES_DISPLAY_REQUIRED` from start to end, and preflight checks the lid on battery is Do nothing (built 2026-10-19 for recordings and jobs; the laptop's lid on battery already reads Do nothing); (2) approved as proposed: on battery, warnings only above `battery_entry_floor_pct` (start 30 %); at or below it no new entries (open trades keep their brackets, pending entries removed as on a feed failure), entries resume when AC returns or the battery is back above it on AC; warnings in the log and the event log (Discord at step 22) on the switch, at each further 10 % drop, at the 30 % line and when AC returns (the live parts built with step 22; the warnings built for recordings now); (3) approved: the session report records each battery stretch (built). The proposal as written: On 10-19 the recording lost its last 14 min 44 s: the laptop was unplugged at 14:37:22 ET (Kernel-Power 105, AC online false); the battery display timeout (300 s) turned the screen off and Windows entered Modern Standby at 14:44:52 (reason Idle Timeout); the stream kept running in connected standby until 15:45:10–16, one hour later (the battery "sleep after" is 3,600 s), when Windows logged "connectivity in standby: Disconnected, reason Adaptive Connected Standby" and cut the network: ReadError, then five ConnectErrors to 15:54:48, no board after 15:45:16. **Why the stay-awake call did not help:** `ES_SYSTEM_REQUIRED` only holds off the sleep idle timer of a classic (S3) PC; on a Modern Standby laptop standby starts when the display turns off, and a desktop app's system-required request does not keep it out of standby or keep its network up once the screen is off (the same finding as 10-11 on AC: screen off = Modern Standby = SSD drop; on AC the owner's display timeout is never, so it never showed). **Proposed (for recordings now, live trading at step 22; owner decides):** (1) every recording and live session also holds `ES_DISPLAY_REQUIRED` (with `ES_SYSTEM_REQUIRED | ES_CONTINUOUS`, per thread, cleared however the command ends, like today's call) from start to the end of the session, so the display idle timeout cannot start standby on AC or battery; it does not stop the lid, the power button, the critical-battery action or a forced restart, so preflight also checks the battery lid action is Do nothing; cost: the screen stays on (battery drains faster). (2) A power-source watch in the recording and live processes (`GetSystemPowerStatus`, polled every few seconds, a named parameter): the moment it switches to battery: a WARNING log line (and an entry in the SSD/power event log) with the battery %, then a line at each further 10 % and when AC returns; live (step 22) also a Discord warning (§9c) at the switch and at low battery. Whether live blocks new entries on battery, or only below a battery %, is the owner's call (a proposal: warn only above 30 %, no new entries below it, like a pending feed failure; open trades keep their brackets). (3) The session report and log record the battery stretches.\n  - **Q8b-3 R estimator. Answered (owner 2026-10-08), written into §5.1 and F1.7 (`ratio_window_sec`, `ratio_gap_reset_sec`; NDXP pending):** QQQ trailing 60-s median; SPY trailing 60-s median (the lowest event-moment level error of per board / 15 / 30 / 60 s: 0.11 ES vs 0.16, 0.26 and 0.89; findings R8); SPX per board (aligned) with F1.2; NDXP per Q8b-1. Trailing only, over applied boards by content time; reset at the session's first applied board (after `ratio_open_skip_sec` for SPX/NDXP), at a contract change (only ever between sessions) and after a board gap > `ratio_gap_reset_sec` (10 s); warm-up: after a reset the median uses the applied boards since it, the first board alone at first, no minimum count; one function for backtest, rebuilds and live.
  - **Q21-resume Hourly reconnect with `gapCovered=False` (for step 21, the live tests; 2026-10-10; open).** Observed on the 2026-10-09 recording (`docs/step8b_findings.md` R14): Skylit ends every stream connection after an hour (`reconnect`, reason `max_duration`, at 10:27, 11:27, 12:27, 13:27, 15:09 ET) plus occasional `server_shutdown` reconnects (14:09, 15:26); every resume took 0.2–0.4 s. 3 of 7 resumes said `gapCovered=False` (10:27 NDXP, 12:27 QQQ, 15:09 NDXP), each with that symbol `advanced` from `lastAsOf` to a `currentAsOf` exactly one second later, and the recording holds every second's board around all three: nothing was missed. §3.1 already says a resume sends only the latest board and that a gap is pulled from `/v1/historical/range` only after `skylit_stale_sec` or a `lagged` event. Proposal: on every resume, the engine reads `gapCovered` and each symbol's `lastAsOf`/`currentAsOf`; if a symbol advanced by more than one board step (currentAsOf − lastAsOf > 1 s), it pulls the missed seconds for that symbol from `/v1/historical/range` (the §3.1 gap fill, rule-2 exception; a few credits) and applies them by the ingest rule before the current board, logging the gap; a one-second advance needs nothing. Entries stay blocked only by the existing staleness rule. Step 21 tests: a resume with `gapCovered=False` advanced by 1 s (no pull, no block), by 30 s (pull, boards applied in order, no lookahead), and `gapCovered=True` (nothing). The step-8b scores leave out a window around each uncovered resume anyway (findings R14). Separate observation: the Windows Time service was not running on 2026-10-09 (the laptop clock was at least ≈ 0.6 s behind: boards 'arrived' before their content; arrival − asOf drifted ≈ −0.1 s more over the session); the owner started it and resynced on 2026-10-10. §9 Clock needs it synced before live, and `map_latency_sec` is re-measured on the 10-12 and 10-13 recordings. **Which way the clock biases `map_latency_sec` (owner 2026-10-10):** the delay is arrival (laptop clock) − `asOf` (Skylit's clock). A laptop clock running δ behind stamps every arrival δ too early, so the measured delay is the true delay − δ: a clock behind makes `map_latency_sec` **too small**, never too large. 0.59 was measured on 10-07 (14:59 recording, p99.9 0.585 s), not 10-09; that day's bound says the clock was at least ≈ 0.07 s behind, with no upper limit, so the true 10-07 delay was at least ≈ 0.66 s and possibly more. (10-09's clock, ≥ 0.6 s behind, would have understated it by ≥ 0.6 s.) Too small is the risky direction: the backtest would apply a board up to δ before live could have it (a small lookahead). **Decision (owner 2026-10-10):** the 10-12 and 10-13 recordings, with the clock synced, re-measure the delay (p99.9 over both, and the arrival − content bound to confirm the clock); Wednesday sets `map_latency_sec` from them before the gate re-run, and if the value changes, the gate (bias, estimator), the Atlas tables and the volatility numbers are re-run with it. Content offsets, alignment and the gate's `asOf`-based timing don't use the laptop clock and are unaffected. (findings R14)
  - **Q11-instab Mapping-instability feature. Answered (owner 2026-10-13): not built for the first model;** `step8b dislocations` stays a report, re-run on the walk-forward, top-up and live months (automated counts only, §7 rule 2b), and the feature is revisited if the bot's R shows about one 0.1% event a week or more. Original proposal (owner 2026-10-09): Add per chain (QQQ, SPY, SPX; NDXP once its method is chosen) the trailing dispersion and the largest change of the per-board R over the last 30–120 s (trailing only: boards applied by t, no lookahead), so the model can learn that ETF-mapped levels are unreliable in dislocations like 2026-03-31 12:43 (R moved ≈ 0.5% in two minutes, findings R12). Questions for step 11: the window (30, 60 or 120 s, or all three), dispersion as the MAD or the range of per-board R in bps, and whether the largest change is measured board to board or as max − min over the window. Evidence on how often such dislocations happen: the dislocation count (findings R13). **Result (2026-10-13, findings R13; 121 sessions of months 1–6):** on the R the bot uses, real dislocations are rare: QQQ 2 events and SPY 1 at 0.1% within 2 minutes, all on 2026-03-31 (at 0.05%: QQQ 5 on 4 days, SPY 4 on 3, all but three on 2026-03-30/31), none in a news blackout; the per-board R's 1,617 / 826 events at 0.05% are spot noise the 60-s median already removes. SPX (per board) has 94 events at 0.05% and 11 at 0.1%, mostly from the 2026-03-17 stamping change on; a trailing median would not remove them without making SPX's crossing error worse (Q8b-6), so at least part is real movement the per-board R follows. **Recommendation: don't build it for the first model.** A feature that is non-zero on one or two sessions in 121 gives the model a handful of event rows to learn from (only touches during those few minutes), all from one day, so it cannot learn a reliable effect and invites fitting that day. The large tail it was meant for (2026-03-31 12:40–12:47) is one event. Instead: (1) keep `step8b dislocations` as a report and re-run it on the walk-forward and top-up months (automated counts only, §7 rule 2b) and on the live months; (2) revisit the feature if the bot's R shows roughly one or more 0.1% dislocation a week there; (3) if SPX's events keep rising in later months, look at SPX on its own first (Q8b-6; answered: per board).
  - **Q13-vol Volatility-scaled stop floor (for step 13, when stops are tuned; owner 2026-10-08). ✅ Decided (owner 2026-11-08, §13 Q-step13plan (5)): by the data at the `stop_buffer` tuning (§7.1 Tuning, row 13 of the step-13 order) with the keep-current rule: the fixed floor stays unless the volatility-scaled floor (re-fitted on months 1–4) has a higher total net R per month with the 95% day-block bootstrap interval of the difference excluding 0.** **Owner's leaning (2026-10-09): the volatility-scaled floor; decided at step 13 with the full stop tuning as planned.** The step-8b study (findings R12, read-only, 22 development days, re-run 2026-10-09 with `map_latency_sec` 0.59 and the board in force by its apply time) shows the crossing error grows with trailing volatility, moderately: Spearman QQQ 0.29, SPY 0.20, SPX 0.24; p95 from the quietest to the busiest quartile of the trailing 15-minute mean 1-minute futures range QQQ 2.53 → 5.98 NQ, SPY 0.52 → 1.00 ES, SPX 0.52 → 1.01 ES. The fixed `stop_buffer_floor` (QQQ 4.0 NQ) covers 99.4% of months 5–6 crossings in the quietest quartile but 88.6% in the busiest. Proposed: floor = max(fixed minimum, k × trailing volatility), fitted on months 1–4 and checked on months 5–6: QQQ max(2.79, 0.195 × vol15) NQ (months 5–6 coverage 96.5% overall, 95.9–97.3% per quartile; mean floor 2.79 quiet → 6.73 busy vs 4.0), SPY max(0.53, 0.20 × vol15) ES (96.6%, 94.7–98.0%; 0.56 → 1.34 vs 0.78); SPX max(0.52, 0.165 × vol15) ES generalizes worse (93.7%; SPX creates no NQ stops). Versus the 0 s run: QQQ minimum 2.77 → 2.79, SPY 0.52 → 0.53, SPX 0.45 → 0.52; slopes unchanged. The tail (2026-03-31 12:40–12:47, an ETF-vs-futures dislocation of ≈ 55 NQ) is covered by neither. Question for step 13: replace the fixed floor with the scaled one (re-fitted then on months 1–4), keep the fixed floor, or offer both to the stop tuning? Nothing changes until then.
  - **Q8b-5 Atlas tolerance. Answered (owner 2026-10-08): option (1)** — the automated Atlas match is information only and the owner's replay check is the Atlas gate condition, since (a) and (b) already gate the crossing error; kept as a regression check for later re-runs with the tolerances frozen at today's crossing p95 (`atlas_match_tol` QQQ 4.0 NQ, SPY 0.78 ES), alerting if fewer than `atlas_regression_min_share` (90%) of crossings fall within them, judged on the share pooled over all sample days (owner 2026-10-08; per-day shares information only; **baseline 2026-10-11** with `map_latency_sec` 0.59 and §5.2's relevance rule with the 20% hold applied to the listing and to each crossing (owner 2026-10-11, findings R11): **QQQ 94.6% of 519, SPY 94.6% of 574**; tolerances unchanged (the owner decides on any change: the chosen-estimator p95s moved to QQQ 4.015 NQ / SPY 0.792 ES). Before, with a plain ≥ 25% per board: QQQ 94.2% of 500, SPY 95.2% of 495 (0 s: 94.4% of 500 / 95.0% of 535; the 40 SPY crossings that drop out are all strike 651 on 2026-03-24 at 09:35: it was exactly 25.0% of King on the 09:34:59 board, in force at 0 s, and just under 25% on the 09:34:58 board, in force once the delay applies; timing, not a lost board). Written into §5.1 and §12 step 8b. Question as asked: With the approved rule, gate (a), (b) and the alignment check pass for QQQ, SPY and SPX on 22 development days (findings R11), but the Atlas tables' automated match within `atlas_match_tol` (1.0 NQ / 0.5 ES) cannot pass: it sits below the measured crossing floor (QQQ p95 4.0 NQ, SPY 0.78 ES; 37–69% of QQQ crossings and 83–94% of SPY crossings are within it, 90–100% / 93–97% within the crossing p95). Options: (1) the automated match becomes information only and the owner's replay check (same strikes, similar size) is the Atlas condition; (2) `atlas_match_tol` = the chain's crossing p95 (4.0 NQ / 0.78 ES), checked as a share (≥ 95% of crossings) rather than every crossing. Recommendation: (1), since the crossing error is already gated by (a) and (b). No change until the owner answers.
  - **Q8b-6 SPX's R: trailing median or per board? Answered (owner 2026-10-13): keep per board** (`ratio_window_sec` SPX 0 unchanged; written into §5.1). The dislocation count (findings R13) shows SPX's R, which is per board (`ratio_window_sec` SPX 0), moving more from the 2026-03-17 stamping change on (0.34 → 5.2 events per day at 0.05% within 2 minutes), while QQQ/SPY's 60-s medians nearly never move. Question: should SPX's R get a trailing median like QQQ/SPY, or does per board stay right? What-if, read-only (config unchanged; 22 gate days, the same 6,951 crossings): gate p95 per board **0.779 ES** (best 0.785), 30 s 0.808, 60 s 0.849, 120 s 0.873 (fails the 10% margin, limit 0.863); from 03-17 alone 0.809 / 0.842 / 0.878 / 0.889; bias stays within one tick in every case. Per board is best at every window and in both eras: SPX's spot is the index, not an ETF last trade, so a median adds lag without removing noise. Re-check with the dislocation count and the gate on the walk-forward months.
  - **Q-dislguard Mapping-dislocation guard (proposed 2026-10-13 at the owner's request). ✅ Answered (owner 2026-10-13/14): approved with two triggers, either one starting a block: the per-board trigger (within a chain's trailing 2 minutes, a level held by two boards within 10 s and an opposite level held by two boards within 10 s more than 0.2% of R apart; the first version, which confirmed only the new level, fired on a one-board dip on 2026-02-20 and was fixed before approval, findings R13) and the bot's-R trigger as a backstop; 60 s hold, no opening exception; while flagged no new entries on that chain's levels, pending entries there removed as on a feed failure, open trades keep their brackets; the same in labels, both simulators and live, events inside a block not tradeable; every fire logged (trigger, chain, size; a Discord note live) and counted in the dislocation report on the walk-forward, top-up and demo months, revisit above about one false fire a week; thresholds fixed by data quality, never tuned on trade results. Written into §1.2, §5.1, §5.5b, §9, §10.3, §11, feature_spec F1.8 and F5.7, features.yaml (`disl_guard_*`, `features_version` 3), `features/disloc_guard.py` (tests on hand-built examples) and `step8b dislocations` (fires, blocks, per-board-only blocks and false fires per chain; `--months walk-forward`). Follow-ups (owner 2026-10-14): the hold is causal (a flag during the hold extends the block forward; nothing is merged after the fact); a **false fire** = a block during which the levels mapped with the bot's R stayed within the chain's tolerance of the levels mapped with a hindsight R (the centred median of the per-board R over ±60 s; report only, never a feature) at every relevant node of every board in the block, i.e. trading would have been safe without it (tolerance: QQQ its `stop_buffer_floor` 4.0 NQ; SPY/SPX have none, so the closest existing setting, `atlas_match_tol` ES 0.78, the frozen SPY crossing p95; owner 2026-10-14); the S&P features during an S&P block and the guard with live board drops are proposed below (Q-dislguard-2).** Proposal as written: A data-quality guard like the §10.3 data-health rule ("feed or bridge fails: no new entries; existing brackets stay"): if a chain's R as the bot uses it (F1.7, at each board's apply time) moves more than `disl_guard_pct` (0.2%) within `disl_guard_window_sec` (120 s, trailing range, same definition as `step8b dislocations`), new entries on levels mapped from that chain (QQQ → its NQ levels, SPY/SPX → their ES-mapped levels; NDXP if its R comes from QQQ × k) are blocked while it stays flagged plus `disl_guard_hold_sec` (start 60 s); levels from the other chains and chart levels trade normally; open trades keep their brackets. Open sub-question: whether pending entries resting at that chain's levels are also removed with `exit` (as on a feed failure). **Rule 6:** it fits as a risk/data-quality guard, not a trading rule, if (1) it reads only the mapping inputs (futures and spot, never an outcome or a feature the model scores), (2) its threshold is fixed from data quality, not tuned on trade results (0.2% is where the bot's QQQ/SPY R fires only on the one real dislocation day in months 1–6), and (3) it runs identically in labels, both simulators and live (rule 4), so an event inside a block is marked not tradeable like a news-blackout event (§1.2), never dropped silently. **How often it would have fired (months 1–6, 121 sessions, findings R13):** 4 times, all on 2026-03-31: QQQ 12:41:47 for 335 s, SPY 12:41:47 for 330 s, SPX 09:32:22 for 118 s and 12:40:08 for 551 s (about 6, 6 and 11 minutes blocked in 121 sessions); never on 03-30 or any other day. **What it would have blocked (read-only what-if, relevant-node crossings with the gate's errors, `<reports_dir>/step8b/guard_whatif.json`):** 03-31 QQQ 12 crossings, 11 of them beyond the 4.0 NQ floor (p95 52.6 NQ inside vs 3.3 the rest of the day; the day's five worst, 40–55 NQ at 12:43:46–12:44:14, all inside); SPY 16 crossings, 14 beyond 0.78 ES (p95 11.2 vs 1.45), but SPY's five worst (12.6–16.1 ES at 12:41:23–12:41:31) came 16–24 s **before** the guard fired: a trailing range reacts only after R has moved; SPX 1 crossing (10.3 ES, the day's worst), but 09:32:18's 4.9 ES came 4 s before its flag. 2026-03-30: nothing (QQQ's worst 7.6 NQ, SPY's 4.75 ES and SPX's 5.2 ES all stay under 0.2%); at 0.1% it would add only SPX on 03-30 (three 2-minute blocks, 4 crossings, one beyond the floor) and 03-31 09:30 for QQQ. A 120-s hold instead of 0 adds 0–7 crossings per chain, almost all within the floor. **Read:** it removes the worst of the one large dislocation (QQQ fully, SPY partly) at the cost of about 11 minutes in 121 sessions; it cannot catch the first seconds of a jump, and it is too rare to tune. Decide with step 13 (labels) or step 21 (live safety). **The owner decides.**
  - **Q-orfib Opening-range fib: 09:45 start, 10:30 redraw, orientation flips (owner 2026-10-16). ✅ Decided and written into §1.2, §5.3, §5.4, §5.8, §11, §12 step 12, feature_spec F3.6 / F7 / F10 / F12 and features.yaml (`ib_fib_start_minute` 20 → 15, new `ib_redraw_minute` 60, `features_version` 4); no feature code existed yet (step 11).** (Owner 2026-10-26: the orientation flip and the 10:30 redraw are now pre-declared step-13 variants, §13 Q-fibvariants.) (1) The range is established and the fib drawn from 09:45, redrawn on new extremes until the 10:00 lock; golden-zone trades from 09:45 (the only setup until 10:00); reports split golden-zone results 09:45–09:50 vs later. (2) From 10:00 the fib is static; at 10:30, if price made a new high or low beyond the 10:00 range since 10:00, it is redrawn once on the 09:30–10:30 range (orientation by the swing rule), else it stays. (3) IB high/low lock at 10:00 and move with that redraw. (4) Orientation flip: a 1-minute candle settling past the zone's 0.79 edge redraws the fib the other way on the same high and low (Example: low 20,000 first, then high 20,100 → long zone 20,021–20,050; a candle settles below 20,021 → redrawn high → low → short zone 20,050–20,079; a candle later settling above 20,079 redraws it low → high again (long zone 20,021–20,050)); flips continue all day and a 10:30 redraw resets the anchors. New features: flip count, minutes since the last flip, orientation, whether the 10:30 redraw happened, range size at 10:00 and 10:30, which drawing an event used. Owner's answers to the four points asked (2026-10-16): flips apply from 09:45 (a new extreme before 10:00 still redraws by the swing rule); the flip count and minutes since the last flip restart whenever the anchors change, including a new extreme before 10:00 (owner confirmed 2026-10-16); at 10:30 an exceeded IB level is replaced and the new extreme starts fresh (unbroken); the redrawn golden zone is a new zone (the old event ends, its pending entries are removed with `exit`, open trades keep their brackets).
  - **Q-featB Feature additions (owner 2026-10-16). ✅ B2–B6 decided and written into §7 rule 5, §7.1, §11, feature_spec F5.16 / F5.18 / F9.9 / F12 / F16.8, features.yaml (`seq_window_sec` 30, fixed) and settings.yaml (`ablation_groups` 8 order-flow extras, 9 approach); one `features_version` bump (4) with Q-orfib; every new feature is in the step-11 no-lookahead shift test.** B2 sequence inputs (trigger model, reduced form: seconds since each detector last held and since the last big trade with its side, distinct detectors fired, absorption then imbalance within 30 s; no first-detector or event-order features). B3 the last 5 minutes' range in ticks and vs today's median 5-minute range (trigger model). B4 approach move and range over 1 and 15 minutes plus 15-minute straightness, ATR known at the touch, pre-09:30 bars counting (structure model). B5 NQ vs ES relative strength over 5 and 15 minutes (structure model at the touch, pair scorer at `decision_ts`; group 2). B6 an LSTM trigger challenger, recorded in §7.1 Retraining only. **B1 decided (owner 2026-10-17), written into §5.7, §7 rule 5, §7.1, §11, feature_spec F9.2 / F9.4 / F9.5 / F9.10 / F16.8 and features.yaml (`features_version` 5):** approved as proposed (N(m) over the prior 20 full sessions, `vol_tod_w`, `rvol_today` from 09:30 with `rvol_clip_mult` 5, relative `abs_volume` and `imb_min_volume` with the raw sizes kept, big trades per 30-minute slot by time of day only within 0.5–2× the session value, `rvol_today` in the trigger model, structure model and pair scorer, `minutes_since_open` in the trigger model, windows 5 s and 60 s with the no-duplicate rule, every edge-case rule including leaving out damaged sessions; no overnight volume feature), with one change: the floor and cap apply to today's part only, scale = tod_norm_w × clip(`rvol_today`, 0.5, 2.0). On months 1–6 the time-of-day part is about 2.7 at 09:30–10:00 (8.1 in the 09:30 minute), 0.94 at lunch and 1.0 at 15:30–16:00 (2.0 in the last 10 minutes), so capping the product would have undone it exactly there (F9.10). Built: `features/volume_norms.py` with tests. While the scale is null (only after an unrebuilt feed gap, when the outage rule already blocks entries) the scaled detectors do not fire (filled in by Claude Code, approved by the owner 2026-10-17).
  - **Q-dislguard-2 Features during a block, and the guard with live board drops (proposed 2026-10-14 at the owner's request). ✅ Answered (owner 2026-10-17), written into §5.1, §9, feature_spec F1.8 / F2.6 / F2.11 and the code (`features/block_state.py`, `features/disloc_guard.py`, `live/map_freshness.py`, with tests); `features_version` 5:** (a) approved as proposed, with the block-end check: at the block end each paused state is judged at once against the band then in force, as a level check (the last trade at or before the block end beyond the band → crossed at the block end time), so a real cross during the block is not missed; a cross undone inside the block leaves nothing. (b)(i) approved: the guard's windows reset only at the session start; the dislocation count is re-run with it at the next gate run (day files v5), not at once. (b)(ii) approved: live only, every case logged (first board, confirmed, not confirmed in time); the session-recording report counts the confirmation wait. The proposal as made:
    - **(a) S&P features during an S&P block.** Entries are not affected (S&P nodes create no NQ event), but these features convert SPY/SPX strikes to ES with that chain's R, so during a SPY/SPX block they read bands that can be off by strike × ΔR (on 2026-03-31 SPY's crossing errors reached 12.6–16.1 ES inside the block): F2.6 node state on ES (crossed / flipped / reclaimed against the mapped band: a wrong band makes crossings or flips that **persist after the block**), F2.7 beach ball (overshoot depth and seconds beyond), F2.11 pause / resume of S&P ceilings and floors (ES trades against the mapped band), F2.10 magnet pull and F2.15 air pockets ahead / behind on S&P maps (distances to mapped levels), F2.19 Trinity (SPY / SPX aligned = mapped band ± `es_zone_proximity` contains the ES price: `trinity_aligned`, `trinity_count`, `maps_with_node_here` and its node stats), F2.20 S&P path nodes (obstacles between the ES price and the ES target, also used in F11.2 target selection), F5.16 `es_at_level` / `es_stack_count` (when the ES zone holds SPY/SPX nodes) and the S&P maps in the F10 golden-zone node features. **`es_at_level` in detail (owner 2026-10-26, level and area events alike; F1.8 fixed to match, F5.16):** true if an ES zone with no member from a blocked chain meets the overlap rule (ES chart levels built from ES trades are not affected); otherwise null if the SPY or SPX chain was blocked at any moment in the window (a match at a SPY/SPX-node zone can't be ruled out); otherwise false; `es_stack_count` and the ES member types from the zone that made it true, null when it is null; when it is false, `es_stack_count` 0 and no member types (approved by the owner 2026-10-26: false = known no match, null only when unknown). Not affected (no R): King, % of King, node type and sign, velocity and velocity lean, gamma regime, rug / pika, rainbow, King dominance / migration, whipsaw, SMT and relative delta, and the ES chart levels (ON, PDH/PDL, expected-move lines). **Proposed:** treat the blocked chain like a feed gap on that chain: its R-dependent features are **null** (F0.7: unknown, never the last value carried) for every decision inside the block, and its F2.6 / F2.11 state machines **pause** (no transition from ES trades against the band during the block; they resume at the block end against the band then in force), so a bad map leaves no lasting crossing or flip; target selection skips that chain's path nodes while blocked. Same `ChainGuard`, the same in labels, both simulators and live (rule 4); applied to QQQ's R-dependent features as well, for events that are not on QQQ zones (QQQ zones are already blocked). Bumps `features_version`.
    - **(b) The guard with live board drops (step 7, 10-12 12:30–12:45: NDXP 28%, SPY 9%, QQQ 0.1% of boards never sent, gaps up to 25 s; `live/map_freshness.py`, 5-s per-map freshness block).** How it behaves live: (1) a dropped board only removes boards, so the per-board trigger fires the same or later, never earlier: a real jump needs two boards within 10 s on each side, which SPY at a 9% drop rate still has within about a second; QQQ is unaffected; (2) a gap longer than `ratio_gap_reset_sec` (10 s) **resets** both guard windows and F1.7's trailing median, so after it the R in force is the first board's own ratio, unchecked: the guard cannot compare it with the levels before the gap, and cannot fire until two boards on each side of a move exist inside the new segment; (3) the freshness block covers the gap itself (from 5 s), but lifts at the first board after it, which is exactly the unchecked single-board R. History has a board every second, so the backtest never sees (2) or (3) (the known live-vs-backtest difference of Q-mapstale). **Proposed:** (i) the guard's windows no longer reset on board gaps, only at the session start (the 2-minute window runs by time across a gap; the held-level rule already keeps a single board from firing), so a move that happened inside a gap fires on the second board after it; (ii) after a freshness block, the map stays blocked until a second board has arrived within `disl_guard_confirm_sec` of the first (the guard's own confirmation), so no entry is placed on a map whose R rests on one post-gap board. (i) runs in history too (it changes nothing on days without a board gap over 10 s; the dislocation count is re-run); (ii) is live only, like the freshness block.
  - **Q-dtrvol Day type and today's relative volume (proposed 2026-10-17 at the owner's request).** First proposal: swap `rv_iv_ratio` for `rvol_today` at each snapshot. **Held by the owner 2026-10-17**: `rv_iv_ratio` is the only input that carries implied volatility directly (`rv_nq` ÷ SVX30d(QQQ)); `range_vs_em` carries it only indirectly (the expected move = close × SVX1d(QQQ) ÷ √252, F0.8); `regime_net` (NDXP) is dealer positioning, not an IV level; the other five carry none. **Second proposal (open, nothing changed):** replace `ib_width_ratio` with `rvol_today`, keeping both IV inputs: after 10:00 `ib_width_ratio` is a constant per session that measures the same opening range `range_vs_em` contains, and it is the input that a busy open shares with `rvol_today`, while `rvol_today` keeps moving through the day. Not a 9th input: fold 7 trains on about 120 sessions, one effective row each, so with a trend-day share of 20–30% the 8 inputs already have only about 3–4 trend days each; the cap of 8 (`daytype_max_features`) exists for that. **Overlap check (owner 2026-10-17; months 1–6, no labels, 119 full sessions, 2,856 snapshots 10:00–15:45):** rank correlation of `rvol_today` with each input at the same snapshot time (median): `ib_width_ratio` +0.57 (+0.68 at 10:00), `range_vs_em` +0.36, `new_extreme_skew` +0.26, `rv_iv_ratio` −0.26, `regime_net` (NDXP) +0.10, `vwap_crosses` −0.07, `vwap_side_share` −0.07; `minutes_since_open` +0.07 pooled. At 10:00 `ib_width_ratio` vs `range_vs_em`: Spearman +0.57, R² 0.22 (log-log 0.32); `ib_width_ratio` vs `rvol_today` given `range_vs_em`: partial +0.66. So `ib_width_ratio` is the closest overlap but is **not** largely covered by `range_vs_em`: by the owner's rule the swap is **not made**. **✅ Answered (owner 2026-10-18): the 8 day-type inputs stay as they are; `rvol_today` is a candidate input of the later LightGBM day-type challenger (§7.1 Retraining, with the challenger's fixed input list).** (Check approximations: VWAP from 1-minute bars, NDX spot = QQQ spot × the previous NDX/QQQ close ratio, `regime_net` missing at 223 snapshots without an NDXP board in the last 15 s.)
  - **Q8b-4 Gate (findings R7–R10). Answered (owner 2026-10-08), written into §5.1, §6.2, §12 step 8b, F1.6 and F7:** gate pass = (a) bias 95% interval contains 0 or is within one futures tick, (b) the chosen estimator's p95 within 10% of the best possible R, the alignment check in place of spot lag, plus the owner's Atlas check; F1.6 stays a diagnostic; stop floors per level source (`stop_buffer_floor` QQQ 4.0 NQ = the crossing p95, NDXP pending its method, chart levels 0; SPY/SPX no NQ stop floor; the `stop_buffer` grid no longer drops values, since the floor applies per stop). `step8b precision` prints the per-source floors; `step8b bias`, `estimator`, `alignment` and `recorded-day` built 2026-10-08 (results in `docs/step8b_findings.md` R11). Test A: the crossing floor is **not** just the 1-second resolution. With the futures completely still across the crossing's board interval, 81% / 80% / 68% of the floor remains (QQQ 3.3 NQ, SPY 0.62 ES, SPX 0.53 ES at p95). So, by the owner's rule (c), stop floors come from the crossing p95, not the level error. Proposed: (a) bias within its day-block interval or one futures tick (all pass); (b) the chosen estimator's crossing p95 within 10% of the best possible R (gate run 2026-10-09 with `map_latency_sec` 0.59, each crossing using the board in force by its apply time: QQQ 4.010 vs 4.040 NQ, SPY 0.775 vs 0.791 ES, SPX 0.779 vs 0.781 ES (was 0.731 on 10-08, before the latency entered the gate): all pass; bias QQQ +0.022 [−0.017, +0.058] NQ, SPY 0.000 [−0.005, +0.005] ES, SPX +0.001 [−0.008, +0.010] ES; findings R11); (c) a stop floor per level source (the zone member that sets the far edge): QQQ 4.0 NQ, NDXP ≈ 14–15 NQ pending its method, chart levels 0, SPY/SPX none (they create no NQ stops); each node band ≥ its chain's crossing p95 (all pass; NDXP ±20 vs ≈ 15 is tight); F1.6 a diagnostic; spot-lag becomes the alignment check; the owner's Atlas replay check stays.
- **Golden-zone upper fib (owner decision 2026-10-14): 0.79, not 0.786.** Written into §5.8 (zone 0.5–0.79, the settled-past-0.79 invalidation and re-arm, stop candidate 2 past 0.79 + `stop_buffer`, `fib_level_reached` 0.5 / 0.618 / 0.705 / 0.79), §5.4 (containers, unresolved break), §5.5 (settled bar), §5.5b (pending-entry removal), §7.1 (structure label), §11 (features.yaml list), feature_spec (Values note, F5.6, F7, F10 with its test numbers, F14), CLAUDE.md's glossary and `features.yaml` (`golden_fib_levels`, `golden_zone_upper_fib`, new named parameters); `features_version` 0 → 1. The test example's zone edge moves from 20,042.8 to 20,042. No events, labels or models were built under version 0, so nothing is rebuilt.
- **Proposals of 2026-10-14 (owner's request; propose only, nothing changed; the owner decides):**
  - **Q-svxwknd Weekend-adjusted SVX for the expected move. ✅ Answered (owner 2026-10-22): the expected-move unit (F0.8, §5.2 Distances) uses Tempest's `em_1d_pct` as stored, never recomputed, the same m as the F3.11 lines; written into §5.2, feature_spec F0.8 / F3.11; `features_version` 7 (nothing built, nothing rebuilt).** Study (2026-10-22, read-only, the stored rows, sessions of months 1–6 only, §7 rule 2b: 125 sessions, 29 after a weekend or holiday, 96 the next day; realized move of session T ÷ the expected move from row T − 1; weekend-minus-next-day difference of the median ratio with a bootstrap 95% interval): RTH range with F0.8 as it was (SVX1d ÷ √252) QQQ +0.53 [+0.19, +0.76], SPY +0.44 [+0.16, +0.78] (also beyond 0 for close-to-close and open-to-close on both; ±1σ lines of that size touched on 83% of weekend sessions vs 31% of the others, QQQ); with Tempest's `em_1d_pct` −0.10 [−0.33, +0.08] / −0.13 [−0.33, +0.10] (touched 48% vs 53%); with `svx1d_adj` ÷ √252 −0.00 [−0.25, +0.19] / −0.04 [−0.27, +0.20]. Both alternatives remove the weekend bias within noise; `em_1d_pct` was chosen because it is the number of the lines and of Tempest's chart and needs no parameter. On every stored QQQ row `em_1d_pct` = SVX1d × √(minutes from that close to the next session's close ÷ 525,600) (early closes included, e.g. 2025-12-23: 1,261 min), except that on 2025-11-24 the row's `front_min` read 2,881 while the move used 1,441: so it is read as stored, never rebuilt from `front_min`. Monday check: the 10-16 (Friday) row, pulled 2026-10-22 as a check copy (`repull/tempest_recheck/`, 1 credit), has `em_1d_pct` 1.9090 (QQQ) / 1.3170 (SPY) and closes 709.82 / 763.335, exactly Tempest's lines for Monday 10-19 (seen in the 10-20 09:05 cones pull), so no live Monday reading is needed. Caveat: 29 weekend/holiday sessions, intervals wide. As first written (open): Today the expected move (§5.2 Distances) uses SVX1d(QQQ) from session T − `tempest_lag_sessions` (1), so a Monday or a session after a holiday uses the previous trading day's row, whose 1-day vol prices one trading day although a weekend or holiday sits in between; Tempest's weekend-adjusted value (`svx1d_adj` in the stored `/v1/vol/history` rows) is stored but is not a feature. **Proposed study (read-only, development months 1–6 only, §7 rule 2b; the rows are already stored, no credits):** (1) for every session T, the ratio `svx1d_adj` ÷ `svx1d` of row T − 1, by group: Monday, first session after a holiday, Tuesday–Friday otherwise (median and spread); (2) NQ's realized RTH range (high − low, 09:30 to the close) ÷ the expected move, with each SVX version, by the same groups: median, IQR and a bootstrap 95% interval of the difference Monday/post-holiday vs the others; the same for the absolute open-to-close move. **Proposed rule:** use the weekend-adjusted value for Mondays and post-holiday sessions (backtest and live alike, rule 4) only if, with `svx1d`, their range ÷ expected move differs from the other weekdays by more than its interval and `svx1d_adj` removes that difference; otherwise keep `svx1d`. **Caveats:** months 1–6 hold about 25 Mondays and 4–5 post-holiday sessions, so the intervals will be wide and the report says so; `svx1d_adj` also differs from `svx1d` on a mid-week row (QQQ 2026-10-07, a Wednesday: 15.44 vs 13.11), so it is not purely a weekend correction: its definition is checked in Skylit's documentation first and stated in the report. **Read-only finding (2026-10-14, the 554 stored QQQ/SPY pre-open rows 2025-08-28 … 2026-10-09):** `svx1d_adj` ÷ `svx1d` is 0.849 on every Monday–Thursday row and 1.342 on every Friday row, so it is a fixed re-weighting of weekend variance, not random; and Tempest's own one-day move in the same row, `em_1d` (= `close` × `em_1d_pct` ÷ 100, with `range_1d_high/low` = close ± `em_1d`), is `svx1d` ÷ 100 × √(calendar days to the next session's close ÷ 365): ÷ √365 on a Monday–Thursday row, × √3 on a Friday row (√4 before a 3-day weekend). Our F0.8 expected move uses ÷ √252 with no weekend term: on a Tuesday it is 1.20 × Tempest's, on a Monday 0.69 × (Friday's row × √3). The study therefore compares three versions: F0.8 as now, Tempest's `em_1d_pct` (calendar time) and `svx1d_adj` ÷ √252. Adopting it changes the expected-move unit of every distance feature, so it would bump the features version (§5.0). Not a new feature.
  - **Q-gzfib Golden-zone report by fib level reached. ✅ Approved (owner 2026-10-27), extended to report by zone (shallow / deep, Q-gzsplit below); written into §5.8 (Zone and fib-level report).** Add to the step-15/16 backtest report, next to the existing split by how many maps have a node in the zone (§5.8), the golden-zone results split by `fib_level_reached` (0.5 / 0.618 / 0.705 / 0.79, the deepest level reached by `decision_ts`): events, trades, win rate, mean R and expectancy per bucket with bootstrap intervals, and the §5.8 warning on any bucket under ~100 events. Development months 1–6 only (§7 rule 2b: a report that could change rules). Report only: no rule follows from it (rule 6); `fib_level_reached` is already a feature.
  - **Q-dtcross Day-type crosses on settled 5-minute bars (owner 2026-10-27). ✅ Decided and written into §5.3 (Day type inputs), §7.1 (day-type table and EOD label, the LightGBM day-type challenger list), §11, §12 step 13 (count report), feature_spec F3.10 and F11.6, features.yaml (`daytype_cross_bar_minutes` 5, `features_version` 23) and `features/daytype.py` with tests.** A VWAP or POC cross for the day type is a clock-aligned 5-minute bar settled on the other side (F0.5: open beyond as of the bar's open, close beyond as of its close), not a 1-minute close. Owner's reason: on trend days the pullback often pokes back through VWAP on 1-minute closes, which miscounts trend days as range days. Applies to the day-type input `vwap_crosses` (still one of the 8 `daytype_inputs`, same name), the EOD day-type label (crosses of the 09:30-anchored VWAP ≤ `trend_max_vwap_crosses`; until the follow-up below also of the developing daily POC ≤ `trend_max_poc_crosses`) and `poc_crosses` on the LightGBM day-type challenger list. The developing POC and VWAP are judged as of each bar's open and close, like moving bands. The first settled bar sets the side without counting; a bar that doesn't settle leaves the side unchanged. Thresholds stay 2 for now; the step-13 count report (months 1–6, counts only) shows the share of trend vs range days under the old 1-minute rule and the new 5-minute rule side by side, so the owner can revisit them before step 15. `vwap_side_share` and the other inputs are unchanged. Tests: a trend day whose pullback has three 1-minute closes below VWAP but no settled 5-minute bar below → 0 crosses (the old rule: 2); a chop day with settled 5-minute bars on alternating sides → counted.
  - **Q-dtcross follow-up: POC crosses out of the day-type label (owner 2026-10-27). ✅ Decided and written into §7.1 (day-type row, challenger list), §11, §12 step 13, feature_spec F3.10 and F11.6, features.yaml (`trend_max_poc_crosses` removed, `features_version` 24) and `features/daytype.py` (`eod_label`) with tests.** Reason: the developing POC can jump across price on trend days without price crossing anything, which would miscount trend days as range. The EOD label is now: trend if settled 5-minute crosses of the 09:30-anchored VWAP ≤ `trend_max_vwap_crosses` (2) and the close is in the outer `trend_close_pct` (20%) of the RTH range in the direction of the new highs/lows; else range. `poc_crosses` stays on the LightGBM day-type challenger list as a candidate input only. The step-13 report shows the trend share under the old rule (1-minute, VWAP + POC), the 5-minute rule with POC and the 5-minute rule without POC (now used).
  - **Q-dtside Which side are "the new highs/lows" in the day-type label? ✅ Answered (owner 2026-10-27; written into feature_spec F11.6 and `features/daytype.py` `trend_side` / `eod_label` with tests).** The trend side at the close = the sign of `new_highs` − `new_lows` at the close, the same F3.10 counts as the day-type input `new_extreme_skew` (its absolute value; no separate count): positive → up, negative → down, zero → range. The close must be in the outer `trend_close_pct` of the RTH range on that side, otherwise range. `eod_label` works the side out itself. Tests: a V-reversal day (the morning's new lows dominate, close in the top 20%) → range; a steady up day → trend (up). Part of the `features_version` 24 change (Q-dtcross follow-up). Confirmed (owner 2026-10-27): `new_extreme_skew` is |`new_highs` − `new_lows`| and has no sign, so the side is the sign of (`new_highs` − `new_lows`) at the close, as implemented.
  - **Q-trenddir The trade's direction against the day (owner 2026-10-27). ✅ Decided and written into §7.1 (structure model and pair scorer inputs), feature_spec F11.7 and F16.8 (ablation group 4), features.yaml (`features_version` 25) and `features/daytype.py` with tests; an owner exception to the feature freeze (§5.0 item 7).** Before this, the models had only the directionless P(trend) and `yesterday_day_type`; the direction-free day-type inputs (`vwap_side_share`, `new_extreme_skew`) feed only the day-type model, and §7.1's "distance to VWAP/bands" has no column in feature_spec and isn't signed to the trade. Reason: the owner's core read is "with the trend off retracements on trend days, fade extremes on range days"; P(trend) has no direction, so the models need the trade's direction against the day to learn it. Two features for the structure model (at the touch) and the pair scorer (at `decision_ts`), in ablation group 4 (day-type input): `with_day_direction` = trade direction (+1 long, −1 short) × sign(`new_highs` − `new_lows`) at t (the same F3.10 counts as `new_extreme_skew` and the label's trend side, §13 Q-dtside), 0 when equal; `vwap_side_vs_trade` = +1 if price is on the trade's side of the 09:30-anchored VWAP at t (long above, short below), −1 if not, 0 if within its band (VWAP ± `chart_level_half_width[vwap]`, §5.4b). Tests: a long pullback on an up day above VWAP → +1 / +1; a long fade at the low on a down day below VWAP → −1 / −1.

No rule change now; two step-13 reports and one Q-v2 item (docs only, no `features_version` change).** **(0) Question answered (no text changed):** under F2.11 as written, the confirmed ceiling held by the Q9 inside-band exception (NQ inside its band, the board spot above its strike) is **not excluded from the floor side**: the floor's candidates are the strikes below the board spot ("ceiling candidates = strikes above the board's spot (F2.1 two clocks); floor candidates = strikes below it"), the exception only keeps the node on the ceiling side ("Exception: the confirmed ceiling stays a candidate, and the ceiling, while the futures price … has not traded beyond its band's upper edge, even if the board spot is above its strike"), and the two sides run separately ("Floor roll-ups mirror all of this. Both sides can be active at once."). So if that node leads the floor candidates long enough for the Q6 rule, it becomes the confirmed floor while staying the confirmed ceiling: both at once (a floor roll-up if it was a floor candidate for the whole window W, else a price switch); `features/rolls.py` does the same (one `RollSide` per side, `_candidates` adds only that side's own confirmed node). **(1)** a reaction-distance report for QQQ and SPY nodes, report only, the ±0.5 bands stay fixed unless the owner changes them after reading it; **(2)** a count of the owner's scenario (a node held as the ceiling by Q9 while the Q6 rule makes it the floor, and the mirror) with what NQ did next; both in §12 step 13. **(3)** map-role events recorded in Q-v2 (Idea 4), to be reconciled with Q9, tested after the step-16 gate, sized by report (2). **Known case, kept as is (owner 2026-10-28):** F2.11's ceiling and floor sides stay independent, so a node may be the confirmed ceiling and the confirmed floor at once while NQ is inside its band; no rule change, the case is measured by report (2).

  - **Q-sideN Trades without an aggressor side (owner 2026-10-29). ✅ Decided and written into §3.2, §5.7, §12 step 17, feature_spec F0.1/F9.2–F9.5/F9.9/F9.10 and features.yaml (`side_n_max_share`); a clarification of existing definitions, allowed under the feature freeze; no `features_version` change (no built feature's output changes: `features/bars.py` already counted N in volume only; big trades, delta and the delta ratio are built at step 17).** Measured on months 1–6 (front contract): NQ (130 sessions) side N = 0.0087% of whole-day volume (6,043 of 69.3 M contracts), 0.00025% of RTH volume (131 contracts, two prints); 85% of it the 18:00:00 Globex reopen print, the rest in data-release seconds (08:30) and 16:30–16:31; the highest day 0.095% (2025-10-27); roll weeks no higher. ES (128 sessions): 0.0095% of whole-day volume (17,629 of 186.1 M), **no** RTH side-N print; 96% at 18:00:00, the rest 07:05–07:07, 08:30, 16:31; the highest day 0.25% (2026-01-19, the MLK holiday Globex day, thin volume), next 0.12% (2025-10-27). ES looks like NQ, so the same rule covers ES deltas (`rel_delta`, `xb_delta_rel`). Rule: a side-N print counts in volume wherever volume is used (bars, profiles, VWAP, the F9.10 normals, `vol_tod_w`, `rvol_today`) and is left out of everything signed or aggressor-based (delta, CVD, CVD slope, footprint A(p)/B(p) and imbalances, absorption, exhaustion, `aggressive_no_progress`, NQ/ES deltas, the delta ratio's numerator and denominator: ÷ B + A, so one-sided flow is ±1) and is never an aggressive order (never combined, never a big trade, never in the slot percentile). Check: the step-17 order-flow report lists each session's side-N share of RTH volume (NQ and ES) and flags one above `side_n_max_share` (0.01) for the owner. Tests: the bar test now; the big-trade and delta-ratio tests at step 17 (§12 step 17 checklist).

Spread legs (same check): B/A prints strictly outside the prior best bid/ask without a print at the touch are a median 0.10% of RTH volume (roll periods 0.185%); no fix (owner 2026-10-30); the step-17 report shows the share per session (§12 step 17).
  - **Q-gzsplit Golden zone split into a shallow and a deep zone (owner rule 2026-10-27). ✅ Decided and written into §5.4 (Containers, pending-entry removal), §5.8, §11, §12 step 13 (count report by zone), feature_spec F5.7 (event tags), F5.9 (Containers), F7, F10, features.yaml (`golden_zone_split_fib` 0.618, `features_version` 21) and `features/golden_zone.py` with tests.** Shallow zone 0.5–0.618, deep zone 0.618–0.79, both drawn from the same opening-range fib (F10: same orientation, flips and 10:30 redraw); whenever the drawing changes (a new extreme before 10:00 with an orientation change, a flip, the 10:30 redraw) both get new `zone_id`s. Each is its own golden-zone container (§5.4) with its own `zone_id`, touches (the normal touch rule with `zone_proximity`), re-arm, freshness, touch number today, earlier-touch outcomes (resolved only), structure label and events; levels inside each are that zone's confluence (nodes inside, Trinity inside, chart levels inside). Breaks: the shallow zone breaks when a 1-minute candle settles past 0.618 (open and close both past it, F0.5); this does not invalidate the setup or flip anything, and the deep zone is simply the next zone. The deep zone breaks on the existing 0.79 rule, which still triggers the orientation flip (with `golden_zone_flip` on). Each re-arms by the existing container rule against its own far edge (back on the original side and `rearm_dist` away). Unchanged: the 09:45–10:00 window and `in_golden_zone` apply to both zones; `fib_level_reached` stays; stop candidates (far edge + `stop_buffer` per zone, the next structure behind, candidate 6 past the range extreme) and targets work as now; the golden-zone variant tests (flip, redraw) apply to the split drawing. New feature `golden_zone_part` (shallow / deep). Reports: golden-zone results by zone and by fib level reached (Q-gzfib), any bucket under 100 events flagged. Tests: a pullback through 0.5 then 0.618 makes two touches (shallow, then deep) with separate `zone_id`s; a settle past 0.618 breaks the shallow zone only; a settle past 0.79 breaks the deep zone (the shallow one too, being past 0.618) and flips the orientation; both get new `zone_id`s after a flip. **Touch rule (owner answer 2026-10-27, `features_version` 22; replaces the first version's normal touch rule for the deep zone, under which price within `zone_proximity` of 0.618 touched it and one trade could touch both zones in one touch group):** the deep zone is touched only by a trade past 0.618 into it from the shallow side, never by coming within `zone_proximity` of 0.618 (owner 2026-10-27, §13 Q-gzsplit answer); its touch is a new touch with its own touch group, never shared with a shallow-zone touch, so after a shallow-zone trade has ended (e.g. stopped past 0.618) the deep zone can still trade, subject as always to one trade per account at a time and the direction lock; `zone_proximity` still applies to touches from outside the golden zone (the shallow zone approached from the 0 side); the deep zone re-arms back on the shallow side of 0.618 and `rearm_dist` away, and the next trade past 0.618 is a new deep-zone touch. Owner tests: (1) a long shallow-zone trade fills at 0.55 and is stopped past 0.618; price then trades to 0.66 → a deep-zone event with its own touch group, eligible to trade (the event starts at the first trade past 0.618, the stop trade in the test, and 0.66 is inside it); (2) price within `zone_proximity` of 0.618 that turns at 0.61 → no deep-zone touch; a turn at 0.625 → a deep-zone touch (deeper fib numbers = a deeper pullback). `features/golden_zone.py` `GoldenTouches` with tests; written into §5.4 Containers, §5.8, feature_spec F5.6, F5.7, F5.10, F10.
  - **Q-dayview Day-level view (report only, open).** Add to the step-15/16 report (months 1–6 only) a table of the bot's daily results (trades, net R, win rate, the day's worst drawdown in R) next to conditions known at the open: the gap (RTH open vs the previous RTH close, points and ATR), the overnight range (points and ATR), the vol context (SVX1d, realized vs implied, §5.2), the regime at the open (§5.2), news day (a release on the owner's high-impact list, §9) and the day type as known at the open (yesterday's day type, §5.2 Day memory; the day-type model's reading at 10:00; the realized day type only in a separate column marked not known at the open). Each condition in 2–3 buckets with the days per bucket, mean daily R and a bootstrap interval. **Multiple comparisons:** about 6 conditions × 3 buckets ≈ 18 comparisons on about 120 days, so about one bucket would look different at 5% by chance alone; the report prints the number of comparisons and Holm-adjusted p-values, and nothing in it changes a rule (rule 6). No new feature: day memory (§5.4) and the day-type model already carry "how today is going".
  - **Q-sigma Expected-move (sigma) levels as a new registry level type (proposed 2026-10-14 at the owner's request). ✅ Answered (owner 2026-10-14): approved as written; ±1σ and ±2σ only; the lines use Tempest's convention (previous RTH close, QQQ's `em_1d_pct`, calendar days, Friday rows wider) so they match its chart; the expected-move unit (F0.8) stays as it is until Q-svxwknd is answered; weekly and monthly lines later. Written into §3.1, §5.4 (registry, Distances), §11, feature_spec F3.11 / F5.1 / F5.14 / F12 and features.yaml (`em_level_sigmas`, the `em_level` half-width, cap and flip-margin keys, in the `chart_lines` flip-margin group); `features_version` 1 → 2 (nothing built, nothing rebuilt). Live check approved and built: `nqbot step7 sigma-check` (1 cones credit, plus 1 for the pre-open history row if not yet stored), run at 09:05 and 12:00 ET on three sessions without a recording (2026-10-16, 10-19, 10-20; moved off 10-15, which took the third NDXP recording after 10-14's was lost to the restart; Windows task `nqbot sigma check`).** **Live check done (2026-10-20 and 10-22; the 10-21 runs failed with the SSD unplugged); ✅ answered (owner 2026-10-22): F3.11 kept as written (the stored pre-open row's `em_1d_pct`, symmetric ±1σ/±2σ, fixed all session); from step 22 the pre-open job logs the gap between the cones' `day` lines and the row; the check's two issues are fixed when it is next touched.** Findings: (a) 10-20 12:00: Tempest's lines = the bot's from the stored row within $0.0006 on all four lines, QQQ and SPY (anchor = the row's close, 1σ = its `em_1d_pct`); (b) at 09:05 on both days Tempest still showed the previous session's `day` set (`until` = the previous session), switched by 12:00, so those two runs compared the wrong day (the check reported `stale: false`: issue 1, flag a set whose `until` is before today as not switched yet); (c) ±2σ is symmetric about Tempest's own anchor in all 8 `day` sets (the check measured it about the bot's anchor: issue 2); the skew-shaped up/down moves exist only in the moving `horizons`; (d) the week and month lines were identical in all four pulls across three sessions (lines fixed once priced; the 1d horizon priced from spot moved: QQQ 1.267% at 09:05 → 1.467% at 12:00); (e) 10-22 12:00: Tempest priced the 10-21 lines at QQQ 725.25 / 1.3336% and SPY 773.13 / 0.7532%, the stored pre-open copy read 725.705 / 1.3317% and 773.44 / 0.7437%: that copy was an unfinished draft (QQQ/SPY/SPX: no OHLC, a fractional `front_min`, about half the `n_used`; a re-pull at 20:21 ET equals the cones exactly; NDXP and VIX were final; answered 2026-10-22: `tempest_lag_sessions` stays 1 with the draft rule, §3.1 Tempest, and the stored copy is replaced by the final row, §13 Tempest finality); on the NQ lines (anchored at NQ's own close) the gap was ≈ 0.6 pts at 1σ, 1.1 at 2σ. Credits: 8 in all (6 sigma-check, 2 follow-up pulls). Proposal as written: Tempest's sigma and cones modules have no history (§3.1), so the bot draws the lines itself, from the stored pre-open `/v1/vol/history` row, identical in backtest and live (rule 4). They are structure points like the VWAP bands: registry levels that create events, merge into zones, count as confluence and serve as stop-behind and target candidates, and features of the structure model and the pair scorer.
    - **Which anchor and tenor match Tempest's own lines:** Tempest's `/v1/vol/cones` `levels[]` `day` set (the lines it draws on the chart) is **anchored at the prior close and priced at that close, from the prior close to today's close, and does not move intraday** (`hi1`/`lo1` 1σ, `hi2`/`lo2` 2σ); `/v1/vol/sigma`'s `running_sigma` measures today's move in units of that same move. So: **anchor = the previous RTH close, tenor = 1 day (SVX1d)**, not today's open (Tempest's `horizons[]`, priced from the current spot, move with price and are not lines). The stored row already carries Tempest's one-day 1σ move: `em_1d_pct` (with `range_1d_high/low` = close ± `em_1d` in every stored row checked), which is SVX1d in **calendar time** (÷ √365, × √3 over a weekend; see the finding under Q-svxwknd), not F0.8's ÷ √252. To match Tempest's lines the bot uses `em_1d_pct` as stored.
    - **Proposed feature_spec wording (new F3.11; registry row in §5.4 "Expected-move levels | ±1σ, ±2σ around the previous RTH close | no"; level-type key `em_level` in F5.14):**
      > **F3.11 Expected-move (sigma) levels.** *Definition:* for session T, with row = the pre-open `/v1/vol/history` row of session T − `tempest_lag_sessions` (QQQ for NQ, SPY for ES) and C = the NQ RTH close of session T − 1 (the last front-contract trade at or before the RTH close, roll-shifted to today's front contract; ES: the ES close): m = `em_1d_pct` ÷ 100 of the row; the levels are C × (1 + k·m) for k ∈ `em_level_sigmas` (start {−2, −1, +1, +2}; owner rule, never tuned). Fixed for the whole session (they do not move intraday). With `tempest_lag_sessions` = 2 the row's own move spans the wrong day, so m = SVX1d of that row ÷ 100 × √(calendar days from the T − 1 close to the T close ÷ 365) instead (the same calendar-time rule). NQ lines are registry levels (chart-level state machine F5.2: crossed = a settled 1-minute candle beyond the band, flipped = a settled 5-minute bar, F5.14 half-width `chart_level_half_width[em_level]` frozen at step 13 from the reaction-distance report); ES lines are cross-market features only (F0.9). Not sweep-eligible (they are computed lines like the VWAP bands, not traded highs/lows). *Outputs:* the levels as zone members; `running_sigma` = (price − C) ÷ (C·m), signed; the distance to the nearest line above and below (pts, ATR, EM); which line (k) a zone member is. *Known at:* the pre-open job (the row is pulled at or after 08:30 ET, §3.1 Tempest; C is known at T − 1's close). *Missing row:* no lines that session (never a stale row), as for the other vol-context features. *Test:* C = 20,000, `em_1d_pct` 0.80 → ±1σ 19,840 / 20,160, ±2σ 19,680 / 20,320; a Friday row with SVX1d 15.4381 and `tempest_lag_sessions` = 2 → m = 15.4381 ÷ 100 × √(3 ÷ 365) = 1.400% from Friday's close to Monday's.
    - **Live check (proposal; a small step-7-style command after approval):** on 3 sessions, at 09:00–09:29 ET pull `/v1/vol/cones?symbols=QQQ,SPY` (1 credit per call; one more call at about 12:00 to confirm the `day` lines did not move; ≈ 6 credits in all, printed first, cached under `raw/skylit/vol/cones/`, never a feature input). Compare `levels[day]`: `anchor`/`priced_at` = the previous close, `em1_pct` vs the stored row's `em_1d_pct` (within 0.001 percentage points), `hi1`/`lo1` vs close ± `em_1d` (within $0.01), and whether `hi2`/`lo2` are close ± 2·`em_1d` (symmetric) or skew-shaped (then the ±2σ lines need the owner's decision: the history has no skewed 2σ, so a skewed live line cannot be rebuilt for the backtest and the bot keeps the symmetric one). The NQ lines are the bot's own conversion (C × the QQQ percent), which Tempest does not draw; the check covers the QQQ/SPY lines and the method. A mismatch is reported to the owner, nothing is changed by itself.
    - **Already used today (answer to the owner's question):** the expected move is already an input: every distance feature is also stored in expected-move units (F0.8; nodes' magnet pull is in EM units, F2.10), the day-type model gets `range_vs_em` (the RTH range so far ÷ the expected move, F3.10) and the daily vol context (SVX 1d/9d/30d/3m/6m, realized vs implied, F2.21) goes to the structure, day-type and pair-scorer models. The gamma regime (F2.12) does not use it (`regime_band` is 1% of spot). What is new here is the lines themselves as structure (levels that create events and act as stop and target candidates) and `running_sigma`.
    - **To decide:** (1) approve the level type and the F3.11 wording; (2) the line set: ±1σ and ±2σ only, or also ±0.5σ (start {−2, −1, +1, +2}); (3) Tempest's calendar-time move for the lines (recommended, to match its chart) while F0.8's EM unit stays as it is until Q-svxwknd is decided, or one convention for both; (4) whether the weekly and monthly Tempest lines (`levels[]` `week`/`month`, anchored at last week's close / the last monthly expiration's close) are wanted later (they can be rebuilt from SVX9/SVX30 the same way; not proposed now). Adopting it bumps the features version (§5.0); the dataset grows by one level type (more events), so the step-13 reaction report and the half-width freeze include it.
  - **Q-riskfrac Risk per trade as a per-account fraction of the drawdown (proposed 2026-10-14 at the owner's request). ✅ Answered (owner 2026-10-14): approved with whole numbers only (N = 2, 3 default, 4; nothing below 1/4, since Conservative and Protect already lower risk); the risk amount follows the setting automatically; the L-loss limit stays 0.15 for every N; the field is renamed `max_loss_run_rate` (the gate's `default_max_three_loss_rate` keeps its name and 1/3); a non-default fraction makes the account stale for one sweep night (no preset entries at all three fractions); the confirmation wording as proposed. Written into §6.1, §6.5b, §6.7, §6.8, §9b, §11, feature_spec F16, settings.yaml (`max_loss_run_rate`) and `nqbot/risk.py` (the divisor check in the config loader, the cap, lives, L and the loss-run rate, with tests).** Proposal as written: Today every account risks at most drawdown ÷ 3 (`max_risk_fraction_divisor` 3, fixed). Proposed: the owner may set it per account between 1/4 and 1/2.
    - **§6.1 (proposed wording):** "**Per-trade risk cap = a static 1/N of the account's total drawdown**, N = `max_risk_fraction_divisor`, set per account on the Accounts page: **2, 3 (default) or 4** (1/2, 1/3, 1/4; upper limit 1/2 = N ≥ 2, enforced in the UI, in the config loader and again right before every webhook). Actual risk = min(`risk_usd_per_trade` × profile multiplier (Custom: `custom_risk_usd`), `drawdown_usd` ÷ N). `risk_usd_per_trade` follows the setting: when N changes the UI sets it to floor(`drawdown_usd` ÷ N) (the owner may type a lower amount). N full losses use up the drawdown; the next loss fails the account. The last trade is still not sized down to fit what's left." Every "÷ 3" / "3 lives" in §6.1 (the stop-slippage note, Account R's nominal-risk cap) becomes ÷ N / N lives. Examples on a $1,000 drawdown: 1/4 = $250 (4 lives), 1/3 = $333 (3), 1/2 = $500 (2: after one full loss the account is on its last life).
    - **Raising risk needs a typed confirmation (§6.7/§9b, proposed):** lowering risk (N goes up, e.g. 3 → 4) applies from the next trade with the previous thresholds (an exposure-reducing edit, §6.8); raising risk (N goes down, e.g. 3 → 2) opens a dialog that states the new risk in dollars, the lives it leaves and the full losses that fail the account, and saves only after the owner types the account name and the new fraction (e.g. `mffur1 1/2`); it then waits for the account's sweep and takes effect at the next session after it (an edit that raises risk, §6.8). `!` Discord commands cannot change N.
    - **§6.5b, the loss-run limit follows the lives the risk allows (proposed):** the "three straight full losses" limit becomes an **L-loss-run** limit, L = the lives at a full drawdown = floor((`drawdown_usd` − `lives_dust_usd`) ÷ risk) + 1 (1/2 → 2, 1/3 → 3, 1/4 → 4): the bootstrapped share of overlapping L-trade runs that are all full losses ≤ the account's limit (field renamed `max_loss_run_rate`; same method, runs across days, day-block bootstrap). The lives formula itself already works for any N and stays; the last life is still lives ≤ 1, so at 1/2 the bot is on its last life after any single full loss and the last-life cutoff (`last_life_top_pct`, auto from {0.10, 0.20, 0.33}) applies much more often; the prop simulator's lives on/off report shows it per fraction. **gate_account stays at 1/3** (`default_risk_usd` 333, a 3-loss run, `default_max_three_loss_rate` 0.15): account-independent choices and gates 16/20 do not change, and the models work in R, so no retraining.
    - **§6.8, profiles and the sweep (proposed):** the profile multipliers apply to the account's own base risk (drawdown ÷ N); "risk never goes above drawdown ÷ 3 in any profile" becomes ÷ N (the account's setting, at most 1/2); Custom's `custom_risk_usd` ≤ drawdown ÷ N. N (with the derived L and `max_loss_run_rate`) joins the sweep-relevant fields: changing it marks the account stale and re-runs its threshold sweep (§6.8 overnight queue). The step-15/19 sweeps and the production fit write `profiles.yaml` entries for every preset stage at each of 1/4, 1/3 and 1/2 (3 × the preset sweep time), so a new account on a preset stage at any allowed fraction copies its entry and is not stale.
    - **§6.7 (proposed):** the field comment becomes `max_risk_fraction_divisor: 3 # 2, 3 or 4: risk ≤ 1/N of TOTAL drawdown (static, never shrinks; owner sets per account, raising risk (N down) needs the typed confirmation)`; `max_three_loss_rate` → `max_loss_run_rate` (start 0.15). **Prop-simulator report** per account and stage: 1/4, 1/3 and 1/2 side by side, each with its own threshold sweep: pass rate, days to pass (median and 75th percentile), payouts (count and dollars per funded run, dollars per evaluation started), breach rate (drawdown breaches; `inactive_close` apart), expected profit per evaluation started, and the share of trades whose size a cap cut below the full risk (by cap). Out-of-fold months only, as the §6.8 profile figures.
    - **Where the caps limit size anyway (passive-limit entry, 1 tick exit slippage, MFFU commissions; a stop tighter than this cannot use the full risk):** 25k accounts (max 2 minis): 1/4 ($250) below 5.8 NQ pts, 1/3 ($333) below 7.8, 1/2 ($500) below 12.0. Pro 50k evaluation (3 minis, $2,000): $500 below 7.9 pts, $666 below 10.6, $1,000 below 16.2. Pro 50k funded (5 contracts in total): $500 below 4.5, $666 below 6.2, $1,000 below 9.5. **Consistency caps:** Rapid EOD evaluation $450/day: at 1/2 ($500) every trade's 1:1-or-better win exceeds $450, so every trade is sized down to win ≤ $450 (effective risk ≤ $450 ÷ RR): 1/2 gives that stage almost nothing over 1/3 (at 1/3 a trade up to 1.35 RR is full size). Builder funded $1,050/day: full size at 1/2 up to 2.1 RR; after the first payout ($500/day) only up to 1.0 RR. Pro 50k evaluation $1,500/day: 1/2 ($1,000) full size up to 1.5 RR. **Builder's $600 DLL:** at 1/2 a full loss leaves $100 for the day (about one full-size trade a day); at 1/3, $267. The UI shows these effects next to the setting (the effective risk at a typical stop and RR).
    - **To decide:** (1) the values: 2, 3, 4 only (recommended: whole numbers keep 'N losses fail the account' exact; at 2.5 = $400 three losses would exceed the drawdown, so only two are survived) or any fraction in [1/4, 1/2]; (2) whether anything below 1/4 is allowed (it only lowers risk); (3) `risk_usd_per_trade` following the setting automatically (recommended) or typed separately; (4) the loss-run limit at L ≠ 3: keep 0.15 for the L-run (recommended: the chance of a run that fails the account from a full drawdown stays the same, so 1/2 needs a higher win rate, about 61% vs 47%, and 1/4 less, about 38%) or scale it to keep the same implied loss rate (0.15^(L/3): 0.28 at L = 2, 0.08 at L = 4); (5) the field rename `max_three_loss_rate` → `max_loss_run_rate` (the gate's `default_max_three_loss_rate` keeps its name); (6) preset entries at all three fractions (3 × preset sweep time, recommended) or a one-night stale period for a non-default fraction; (7) the confirmation wording. Nothing is changed until the owner approves; then §6.1, §6.5b, §6.7, §6.8, §9b and feature_spec F16 (sweep) are updated in one commit.
  - **Q-flipretest Post-cross retests and cross features (proposed 2026-10-23 at the owner's request). ✅ Approved (owner 2026-10-23), items 1–6 as proposed; written into §5.2, §5.4, §7 rule 5, §7.1, §9b, §12 steps 13 and 15, feature_spec F2.6 / F2.6b / F3.9 / F5.2–F5.7 / F5.9 / F5.15 / F8 / F11.4 / F16.8 and settings.yaml (`ablation_groups` 10 `post_cross_retests`); `features_version` 8 (nothing built, nothing rebuilt).** (1) After a cross a level holds the opposite role provisionally, arms on the crossed side by the F5.6 rule and its retests from that side are events, before any 5-minute settle; one fixed set of cross features for every level type (F2.6b), the settled 5-minute bar one of them; the structure model's held/broke label learns which crossed levels hold in their new role; no new model; the golden zone keeps its own flip (§5.8). (2) The model weighs distance, volume and time freely; a SHAP and single/pair/all-three table against the 5-minute rule is for the owner's understanding only (step 15). (3) Gameplan zones break when price trades past the far edge (their `invalidation` a feature only; owner 2026-10-23, which fixed §7.1's old wording); after the cross they follow the same post-cross retests (`flip_margin` 0, containers have no key); a flip the text states ("lost → becomes resistance") is recorded by the parser version of the step-20 re-parse (F8 `stated_flip`, `gp_stated_flip_agrees`), never inferred; the archived gameplans are counted read-only for stated flips. (4) Fixed checkpoints: the step-13 count-only report (crosses, reclaims, flips, retests before vs after a flip, post-cross bucket sizes per group, any under 100 flagged); touch groups unchanged (a retest needs `rearm_dist` first, so it never shares the cross's trade); freshness per role as F5.11 (retests count as tests of the new role); ablation group 10; lookahead: every cross feature a running value ≤ t, settled bars only once closed, node changes from boards applied ≤ t, volume normals from prior sessions, null in a dislocation block (F2.6 step 6), the rule's bucket rates fitted on training folds only, a causal truncation test per feature. (5) The pre-declared step-15 test (§12 step 15): beat the 5-minute rule on pooled held-out log loss over months 1–6 with a by-day bootstrap 95% interval excluding 0 and no worse after a flip; if it fails, the retests and cross features are removed and nothing else changes. (6) The Control Panel level ladder and chart styles (§9b Live status).
